# Plan: Phase 10, Android

**Source:** `docs/PLAN.md` §3 (the technology table), §6 Phase 10 ("Android (iOS moves to the next release)") and "Next release: iOS", §9 (risks); ADR 0002 (tiers and honesty), ADR 0004 (Kotlin Multiplatform), ADR 0008 (flows decided on the server, written by Phase 9), and the Phase 5, 7, 8 and 9 plans, which Phase 10 builds on. Two research notes were taken on 2026-10-29 for this plan and are committed with it under `docs/research/`:
- `phase10-android-behaviour.md` (cited **behaviour**): the public behaviour of the vendor's Android SDK and its mobile integration (entry points, result contract, events, permissions, capture, barcode, selfie, NFC, device signals, theming, languages, accessibility, releases), the gap tables M1–M43, the items earlier plans carried to Phase 10 (behaviour K1–K16), the proposed parity and ours lists, owner questions O1–O12 and a fact-check (20 confirmed, 1 corrected: the list of minor releases with breaking changes also needs 2.2.0);
- `phase10-android-engineering.md` (cited **engineering**): the Phase 10 items against the code at `91c83e02`, the carried items (engineering K1–K21), CameraX and the frame pipeline, on-device document quality, ONNX Runtime and YuNet, PDF417, device integrity and Play Integrity, NFC and the licence boundary, Maven Central publishing, R8, 16 KB pages, target API, size budgets, API compatibility, the tests, keeping `commonMain` iOS-ready, the pins, "Needs the owner" 1–15 with defaults, its gaps and a fact-check (30 confirmed, 0 corrected). Decision 60 records how each note's fact-check and gaps are taken.

The two notes number their carried items independently; this plan cites them as behaviour K<n> and engineering K<n>. The Phase 11 notes (`phase11-hardening.md`, `phase11-connect.md`) were read for what Phase 11 takes from Phase 10 (the Android threat-model rows B11-T1, B11-I1 and B11-S1, the MASVS v2.1.0 checklist, Gradle dependency visibility for scanning, the share-consent step in `commonMain`), so Phase 10 neither duplicates nor blocks them (C223). Downloads made during research stay out of git.

**Complexity:** Very large. Five Workflows of at most ten agents each:
- **10A, the renderer (ten agents):** contracts and the Phase 10 inventory; plumbing (the Gradle hygiene, the version property, the API dump, dependency locking and verification, the server's frozen routes); then the pure Kotlin logic (the strict visibility evaluator, the pattern matcher, digit normalisation, SHA-256, copy and locale resolution) and the five SDK locales in parallel; then the server-position driver with the migration of the existing coordinator suites, the public API's plumbing with the registry, events and consent digests, the screen step and its components, themes and the help slot, and the server amendments; then integration.
- **10B, capture and device integrity (nine agents):** contracts; server plumbing for device integrity; then the frame pipeline, document guidance, PDF417 guidance, the selfie guidance's common half, the device-integrity heuristics and the server's integrity provider in parallel; then integration.
- **10C, NFC in the SDK, shipping and the demo (eight agents):** contracts; then the NFC step and its screens with the sandbox test chip, the server's sandbox chip and client-unable routes, the web flow's NFC screen, R8 and the release checks, the demo app, and the Android docs; then integration.
- **10D, accessibility, review and fixes (nine agents):** the Android accessibility pass; a review of the whole phase in four lenses; a verifier; three path-owned fixers.
- **10E, the owner-gated artifacts (up to eight agents):** `openverify-face` (ONNX Runtime and YuNet), the JMRTD reader in `openverify-nfc`, `openverify-integrity-play`, and the first Maven Central release; each part starts only once its owner decision is recorded (decisions P1–P4), and each has a stated outcome if the owner declines.

**Why this split.** The server now decides every step (ADR 0008), so the first thing the SDK needs is a driver that renders the server's position and the screens Phase 9 adds; capture, NFC and shipping all sit on that driver and its contracts, which 10A freezes in its Stage 1a, as Phase 9's 9A froze the flow. Capture guidance and device integrity (10B) share the new frame pipeline and the Phase 7 signal contract but need nothing of the NFC work; the NFC step (10C) needs the driver and the server's chip routes, and its sandbox test chip lets every NFC screen be built and tested end to end without the LGPL reader. Shipping lands in 10C because R8 rules, the size baseline and the 16 KB checks must measure what 10A and 10B added. 10D reviews everything built. Everything that needs an owner decision reserved by the binding rules (an exception to `CLAUDE.md` rule 4, or the owner's accounts and keys) is in 10E, in separate optional artifacts, so 10A–10D build, test and ship without it. Each Workflow is pushed once, after a full local validation (Rules).

**Stage 1b carries plumbing only**, as in Phases 6–9: migrations, repository and route stubs with frozen schemas, Gradle wiring, masking lists, shared hooks and configuration. Every algorithm stays with its owner behind a declared interface. Stage 2 starts as soon as the Stage 1a commits it needs land, beside Stage 1b, and each Stage 3 agent waits for its family's Stage 1b checkpoint and its named Stage 2 dependency. Each checkpoint is committed with every suite green, so a pause resumes from the last one.

**Phase 7, 8 and 9 dependency.** Phase 10 starts only once `docs/PROGRESS.md` records Phase 9 done; until then the Phase 7, 8 and 9 plans are the interfaces it relies on, and each is named where it is used. In short:
- **Phase 9, the flow:** flow-spec v2 and its step types (C147), screens and the component catalogue v1 with field binding, validation, the restricted pattern grammar and its linear-time matcher (C148), template fields and `prefillFrom` (C148.7, C149), the strict visibility subset (C148.4), the server engine with `position`, the client view `step`, `values`, `locales`, `copy`, `theme`, `completion` and `spec-version` in the session response, the `OpenVerify-Flow-Spec` negotiation and the v1 projection (C151.5–C151.7), waits (C150.5), `exhausted` fallbacks (C150.6), the adverse-path contest route (C150.7), the halted state (C151.2), screen submission (C148.5), `captureMethodsByDevice` and the upload's `capture-method` (C153.2), the theme contract (C160.7) with the hex palette and the contrast gate (C160.3, C160.4), the theme-asset flow route (C160.6), locales and right-to-left (C161), operator copy and its rules (C162.2–C162.6), the consent evidence (C162.9) with `contracts/fixtures/i18n/` and `consent-texts.json` (C161.8), the selfie-capture extension route (C171.4), and C175.1, the list Phase 9 hands to Phase 10;
- **Phase 7:** `FlowApi.sendSignals`, the `FlowSignals` model, the `DeviceSignals` seam with debugger facts and the `ANDROID_ID` HMAC, collection tiers after consent (C94.2, C94.6, C94.7), the signal registry with tiers, `null` reasons and `ON_NULL_DEFAULTS` (C90), the SDK header parsed into `mobile-sdk-name` (C91, C94.5), the `device_handoff` step and its Android model entry (C96.1), the SDK README per tier (C109);
- **Phase 8:** the adverse-decision rules a flow failure inherits (C128), read only through Phase 9;
- **Phase 5:** the chip challenge, upload and submit (C62.5), Passive and Active Authentication on the server (C62.2–C62.6), the synthetic chips of `@openverify/emrtd/testing` and the Utopia sandbox anchor (C62.8, C65.3), the provider kinds and sandbox bindings (C63).

Each Stage 1a re-verifies every fact under "Starting point" and every Phase 5, 7, 8 and 9 interface it uses against the code it finds, and records drift as a "Workflow 10A (or 10B, 10C, 10E) Stage 1a amendment".

**Review of this plan.** As for Phases 6–9, three adversarial reviews (privacy, security and law; honesty and product coverage; feasibility and fit with the code) read the first draft, and `docs/plans/phase-10-review-resolution.md` disposes of each of their 80 findings (the decisions' "Review" subsection records the counts and the main changes); a verification round checked the resolutions, and its findings (V01–V14) are applied in this plan, as the resolution's "Verification round" section records.

## Summary

OpenVerify's Android SDK renders today's flows from a list of fixed steps it walks itself, with its own JSONLogic subset; Phase 9 moves every routing decision to the server and adds screens, components, waits, fallbacks, themes, operator copy, five locales and consent evidence that the SDK does not know, so after Phase 9 today's SDK runs linear flows through the v1 projection and stops on its unsupported screen at the first v2-only step (Phase 9 C151.6). The SDK also takes every photo with a manual shutter, measures nothing before upload, cannot read a barcode or a chip, reports no device-integrity facts, is unpublished, has no consumer R8 rules, no API dump and no size or page-size checks, and its NFC module is empty. Phase 10 builds them, each at its tier (ADR 0002):
- **R** (real logic): the server-position driver and the renderer for flow-spec v2's client view, screens and components; the strict visibility evaluator, the pattern matcher and digit normalisation, ported to Kotlin against the server's own vectors; themes, operator copy, locales and right-to-left; consent digests; the chip reader's protocol order, upload and submit, with Passive and Active Authentication still verified on the server; the release machinery (API dump, R8 rules, 16 KB and size checks, a staged publication).
- **H** (heuristic, guidance or a signal, never a result): on-device document quality gating, PDF417 readability and selfie pose guidance, which only decide when to take a photo and what hint to show; root, emulator and hooking indicators, which become `beta` signals and are never decisive alone (C210).
- **A** (an adapter with a deterministic sandbox provider): Google Play Integrity, off by default, decoded and judged on the server (C211).

What lands:
- **The public API** (C190–C192): the activity-result contracts unchanged in shape, with `locale` and `appearance` on `Request` and `OneTimeLink`; results that carry no field value and no token, with a published reason table and one new reason, `FLOW_HALTED`; an optional, PII-free event listener; one process-wide `OpenVerifyAndroid.install` for the optional components (chip reader, face detector, integrity token provider) and the host's transport, so `:openverify-sdk` never depends on an optional artifact; a public surface narrowed to the entry points, results, registry, events and seams before the first ABI dump.
- **The renderer** (C193–C197): the SDK declares flow-spec 2, renders the step the server's `position` names from its client view, never evaluates a transition, and shows its unsupported screen for anything it cannot render; the `screen` step with C148's catalogue in Compose; visibility and patterns through Kotlin ports that pass `packages/flow-spec`'s vectors; prefilled values, write-once and masked fields; waits, the halted state, fallbacks, the contest route on a failed inquiry, and the help and privacy links in a fixed slot.
- **Themes and languages** (C198–C200): the session theme mapped onto Material 3 with an `appearance` option, logos from the theme-asset route, the system font; `en`, `es`, `fr`, `de` and `ar` for every SDK string, machine-drafted and marked so, with right-to-left layout set by the SDK itself; operator copy with `{name}` placeholders, mapped onto the SDK's own strings, failing closed for legal texts; the consent digests, copy source and catalog version on every consent, with Android's consent texts appended to the server's table.
- **Capture** (C201–C206): a CameraX `ImageAnalysis` stream beside preview and capture, with a fallback to today's manual shutter; document sharpness, glare, brightness and framing measures in `commonMain` with client constants in a shared fixture; PDF417 readability through zxing-cpp; the selfie guidance's YuNet pre- and post-processing and pose machine ported from the web flow; auto-capture on, with the shutter always available and promoted after 10 s; the 2.2.1 warning and extension; `capture-method` and a client-reported `capture-trigger` on uploads.
- **NFC** (C207–C209): the `nfc` step on Android with the MRZ or CAN entry, the tap screen, progress, retries and the DG1 review; the challenge fetched before the tap so one tap reads and signs; a `client-unable` route so a phone without NFC takes the author's fallback; a sandbox test chip built by the server; the JMRTD reader in `openverify-nfc` (PACE first, BAC fallback, Chip Authentication reported, Active Authentication over the server's nonce), pending the SCUBA decision (P1).
- **Device integrity** (C210–C211): emulator, root and hooking indicators in Phase 7's session-integrity tier, as three `beta` signals; a Play Integrity challenge, a server provider with a sandbox and a common `IntegrityTokenProvider`; the Play client artifact pending the owner (P3).
- **Quality, security and shipping** (C212–C220): an Android accessibility pass mapped to WCAG 2.2 AA with automated checks and a manual TalkBack checklist; `FLAG_SECURE`, no PII in logs, captures in memory; `explicitApi()`, an ABI dump with a CI check, one version property, dependency locking; consumer R8 rules proven on a minified demo build; 16 KB alignment and per-ABI size checks; Roborazzi screenshots; a staged publication with sources and Dokka docs, uploaded to Maven Central only once the owner's namespace and keys exist (P4); the demo app; the README, changelog and Data safety guidance.
- **Owner-gated** (C205, C209, C211.6, C217.5): the face detector on ONNX Runtime with Eigen inside (P2), the JMRTD reader (P1), the Play Integrity client (P3) and the Central upload (P4), each in its own artifact or workflow so the rest ships without it.

ADR 0009, "Android: optional artifacts, device guidance and the licence boundary", records five rules:
- the device guides and reports, the server judges: no quality measure, pose, barcode read, chip protocol outcome, integrity indicator or Play Integrity verdict passes or fails anything on the device; nothing measured on the device is sent as a result, and what is sent (`capture-method`, `capture-trigger`, the integrity indicators, the chip's `device-report`, an integrity token) is marked client-reported (review H21);
- the SDK renders what the server's position names and never chooses a path; a step it cannot render is the unsupported screen, never a skip;
- `:openverify-sdk` stays Apache-2.0 and depends on no optional artifact; each optional capability (chip reading, face detection, Play Integrity) is an interface in `commonMain` that the host installs, so iOS can implement it in the next release;
- copyleft or proprietary code lives only in its own artifact (`openverify-nfc` for LGPL, `openverify-integrity-play` for Google's library), and nothing ships before the owner's decision on it;
- no personal data leaves the SDK except to the flow API: no field value, token, image or chip byte in results, events, logs, intents or saved state.

## Starting point (interim, from the research notes; re-taken at the Phase 10 base)

The research notes and this plan read the code at `91c83e02`–`6e6dd06c` (Phase 6 being closed by Workflow 6D, Phases 7, 8 and 9 planned and not built); each "today" column in the notes carries its sources. The full inventory at the Phase 10 base (Phase 9 done) is `contracts-10a`'s first deliverable, written to `docs/research/phase10-inventory.md`, and every claim below is re-checked there. In short:

| Area | Exists (at the research commits) | Does not exist |
|---|---|---|
| Build | `android/` Gradle 9.8.2, AGP 9.4.1, Kotlin 2.4.21 (`gradle/libs.versions.toml`); `:openverify-sdk` on `com.android.kotlin.multiplatform.library` with `android`, `iosArm64`, `iosSimulatorArm64`; compileSdk and targetSdk 36, minSdk 26, JVM toolchain 21; Compose BOM 2026.06.01 (Compose UI 1.11.4), CMP 1.11.1 with Material3 1.9.0, Ktor 3.5.2 over OkHttp 5.4.0, CameraX 1.6.2, kotlinx.serialization 1.11.0, Robolectric 4.17, Kover 0.9.11 with an 80% line gate and CameraX glue excluded (`openverify-sdk/build.gradle.kts`); `android.lint` applied for the KMP module; `~/.konan` cached in CI | No publishing plugin, signing, POM, version property, `explicitApi()`, API dump, consumer rules file, minified build, dependency locking, size or page-size check; the unused `lifecycle-viewmodel-compose` still in `androidMain` and `kotlinx-io` and skiko not declared directly (engineering K20) |
| Public API | `OpenVerifyInquiry.Contract` over `Request(inquiryId, sessionToken, apiBaseUrl)` (a `data class`), `fromOneTimeLink(url, apiBaseUrl?)` (a `null` takes the API from the link's origin) and `OneTimeLinkContract`; `InquiryResult` `Completed(inquiryId, inquiryStatus)`, `Failed(inquiryId?, reason)` with 11 `FailureReason`s, `Canceled(inquiryId?)`; tokens redacted in `toString`; no field values or tokens returned (`androidMain/.../OpenVerifyInquiry.kt`, `commonMain/.../InquiryResult.kt`) | No `locale`, appearance, event listener, optional-component registry, inline entry, template entry |
| Hosting | `OpenVerifyInquiryActivity` (not exported) with `FLAG_SECURE`, edge to edge, the system back routed to the flow; manifest `INTERNET` and `CAMERA`, `camera.any` not required (`androidMain/AndroidManifest.xml`) | No NFC permission or feature declaration anywhere |
| Flow rendering | `FlowCoordinator` (1,351 lines) walks `session.flow.steps` with `FlowNavigator` and its own `JsonLogic` subset over `{ inquiry: { status } }`; `model/Flow.kt` decodes 13 step types through a `known` map (line 423) and everything else, `nfc` and `form` included, as `UnsupportedStep`, which ends `UNSUPPORTED_FLOW`; `specVersion: Int`; `ignoreUnknownKeys = true`; `ContractFixturesTest` decodes every `contracts/fixtures/{api,flows}` file or lists it server-only | No `position`, `step`, `values`, `theme`, `copy`, `locales` or `completion` decoding; no `screen` step or component; no waiting, halted or `exhausted` handling; no client header |
| Strings and locale | 296 English strings and Spanish in `commonMain/composeResources/values{,-es}`, read through an internal `Res`; CMP follows the JVM default locale (ADR 0004 decision 5); the consent screen records `openverify_locale`; `ResourcesTest` refuses non-positional placeholders and Android escapes | No `fr`, `de`, `ar`, no right-to-left layout, no locale override, no copy overrides, no consent digests |
| Theme | `OpenVerifyTheme`: Material 3 default light and dark schemes, following the system | No server theme, appearance option or logo |
| Capture | `CameraXCaptureCamera`: `Preview` plus `ImageCapture` (minimum latency, 1920×1440 target), upright JPEG at quality 90, ≤ 2,048 px, no EXIF, never mirrored (`ImageEncoding.MIRRORS_IMAGES = false`); a card and page guide; production photos only from the live camera (`FlowCoordinator.uploadAllowed`, line 551); server retry hints mapped in `ui/Messages.kt`; one device test (`androidDeviceTest/.../CameraXCaptureCameraTest.kt`) | No `ImageAnalysis`, quality measure, auto-capture, barcode reader; no `capture-method` on uploads |
| Selfie | C40: server-ordered prompts, nonce, 180 s capture TTL, one camera session, per-pose upload with `client-elapsed-ms`, the C40.4 device snapshot; `SelfieCaptureGuidance` decoded and unused; constants in `contracts/fixtures/flow/selfie-capture.json` (`hold-ms` 500, `side-trigger-factor` 1.25, `center-trigger-factor` 0.8, `guidance-hz` 8, `preview-width-px` 320, `manual-shutter-after-ms` 10,000; server 0.021 and 0.077); the web flow's YuNet guidance in `apps/flow/src/guidance/` | No ONNX Runtime, model asset, face detector, guidance machine or capture extension call |
| NFC | `android/openverify-nfc`: an empty `com.android.library` with `README.md` and `NOTICE` (LGPL-3.0 by Phase 5 C67); `BacKeySeed.mrzInformation` and the chip `FileKind`s in common code; server: `POST /api/v1/flow/verifications/:id/chip-challenges` (`routes/flow/government-id-nfc.ts`), which requires only an `initiated` verification (`services/chip-challenges/create.ts:83`), so a challenge may be taken before any chip file is uploaded; the submit's `chip` member, Passive Authentication, Active Authentication over the server nonce and the DG1/DG2 bindings in `@openverify/emrtd` (C62); synthetic chips and the Utopia CSCA in `packages/emrtd/src/testing/` (`chip.ts`, `utopia.ts`); `docs/checks/nfc.md` states the client sequence | No NFC step model or screen, no `FlowApi` chip method, no reader, no sandbox chip for a device, no way for a client to say it cannot read a chip |
| Device signals | The C40.4 camera snapshot and `User-Agent: OpenVerify-Android/0.1.0` (`api/FlowHttp.kt:117`, hand-written); Phase 7 plans `sendSignals`, `DeviceSignals` with debugger facts and the `ANDROID_ID` HMAC, and leaves emulator, root and hooking heuristics and Play Integrity to Phase 10 (Phase 7 C94.7, C110, decision 12) | No emulator, root or hooking indicator, no integrity token, no integrity signal or provider on the server |
| Demo | `android/demo-app`: one screen that launches either contract from a pasted inquiry id and session token or a one-time link; secrets in memory only; debug-only cleartext config; 12 tests | No release build type, R8, NFC wiring, optional components or App Links guidance |
| Tests and CI | 62 host-test files (620 `@Test`, 51 `@ParameterizedTest`), 15 `commonTest`, 3 `iosTest`, 12 demo tests; the `android` job runs `testDebugUnitTest testAndroidHostTest koverVerify assembleDebug :openverify-sdk:assemble lint` with a floor of 1,264 SDK host tests, none skipped; `android-instrumented` boots an API 34 `google_apis` x86_64 emulator (about 6 minutes with the build) and runs `:openverify-sdk:connectedAndroidDeviceTest` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:289-450`); an API 36 image never booted within 600 s on the private 2-vCPU runner | No screenshot, accessibility-check, ABI, R8, 16 KB or size step; no nightly or release workflow; `openverify-nfc` has no tests and no Kover |
| Licences | `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` §2.2 plans "zxing (Java)", JMRTD 0.8.9, cert-cvc 1.4.13, SCUBA 0.0.21 and 0.0.27 with the two GPL-2.0-only Classpath-exception files; §3 YuNet (MIT, WIDER FACE caveat); §5 line 385: Eigen inside onnxruntime-web 1.30.0 needs an owner exception (Phase 4 decision 11, pending) | No row for `onnxruntime-android`, zxing-cpp's Android AAR, Roborazzi, Dokka, the publishing plugin or Play Integrity |
| Records | ADRs 0001–0005 (Phase 7 writes 0006, Phase 8 0007, Phase 9 0008); PLAN §6 Phase 10 names "zxing (Android)" and "selfie liveness ... in `androidMain`" | No ADR for the optional artifacts, no SDK changelog |

Records Phase 10 corrects are handled in C222. Items earlier plans carried to Phase 10 are behaviour K1–K16 and engineering K1–K21; this plan's own search of `docs/plans/phase-9.plan.md` adds the Phase 9 hand-overs, which C175.1 lists and C194 places one by one: the client header, the position and client view, the screen catalogue, visibility, the pattern matcher's Kotlin port, the theme, copy, the three new locales with a right-to-left review, digit normalisation, waits, `exhausted` fallbacks, the 2.2.1 warning and extension, `prefillFrom`, the consent digests, `device.platform`, `locale` passed as `language`, and the native-font question of C160.7 and C177. Each is placed in C190–C224 (decision 57).

## Patterns to mirror

| Category | Source | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| Public Kotlin API | `androidMain/.../OpenVerifyInquiry.kt`, `commonMain/.../InquiryResult.kt` | `public` spelled out; validation in `init` with messages that never contain a credential; `toString` redacts tokens; KDoc on every public member with a usage sample |
| Platform seams | ADR 0004 decisions 2–3; `camera/CaptureCamera.kt`, `api/FlowHttpPlatform.kt`, `ui/CameraPermission.kt` | An interface in `commonMain` the host or the activity passes in, or an internal `expect` with Android and iOS actuals; no `expect class`; an iOS placeholder never claims a capability |
| Wire models | `model/Flow.kt`, `model/Resources.kt`, `model/OpenVerifyJson.kt`, `ContractFixturesTest.kt` | `@Serializable` data classes with `@SerialName` kebab-case members, `ignoreUnknownKeys`, unknown step types as `UnsupportedStep`; every `contracts/fixtures` file decoded or listed server-only |
| Flow API | `api/FlowApi.kt`, `api/FlowApiClient.kt`, `api/FlowHttp.kt` | One `suspend` method per route with its contract number in KDoc; Ktor over the shared `OkHttpClient`; no redirects or retries; RFC 9457 problems mapped to `FlowApiException` |
| Flow state | `flow/FlowCoordinator.kt`, `flow/FlowState.kt` | One `exclusive` mutex per action; a sealed `FlowState` per screen; errors with retry; `Resolution` values; time from an injected `TimeSource` |
| Shared constants | `contracts/fixtures/flow/selfie-capture.json`, `contracts/fixtures/mrz/` with `MrzVectorsTest` | One JSON file read by TypeScript, Python and Kotlin, each with a test that it uses that file; constants provisional with a basis |
| Compose UI | `ui/steps/*.kt`, `ui/CaptureScreen.kt`, `ui/StatusScreens.kt`, `ui/TestTags.kt` | Screens in `commonMain`, strings through `Res.string`, test tags, live regions for status, Robolectric screen tests with `@Config(qualifiers = …)` |
| Server contracts | `packages/contracts/src/chip-challenges.ts`, `flow-api.ts`, `contracts/fixtures/api/` | Zod schemas as the source of truth, golden fixtures, the Android decoder list; RFC 9457 problems with golden fixtures |
| Server routes and services | `apps/api/src/routes/flow/government-id-nfc.ts`, `services/chip-challenges/`, `services/flow.ts` | Plugin-registered routes; the inquiry row lock; events staged in the transaction; no fraud detail in flow responses |
| Signals | Phase 7 C90.1 (`packages/core/src/signal-registry.ts`), C94 | A registry entry per signal with tier, stage, version, collection tier, `null` reasons and `ConfigDefault` thresholds with basis and source |
| Providers | Phase 5 C63 | A provider kind with a real and a deterministic sandbox implementation; production refuses the sandbox binding; an unbound provider answers `not_applicable` with `provider_not_configured` |
| Device tests | `androidDeviceTest/.../CameraXCaptureCameraTest.kt`, CI `android-instrumented` | Compiled locally, run only on the CI emulator; the job fails when a test skips itself (`infra/scripts/check-connected-results.sh`) |
| Tests | Vitest colocated; Testcontainers; JUnit 5 and Robolectric in `androidHostTest`; kotlin.test in `commonTest` | RED commit first; ≥ 80% per package and per Android module; `docs/tdd/phase10-<agent>.tdd.md` |

## Locked contracts

C1–C189 stay locked except where a section below amends them. Stated amendments of earlier contracts:
- C3 and Phase 9 C151 (the flow API): the client view of an `nfc` step gains `fallback-available`, `sandbox-chip-available` and the PII-marked `prefill-mrz` (C207.2, C207.9, C208.2); `prefill-mrz` amends Phase 9 C151.5's rule (its review P16) that no field other than the current screen's leaves the server, and is served only to an SDK client at the current `nfc` step (decision 63); `GET /api/v1/flow/session` takes an optional `language` query that sets the session's locale, unless the inventory finds a Phase 9 equivalent (C193.9, C221.12; review V10); the session's `organization-display-name` is served NFC-normalised, and the organisation's display name is refused on save when its first character is not a stable NFC boundary, an amendment of the contract that owns the organisation's settings, which the inventory names (C200.2, C221.12; reviews V10, V13);
- Phase 9 C151.7 (negotiation): a client-view member whose absence would change what a person is told or asked is added only with a new spec version; an additive member that changes appearance only may be added within a version, and Android's member test (C194.4) lists it; the Stage 1a agent that adds a member classifies it as appearance-only or not and records the reason in its inventory, where the integration agent and 10D's correctness lens review it (reviews H03, V10); the session response gains `integrity { request }` (C211.3); `POST /api/v1/flow/verifications/:id/client-unable` (C207.9); `POST /api/v1/flow/verifications/:id/sandbox-chip` in sandbox environments only (C208); `POST /api/v1/flow/integrity-challenges` and `POST /api/v1/flow/integrity-tokens` (C211.3); the upload's optional `capture-trigger` field (C206.2);
- Phase 9 C162.9 and C161.8: `contracts/fixtures/i18n/consent-texts.json` gains the Android SDK's consent texts as rows with client `android` (C200.3), and the server's digest check reads them;
- Phase 7 C94.2 and C94.7 (the flow signals contract): the `integrity` member gains `android { emulator-indicators[], root-indicators[], hooking-indicators[] }` (C210.2); C94.6: `notice.session-integrity` gets a new built-in text naming the device-integrity probes, and `notice.play-integrity` is a new required notice key wherever a `device-integrity` provider is bound (C210.1, C211.9; decision 66); C94.7's `DeviceSignals` is internal (C210.1); C90.2: three device signals and two integrity signals join the registry (C210.4, C211.5), and C90.1 gains the entry flag `decisive-alone` (C210.5);
- Phase 9 C154.9 (publish rules) and Phase 8's workflow publish checks: a `failure` step, or a workflow decline or other adverse step, downstream of a route that reads a signal marked `decisive-alone: false` is refused unless a verification outcome is read between them (`signal_not_decisive_alone`, C210.5); every route that reads such a signal needs `heuristic:<step id>` whatever its tier (Phase 9 C150.7 widened, C210.5); a `client-unable` exit goes to review unless the version carries `client-unable:<step id>` (Phase 9 C150.6 amended, C207.9); an `nfc` step without an `exhausted` edge warns (C207.9);
- Phase 7 C90.3 (`SIGNAL_NULL_REASONS`, `ON_NULL_DEFAULTS`): `no_indicator_found`, `not_android` and `integrity_token_unavailable` in the `ignore` class, `integrity_unevaluated` and `integrity_decode_failed` in the `needs_review` class (C210.4, C211.5; reviews H13, V09); Phase 7 C96.1: the server always skips `device_handoff` for a session with `mobile-sdk-name` set, whatever `skip-on-mobile` says (C194.2, C221.13; reviews H20, V05);
- Phase 5 C63 (provider kinds): the kind `device-integrity` with the `play-integrity` and `sandbox` implementations (C211.4);
- Phase 5 C64.1 (the web flow at an `nfc` step): the unsupported screen is reworded and gains "use another way" when the server says a fallback exists (C207.10);
- `files`: the column `capture_trigger` (C206.2);
- Phase 9 C175.1: the SDK declares spec 2 only (C193.1), and Phase 9's test-only `FlowCoordinatorProjectionTest.kt` is retired with the projection path it tested (10A shared files);
- PLAN §3 and §6: zxing-cpp for Android PDF417 and the face detector in its own artifact (C222).

### C190. The public API: entry points, requests and results (M3–M9, M32)

1. **Entry points** (behaviour M4, M5, M11; decision 1): `OpenVerifyInquiry.Contract` (`ActivityResultContract<Request, InquiryResult>`) and `OpenVerifyInquiry.OneTimeLinkContract` (`ActivityResultContract<OneTimeLink, InquiryResult>`) stay the only ways to run a flow. No template, template-version, reference-id, account or prefill entry exists on any client (decision 2; behaviour M3, M6, M7: the customer's server creates the inquiry and its fields, which the public guidance itself calls tamper-resistant).
2. **Requests** (`androidMain`, public; decision 3):

   ```kotlin
   public data class Request(
       val inquiryId: String,
       val sessionToken: String,
       val apiBaseUrl: String,
       val locale: String? = null,                 // BCP 47; null follows the device
       val appearance: Appearance = Appearance.SYSTEM,
   )
   public class OneTimeLink internal constructor(
       public val inquiryId: String, internal val oneTimeToken: String, public val apiBaseUrl: String,
       public val locale: String? = null, public val appearance: Appearance = Appearance.SYSTEM,
   )
   public fun fromOneTimeLink(url: String, apiBaseUrl: String, locale: String? = null,
                              appearance: Appearance = Appearance.SYSTEM): OneTimeLink
   ```

   `apiBaseUrl` is now required on the link path (decision 65; review P03). Today, a `null` takes the API from the link's own origin (`OneTimeLinks.kt:35-38`). Then any link a host receives, or any intent another app sends the host's link handler, could point the SDK at a server that renders its own server-driven screens under the host app's name. Now the link supplies only the inquiry id and the one-time token, and the API is always the host's compiled-in base URL. A host test asserts that two links on different origins reach the same compiled-in API. Plain `http` to `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `::1` and `10.0.2.2` (`ApiBaseUrl.kt:40`) is accepted only when the internal seam `expect fun loopbackHttpAllowed(): Boolean` (`commonMain/.../api/LoopbackPolicy.kt`, with its actuals; review V01) allows it, since on a real phone `10.0.2.2` is an ordinary private address. Its Android actual returns `true` when the host app is debuggable (`ApplicationInfo.FLAG_DEBUGGABLE`, read from the application context the activity holds) or when an internal test override is set; only the SDK's own `androidHostTest` (with `stackTest`) and `androidDeviceTest` code sets it. Its iOS actual returns `false`. Host tests cover a debuggable host accepting `http://127.0.0.1`, a non-debuggable host without the override refusing it, and `https` accepted in both. `android-sdk-api` owns the seam and its actuals. The minified demo's smoke test never relies on this rule: it runs over TLS (C215.2).

   `Appearance` (`commonMain`, public enum): `SYSTEM`, `LIGHT`, `DARK` (behaviour M32's public `styleVariant`, our name). `locale` must parse as a BCP 47 tag of at most 35 characters (`IllegalArgumentException` otherwise, with no credential in the message); an unsupported tag falls back without error (C199.2). Both travel as intent extras beside today's (`EXTRA_LOCALE`, `EXTRA_APPEARANCE`), never in saved state beyond the activity's own `Intent`. Adding constructor parameters changes the `data class`'s binary shape (`copy`, `componentN`); that is accepted under 0.x before the first publication (ADR 0004 decision 6) and recorded in the ABI dump's first commit (C214.2).
3. **Results** (`commonMain`, unchanged shape; behaviour M9; decision 4): `InquiryResult.Completed(inquiryId, inquiryStatus)`, `Failed(inquiryId?, reason)`, `Canceled(inquiryId?)`. They carry **no field value and no session token** (ours: the host's server holds both, and a one-time link's token never leaves the SDK), and `Completed` never means the person passed: the KDoc and README say to confirm on the server through webhooks or the API (behaviour 1.3).
4. **Failure reasons:** the eleven of today plus `FLOW_HALTED` (the server stopped the flow at its loop caps, Phase 9 C151.2; an operator must resume or expire the inquiry). The README publishes the table of every reason with what the host should do (behaviour M9: the public code list is not documented; ours is closed). A new reason is a minor-version change in 0.x and a major one after 1.0 (C214.4).
5. **No callback is authoritative** (behaviour 1.3, D15): the result and the events (C192) coordinate the host's UI; the inquiry's state is read on the server.
6. **Honesty:** the contract returns how the flow ended on this device, never a verification outcome; a field the person entered or a chip value is never returned to the host.
7. **The public surface** (decision 68; review F02): today 161 declarations in `commonMain` and `androidMain` are `public`, the whole wire model (`model/`), `FlowApi` with every route, `FlowApiClient`, `FlowApiClients`, `FlowApiException`, `CapturedImage` and `CameraLens` among them, while the demo uses five types. Before the first ABI dump, `contracts-10a` makes all of these `internal`. This is a 0.x binary break, accepted before the first publication (ADR 0004 decision 6). Exactly these stay public:
   - `OpenVerifyInquiry` with `Request`, `OneTimeLink`, both contracts and `fromOneTimeLink`;
   - `InquiryResult` with its kinds, `FailureReason` and `InquiryStatus`;
   - `Appearance`;
   - `OpenVerifyAndroid`, `OpenVerifyComponents` and `FlowTransport` with `OpenVerifyAndroidTransport`;
   - the events (C192);
   - the seam interfaces and their value types: `ChipReader`, `ChipAccess`, `BacKeySeed`, `FileKind`, `ChipRead`, `ChipDeviceReport`, `ChipProgress`, `ChipReadException`, `ChipReadFailure`, `FaceDetectorFactory`, `FaceDetector`, `YunetOutputs` and `IntegrityTokenProvider`;
   - `ExperimentalOpenVerifyApi` (C207.3).

   `OpenVerifyTheme` becomes internal too, and the demo gets its own two-line `MaterialTheme` in the same commit. The ABI dump's expected declaration list is committed beside it (C214.2), so `checkKotlinAbi` catches an accidental `public`. A wire change is then not a public signature change, and a host can no longer implement `FlowApi`.

### C191. Optional components and the install registry (ours)

1. **The registry** (`androidMain`, public; decision 6): `OpenVerifyAndroid.install(components: OpenVerifyComponents)`, called from the host's `Application.onCreate` so it holds again after process death; a second call replaces the first; `OpenVerifyAndroid.installed(): OpenVerifyComponents` for tests.

   ```kotlin
   public class OpenVerifyComponents(                       // commonMain
       public val chipReader: ChipReader? = null,           // C207.3; openverify-nfc implements it
       public val faceDetectorFactory: FaceDetectorFactory? = null, // C204.2; openverify-face implements it
       public val integrityTokenProvider: IntegrityTokenProvider? = null, // C211.2
       public val eventListener: OpenVerifyEventListener? = null,         // C192
       public val transport: FlowTransport? = null,                       // C213.4; review P11
   )
   ```

   `FlowTransport` (`commonMain`, public) is an opaque interface with no members a host implements. Its only Android factory is `OpenVerifyAndroidTransport.okHttp(client: OkHttpClient): FlowTransport`. The factory derives a client from the host's (`newBuilder()`) with redirects forced off, as `okHttpFlowClient` does today, and keeps the host's `CertificatePinner`, timeouts and proxy. Both the activity path (`InquiryViewModelFactory`) and the one-time-link redemption use it when it is installed, and the shared client otherwise. The README says that the host's interceptors run on every flow request (C213.5).

2. **Dependency direction:** `:openverify-sdk` depends on no optional artifact (`openverify-nfc`, `openverify-face`, `openverify-integrity-play`); each optional artifact depends on `:openverify-sdk` and exposes one factory (`OpenVerifyNfc.chipReader(context)`, `OpenVerifyFace.detectorFactory(context)`, `OpenVerifyPlayIntegrity.tokenProvider(context, cloudProjectNumber)`). A Gradle test fails the build if `:openverify-sdk`'s resolved runtime classpath names any of them, or JMRTD, SCUBA, cert-cvc, ONNX Runtime or `com.google.android.play` (C214.6). No `ServiceLoader`, reflection or App Startup initializer discovers components (ours: explicit wiring is testable and needs no keep rules).
3. **Absent components** (each tested): no chip reader: the `nfc` step shows the "this app cannot read document chips" screen of C207.9; no face detector: the selfie keeps today's manual shutter with the server's prompts (C204.6); no integrity provider: no token is sent and the integrity signals read `null` (C211.5); no listener: no event is built.
4. **iOS-ready:** `OpenVerifyComponents` and every interface it names live in `commonMain`; only `install` and the Android factories are Android code, so the next release adds an iOS `install` with the same components.
5. **Honesty:** an installed component changes what the device can guide or read, never what the server decides; the README states which features need which artifact.

### C192. Events (M10)

1. **Listener** (`commonMain`, public; decision 5): `public fun interface OpenVerifyEventListener { public fun onEvent(event: OpenVerifyEvent) }`, called on the main thread, never blocking the flow (exceptions it throws are caught and dropped).
2. **Events** (a sealed `OpenVerifyEvent`, ours; behaviour M10 lists the public kinds as UI coordination): `Started(inquiryId)`; `StepShown(stepType)` with the flow-spec step **type** only (`screen`, `government_id`, `nfc`, …), never a step id, component id or name (Phase 9 C151.8: step ids can reveal a path); `CameraPermission(granted: Boolean)`; `CaptureFailed(reason)` with a closed `CaptureFailureReason` (`camera_unavailable`, `camera_error`, `out_of_memory`); `Submitted(stepType)`; `Waiting` (the server is deciding); `Ended(result: InquiryResultKind)` with `COMPLETED`, `FAILED`, `CANCELED`.
3. **Never in an event:** a field value, token, image, chip byte, MRZ, signal, check result, error message from the server or URL. A contract test enumerates every event's members against an allowlist.
4. **Delivery:** events from the activity reach the listener installed through C191; with none installed, no event object is built.
5. **Honesty:** the KDoc and README say events are for UI coordination and may be missed (process death, a crash), and that the server is the source of truth.

### C193. Spec negotiation and the server-position driver (Phase 9 C151, C175.1)

1. **The header** (decision 7): every flow API request sends `OpenVerify-Flow-Spec: 2`. This departs from C175.1's text (`1, 2`), labelled ours: Phase 9 C151.7 defines the header as the versions a client *renders*, and the Phase 10 SDK no longer renders the v1 projection or evaluates a v1 transition, so declaring 1 would claim a capability it dropped (ADR 0004's "a placeholder never claims a capability"). The server serves 2 to it either way.
2. **What the SDK requires:** a session answer with `spec-version: 2` and a `position`. An answer without them (a server from before Phase 9) ends with `Failed(UNSUPPORTED_FLOW)` after the unsupported screen, which says the server is older than the SDK; nothing has been released, so no installed base is stranded (decision 7). `minimum-server` is stated in the README and the changelog.
3. **The driver** (`commonMain/.../flow/`; replaces `FlowNavigator` and `JsonLogic`, whose files and tests are deleted, with the removed test names recorded for the integration agent, who lowers the floor in `ci.yml`, C219.6): `FlowCoordinator` holds the last `FlowSession` and renders `session.position` with `session.step`:
   - `position.state: active`: the client view of `position.step-id` is rendered by the step registry (C194);
   - `waiting`: the waiting screen, polling `GET /flow/session` after `retry-after-ms` or 2 s, with a polite live-region announcement once (C197.1);
   - `halted`: the "cannot continue" screen, ending `Failed(FLOW_HALTED)` (C197.2);
   - `ended`: the inquiry's terminal status decides `Completed` or `Failed(INQUIRY_FAILED)`, as today.
   After every exit the SDK reports (consent recorded, screen submitted, verification submitted and decided, a chip submitted, a client-unable report), it reads the session again, or takes the session the route answered (screen submission, C148.5), and renders the new position. It **never** advances on its own: no list order, no transition, no attempt count decides the next step (Phase 9 C151.12; ADR 0008).
4. **Verification steps under the driver:** the step's own work is today's (create or reuse the verification, capture or enter, upload, submit, poll until decided); the decided verification is followed by a session read. A verification whose attempts are used up no longer triggers `Resolution.Finalize`'s wait for an inquiry failure (Phase 9 V02): the server's position says whether an `exhausted` fallback follows, the inquiry failed, or the step retries.
5. **Only the current step:** the SDK calls step routes only for `position.step-id`; a 409 `flow_step_not_current` is answered by reading the session and rendering its position, never by retrying the call (a test per route family). **Single-use POSTs** (review P26): the shared OkHttp client may resend a POST whose response was lost (`retryOnConnectionFailure`), and flow calls have no idempotency key (the C12 plugin acts only for an API-key or user principal), so every 409 from a single-use flow POST (`client_unable_not_allowed`, `integrity_challenge_invalid`, `chip_challenge_invalid` after a submit that may have landed, `selfie_capture_not_extendable`) is answered by reading the session and rendering its position before any error screen; a host test per route where `MockWebServer` drops the connection after the body and the retry gets the 409.
6. **Resume:** a relaunch with the same session renders the server's position; values the person typed and did not submit are not kept across process death (ours, as today: no PII in saved state).
7. **`device.platform`:** the `User-Agent` stays exactly `OpenVerify-Android/<version>` (Phase 7 C94.5, behaviour K10), with `<version>` from the generated constant of C214.3; `device.kind` is the server's mapping (Phase 9 C150.2).
8. **Honesty:** the driver renders what the server decided and reports only the current step's own submission; a modified client can still choose only the facts it reports itself, which Phase 9 marks by source (C150.10).
9. **The session's language** (decision 71; reviews H15, F29; Phase 9 C175.1's last hand-over): the SDK tells the server the base tag it chose (C199.2) on its first session read and again after every selector change. Phase 9 defines `language` for the hosted flow (C161.2) and `session.locale` as a client fact (C150.2, C150.10), but no wire for an API client. So `contracts-10a` records in the inventory the mechanism Phase 9 built. If there is none, the mechanism is the optional query parameter `language` on `GET /api/v1/flow/session` (the public parameter name; a base tag of at most 35 characters, ignored unless enabled), which sets the session's locale as the hosted flow's parameter does; this is a C3 amendment owned by `api-android-10a`, with its zod schema and golden fixture (C221.12). A host test checks the parameter on the first read and after a selector change. The 10A spec asserts that `session.locale` equals the SDK's choice.

### C194. The step registry and Phase 9 coverage

1. **Registry** (`commonMain/.../flow/StepRegistry.kt`; decision 8): a map from client-view step type to a renderer; an unknown type, a known type whose client view fails to decode, or a member the SDK cannot honour (2) renders the unsupported screen, which names no step id and ends `Failed(UNSUPPORTED_FLOW)` if the person leaves; it is never skipped (Phase 9 C151.12). Phase 11 adds `share-consent` to this registry (`phase11-connect.md` R17) without a format change.
2. **What the SDK renders at spec 2** (every Phase 9 feature, handled or shown unsupported):

   | Phase 9 feature | Android in Phase 10 |
   |---|---|
   | `consent` with built-in or custom copy (C162.4, C162.9) | Rendered; digests sent (C200) |
   | `government_id`, `selfie`, `document`, `phone_otp`, `email_otp`, `database`, `aamva`, `crypto_wallet` (paste only), `database_business`, `business_website` | Rendered as today, under the driver (C193.4), with `prefillFrom` values (C195.6) and `captureMethodsByDevice.native` (C206.1) |
   | `nfc` | Rendered (C207) |
   | `screen` with C148's catalogue (content, form, buttons) | Rendered (C195) |
   | `branch`, `action` | Never sent to a client; their waits render as `waiting` (C197.1) |
   | `device_handoff` (Phase 7 C96) | Always skipped by the server for SDK sessions (`mobile-sdk-name` set), whatever the step's `skip-on-mobile` says (a Phase 7 C96.1 amendment built by `api-android-10a`, C221.13; reviews H20, V05: with `false`, every Android session would end on the unsupported screen); if served anyway, the unsupported screen |
   | `completion`, `failure` with `decisionBasis`, `contestRoute` (C150.7) | Rendered; the failed screen shows the contest route (C197.3) |
   | `exhausted` fallbacks (C150.6) | Followed through the position (C193.4) |
   | Visibility and enablement (C148.4) | The strict evaluator (C196.1) |
   | `pattern` (C148.3) | The Kotlin matcher (C196.2); the server stays the final check |
   | `values`, masked and write-once fields (C151.5) | Rendered (C195.5) |
   | Theme (C160.7) | C198 |
   | Per-component style overrides (C148, C160.7; behaviour note T8 of Phase 9) | C198.9: each override's token mapped onto the component's Material role; an override the mapping cannot place renders the screen unsupported |
   | Locales, selector, `copy` (C161, C162) | C199; the chosen language reaches `session.locale` (C193.9) |
   | `completion.redirect-url` (C158.5) | Ignored on Android: the activity result replaces it (decision 10; documented) |
   | `embed` (C159) | Ignored (web only) |
   | Waits, `halted` (C150.5, C151.2) | C197.1, C197.2 |
   | 2.2.1 warning and the selfie-capture extension (C171.4) | C204.5 |
   | Arabic-Indic digits (C161.4) | C196.3 |
   | Help and privacy links (C162.3) | C197.4 |
   | Uploaded fonts (`font.platforms: ["web"]`) | System font (C198.4) |
   | Modules (C155.4) | Editor macros: nothing to render |

3. **Component kinds the SDK cannot render** are none in C148's catalogue v1; a later kind renders the whole screen as unsupported, never a partial screen (a test with a synthetic `kind`).
4. **Honesty:** the table is a contract test. Every v2 step type and component kind in `packages/flow-spec`'s schema export is either in the registry or listed as server-only, as `ContractFixturesTest` does for fixtures. **Members too** (review H03): for every client-view and component schema in that export, each property is listed in `android/openverify-sdk/flow-spec-members.json` as `honoured`, `ignored` with a stated reason (for example `embed`), or `server-only`, and the test fails on an unlisted property, so a member Phase 9 or a later phase adds without an Android treatment fails the build before it ships. `contracts-10a` writes the file complete for Phase 9's export. From 10B on, the Stage 1a agent that adds a client-view member lists it in the same commit (`contracts-10b` the session's `integrity`, `contracts-10c` the three `nfc` members) and records in its inventory whether the member is appearance-only, with the reason (reviews V10, V14); between Workflows, the file is shared. The wire models keep `ignoreUnknownKeys` at run time: strict decoding would stop every released SDK at the first additive member a newer server sends. Instead, Phase 9 C151.7 is amended so that a member whose absence would change what a person is told or asked comes only with a new spec version (Locked contracts).

### C195. Screens and components in Compose (Phase 9 C148)

1. **The `screen` renderer** (`commonMain/.../ui/screen/`): components in order, `container` nesting ≤ 3, `stepper` as a static indicator, `spacer` sizes from the theme's spacing, `footer` children then the fixed help slot (C197.4), `image` from a theme asset (C198.3) with its `altKey`, or `decorative` with no content description.
2. **Form components** (one composable each, behind one `FieldState` model): `text_input`, `text_area`, `number`, `date` (a date entry with a picker, values ISO 8601), `email`, `phone` (allowed countries, as the phone step's selector), `select`, `radio_group`, `checkbox`, `checkbox_group`, `multi_select`, `country_select` (allowed countries, names from `CountryNames`), `url`, `masked_text` (shown masked once entered; `{set: true}` from the server shows "set" and offers re-entry), `address` (street lines, city, subdivision, postal code, country; no autocomplete). Keyboard types and Android autofill hints by type (`emailAddress`, `phone`, `postalCode`, `postalAddress`, `birthDateFull`), as C148.7's `autocomplete` tokens. `masked_text`, document-number, MRZ, CAN and OTP fields carry no autofill content type and are not important for autofill, so no autofill service receives or offers to save them. They ask the keyboard not to learn (no personalised learning in the field's IME options where Compose exposes them, else a keyboard type that implies it, with autocorrect off). A host test per field kind inspects the field's semantics and its `EditorInfo`, and the TDD file records any flag Compose 1.11 cannot set (review P07).
3. **Buttons:** `submit` with its port (`next` or `button:<key>`), `cancel` (a confirmation, then `Canceled`; the inquiry stays resumable), `link` (an allowlisted `https:` URL from the version's link fields, opened in the system browser; any other scheme is refused and logged as a PII-free event).
4. **Validation on the device** mirrors the server's rules (required among visible fields, lengths, ranges, options, countries, patterns through C196.2, digits normalised first) to show errors at once; the server validates again and its 422 pointers map back to the fields (as the forms of C52.3 do today). Errors are announced, linked to their field (`semantics { error(…) }`) and summarised at the top on submit.
5. **Values** (C151.5, review P16 of Phase 9): the session's `values` prefill only the fields bound on the current screen; a write-once field with a value shows as set and is not editable; nothing the person typed is kept beyond the screen's composition and the view model's memory.
6. **`prefillFrom`** (C148.7): a phone, email, database or AAMVA step whose client view carries a prefilled value shows it filled and editable (3.3.7).
7. **Submission:** `POST /api/v1/flow/screens/:step-id/submit` with `{ values, button }` (new `FlowApi.submitScreen(stepId, values, button): FlowSession`); a resubmission of an exited step renders the returned session.
8. **Built in** (WCAG 2.2; C212): visible labels from copy keys; 48 dp targets; focus to the screen heading on arrival; logical start and end padding only, so right-to-left mirrors; error text never by colour alone.
9. **Limits:** the SDK refuses to render a screen above C148.6's limits (40 components, nesting 3, 1,000 options) as unsupported, so a malformed answer cannot exhaust memory.
10. **Honesty:** the device's validation is guidance for the person; the server's validation decides what is stored.

### C196. Visibility, patterns and digits in Kotlin (Phase 9 C148.3, C148.4, C161.4; Phase 9's review V10)

1. **Visibility evaluator** (`commonMain/.../flow/logic/Visibility.kt`): the strict subset only (`var` over `screen.<field>` of the same screen, `===`, `!==`, `==` as an alias of `===`, `!`, `!!`, `and`, `or`, `in`); any other operator or a path outside the screen throws `VisibilityRuleRefused`, which renders the screen unsupported (the server refuses such a rule at publish, so meeting one means a server defect). A hidden component's value is dropped from the submission, as the server does.
2. **Pattern matcher** (`commonMain/.../flow/logic/pattern/`): a Kotlin port of Phase 9's Pike VM over the restricted grammar (code points, escaped metacharacters, `.`, bracket classes with ranges and negation, ASCII `\d` and `\w`, ECMAScript's `\s` list, `^`, `$`, alternation, groups, `*`, `+`, `?`, `{n}`, `{n,}`, `{n,m}` with `m` ≤ 100, the `u` flag only), linear in the input, inputs capped at 10,000 characters. No `Regex` is ever built from operator text (ours, mirroring Phase 9's rule).
3. **Digits** (`normalizeDigits`): U+0660–U+0669 and U+06F0–U+06F9 to ASCII before validation and submission in code, phone, date, number and ID fields; MRZ, BAC and PACE keys, CAN and dates sent to the chip are always ASCII (behaviour M34). **The device's own default locale** (review H08): the SDK leaves the JVM default alone (C199.4), so formatting follows the host device's own setting, and plain `ar` formats Latin digits where `ar-EG` does not (Phase 9 C161.2). The key derivation, every value sent to the server or the chip (dates, numbers, the OTP code, the CAN), and the consent fill are therefore tested under `Locale.setDefault` and the Robolectric qualifier for `ar-EG`, `fa-IR`, `bn-BD`, `mr-IN` and `my-MM`. Each runs with the SDK's chosen locale both equal to and different from the device's. A source-scan rule refuses locale-less `String.format`, `"%d".format`, `DateTimeFormatter` and `NumberFormat` use in the packages that build those values. Common code builds them by hand (Kotlin's own number `toString()` is ASCII on every platform), and `androidMain` passes `Locale.ROOT`.
4. **Shared vectors** (ours; decision 9; the cross-language rule of `selfie-capture.json`): `api-android-10a` adds an assert-only test in `packages/flow-spec` that checks `contracts/fixtures/flow-spec/{visibility,pattern,digits}-vectors.json` against the TypeScript implementations over its own case table (Phase 9's golden cases copied as data, the pathological patterns with 10,000-character inputs, and the refusals), importing only the implementations, and writes the files only in C221.8's explicit update mode (reviews F10, V12). The Kotlin ports pass every vector in `commonTest`, with a time budget for the pathological ones. A vector file that disagrees with the TypeScript output fails the test, so the two cannot drift. **How `commonTest` reads a fixture** (reviews H14, F01): `commonTest` has no classpath, and `:openverify-sdk:assemble` compiles the iOS test klibs. So `contracts-10a` adds one internal `expect fun readContractFixture(path: String): String` in `commonTest`. Its `androidHostTest` actual reads the classpath that `contracts/fixtures` is already on, as `MrzVectorsTest` does. Its `iosTest` actual reads the file through `platform.posix` from a directory named by an environment variable; it compiles in Linux CI now and runs on the simulator in the next release. The vector suites' assertions live in `commonTest`; the Phase 10 TDD files list which suites will run on the simulator.
5. **Honesty:** the client evaluates only same-screen visibility and input guidance; no condition over a server fact ever runs on the device.

### C197. Waiting, halted, failure, contest routes and the help slot

1. **Waiting** (C150.5, C152.2): a neutral "one moment" screen with a progress indicator, announced once; polling at `retry-after-ms` or 2 s; no step name, reason or count shown (the wait may be a signal snapshot or a workflow, which the person must not learn, Phase 9 C150.11); back offers cancel.
2. **Halted** (C151.2): a screen saying the verification cannot continue on this device and that the organisation can resume it; the result is `Failed(FLOW_HALTED)`.
3. **Failure screens:** a `failure` step's client view, with its message copy and, when the server sends `decision-contest-route` (C150.7, C128.7), the contest text and its `https:` link or contact text shown on the failed screen; an inquiry failed without a failure step shows today's failure screen.
4. **The help slot** (C162.3, 3.2.6): the version's `help-url` and `privacy-url` with their copy keys, after every step's content in the same place, opened in the system browser; the language selector beside them (C199.3).
5. **Honesty:** a wait reveals nothing about what is awaited; a failed inquiry offers the contest route the operator declared and never implies a different outcome.

### C198. Themes on Android (Phase 9 C160.7; behaviour M31, M32)

1. **Decoding:** the session's `theme {theme-set-id, revision, mode, light, dark, radius, font {kind, asset-id?, platforms}, logo {asset-id, alt-key, decorative}, placement}`; every palette value must match `^#[0-9a-f]{6}$` and every token must be one of the twelve of C160.3, else the built-in theme is used for the whole session (fail closed, a PII-free event) (ours).
2. **Mapping** (`commonMain/.../ui/theme/ThemeMapping.kt`, pure, golden-tested): the twelve tokens onto a Material 3 `ColorScheme` (`primary`, `on-primary`, `surface`, `canvas` as `background` and `surfaceContainer`, `ink` as `onSurface` and `onBackground`, `ink-muted` as `onSurfaceVariant`, `line` as `outlineVariant`, `control` as `outline`, `danger` as `error`, `on-primary` as `onError`, `focus` for the SDK's focus indicator, `primary-hover` and `danger-hover` as pressed states). **Every `ColorScheme` role is set** from the twelve tokens, and none keeps Material's baseline (review H04). For example, `surfaceVariant` and the `surfaceContainer*` family take `surface` or `canvas`; `primaryContainer` and `secondaryContainer` take `canvas` with `ink` as their `on` colour; `inverseSurface` takes `ink` on `surface`; `errorContainer` takes `surface` with `danger` text; `scrim` is black at the Material alpha. Every pair the SDK's composables draw is thus one of the gated pairs of Phase 9 C160.4, or a pair the table states and tests. A host test enumerates `ColorScheme`'s properties and fails on any role the mapping does not set. A contrast test runs every foreground and background pair the SDK's composables use, disabled states included, through the mapped scheme against boundary palettes that pass the gate at exactly 4.5:1 and 3:1. `radius` (0–24) maps onto `Shapes`. `mode` (Phase 9 C160.7) and whether the session carries a `dark` palette decide the dark scheme (review H21: `settings` belongs to the stored styles, not to the session member). The built-in light and dark palettes of Phase 9 (`packages/ui/src/styles.css` as hex) are the SDK's defaults, read from a fixture `api-android-10a` exports to `contracts/fixtures/theme/builtin-palettes.json`, so web and Android start from the same colours.
3. **Logo:** `GET /api/v1/flow/theme-assets/:id` with the session token (C160.6), decoded on the device only after a magic-byte check for the one format the server's rendition emits (Phase 9 C160.6 re-encodes every logo; any other format is refused) and ≤ 256 KiB, and after its header bounds are read without decoding (`inJustDecodeBounds` or `ImageDecoder`'s header callback) and refused above 2,048 × 2,048 px, with a host test over an oversized header (review P20); shown with its `alt-key` text or as decorative with the organisation's name as text; a failed fetch or decode shows the name only.
4. **Fonts:** the system font on Android, always (decision 11): an uploaded font is `platforms: ["web"]` (C160.7), Android's `Typeface` parses font bytes in the app's process without the browsers' sanitiser and does not load WOFF2, and no permissive sanitiser has been evaluated; operator fonts on native clients stay deferred (C223), as Phase 9 C177 left the choice to this plan.
5. **Appearance** (C190.2): `SYSTEM` follows the device as the session's `mode` allows (review H21), `LIGHT` and `DARK` force one; a forced dark with no dark palette uses the built-in dark palette (the server's gate passed only what exists).
6. **Contrast:** the server's gate (C160.4) passed the palette; the SDK does not re-judge the palette, but it tests that its own mapping draws only gated or tested pairs (2), and a host cannot inject colours (no resource-name overrides; behaviour M32, decision 11).
7. **Window:** edge to edge as today; status and navigation bar icons follow the palette's luminance.
8. **Honesty:** the theme changes appearance only. The README says the contrast proof is the server's palette gate together with the SDK's pair test over its own mapping (2), not a per-screen audit; C212 checks screens, and every screen has one golden with a non-default, gate-passing operator palette in light and dark (C219.3).
9. **Per-component style overrides** (Phase 9 C148, C160.7; review H03): an override names a token for a component's attribute (a `text`'s colour, a button's variant). The SDK maps that token onto the component's Material role through the same table, and the gate passed it against its screen's surface (Phase 9 C160.7). An override the mapping cannot place renders the screen unsupported, never a silently unstyled screen. Goldens cover a danger-styled `text` and an override in dark.
10. **Placement** (Phase 9 C160.7, review H21): the theme's `placement` puts the logo in the header or above the content as the web flow does; an unknown value takes the header.

### C199. Languages, right-to-left and operator copy (Phase 9 C161, C162; behaviour M8, M33, M34)

1. **SDK strings in five locales** (decision 12): every string in `composeResources/values` exists in `values-es`, `values-fr`, `values-de` and `values-ar`. Where an SDK string has the same meaning as a key of `contracts/fixtures/i18n/<locale>.json` (C161.8), the key map `android/openverify-sdk/i18n/key-map.json` (SDK resource name → flow key) records it and the generator `:openverify-sdk:generateLocaleStrings` writes the value from the fixture into the generated `strings_catalog.xml` of each of the five folders, its only output, `en` and `es` included (review V07); the other strings are drafted by the build agent. From 10B on, the Stage 1a agent that adds a key-map entry re-runs the generator in the same commit. **Legal texts are never agent-drafted** (review H16): every SDK string whose meaning is a `legal`-marked `COPY_KEYS` entry (consent, notice, decision and contest, retention) must come from the web fixture through the key map, in every locale. A host test over `copy-keys.json` and the key map fails on a legal string that is not mapped. Phase 9's `locale-unreviewed:` acknowledgement, judged from the web's `LOCALE_REVIEW_STATUS`, therefore covers the Android texts too, because they are the same words. An Android legal text with no web key is added to the web catalogs first, by a borrowed edit of the agent that needs it. Every non-English SDK catalog is `machine-drafted` in `android/openverify-sdk/i18n/review-status.json`, in Phase 9's `LOCALE_REVIEW_STATUS` form (`source`, `machine-drafted`, `partially-reviewed`, `reviewed {by, date, catalog-sha256}`), with per-key digests that downgrade a reviewed locale when a key changes (a host test), so no text is called reviewed without a record.
2. **Choosing the language** (C161.2's order): `Request.locale`, then the person's choice in the selector, then the device's locales, matched by RFC 4647 lookup on the base tag against the version's `locales.enabled` that the SDK also bundles; then the session's `locales.default` (Phase 9 C151.5; review H21); then `en`. Number and date formatting use the full tag, with ASCII digits wherever a value is sent to the server or the chip (C196.3).
3. **The selector:** offered among the enabled locales the SDK bundles, in the fixed slot of every screen (C197.4), each language named in its own language.
4. **Per-composition language** (ours): the SDK's language is applied to its own composition only, never by changing the JVM default or the host's configuration. `contracts-10a`'s spike establishes whether Compose Multiplatform 1.11.1 resolves `Res.string` against a per-composition locale through a public API; if it does not, the internal facade `sdkString` and `sdkPlural`, which every SDK string goes through (Workflow 10A Stage 1a; review F09), resolves the generated per-locale tables itself, with plural rules copied from CLDR for the five locales (decision 12), and `ResourcesTest` asserts that both paths yield the same string for every key and locale.
5. **Right-to-left:** the SDK sets `LocalLayoutDirection` from the chosen locale, so Arabic lays out right to left even in a host without `android:supportsRtl` (behaviour M34); directional icons mirror; selfie pose prompts, the camera preview, document outlines, check marks and logos never mirror (Phase 9 C161.3); interpolated values are isolated with U+2068/U+2069 in the displayed `AnnotatedString` only, so a Latin name inside Arabic text keeps its order; the isolates never enter the text the consent digest is taken over (C200.2). Every screen has an `ar` screenshot (C219.3) and a host test under `@Config(qualifiers = "ar")`.
6. **Operator copy** (C162.6): the session's `copy` for the current step replaces the SDK's text for each mapped key (the key map's reverse), with `{name}` placeholders filled from the closed list (`{organization}`, `{days}`, `{attempts}`, …) in a few lines, no other syntax interpreted; component texts (`<step>.<component>.<attribute>`) are always operator copy. Fallbacks follow C162.5 (the person's locale's custom text, its built-in text, the default locale's custom text, its built-in text), never a raw key.
7. **Unmapped keys fail closed for legal texts** (decision 13): an override of a key the SDK cannot place is ignored with a PII-free event when its `legal` mark is `null`; when it is `consent`, `notice`, `decision` or `retention`, the step renders unsupported, because showing the SDK's own words where the operator wrote legal text would misstate it. A host test runs every `COPY_KEYS` entry (exported to `contracts/fixtures/i18n/copy-keys.json` by `api-android-10a`) through the mapping.
8. **Plurals:** every plural defines each category of its locale (Arabic six), by a host test over the resource files.
9. **Honesty:** each locale's review status is in the README; the SDK never translates operator copy and never machine-translates at run time.

### C200. Consent evidence on Android (Phase 9 C162.9)

1. **The consent body** (C3 amended by Phase 9): `text-keys`, `locale`, `optional-text-keys`, and `text-sha256[]`, `copy-source` (`custom` or `built-in`) and `catalog-version` (the SDK's consent-text catalog version, `android-<sdk version>-<sha256 prefix>`, where the digest is taken over the consent texts only, so a change to any other string does not create a version; review F07).
2. **The digests** (reviews H07, F06; decision 14): SHA-256 over each consent text's **logical** form. That is the template text with its placeholders filled (`days` from `face-template-retention-days`, `organization` from the session), UTF-8, NFC, and with no isolate or other format character the renderer adds for display: the input the server computes in Phase 9 C162.9, as the web's `<bdi>` elements add no characters either. The displayed string adds the isolates around each filled value (C199.5) and is otherwise the same text. The hash runs in `commonMain` through our own `Sha256` (decision 14: the Kotlin standard library has none, and a pure implementation tested against the NIST SHA-256 vectors keeps iOS-ready code free of a new dependency). Texts are NFC-normalised at build time: a host test with `java.text.Normalizer` fails any SDK string or generated catalog entry that is not NFC. Operator copy arrives normalised (C162.3). The session serves `organization-display-name` NFC-normalised, and the server refuses on save a name whose first character is not a stable NFC boundary (canonical combining class 0 and `NFC_Quick_Check=Yes`: no combining mark and no conjoining Hangul vowel or trailing consonant, U+1161–U+1175 and U+11A8–U+11C2, which have class 0 but compose with what precedes them; a C221 amendment; review V13). The build-time NFC test also requires every placeholder in an SDK consent text to be followed by the end of the text or a stable boundary. A filled text made of NFC parts joined at stable boundaries is then NFC (Unicode UAX #15), and the device needs no normaliser.
3. **The server's table** (C161.8): `api-android-10a` appends the SDK's consent texts, unfilled, to `contracts/fixtures/i18n/consent-texts.json` as rows `{catalog-version, client: "android", key, locale, text}`, written by a Gradle task from the resource files with Android's positional placeholders rewritten to the table's `{{days}}` form; a host test fails when a released row is missing or changed (the table is append-only), and when the SDK's current consent texts have no row under its current `catalog-version`; the server's digest check (C162.9) reads Android rows like the web's. Phase 9's exporter keeps the rows whose `client` is not `web` (a borrowed edit by `api-android-10a`). In every later Workflow, the integration agent (and `fix-sdk` in 10D) re-runs `exportConsentTexts` and the digest export whenever a consent text changed (review F07).
4. **Cross-language vectors:** a TypeScript test computes, for each Android row at two retention values, the digest the server expects, into `contracts/fixtures/i18n/android-consent-digests.json`; a Kotlin host test renders each text through the real consent screen and asserts the same digest. The vectors include an `ar` text with a Latin `{organization}`, an organisation name supplied in decomposed form (normalised by the server before it is served), and a Korean name of precomposed syllables; a name that starts with U+1161 is refused on save (review V13). Arabic digits never enter a filled placeholder (ASCII, C196.3), on both sides.
5. **Custom consent copy** is rendered from `copy` and recorded with `copy-source: custom`; the server's 422 `consent_text_mismatch` shows a retry screen and a PII-free event, never a silent consent.
6. **Honesty** (review P14): the digests record which built-in or custom text version the client **reports** it rendered. They show that the server accepted a matching text, not that a person read it, and a modified client can send a matching digest without showing anything. The docs say so, and the console labels the consent record's `client` and digests as client-reported (C221.9, built by `integration-10a`; review V05). A consent the server cannot match is refused, not recorded.

### C201. The frame pipeline (engineering 3.1)

1. **Common seam** (`commonMain/.../camera/`; decision 17): `CaptureCamera.frames(): Flow<AnalysisFrame>` with an empty default, so fakes and the iOS placeholder compile and claim nothing; `AnalysisFrame(width, height, rotationDegrees, layout: PixelLayout.RGBA_8888, bytes: ByteArray, timestampNanos)`, buffers reused by the producer (one allocation per frame size, ours).
2. **Android:** `CameraXCaptureCamera` binds `Preview`, `ImageCapture` and `ImageAnalysis` in its one `bindToLifecycle` call; analysis at about 640 px wide for documents, at 1,280 px or more on a side the server marks barcode-bearing (or a full-resolution crop of the guide area, whichever the device test shows is cheaper; review F11: at 640 px a 290-module AAMVA row falls near 1.5 pixels a module), and the fixture's 320 px for selfies, `STRATEGY_KEEP_ONLY_LATEST`, `OUTPUT_IMAGE_FORMAT_RGBA_8888`, frames sampled at `guidance-hz` (8) and the rest closed at once; every `ImageProxy` closed in a `finally`.
3. **Fallback:** if binding three use cases fails at session creation, the camera rebinds `Preview` and `ImageCapture`, `frames()` completes empty, and the step runs today's manual shutter; a device test forces the failure through a test hook.
4. **Mirroring:** analysis reads the raw, unmirrored frame; preview mirroring is display only, so a left prompt means the same head turn on the device, in the web flow and on the server (engineering 3.1).
5. **The screen stays on** (review H20; behaviour 2.1): the capture screens, the NFC tap screen and the waiting screen keep the screen on (`keepScreenOn` on their root view) while they are visible, so a screen timeout cannot lock the phone during a 180 s selfie capture or stop reader mode; a host test per screen.
6. **Honesty:** the pipeline only feeds guidance; the photo uploaded is always the `ImageCapture` still, encoded as today.

### C202. Document quality guidance (M17; engineering 3.2)

1. **Measures** (`commonMain/.../capture/document/`, pure Kotlin, no OpenCV; decision 18): variance of the Laplacian and a Tenengrad score for sharpness, the share of pixels with HSV value ≥ 250 and saturation ≤ 40 for glare, mean luminance for brightness, and edge energy along the card or page guide's four borders against the area outside it for framing; each a port of `services/vision/src/openverify_vision/quality.py` where the server has one, checked against vectors that a Python test asserts from the server's own functions over the same frames (`contracts/fixtures/flow/document-quality-vectors.json`; review F10). The frames are small synthetic renders (96 × 60) stored in the file as base64 raw RGBA. The file names the OpenCV conversions the port must reproduce: the grey coefficients and fixed-point rounding of `cvtColor`, the HSV scale, and `BORDER_REFLECT_101` for the Laplacian and Sobel. It also states a tolerance per measure, since a pure Kotlin port matches OpenCV only to within one.
2. **Client constants** (`contracts/fixtures/flow/document-capture.json`, new, ours, mirroring `selfie-capture.json`'s `client` block): per-measure thresholds for an analysis frame, `hold-ms`, `guidance-hz`, `analysis-width-px`, `manual-shutter-after-ms` 10,000; provisional, with a basis, calibrated on the synthetic ID renders with the Phase 4 harness pattern, never tuned on a test's expected answer; read by Kotlin and Python, each with a test that it uses the file. The calibration report says it covers synthetic renders only and nothing about real camera frames, lenses or lighting (review H21).
3. **Guidance:** hints in a fixed order (move closer, hold still, tilt away from the light, more light, fit the card in the frame), one at a time, announced once per change; MRZ-zone presence (an edge-dense band in the guide's lower third) only as a hint for passports (behaviour M17's public MRZ trigger, our measure).
4. **Auto-capture** (decision 19): when every gate holds for `hold-ms`, the SDK takes the `ImageCapture` still and shows the review screen as today; the shutter is always on screen and becomes the primary action after `manual-shutter-after-ms`, at once when frames are unavailable (C201.3), and after any failed auto-capture.
5. **Server unchanged:** the R-tier blur and glare checks re-measure every upload (PLAN §4.1); the device's measures are never sent as results.
6. **Honesty:** the README says the guidance decides when to take the photo and what hint to show, nothing else; a photo the device would have accepted can still fail on the server, and the reverse.

### C203. PDF417 guidance (M26; behaviour K2, engineering K1, 4.1)

1. **Library** (decision 20): `io.github.zxing-cpp:android` 3.1.1 (Apache-2.0, 2026-07-29; arm64 library 1.75 MB uncompressed, 0.73 MB compressed; every segment 16 KB-aligned; consumer rule `-keep class zxingcpp.** { *; }`), the same decoder family as the server's PDF417 checks (Phase 3), in `androidMain`. zxing's Java core 3.5.4 is the documented fallback only (maintenance mode). `contracts-10b` re-reads the AAR's bundled native sources' licences (zint and any other backend) for `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` before adoption.
2. **Seam** (`commonMain`): `internal interface BarcodeReader { suspend fun readPdf417(frame: AnalysisFrame): ByteArray? }`, raw bytes because AAMVA control characters matter; the zxing-cpp implementation in `androidMain`; none on iOS (the next release's Apple Vision).
3. **Use:** on a `government_id` side that the server's guidance marks as barcode-bearing (the existing `barcode_not_found` hint family), a decoded PDF417 with an AAMVA header shows "barcode found" (never "readable": the server re-decodes a re-encoded JPEG, review H21) over a barcode guide overlay on that side (behaviour M26), and counts as a hint, not an auto-capture gate, until the calibration of C202.2 shows that it decodes at the analysis resolution (review F11); the decoded bytes are dropped at once and never uploaded or logged.
4. **Honesty:** the server re-decodes the uploaded image and decides every barcode check; the device's read is guidance only.
