### C204. Selfie guidance: the common half (M15, M16; behaviour K3, K4; engineering K2, K3, 3.3)

1. **Port** (`commonMain/.../capture/selfie/`; decision 21): the web flow's `yunet-input` (sizing and letterboxing), `yunet-decode` (strides 8, 16, 32, score 0.9, NMS), `head-pose` (the five-landmark yaw ratio) and `guidance-machine` (pose hold, `side-trigger-factor`, `center-trigger-factor`, `hold-ms`), tested against vectors that `selfie-guidance`'s assert-only TypeScript test produces from the web modules (C221.8) (`contracts/fixtures/flow/selfie-guidance-vectors.json`: model outputs → boxes and landmarks → yaw ratio → machine transitions) and the constants of `selfie-capture.json`.
2. **Seam:** `public interface FaceDetectorFactory { public fun create(): FaceDetector }` and `public interface FaceDetector : AutoCloseable { public suspend fun detect(input: FloatArray, width: Int, height: Int): YunetOutputs }`. Its result holds the raw model outputs, and the decoding is common. `YunetOutputs` (public) holds the twelve named tensors `cls`, `obj`, `bbox` and `kps` at strides 8, 16 and 32, each a `FloatArray` flattened row-major, as the web's `YunetOutputs` record (`apps/flow/src/guidance/yunet-decode.ts:14-15`), so the common decoder and the ONNX Runtime implementation cannot disagree on a concatenation order (review F17). The selfie vectors of C204.1 feed it in that form. The model bytes and their expected SHA-256 are the implementation's concern: `ModelSource` is an internal type of `openverify-face`, declared there by `contracts-10e` (review V11), so the SDK declares no such type. A fake detector lives in `commonTest`.
3. **Auto-capture per prompt** (decision 19): with a detector installed, each server prompt (`center`, `left`, `right`, in the server's order) is captured when the machine holds it; the manual shutter stays on screen and becomes primary after 10 s; every state change is announced once (not at 8 Hz); the prompt text never mirrors ("left" is the person's own left).
4. **What is sent:** the C40.3 uploads as today, plus the client-reported `capture-trigger` (C206.2); never a yaw ratio, score or verdict.
5. **2.2.1 on Android** (Phase 9 C171.4, decision 25): 20 s before the capture's 180 s lifetime ends, the SDK announces it in a polite live region and offers "more time", which calls `POST /api/v1/flow/selfie-captures/:id/extend` (new `FlowApi.extendSelfieCapture`), usable at least ten times; a 409 `selfie_capture_not_extendable` after submit is shown as "already sent". A host test extends ten times on a frozen `TimeSource` and then uploads.
6. **Without a detector** (P2 declined, or the host did not install `openverify-face`): the prompts, the manual shutter, the extension and the server's decisions work as today; the README says auto-capture for selfies needs `openverify-face`.
7. **Honesty** (behaviour M16, engineering 3.3): device guidance is never a liveness verdict; liveness is decided on the server (MiniFASNet, pose verification, pose repeat); the guidance does not cover camera injection, replays on a rooted device or a modified app (PLAN §9 risk 1), and no certification is claimed (ADR 0002 rule 3g).
8. **On-device face processing** (review P24): frames are analysed only while the selfie capture screen is shown, after consent (Phase 9 C147.6). The detector's boxes and landmarks live for one frame callback; only the guidance machine's pose state outlives it. Nothing is stored or sent. A host test asserts that no box or landmark is reachable after the frame callback returns. `docs/checks/selfie.md` and `docs/android/privacy.md` state the on-device detection, so operators can write an accurate biometric notice.

### C205. `openverify-face`: ONNX Runtime and YuNet (owner-gated: P2)

1. **Artifact** (decision 24; ours, departing from PLAN §6's "in `androidMain`", which C222 corrects): `android/openverify-face`, an Android library depending on `:openverify-sdk` and `com.microsoft.onnxruntime:onnxruntime-android` 1.31.0 (MIT, 2026-10-09; Eigen MPL-2.0 compiled in), so the 34 MB arm64 runtime, its Eigen and its licence question reach only hosts that add the artifact, and `:openverify-sdk` ships without them. Built in 10E, only once P2 is granted.
2. **Telemetry removed** (PLAN §6; Phase 4 decision 1; engineering 3.3; decision 22). There are four layers:
   - The manifest merger removes `ai.onnxruntime.TelemetryInitializer` and ORT's `ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE`. Both remove markers carry `tools:selector` set to the package of ORT's own library manifest, so the permission is removed only where ORT declares it, and a host app's other libraries keep theirs (review P10). `contracts-10e` reads that package from the AAR's `AndroidManifest.xml` and records it in the inventory, since the Maven group `com.microsoft.onnxruntime` need not be the manifest package (the classes are `ai.onnxruntime.*`); the merged-manifest host test below fails on a wrong value (review V14).
   - `Os.setenv("ORT_DISABLE_TELEMETRY", "1", true)` runs before the first `OrtEnvironment`, in the one class that creates it.
   - `OrtEnvironment.setTelemetry(false)` is called before every session is created (review P09). `OrtEnvironment` is a process singleton. If the host app already created one, our class detects it (an environment that existed before our first call), records it as a PII-free event, and the README says that the host's own ORT configuration then governs; a host test covers that case.
   - Tests. A host test over the demo's merged manifest checks for no provider and no extra permission. In the same test, a fake library that declares `ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE` must keep it. A device test runs YuNet with the provider removed. It measures the app UID's bytes with `TrafficStats` (failing if the counters are `UNSUPPORTED`) from before the first `OrtEnvironment`, through inference and the closing of the session, to the end of a fixed quiet period (120 s). It asserts the counters did not move beyond the test runner's own traffic, which it measures separately. It asserts that no class under `ai.onnxruntime.telemetry` was initialised. It asserts that no new file appeared under the app's `files`, `databases`, `cache` and `no_backup` directories, which would be a queued event. Then it measures again after a process restart, which would flush a queue (reviews P09, H06). The README states the measured window, not "no bytes sent" in general.
3. **Model** (decision 23): `face_detection_yunet_2026may.onnx` (229,738 B, SHA-256 `ebafce4e…22f0f0`, MIT at opencv_zoo `47534e2`), fetched by `python3 -I scripts/fetch_models.py --only face_detection_yunet` in a CI step and in Validation before Gradle runs; a Gradle task only copies it into a generated assets directory after checking its digest against `models/manifest.json`, and fails with the fetch command when the file is absent, so no Gradle build reaches the network (review F19); never committed; shipped in the AAR with its MIT notice and the WIDER FACE caveat in `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` §3, the policy the web flow already ships under.
4. **Inference:** one `OrtSession` per capture screen, CPU, one thread pool of 2, closed with the screen; inputs and outputs as C204.2.
5. **Spike** (decision 22): a custom ORT build (`--no_telemetry`, YuNet's operators only) measured for size; adopted only if it saves at least 20 MB on arm64 and builds reproducibly in CI, otherwise the official AAR stays.
6. **If the owner declines P2:** the artifact is not built or published; selfies keep the manual shutter with the server's prompts and decisions (C204.6); a runtime without MPL-2.0 code (LiteRT, OpenCV's `FaceDetectorYN`) is evaluated in the next release (C223). The same answer governs `onnxruntime-web` in the hosted flow (Phase 4 decision 11), which already ships Eigen pending that decision.
7. **Honesty:** as C204.7.

### C206. Upload metadata (Phase 9 C153.2; behaviour M28)

1. **Capture method:** the SDK applies `captureMethodsByDevice.native` when present, else `captureMethods`, and sends the upload's `capture-method` (`camera` or `upload`); production photos still come only from the live camera (decision 26; behaviour M28: stricter than the public default).
2. **Capture trigger** (ours; the public product labels submissions auto or manual, behaviour 2.1): an optional multipart field `capture-trigger` (`auto`, `manual`) on photo uploads, stored in a new `files.capture_trigger` column and served as the file attribute `capture-trigger`, labelled client-reported in the docs; analytics do not read it in Phase 10 (C223).
3. **Honesty:** both fields are what the client says; the server cannot prove how a photo was taken (Phase 9 C153.2).

### C207. NFC in the SDK (M18–M22; behaviour K5, K6; engineering K5, K6, 5.3)

1. **Model:** `NfcStep(id, androidOnly, maxAttempts?, fallbackAvailable)` in the registry (`fallback-available` is the client-view member of 9). `FlowApi` gains `createChipChallenge(verificationId): FlowChipChallenge` (C62.5: `id` `chc_…`, `nonce` 16 lower-case hex, `expires-at`), `uploadChipFile(verificationId, kind, bytes)` (the eight chip kinds, DG2 ≤ 1 MiB, the others ≤ 64 KiB, checked before sending), `submitChip(verificationId, FlowChipSubmit)` (`challenge-id`, `nonce`, `active-authentication-response?` ≤ 1 KiB base64, `device-report?`), and `reportClientUnable(verificationId, reason)`; each with a `ContractFixturesTest` decoder over Phase 5's fixtures.
2. **The sequence** (decision 27; behaviour M21; `docs/checks/nfc.md` order changed, C222):
   - create or reuse the `government-id-nfc` verification;
   - the access key: the MRZ of the inquiry's `government_id` step when the client view prefills it (`prefill-mrz`, the document number, birth date and expiry), else the person types the three values on an MRZ-entry screen. `prefill-mrz` is served only to this step's client view, only while the `nfc` step is the current position, only to a session whose client declared spec 2 with `mobile-sdk-name` set (Phase 7 C94.5), and only when the flow has a decided government ID of a TD1 or TD3 document; the web flow never receives it (decision 63; review P01). This is a stated amendment of Phase 9 C151.5's rule that no other field leaves the server (Phase 9 review P16). A contract test asserts that a session answer for a web client at an `nfc` step carries no `prefill-mrz`. The restriction keeps the values away from the web flow, which cannot use them; it does not stop a holder of the session token who sends the SDK's `User-Agent`, which the docs say; for a card that offers it, the CAN (six digits) on a CAN screen; `BacKeySeed.mrzInformation` derives the key input in common code, ASCII only;
   - **the challenge before the tap** (review H17): the tap screen shows the hint and an "I'm ready" action. The SDK requests a chip challenge when the person presses it and enables the reader then, so one tap covers access, reading and Active Authentication while the chip is in the field, and a person may take as long as they need before pressing. The server accepts a challenge on an `initiated` verification before any file (`services/chip-challenges/create.ts`, re-checked by `contracts-10c`). A challenge that expires unused returns the screen to "I'm ready". The SDK requests at most 9 challenges per verification, under the 10 that cancel it. When the ninth is spent, the screen offers "use another way" when a fallback exists, or "continue later" (cancel, resumable), and says why. A host test on a frozen `TimeSource` waits 20 minutes on the tap screen and can still read;
   - the reader (C209) reads the files, runs Active Authentication over the nonce, and returns `ChipRead`;
   - the DG1 review: the SDK parses DG1's MRZ in common code (`Mrz.kt`) and shows the name, document number, nationality and dates for the person to confirm before anything is uploaded (behaviour M20); DG2 is not decoded (Android has no JPEG 2000 decoder) and no portrait is shown;
   - upload each file with its kind, then submit within the challenge's lifetime; a 409 `chip_challenge_invalid` (expired during a slow upload) asks for one more tap with a fresh challenge;
   - poll the verification and read the session, as every verification step (C193.4).
3. **The seam** (`commonMain`, public; engineering 5.3): `public interface ChipReader { public suspend fun read(access: ChipAccess, challenge: ByteArray, progress: (ChipProgress) -> Unit): ChipRead }`, with `ChipAccess.Mrz(BacKeySeed)` or `ChipAccess.Can(String)`, `ChipRead(files: Map<FileKind, ByteArray>, activeAuthenticationResponse: ByteArray?, deviceReport: ChipDeviceReport)`, `ChipProgress` (`waiting_for_document`, `connected`, `reading(fileKind, percent)`, `authenticating`, `done`), and `ChipReadException` with a closed `ChipReadFailure` (`tag_lost`, `access_denied`, `unsupported_document`, `timeout`, `nfc_disabled`, `reader_busy`). Android's NFC calls live only in the implementation (C209), so iOS can implement the same interface in the next release. **Its iOS fit is checked, not assumed** (review H22): `contracts-10a` desk-checks the documented API of the iOS reader PLAN names (NFCPassportReader 2.3.3). It checks whether that reader accepts an external Active Authentication challenge, CAN access and the MRZ key fields, and records the result in ADR 0009. If any is missing or unclear, ADR 0009 says that the seam's iOS fit is unverified, and `ChipReader` with its types carries an `@ExperimentalOpenVerifyApi` opt-in annotation until the iOS release. A change after 1.0 is then not a break of a stable API.
4. **Data minimisation** (behaviour M20, `docs/checks/nfc.md`): exactly the file kinds the server takes; DG11 and DG12 are never read; chip bytes are held in memory, never written to disk or logs, and dropped after the submit.
5. **States and screens** (behaviour M18, M22): NFC off (a prompt with a shortcut to the system NFC settings), no NFC hardware (the screen of 9), "hold the phone to the document" with the antenna hint for passports and cards, progress by file, a lifted document ("keep it still" and continue where it stopped), retry after `tag_lost` within the step's attempts, the troubleshooting tips on cancel, and a polite live region for each phase; the reader is active only while the tap screen is visible (resumed lifecycle). **Wording** (review H10): the phases read "connecting", "reading", "signing the check code" and "read; we will check it now". The words "verified", "authentic" and "genuine" never appear, and no success icon shows before the server decides, because Passive and Active Authentication and the bindings are the server's (11). A host test over the five locales' NFC strings refuses a short list of verdict words per locale. **The DG1 review's "this is not my data"**: one new read within the same attempt; if the person refuses again, cancel, which leaves the inquiry resumable. Nothing is uploaded in either case.
6. **Process death:** the read restarts from the tap; nothing read is restored (ours: chip bytes never reach saved state).
7. **Attempts and fallback:** the server counts attempts (`maxAttempts`, default 3) and decides whether an `exhausted` fallback follows (Phase 9 C150.6); the SDK shows the position the server returns.
8. **Manifest** (behaviour M13, decision 36): `openverify-nfc` declares `android.permission.NFC` (normal) and `<uses-feature android:name="android.hardware.nfc" android:required="false" />`; `:openverify-sdk` declares neither.
9. **When the device cannot read a chip** (ours; decisions 28 and 64). The cases are: no NFC hardware, no `ChipReader` installed, or NFC present but off with the person choosing another way. The SDK tells them apart through the internal `NfcPlatform` seam (C207.12). Each reason has its own message (review H11):
   - `reader_not_installed`: "this app cannot read document chips";
   - `nfc_unavailable`: "this phone cannot read document chips";
   - `nfc_disabled_declined`: NFC is off, with the shortcut to the system NFC settings, and the person chose another way (review H12).

   When the client view's `fallback-available` is true, the screen offers "use another way". It calls `POST /api/v1/flow/verifications/:id/client-unable` with `reason` `nfc_unavailable`, `reader_not_installed`, `nfc_disabled_declined` or `platform_unsupported`. The server then:
   - records the reason and the client's platform on the step record as client-reported;
   - ends the verification without passing (`canceled` with `client_unable`);
   - advances through the step's `exhausted` edge.

   **A client-unable exit always sends the inquiry to `needs_review`** at completion, whatever the step's `onFallback` says, because the reason is self-asserted and the web flow can always assert it (review P02). The only exception is a version that carries the separate publish acknowledgement `client-unable:<step id>` (a Phase 9 C150.6 and C154.9 amendment, with its publish test): Phase 9's `fallback:<step id>` covers a person who tried and failed, not a claim that no reader exists.

   Without a fallback, the screen offers only "continue later" (cancel, resumable). The route is accepted only for the current step of type `nfc` (409 otherwise), at most once per verification. The person is never told that the fallback goes to review. `docs/checks/nfc.md` says that every reason is self-reported.

   Publish warns (Phase 9 C154.9 style) that an `nfc` step without an `exhausted` edge stops, at that step, every web session and every Android session without a reader (review H11).
10. **The web flow at an `nfc` step** (behaviour O5, K6; Phase 5 C64.1 amended): the unsupported screen says "reading a document chip needs the organisation's app; a browser cannot read it" (never "a phone with NFC", which would send the person to the same link on another phone; review H11), and offers "use another way" through the same route with `platform_unsupported` when `fallback-available` is true; in `en` and `es`, and in `fr`, `de` and `ar` marked machine-drafted (Phase 9 C161.5).
11. **Honesty:** the device reads and the server verifies: Passive Authentication, Active Authentication over the server's nonce, the DG1-to-MRZ and DG2-to-selfie bindings and the repeat check stay C62's; everything the device says about BAC, PACE, Chip Authentication and timing is `device-report` metadata shown to operators only. A client-unable report is self-asserted and never counts as a pass.
12. **What the phone offers** (review F16; ours): an internal `expect` seam `NfcPlatform` in `commonMain`, with `availability(): NfcAvailability` (`READY`, `DISABLED`, `NO_HARDWARE`) and `openSettings()`. The Android actual reads `PackageManager.FEATURE_NFC` and `NfcAdapter.isEnabled`, which need no permission, and opens `Settings.ACTION_NFC_SETTINGS`. The iOS actual reports `NO_HARDWARE` and claims nothing. The NFC screens read the seam before any read; whether a reader is installed comes from the registry (C191). `NfcPlatform` is internal, so it is outside the ABI dump; `contracts-10c` adds it with its stub actuals.

### C208. The sandbox test chip (M43)

1. **Route** (ours, decision 29; how a device obtains synthetic chip bytes is ours to design, behaviour M43): `POST /api/v1/flow/verifications/:id/sandbox-chip` (flow session; sandbox environments only, 404 in production with no hint the route exists; rate policy `flow-sandbox-chip`, 10 an hour per inquiry), body `{ challenge-id, scenario }` with `scenario` one of `match` (default), `mrz-mismatch`, `face-mismatch`, `no-active-authentication`, `tampered-dg1`. The server builds a synthetic chip with `@openverify/emrtd/testing` signed by the environment's sandbox Utopia anchor (C62.8): DG1 from the latest decided government ID's MRZ (or a fixed synthetic one), DG2 from the latest selfie centre (`match`) or a public-domain fixture face of another person (`face-mismatch`), and a per-verification AA key in DG15 that signs the challenge's nonce. The verification's metadata records `sandbox-chip: <scenario>`.
   - **No image leaves the server** (decision 69; review P15). The route attaches DG2 to the verification on the server as a chip file, by reference to the stored selfie, so no image leaves the server. It returns `{ files[] {kind, content} (base64; every file but DG2), dg2-attached: true, active-authentication-response? }`. The client uploads the returned files through the real upload route and never uploads DG2. A Testcontainers test asserts that the route's answer never contains a selfie's bytes. DG1 is returned for the review screen and the real upload. It is the MRZ the same session holder just photographed, and the route is accepted only at the current `nfc` step of an SDK client (as `prefill-mrz`, C207.2).
   - **`face-mismatch` needs fixture faces** (review P15). Those faces are never committed and never shipped (`THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` §4.1), so `face-mismatch` is offered only where the fetched fixture directory is configured (`OPENVERIFY_SANDBOX_CHIP_FACES`, set by the e2e stack and the Testcontainers harness, never by an image). Anywhere else it answers 422 `sandbox_chip_scenario_unavailable`, and the API image never bundles a face.
   - **The anchor** (decision 69; review F27). The route answers 409 `sandbox_chip_anchor_missing` when the sandbox trust store does not hold the current Utopia anchor; `seed:dev`, the only path that installs it today, refuses production mode (Phase 5 C62.8). A new command, `pnpm --filter @openverify/api trust:sandbox-utopia`, installs or refreshes the sandbox-only Utopia anchor through `applyTrustSync` to `openverify-sandbox` in any mode. The anchor is `sandbox_only`, so production environments' loaders never see it (Phase 5 C62.8). The command is documented in `docs/checks/nfc.md` and covered by a Testcontainers test in production mode.
2. **Client:** `SandboxChipReader` (`commonMain`) implements `ChipReader` over that route and is offered, beside any installed reader, only when the `nfc` client view's `sandbox-chip-available` is true. The server sets that member only in a sandbox environment whose trust store holds the current Utopia anchor, so the SDK never guesses the environment (review F27). It is labelled "Test chip (sandbox)"; the rest of the sequence (review, upload, submit, poll) is the real one, so the NFC screens and the server's checks are exercised end to end without hardware or LGPL code.
3. **Isolation:** the route's module imports `@openverify/emrtd/testing` lazily and only behind the environment check; a test asserts the production path never loads it, and a `UTO` issuer in production still fails `specimen_issuer` (C62.3).
4. **Honesty:** a sandbox chip is labelled as such on the verification and in the console; it proves the flow and the server's checks, never a reader.

### C209. The JMRTD reader in `openverify-nfc` (owner-gated: P1; engineering 5.1–5.3)

1. **Stack** (pins re-read: JMRTD 0.8.9, LGPL-2.1-or-later; `scuba-smartcards` 0.0.21 and `scuba-sc-android` 0.0.27, LGPL-2.1-or-later with `CommandAPDU.java` and `ResponseAPDU.java` under GPL-2.0-only WITH Classpath-exception-2.0; `cert-cvc` 1.4.13; Bouncy Castle `bcprov-jdk18on` and `bcutil-jdk18on` 1.86, MIT), in `android/openverify-nfc` only, by Maven coordinates, never shaded or copied. Built in 10E, only once P1 is decided.
2. **Reader** (`OpenVerifyNfc.chipReader(context): ChipReader`): `NfcAdapter.enableReaderMode` for NFC-A and NFC-B with the NDEF check skipped while the tap screen is resumed; `IsoDep` with a raised timeout and extended-length APDUs where supported; `IsoDepCardService` constructed directly; PACE when EF.CardAccess offers it (MRZ or CAN), else BAC (PACE is mandatory for chips issued from 2027-01-01); the files of C207.4; Chip Authentication when DG14 offers it, for secure messaging, reported in `device-report` only; Active Authentication over the server's 8-byte nonce after access, unverified on the device; Bouncy Castle passed as JMRTD's provider object, never inserted globally; all I/O off the main thread; the `java.util.logging` loggers of JMRTD, SCUBA and cert-cvc set to `Level.OFF` for the read and restored after it (C213.3).
3. **Testable core** (reviews H09, F15; engineering 5.3 item 7): the protocol order, retries, progress and error mapping live in a `ChipSession`-driven orchestrator tested on the host with a scripted fake. The JMRTD calls sit in one thin adapter class excluded from Kover like the CameraX glue. **The real JMRTD service is tested on the JVM.** A JVM host test drives `PassportService` through the adapter against an in-process responder. The responder answers the APDUs of a synthetic Utopia chip built from `@openverify/emrtd/testing`'s files, which are exported to a fixture with their keys: BAC with its secure messaging, file selection and reads, and INTERNAL AUTHENTICATE for Active Authentication over a given nonce. It is checked against the ICAO worked vectors for key derivation. PACE is tested against the responder only if the responder implements it within the Workflow; otherwise the TDD file and the release notes say that PACE runs in no automated test. The device test covers only the no-NFC-adapter path (`NfcAdapter.getDefaultAdapter` is `null` on the emulator), named as such. Kover gates the module at 80%.
4. **Licence boundary:** consumer rules keep `org.jmrtd.**`, `net.sf.scuba.**` and `org.ejbca.cvc.**` unobfuscated so the LGPL libraries stay replaceable; the AAR carries the LGPL-2.1, LGPL-3.0 and GPL-2.0-with-Classpath-exception texts and the two files' source location in its `NOTICE`; the module's own code stays LGPL-3.0 (decision 30; Phase 5 C67); the README states the host app's LGPL obligations (relinking, no reverse-engineering ban for the library), not legal advice.
5. **If the owner declines P1:** the module is built on a fork of `scuba-smartcards` whose two GPL files are rewritten from ISO/IEC 7816-4 by an agent that does not read the originals (clean room), licensed Apache-2.0 for the two new files and LGPL-2.1-or-later for the rest of the fork, kept in `android/openverify-nfc/scuba-fork/`. It is **published as its own artifact**, `openverify-scuba-smartcards` (C217.1), because dependency substitution changes only this build's resolution: JMRTD's own POM still names `net.sf.scuba:scuba-smartcards`, so a host resolving `openverify-nfc` would get the upstream jar with the two GPL files (review F28). `openverify-nfc` declares the fork directly and excludes `net.sf.scuba:scuba-smartcards` from JMRTD. A test over the staged POMs and Gradle module metadata asserts that none names the upstream module. The clean-room agent's only source for class and method shapes is the public `javax.smartcardio` API specification, with ISO/IEC 7816-4 for the encoding rules. This adds one agent to 10E. Until either path lands, Android NFC works with the sandbox test chip only, and the README and the console say a production NFC step needs the reader.
6. **Owner steps** (decision 48; review H09): a manual read of a real passport or ID card on a real phone by the owner, in the demo's debug-only **reader check** mode. That mode runs `ChipReader.read` and shows pass or fail per protocol and file. It never calls the API, keeps the bytes in memory only and drops them at once, so no document data is written anywhere and nothing reaches a server (rule 3). The result is recorded as pass or fail per document type and protocol. Without it, the release notes say the JMRTD reader is proven only by the JVM test against a synthetic chip (3); the sandbox chip proves the flow and the server's checks, never a reader (C208.4).
7. **Honesty:** as C207.11; no conformance with ICAO or BSI test specifications is claimed.

### C210. Device-integrity heuristics (M24; behaviour K12; engineering K10, 4.2)

1. **Where:** Phase 7's `DeviceSignals` (C94.7) in the session-integrity tier, collected only after consent under `notice.session-integrity` (C94.6), sent through `POST /api/v1/flow/signals` with `platform: android`; implemented in `androidMain/.../signals/` with an iOS placeholder that reports nothing (decision 31: our own code, RootBeer 0.1.2 read as a reference only). `DeviceSignals` is internal, created by the activity; `OpenVerifyComponents` is the only host-facing registry (review F30; `contracts-10a` records Phase 7's as-built visibility and makes it internal if Phase 7 left it public).
   **The notice follows the collection** (decision 66; review P04; a Phase 7 C94.6 amendment): `notice.session-integrity` gets a new built-in text in the five locales. The new text says that the session's integrity checks read the device's build properties, the app's own process (its memory map, thread names and loaded classes), the mount table and the presence of known files, and send only the codes of C210.2. It joins the web catalogs and the Android strings through the key map (C199.1). `docs/compliance/` gains the probe list of C210.2 in its strictly-necessary assessment template (in the compliance guide's file that `contracts-10b`'s inventory names; written by `integration-10b`, review V05), so an operator's assessment covers each probe. The operator remains the controller who decides whether the exemption applies (not legal advice).
2. **The wire** (Phase 7 C94.2 amended, strict, closed lists): `integrity.android { emulator-indicators[], root-indicators[], hooking-indicators[] }`, each an array of at most 12 codes from:
   - emulator: `build_hardware_goldfish_or_ranchu`, `build_product_sdk`, `build_model_emulator`, `qemu_property`, `qemu_device_file`, `no_telephony_and_generic_board`;
   - root: `su_binary_on_path`, `build_tags_test_keys`, `system_mounted_rw`, `magisk_mount_trace`, `ro_debuggable_and_not_secure`. No package-visibility probe and no `<queries>` entry: what other apps are installed is not read at all (decision 66; review P04; engineering 4.2 finds such checks largely inert without `<queries>`, and they would add "App activity: Installed apps" to every host's Data safety form);
   - hooking: `frida_in_process_maps`, `frida_thread_name`, `xposed_bridge_loadable`, `xposed_in_stack_trace`, `unexpected_native_library`.
   Never read (Phase 7 C94.3, C94.7; decision 31): `QUERY_ALL_PACKAGES`, accessibility services, `ADB_ENABLED`, `DEVELOPMENT_SETTINGS_ENABLED`, IMEI, MAC addresses, local network ports, `Build.FINGERPRINT` parsing.
3. **Probes:** each a pure function over an injected `DeviceProbe` (system properties through the public `Build` fields, file existence, `/proc/self/maps` and `/proc/self/task/*/comm` lines matched against fixed substrings, `Class.forName(name, false, loader)` through the app's class loader so a probed class is never initialised (a host test with a fake class whose initialiser throws; review P22), `/proc/mounts`), so host tests run every rule under Robolectric with shadowed `Build` values and a fake file system; a probe that throws records nothing for itself. The device test records the probes' total time on the CI emulator and asserts a generous bound of 500 ms, since a tight bound on a shared 2-vCPU emulator would be flaky and the Rules forbid retries (review F31); the target of 50 ms is reported, not asserted.
4. **Signals** (C90.2 amended; registry entries with `ConfigDefault` thresholds, basis and source): `inquiry/device_emulated`, `inquiry/device_rooted` (the public product lists a rooted-device signal, behaviour M24 [H9]; the name is ours), `inquiry/device_hooked`; each `true` with its indicator codes as reasons, or `null` with `no_indicator_found` or `not_android` (both new codes in Phase 7's frozen `SIGNAL_NULL_REASONS`, class `ignore` in `ON_NULL_DEFAULTS`, a stated C90.3 amendment with Phase 7's classification test; review H13) or `collector_missing` (Phase 7's, class `needs_review`); H-tier, `beta`, layer `device`, collection tier `session-integrity`, `flow-provisional: true` (Phase 9 C150.9), `default: true`, with a confidence that grows with the number of independent indicator families.
5. **Never decisive alone** (decision 32; ours; reviews P13, H01). The registry gains `decisive-alone: boolean`, `false` for these three signals and for C211's two. The rule is defined over what a route reads, not over what it reads alone, and is applied at both publish checks:
   - **Flows** (a Phase 9 C154.9 amendment): a `failure` step downstream of a route whose condition reads any `decisive-alone: false` signal is refused (`signal_not_decisive_alone`, a `definition_invalid` code). The exception is a path where a verification step and a route that reads that verification's outcome (Phase 9 C150.7's definition) lie between the signal's route and the failure. A condition that only adds a trivially true fact therefore no longer passes. A golden test covers `and` and `or` with an unrelated fact.
   - **Workflows** (a Phase 8 publish-check amendment, the same function): the same rule applies to a `decline_inquiry` step and every other adverse step (Phase 8 C122.1), which are refused, not only warned.
   - **Either direction** (a Phase 9 C150.7 widening): every route that reads such a signal needs the acknowledgement `heuristic:<step id>`, whatever the signal's tier (the two Play Integrity signals are A-tier) and whichever way the route goes. A route that skips the selfie or NFC step on `inquiry/device_integrity_level` therefore needs it too.

   The docs recommend `flag_for_review`. They also state the rule's limit: a condition that couples the signal with an outcome of a verification that does lie on the path passes the check, and Phase 9's decision record (C150.7) then names the signal codes with their tiers.
6. **What they do not cover** (stated in `docs/checks/signals.md` and the README; ADR 0002 rule 3g): every probe runs inside an environment the attacker may control, so root hiding, a modified emulator image, renamed hooking tools and a repackaged app return clean answers; a clean result is never `passed` or "not rooted"; they say nothing about camera injection or a relay. The CI emulator reports itself as an emulator, and its device test expects it.
7. **Honesty:** three `beta` heuristics with reasons, never a pass; publish refuses a flow failure or a workflow decline that follows from them without a verification outcome in between, and every route on them is acknowledged (5).

### C211. Play Integrity: seam, server provider and the client artifact (M23; behaviour K12; engineering K10, 4.3)

1. **Off by default** (decision 33): nothing is requested unless the environment binds a `device-integrity` provider and the host installs an `IntegrityTokenProvider`; the product, CI and every test work without either.
2. **The seam** (`commonMain`, public): `public interface IntegrityTokenProvider { public suspend fun token(requestHash: String): String? }`; a `null` or a thrown exception sends nothing and the signals read `null` with `integrity_token_unavailable`.
3. **The flow API** (ours): when the session response's `integrity { request: true }` says so (a bound provider, the session-integrity tier collected, no verdict yet for the inquiry), the SDK calls `POST /api/v1/flow/integrity-challenges` → `{ id (ich_), request-hash, expires-at }` (TTL 600 s; `request-hash` is base64url of SHA-256 over `openverify/play-integrity/v1`, the session id and 16 random bytes, computed by the server, so the device needs no hashing for it), then `POST /api/v1/flow/integrity-tokens` with `{ challenge-id, token }` (≤ 16 KiB; 202). One request per inquiry (decision 34: the default Cloud quota is 10,000 a day), after consent, never before a capture screen.
4. **The server provider** (Phase 5 C63; kind `device-integrity`): `play-integrity` decodes the token through `POST playintegrity.googleapis.com/v1/<package>:decodeIntegrityToken` with the operator's service account (an RS256 JWT signed with `node:crypto` for the OAuth token, no Google library), checks `requestPackageName`, `requestHash`, the timestamp within 10 minutes, `appRecognitionVerdict` and the certificate digest the operator configured, and stores a fixed projection of the verdict: the `device-recognition` labels, `app-recognition`, the boolean of each server check and `decoded-at`. Every other member (`accountDetails`, `recentDeviceActivity`, `deviceRecall`, `environmentDetails`, `playProtectVerdict`) is dropped after the checks and never stored or logged, and a test with a decoded fixture carrying every optional member asserts that only the projection is stored (review P12); a `sandbox` implementation answers deterministically from magic tokens (`sandbox-strong`, `sandbox-device`, `sandbox-basic`, `sandbox-none`, `sandbox-unrecognized-app`, `sandbox-replayed`); production refuses the sandbox binding; an unbound provider means no request. The decode runs in a job through the outbound client (15 s, no redirects), never in the request; tests use a fake Google endpoint, never the network.
5. **Signals** (C90.2 amended; review H13): `inquiry/device_integrity_level` (`strong`, `device`, `basic`, `none`) and `inquiry/app_recognized` (`true`, `false`), A-tier, `beta`, `decisive-alone: false`. Play Integrity's `UNEVALUATED` app verdict maps to `null`, never `false`. The `null` reasons, with C90.3 amended:
   - `integrity_token_unavailable`: the host installed no provider, or the provider gave nothing. Class `ignore`, since it is the host app's choice.
   - `integrity_unevaluated`: class `needs_review` (review V09). Play reports `UNEVALUATED` when a requirement was missed, typically because the device did not meet integrity: a hooked, rooted or emulated device, which an attacker controls. So it is a data gap with an adverse cause and flags review whatever `onNull` says (Phase 9 C150.3), and `docs/checks/signals.md` states the cause.
   - `integrity_decode_failed`: class `needs_review`, a data gap.
   - `provider_not_configured`: Phase 7's own, `needs_review`, when no provider is bound.

   That last case is the default (1), so, as Phase 7 does for `proxy_detected`, publish refuses a branch or review rule on either signal in an environment with no `device-integrity` provider bound (422 `provider_not_bound`, Phase 7's code). An off-by-default install therefore never sends every inquiry to review. Every value carries Phase 7's adapter provenance (C90.3): `signal-details.sources` names the provider and whether it is the `sandbox`.
6. **The client artifact** (owner-gated: P3): `android/openverify-integrity-play`, depending on `:openverify-sdk` and `com.google.android.play:integrity` (Google's terms, not a rule 4 licence), implementing the provider with a standard request prepared lazily on the first `token()` call with the host's Cloud project number (C211.10; warm-up at launch would contact Google for every app user, before any consent, review P05). Built in 10E only once P3 is granted, with a pin whose publication date `contracts-10e` verifies is at least 14 days old. **If the owner declines:** OpenVerify ships no Google code; the README gives a host-side recipe (a few lines implementing `IntegrityTokenProvider` with the host's own dependency), and the server provider is unchanged.
7. **The terms bind the design** (engineering 4.3): no Play Integrity field feeds a device list, the graph, `device-fingerprint` or `device-class` (a test over the signal registry's `inputs` and the graph's edge kinds); the docs state the 10,000-a-day default quota and that sideloaded or self-built host apps are unrecognised by design.
8. **What it does not cover:** a certified device can still present an injected camera feed through a granted app, or relay a real person; devices without Google Play give no verdict. It is a signal about the app and the device, never about the person, and never a pass.
9. **The notice names Google** (decision 66; review P04; Phase 7 C94.6 and Phase 9 C154.9 amended): a new required notice key `notice.play-integrity` (`legal: notice` in `COPY_KEYS`) names Google as a recipient and says what Google receives (the app's package, version and signing certificate, its licence status and the device's attestation; engineering 4.3). Publish requires the key to be shown on a screen that dominates the session-integrity collection when the environment binds a `device-integrity` provider (`play_integrity_notice_missing`, a `definition_invalid` code). The built-in text exists in the five locales, from the web catalogs through the key map.
10. **Lazy preparation** (review P05): nothing contacts Google before consent. The provider factory creates nothing at construction, and the provider prepares its standard request lazily on its first `token()` call. C211.3 makes that call only after consent and only when the session says `integrity { request: true }`. A host test with a fake `IntegrityManager` asserts that constructing and installing the provider makes no Play call, and the README says so.

### C212. Accessibility on Android (M35; Phase 9 C171.1)

1. **Scope** (WCAG 2.2 AA applied to native screens through W3C's WCAG2ICT guidance, ours): every SDK screen and state (each step, error, retry, waiting, halted, NFC phase, capture guidance, review, final), in the five locales, right to left, light and dark, at font scales 1.0 and 2.0, with TalkBack and with a hardware keyboard.
2. **Automated:**
   - Compose UI tests in `androidHostTest` for every screen: a content description or text on every actionable node, 48 dp minimum touch targets, traversal order (heading first, the error summary before fields), live regions on status changes. **Clipped text** (review H19): the SDK's one text composable records `TextLayoutResult.hasVisualOverflow` and `didOverflowHeight` into a test-only semantics property, which a host test asserts is false on every screen at `@Config(fontScale = 2.0f)` and under Android's `en-XA` pseudo-locale. **Colour alone:** a narrow automated rule requires every error and status node to carry text or an icon with a content description; the rest is a manual-checklist item (4);
   - Compose's accessibility checks (`enableAccessibilityChecks()`, `ui-test-junit4-accessibility`, built on Google's Accessibility Test Framework, Apache-2.0, test scope only): `foundation-10a`'s spike (c) establishes whether they run under Robolectric through Roborazzi's accessibility module; if they do, every screenshot test runs them; if not, a device test runs them on the CI emulator over one state per screen (C219.4);
   - capture prompts and NFC phases announced once per change, never at the frame rate (a test counts announcements).
3. **Timing** (2.2.1; review H17): every server lifetime the SDK meets is listed in `docs/android/accessibility.md` with its 2.2.1 treatment:
   - the selfie capture's 180 s, extendable (C204.5);
   - the chip challenge's 300 s, which starts only when the person presses "I'm ready" and is renewed on request (C207.2);
   - an OTP code's lifetime, renewable on request through a resend;
   - the session's own expiry, which is the operator's setting and is extendable on resume (Phase 9 C151.10).

   No other SDK time limit exists. Auto-capture never times a person out of a pose; the manual shutter is always available (C202.4, C204.3). Host tests on a frozen `TimeSource` back each line.
4. **Manual** (decision 47): a TalkBack pass on a real phone over every screen, the selfie prompts, the NFC prompts and the error screens, a 200% font-scale pass, a hardware-keyboard pass and a check that `FLAG_SECURE`, the hidden overlays and the accessibility-data-sensitive fields (C213.1) hide nothing from TalkBack, run by a person (the owner or a contracted auditor), never an agent, with the checklist and an empty results table in `docs/accessibility/android-audit-<date>.md`.
5. **The statement** (Phase 9 C171.6 amended): `docs/accessibility/statement.md` gains the Android SDK's scope, method and automated results, and says "not yet evaluated for conformance" for Android until the manual pass has run; the manual pass is carried to the release gate (Phase 11's acceptance), as Phase 9 did for the web.
6. **Honesty:** no conformance is claimed beyond the evidence; automated checks do not hear what TalkBack says.

### C213. Security and privacy of the SDK (M36, M41; Phase 11 B11-I1, B11-S1)

1. **Screens:** `FLAG_SECURE` on the flow activity, as today, documented (behaviour M36). It blocks screenshots, screen recording and the recents thumbnail, but not accessibility services. **Every window** (review P19): dialogs, pickers, popups and bottom sheets are separate windows. A source scan refuses `SecureFlagPolicy.SecureOff` and the platform dialog builders in SDK sources, and a Robolectric test opens each dialog and sheet and asserts that its window carries `FLAG_SECURE`. **Overlays and non-assistive services** (decision 70; review P08, taken in part):
   - on API 31+, the flow activity hides non-system overlay windows while it is resumed (`Window.setHideOverlayWindows(true)`, normal permission `HIDE_OVERLAY_WINDOWS`);
   - on API 26–30, the consent, capture and submit controls set `filterTouchesWhenObscured`, since the platform blocks touches through untrusted overlays only from API 31;
   - on API 34+, the typed-value fields, the MRZ, CAN and OTP entry and the DG1 review are marked accessibility-data-sensitive (`ACCESSIBILITY_DATA_SENSITIVE_YES`), which hides them from services that are not declared accessibility tools while TalkBack keeps reading them.

   The whole view tree is not marked: that would also hide every navigation control from assistive apps that do not declare themselves tools, and make the flow unusable for their users. Robolectric tests run per API level, and the manual pass (C212.4) confirms that TalkBack still reads every screen.

   **Who builds each protection** (review V03): the owner of the file it lives in, test-first in its own Workflow. In 10A, `android-sdk-api` builds the activity's overlay hiding, the `HIDE_OVERLAY_WINDOWS` permission in `androidMain/AndroidManifest.xml` and the consent controls' obscured-touch filter; `android-screens` builds the flags of the form components, the typed-value fields of `ui/steps/` (the OTP code of `OtpStep.kt` among them) and their submit controls. In 10B, `capture-pipeline` builds the capture controls' filter. In 10C, `nfc-sdk` builds the flags of the MRZ and CAN entry and the DG1 review. `privacy-checks` keeps the cross-cutting scans and the per-window `FLAG_SECURE` test (C213.1–C213.3), and a finding in another owner's file is a `contractDeviation` that `integration-10c` fixes test-first.
2. **Data at rest** (decision 37): captures, chip bytes, typed values and tokens live in memory only; nothing in `SharedPreferences`, files, `SavedStateHandle` or intents beyond today's credential extras; the SDK's manifest sets no backup rules of its own and stores nothing a backup could take (a host test scans the SDK's code for file, preference and cache writes against an allowlist of none). **Saved state** (review P07): the scan also refuses `rememberSaveable`, `rememberTextFieldState`, `SavedStateHandle` writes and `onSaveInstanceState` in SDK sources outside an allowlist of non-PII UI state (a scroll position, a tab), and a Robolectric test types a canary into every form component, the MRZ entry, the CAN and the OTP code, recreates the activity, and asserts that no saved `Bundle` contains it. `foundation-10a` creates both scans in 10A with today's non-PII entries allowlisted (the consent screen's agreement flags, `ConsentScreen.kt:55-56`, and the camera-permission state), so 10A and 10B code is written under them; `privacy-checks` finalises the allowlists and adds the canary in 10C (review V03).
3. **Logs:** the SDK logs only PII-free error codes, and only when the host app is debuggable (`ApplicationInfo.FLAG_DEBUGGABLE`), since the KMP library has no build types of its own (C215.4); a source-scan host test refuses any `Log.*`, `println` or `printStackTrace` call in the main sources of `:openverify-sdk`, `openverify-nfc`, `openverify-face` and `openverify-integrity-play` outside one `SdkLog` object that accepts only constant codes (ours; a custom lint module would add a `lint-api` dependency for one rule). `SdkLog` is an internal object in `commonMain/.../log/` over an internal `expect fun platformLog(code: String)`, with an Android actual that reads the flag once and an iOS actual that logs nothing; `foundation-10a` creates it with the scan, so 10A and 10B code has its logging path from the start, and `privacy-checks` finalises the allowlist in 10C (review P06, F31). **Exceptions** (review P06): every exception the SDK creates or lets reach a host (`ChipReadException`, the errors behind a result, a crash's cause chain) carries a code and a fixed message, never a serialization excerpt, a response body or a server `detail`; `FlowApiException.InvalidResponse` keeps the exception's class name and its JSON path, not its message or cause. A host test feeds a session holding a canary value through every decoding failure and asserts that the canary appears in no message of the cause chain. `android-driver` builds the sanitised `FlowApiException` and this canary test in 10A, since it owns `commonMain/.../api/`; `nfc-sdk` (10C) and `nfc-reader` (10E) give every `ChipReadException` they throw the same rule, and `privacy-checks` extends the canary over every module then present (review V03). **Dependencies' loggers** (review P06): JMRTD and SCUBA log through `java.util.logging`, which Android routes to Logcat. The reader (C209.2) sets the `org.jmrtd`, `net.sf.scuba` and `org.ejbca` loggers to `Level.OFF` for the read and restores them after it, with a host test that a scripted read emits no JUL record.
4. **Network:** HTTPS only, except loopback and `10.0.2.2` in a debuggable host or under the SDK's own internal test override (C190.2); no redirects. **Trust follows the host app's network security configuration**, which the SDK runs inside and cannot override (review P11). For an app targeting API 24+, the platform default trusts system CAs only; a host that adds `<certificates src="user"/>` makes the SDK trust user CAs too. The README gives the configuration it recommends (system anchors only, cleartext off), and C215.2's host test checks the demo's release configuration against it. Optional certificate pinning is deferred to Phase 11's threat model (B11-S1; decision 38). Its seam is the `transport` member of `OpenVerifyComponents` (C191.1), which exists from 10A, so Phase 11 adds pins without a public signature change.
5. **The README per tier** (Phase 7 C109, behaviour M41, engineering K12): what the SDK sends (images, typed values, chip files, the session-integrity facts of C210, the optional integrity token, the camera snapshot, the `User-Agent`), what it never sends, the permissions (`INTERNET`, `CAMERA`, `HIDE_OVERLAY_WINDOWS`; `NFC` only with `openverify-nfc`), and the Google Play Data safety entries a host declares for each artifact, with the operator responsible for the final answers. `docs/android/privacy.md` holds a fixed table from each wire member the SDK sends to its Data safety category ("Photos", "Personal info", "Device or other IDs", "App info and performance"), and a host test over the SDK's wire schemas fails when a member has no row (decision 66; review P04). A subsection, "Components and clients you install", says what each seam receives: a `ChipReader` gets the MRZ or CAN and returns chip files, a `FaceDetector` gets every selfie frame, and a transport (C191.1) sees the session token, images and chip files. It says each must process on the device only, and that no logging interceptor may sit on a client passed to the SDK (review P23). Another subsection, on biometric notices, says that with `openverify-face` YuNet computes face boxes and landmarks on the device for every analysed frame, after consent, only on the selfie capture screen, held for one frame and never stored or sent (C204.8; review P24).
6. **Honesty:** the README says what `FLAG_SECURE`, the overlay and sensitive-data protections and the network security configuration do not stop: a rooted device, a modified app, an accessibility service declared as a tool, or a host whose configuration trusts user CAs.

### C214. Build, version and API compatibility (M37; engineering K20, 6.1, 6.6)

1. **Gradle hygiene** (engineering K20; decision 46): declare `kotlinx-io` and skiko where they are used directly; drop the unused `lifecycle-viewmodel-compose` from `androidMain`; one `openverifyVersion` property in `android/gradle.properties`.
2. **API dump** (decision 39): `kotlin { explicitApi() }` on `:openverify-sdk` and every optional artifact; the Kotlin Gradle plugin's ABI validation (`abiValidation`, experimental) with dumps committed under `android/<module>/api/` and `checkKotlinAbi` in the `android` job; if `foundation-10a`'s spike (b) shows it does not cover the Android target of `com.android.kotlin.multiplatform.library`, `org.jetbrains.kotlinx.binary-compatibility-validator` 0.18.2 (Apache-2.0, 2026-09-02) instead; the dump names exactly the public surface of C190.7, checked against a committed expected declaration list (`android/openverify-sdk/api/expected-public.txt`) by a host test, so a declaration that becomes public by accident fails the build (review F02).
3. **The version constant:** a generated `commonMain` constant from `openverifyVersion` replaces `FlowHttp.SDK_VERSION = "0.1.0"`; a test asserts the `User-Agent` and the property agree (behaviour K10).
4. **Versioning** (decision 40; behaviour M37, M40, O9): 0.x until the first published release; then semantic versioning with breaking changes only in a major, deprecation before removal, patches only on the latest minor; a dated `android/CHANGELOG.md` entry per release with minSdk, compileSdk, the minimum server and a "Breaking" section. **OkHttp is part of the public ABI** (review V11): `OpenVerifyAndroidTransport.okHttp(client: OkHttpClient)` puts OkHttp's type in a public signature, so OkHttp is an `api` dependency of the Android target and appears in the POM with that scope, and a move to a new OkHttp major version is a breaking SDK change after 1.0, named in the changelog's "Breaking" section.
5. **Dependency locking and verification** (coordination with Phase 11's scanning, `phase11-hardening.md` item 7; decision 67; review P17): `dependencyLocking { lockAllConfigurations() }` with committed `gradle.lockfile`s, so Phase 11's scanners read the resolved tree; and Gradle dependency verification, `android/gradle/verification-metadata.xml` with the SHA-256 of every resolved artifact and plugin (and the PGP keys where an artifact is signed), written by `--write-verification-metadata sha256` over the whole Android gate, so a substituted artifact with the same coordinates fails the build locally and in CI. It is refreshed only by the agent that adds a dependency (a Stage 1a agent, or `foundation-10a` for its spikes' tooling) and by `release-checks` beside the lockfiles; a refresh shows its added entries in the diff. Robolectric's own `android-all` downloads and the Kotlin/Native toolchain are outside Gradle's resolution and keep their pinned versions; the TDD file says so. Phase 11 adds the scanning and the SBOM.
6. **The boundary test** (C191.2): a Gradle check fails when `:openverify-sdk`'s runtime classpath contains an optional artifact, JMRTD, SCUBA, cert-cvc, Bouncy Castle, ONNX Runtime or a `com.google.android.play` module.
7. **Release age** (decision 62): every new pin is at least 14 days old on its adoption day (released on or before 2026-10-15 for this plan's pins, re-checked at each Stage 1a): zxing-cpp 3.1.1 (2026-07-29), ONNX Runtime 1.31.0 (2026-10-09), JMRTD 0.8.9 (2026-10-03), SCUBA 0.0.21 (2026-08-22) and 0.0.27 (2026-08-23), cert-cvc 1.4.13, Bouncy Castle 1.86 (2026-09-11), Roborazzi 1.76.0 (2026-09-29), Dokka 2.2.0 (2026-03-26), `com.vanniktech.maven.publish` 0.37.0 (2026-06-21), BCV 0.18.2 if needed; Play Integrity's date is verified in 10E (C211.6). Five more were missing from this record (review F22), and the verification round adds a sixth:
   - the Accessibility Test Framework and `ui-test-junit4-accessibility`, from Google Maven, whose dates the research could not read;
   - Roborazzi's accessibility module;
   - bundletool;
   - `kotlinx-io` and skiko, declared directly at the versions the lockfile already resolves (C214.1);
   - `okhttp-tls` for the demo's smoke test (C215.2; review V01), at the OkHttp version the project already pins, so its date is that release's.

   The adopting agent (`foundation-10a`, or `contracts-10c` for bundletool and `okhttp-tls`) reads each date from the artifact's publication metadata and records it, and refuses any that is younger than 14 days.
8. **Honesty:** the dump makes every public change visible in review; 0.x minors may break, and the changelog says so.

### C215. Consumer R8 rules and the minified demo (M14; engineering 6.2)

1. **Rules** (decision 41): each published module ships its rules through the KMP plugin's `optimization { consumerKeepRules { publish = true; file("consumer-rules.pro") } }` (or `consumerProguardFiles` for the plain Android libraries): the SDK's `@Serializable` models that cross a reflection or string boundary; ONNX Runtime's JNI-called classes (`openverify-face`); `org.jmrtd.**`, `net.sf.scuba.**`, `org.ejbca.cvc.**` unobfuscated (`openverify-nfc`); zxing-cpp's own rule travels in its AAR.
2. **Proof** (reviews P16, F12, V01): `demo-app` gains a `release` build type with `isMinifyEnabled = true`, `isShrinkResources = true` and R8 full mode, built for the 16 KB and size checks, and a `minifiedSmoke` build type that `initWith(release)` keeps R8 full mode and resource shrinking, and signs with AGP's generated debug keystore (never a committed key, review P25). `testBuildType = "minifiedSmoke"`, so the emulator job runs the demo's smoke device test on a build R8 has minified exactly as `release`. **The smoke test runs over TLS** (P16's held-certificate option, taken in the verification round; review V01): `minifiedSmoke` is not debuggable, so the SDK refuses plain `http` to loopback (C190.2) whatever a network security config allows. The device test starts `MockWebServer` with an `okhttp-tls` `HeldCertificate` for `127.0.0.1`, generated at run time, so no key is committed. It hands the certificate to a launcher activity that exists only in `src/minifiedSmoke/`. That activity installs `OpenVerifyAndroidTransport.okHttp(client)` (C191.1) with a client that trusts that certificate alone, then starts the contract. The build stays non-debuggable and https-only, and R8 minifies the transport path exactly as for a host that installs one. `src/minifiedSmoke/` has no network security config of its own, and its launcher activity is not exported (the instrumentation starts it in-process). `src/main`'s configuration stays https only, and a host test asserts that it permits no cleartext and trusts no user CA (C213.4).
3. **The merged manifest** (review P21): for each demo configuration (SDK only, plus NFC, plus face, plus Play Integrity, each as its artifact exists), a host test compares the merged manifest's permissions, `<queries>`, providers, services, receivers and their `exported` values with a committed allowlist (`android/demo-app/manifest-allowlist/<configuration>.json`), failing on any difference, so a dependency upgrade that adds a permission or component is seen before the README's lists (C213.5) drift.
4. **Honesty:** the rules are proven only by a consumer build, on the `minifiedSmoke` build type, since the KMP library plugin has no build types; the README says host apps need no rules of their own.

### C216. 16 KB pages and the size budget (engineering 6.3, 6.5)

1. **16 KB** (decision 42): Google Play requires 16 KB support for apps targeting API 35+ on 64-bit devices and blocks non-compliant updates from 2027-02-01; every native library Phase 10 adds is 16 KB-aligned (zxing-cpp 3.1.1, ONNX Runtime 1.31.0). Checks in the `android` job: `zipalign -c -P 16 -v 4` on the minified demo APK, `bundletool dump config` showing `PAGE_ALIGNMENT_16K` on its bundle, and our ELF `p_align` reader (`android/scripts/check-elf-alignment.py`, run with `python3 -I`) over every `jni/*/*.so` of every built AAR; a 16 KB emulator image (`google_apis_ps16k`, API 35) in the release matrix, on the runner P5 allows (C220.3).
2. **Size** (decision 43): `bundletool` measures the minified demo's download and install size per ABI for four configurations (SDK only, plus NFC, plus face, plus Play Integrity, each as its artifact exists), committed as `android/size-baseline.json` and compared by `android/scripts/check-size.py`. The `android` job measures the SDK-only configuration on every push and fails on a growth above 10% without a baseline change in the same commit; `android-release.yml` measures all four (review F22). bundletool is pinned and fetched by `scripts/fetch_bundletool.py` with its SHA-256 (C214.7); the README publishes the per-ABI cost of each artifact. An absolute cap is set after 10E's ORT spike.
3. **Honesty** (review H18): the README publishes only configurations whose artifacts exist in full. Until 10E, `openverify-nfc` is labelled "without the reader" (it holds none of the roughly 8.7 MB JMRTD and Bouncy Castle stack), and the face and Play Integrity configurations are absent. Figures measured with bundletool are labelled measured. ONNX Runtime's "about 34 MB on arm64" is the uncompressed library in the AAR before R8 (engineering 6.5) and is labelled so until 10E measures it; it dominates, which is why it is optional.

### C217. Publishing (M1; engineering 6.1; owner-gated upload: P4)

1. **Artifacts** (decision 44): `openverify-sdk` (Apache-2.0; the root KMP module and its Android target only, no iOS klibs in this release), `openverify-nfc` (LGPL-3.0, own licence), `openverify-face` and `openverify-integrity-play` (each only once its owner decision allows), and `openverify-scuba-smartcards` only if P1 is declined (C209.5), at one version; `demo-app` is never published.
2. **Tooling:** `com.vanniktech.maven.publish` 0.37.0 (Apache-2.0; tested upstream to AGP 9.4.0-alpha01 and KGP 2.4.0, so `contracts-10c`'s spike (d) proves it on AGP 9.4.1 and KGP 2.4.21 with `publishToMavenLocal`) and Dokka 2.2.0 for real API docs in the javadoc jar (decision 45; the public SDK ships empty placeholders, behaviour M1); POM `name`, `description`, `url`, `licenses`, `developers`, `scm`; sources jars; checksums.
3. **What this release does without the owner's keys:** `./gradlew publishAllPublicationsToStagingRepository` writes a complete local Maven repository under `android/build/staging-repo/` with POMs, sources, Dokka javadoc and checksums, validated by the plugin's checks and by a test that the root module's Gradle metadata resolves for an Android consumer with no iOS variant published (a consumer project under `android/consumer-check/` resolves the staged repository); the release workflow uploads the staged repository as a workflow artifact. Nothing reaches Maven Central.
4. **The release workflow** (`.github/workflows/android-release.yml`, `workflow_dispatch` only, ours): validates the version, runs the full Android gate and the device matrix (C220.3), signs in CI from an in-memory primary GPG key, and deploys to the Central Portal leaving the release for a manual click; it exits with a notice when the secrets are absent. **Who can reach the secrets** (review P18): a `workflow_dispatch` runs the workflow file of whatever branch it names, and repository secrets reach every such run, so an `if:` guard in the file protects nothing against someone who can push a branch. The signing key and the Portal token therefore live in a GitHub environment `maven-central` with a deployment-branch rule for `main` (and a required reviewer where the owner's plan offers one), never as repository secrets; environments on a private repository need a paid GitHub plan, so this is an owner step under P4, and until it exists the publish job does not run (the workflow stages and uploads the workflow artifact only). The publish job also checks `github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'`; the workflow sets top-level `permissions: contents: read` (the publish job adds nothing more); every action is pinned by commit, as in `ci.yml`. `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh` checks all of it for `android-release.yml`. Before the staged repository is uploaded as a workflow artifact, `gitleaks dir` scans its unzipped contents (AARs, sources and javadoc jars) and fails the job on a finding (review P25).
5. **Owner-gated (P4):** the namespace (`dev.openverify`, verified by a DNS TXT record on `openverify.dev`, if the owner holds that domain; otherwise `io.github.<the owner's sign-up username>`; the Kotlin packages stay `dev.openverify.*`), a Central Portal account and token, a GPG primary key on a public key server, and the GitHub environment `maven-central` holding the key and the token with a deployment-branch rule for `main` (C217.4; on a private repository this needs a paid GitHub plan). **Until then** hosts build the SDK from source or from the staged repository; **if the owner never publishes**, the staged repository is the release artifact.
6. **Honesty:** the README says where the artifacts are published and never names coordinates that do not resolve.

### C218. The demo app (behaviour M5, M42; engineering 1)

1. **Paths:** the two contracts with pasted credentials (today); `locale` and `appearance` pickers; the event log shown on screen (event names only); NFC through `OpenVerifyNfc` when the module is in the build (a Gradle property selects which optional artifacts the demo includes), the sandbox test chip always in sandbox; the face detector and the Play Integrity provider when present.
2. **App Links guidance** (behaviour M5; decisions 53 and 65): a documented intent filter for the operator's flow domain so a tapped one-time link opens the host app, shown in the demo's manifest as a commented example (no domain is claimed). The example verifies the link (`android:autoVerify`), checks the link's host against a host compiled into the app before calling `fromOneTimeLink`, and always passes the compiled-in `apiBaseUrl` (review P03). The integration guide (C222) says that a link from anywhere else can still bind the person to someone else's inquiry on the operator's own API, so hosts should accept links only from their own server.
3. **Builds:** debug (cleartext to loopback only, as today), the minified `release` and the `minifiedSmoke` test build of C215.2; no credentials bundled; the demo is a sample, never a product (behaviour O10, M42).
4. **Honesty:** the demo's README says it is a test harness for sandbox inquiries.

### C219. Tests (PLAN §6 Phase 10; engineering 7)

1. **Where each test runs:**
   - `commonTest` (kotlin.test, Ktor MockEngine; JVM host now, the iOS simulator in the next release): every pure function (visibility, patterns, digits, `Sha256` against the NIST vectors, copy and locale resolution, theme mapping, document measures, YuNet decoding, the yaw ratio, the guidance machine, DG1 parsing, the chip sequence over a fake reader) and every shared vector file through `readContractFixture` (C196.4);
   - `androidHostTest` (JUnit 5, Robolectric through the Vintage engine, Compose UI tests): the driver over today's `FakeFlowApi` and `FakeFlowServer` (the coordinator suites stay where they are; review F01), screens, the activity and its intents, the install registry, the heuristics over a fake probe, the merged-manifest checks, `ResourcesTest`, the key map, the consent digests through the real consent screen, the boundary and source-scan tests;
   - `androidDeviceTest` (the CI emulator only): what needs a real camera stack, native libraries or the platform (5).
2. **Kover:** the 80% line gate stays on `:openverify-sdk`, and each optional module gets its own Kover setup and 80% gate (`openverify-nfc`, `openverify-face`, `openverify-integrity-play`); glue excluded by name, each exclusion listed in its owner's TDD file and reviewed (the `ImageAnalysis` analyzer, the ORT session, the JMRTD adapter, the Play bridge), never a growing list.
3. **Screenshots** (decision 49; review F20): Roborazzi 1.76.0 (Apache-2.0) with Robolectric Native Graphics, from synthetic content only (the fake camera, synthetic document renders, no faces, rule 3). **The budget:** one golden per screen state in `en`, light, font scale 1.0; one state per screen in `ar`, dark, at 2.0; the full locale and font-scale matrix only for the text-heavy screens (consent, the form components, the failure and contest screen, the NFC review); one golden per screen with a non-default operator palette in light and dark (C198.8). The accessibility checks of C212.2 run on every golden rather than on a matrix of their own. Each integration agent records the golden count, the goldens' total size and the task's time in its TDD file. **Their own task:** the screenshot tests carry a JUnit tag and run in their own `Test` task, `:openverify-sdk:screenshotTest`, excluded from `testAndroidHostTest`, as a separate Validation call. Comparison is on (`-Proborazzi.test.verify=true`) in Validation and in the `android` job, so a screenshot regression fails the gate. Goldens are committed under `android/openverify-sdk/src/androidHostTest/screenshots/` and recorded only by an explicit record call (`-Proborazzi.test.record=true`), never by a failing run. **If spike (c) shows Roborazzi's Gradle plugin cannot run on `com.android.kotlin.multiplatform.library`**, the fallback is Roborazzi's runtime libraries (`roborazzi`, `roborazzi-compose`, `roborazzi-junit-rule`) inside `androidHostTest`, with the record and verify modes set as system properties in `tasks.withType<Test>` from a Gradle property. That needs no plugin support and widens no API (review F21: the KMP library plugin has no `testFixtures` source set, and `internal` composables are invisible to another module).
4. **Accessibility checks:** Compose's accessibility checks run with every screenshot test on the host if spike (c) shows they run under Robolectric; otherwise one device test per screen state runs them on the CI emulator (C212.2, C219.5).
5. **Device tests** (each compiled and lint-checked locally, run only on the CI emulator): CameraX binds three use cases, and the forced two-use-case fallback still captures (C201.3); frames arrive at the sampled rate; zxing-cpp decodes the committed synthetic PDF417 assets, at the generator's full size and downscaled to the analysis width of C201.2 (review F11); the heuristics report the emulator and stay within C210.3's recorded bound; NFC's "no reader" path (no NFC controller on the emulator); the `minifiedSmoke` demo's smoke flow against a loopback `MockWebServer` over TLS, through the transport seam (C215.2); the accessibility checks if Robolectric cannot run them (C212.2); in 10E, YuNet through ONNX Runtime on a public-domain fixture frame with the telemetry provider removed, over the measurement window of C205.2, the JMRTD reader's no-NFC-adapter path, and the Play Integrity provider's error path on a `google_apis` image, which has Google Play services but no Play Store (review F31).
6. **The CI floor:** the SDK host-test floor (1,264 at the research commit) is set by each integration agent to the count it measured. Deleting `JsonLogic` and `FlowNavigator` with their tests (C193.3) lowers it once. `android-driver` records the removed test names in `docs/tdd/phase10-android-driver.tdd.md`, and `integration-10a`, which owns `ci.yml`, lowers the floor by exactly that count in its CI commit (review F31). Every other change only raises it. New modules get floors of their own in the same step.
7. **Proving a device-only test before CI** (the task's rule; no KVM locally): the test compiles (`./gradlew :<module>:assembleAndroidDeviceTest` or `assembleAndroidTest`) and passes lint; every assertion it makes about SDK logic also has a host twin over a fake (the device test only checks the platform glue); `infra/scripts/check-connected-results.sh` fails a test that skips itself; a device test that fails after a push is fixed test-first by the next Workflow's Stage 1a or integration agent as its first task, never retried and never marked flaky.

### C220. CI jobs and their minutes (engineering 7.3; the CI cadence)

1. **Per push** (`ci.yml`): the `android` job adds the ABI check, the screenshot task with comparison on, the minified `release` build and its bundle (`:demo-app:bundleRelease`), the 16 KB checks and the size check of the SDK-only configuration. The other three size configurations are measured in `android-release.yml` only, since each is another R8 full-mode build (review F22). The additions are re-estimated at +8 to +10 minutes on a 2-vCPU runner, and `foundation-10a` and each integration agent measure them. In 10E, a YuNet fetch step runs before Gradle (C205.3); `android-instrumented` keeps one API 34 `google_apis` x86_64 emulator and runs the device tests of C219.5 (estimated +4 to +6 minutes over today's 6, mostly the release build). Once 10E's `openverify-face` lands, its telemetry device test adds at least 2 to 3 minutes to `android-instrumented` for C205.2's 120 s quiet period and the process restart (review V14). Together about +12 to +16 minutes on a full run of about 100 through 10D (about $0.10 at $0.006 a minute), and about +15 to +19 once the face test runs. `foundation-10a` measures both jobs at the Phase 10 base before Stage 2, and each integration agent re-measures; a job within 25% of its timeout is split (Phase 8 F08's rule).
2. **No nightly matrix** (decision 50; departs from engineering 7.3's nightly proposal): nightly runs of three emulator jobs of about 15 minutes would bill about 1,350 minutes a month, two thirds of a 2,000-minute (12 dollar) allowance, against the owner's budget of one full CI run per Workflow push.
3. **The release matrix** (`android-release.yml`, `workflow_dispatch` on `main` only, started by the orchestrator, never by an agent): API 26 `google_apis` x86_64 (Automated Test Device images exist only from API 30; review F13) and API 35 `google_apis_ps16k` (the 16 KB image) beside API 34, with AVD snapshot caching. **The 16 KB leg's runner is pending the owner** (P5; review V02). It runs first on the standard 2-vCPU runner with a 900 s boot budget and the snapshot cache, as engineering 7.3 orders it (a larger runner only if the image will not boot). An API 36 `google_apis` image never booted within 600 s there, so the first dispatch, after 10D's push, may show that the API 35 image does not boot either. A 4-core larger runner is the owner's account and billing: GitHub offers larger runners to organisations on its Team or Enterprise Cloud plans, set up by an owner in a runner group, at $0.012 a minute with no included minutes (about 15 to 20 minutes, or $0.18 to $0.24, a run), and the repository is on a personal account. If the leg cannot boot and P5 is not granted, the 16 KB proof is the per-push checks of C216.1 (zipalign, bundletool's `PAGE_ALIGNMENT_16K`, the ELF `p_align` scan), the 16 KB emulator leg is recorded as an open item for the release gate, and the API 26 and 34 legs alone decide whether the matrix is green. In all, about 30 to 40 emulator minutes per run plus the gate. The matrix runs once after 10D's push, once after 10E's push when 10E runs (decision 72 needs a green matrix on the final push; review V14), and once per release, and its minutes and cost are recorded in `docs/PROGRESS.md`.
4. **`infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh`** gains the new steps, the release workflow, its environment, branch check, permissions, commit-pinned actions and the staged-repository secret scan (C217.4).
5. **Honesty:** every timing in the plan is an estimate until measured; each integration agent records the measured minutes per job with the runner's load.

### C221. Server and web amendments Phase 10 needs

1. **Contracts** (`packages/contracts`, zod, golden fixtures, the Android decoder list): the `nfc` client view's `fallback-available`, `sandbox-chip-available` (C208.2) and `prefill-mrz` (PII-marked, served only to the current `nfc` step's client view of an SDK client, never to the web flow, joining the API-log masked members, C207.2; a golden web-client fixture without it); `client-unable` (C207.9); `sandbox-chip` (C208); `integrity-challenges` and `integrity-tokens` and the session's `integrity { request }` (C211.3); the signals wire's `integrity.android` (C210.2); the upload's `capture-trigger` and the file attribute (C206.2).
2. **Identifiers:** `ich` (integrity challenge, ours), joined to both prefix tables with a test.
3. **Tables** (one migration per Workflow, numbered at the Phase 10 base): `files.capture_trigger`; `inquiry_integrity_challenges (environment_id, id, inquiry_id, session_id, request_hash, expires_at, consumed_at)`; `inquiry_integrity_verdicts (environment_id, inquiry_id, provider, verdict sealed, decoded_at)`, where `verdict` holds only C211.4's projection, sealed (row AAD) and joining `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS` and `REWRAP_TABLES`; both deleted with the inquiry's redaction and by its retention.
4. **Signals:** the five registry entries and `decisive-alone` (C210.4, C210.5, C211.5), with the publish rule `signal_not_decisive_alone` at flow and workflow publish and `heuristic:` on every route reading them; the publish rule `play_integrity_notice_missing` (C211.9; review V05); all built by `api-integrity`.
5. **Provider kind** `device-integrity` with `play-integrity` and `sandbox` (C211.4); the binding's service-account key stored as the existing provider secrets are, never logged; `provider_not_configured` when unbound.
6. **Problems** (RFC 9457, golden fixtures): on the flow API, `client_unable_not_allowed` (409), `integrity_challenge_invalid` (409), `sandbox_chip_anchor_missing` (409) and `sandbox_chip_scenario_unavailable` (422); the sandbox chip route answers the generic 404 in production, with no code of its own. At publish (422, `definition_invalid` codes): `signal_not_decisive_alone` (C210.5) and `play_integrity_notice_missing` (C211.9); the integrity signals' `provider_not_bound` reuses Phase 7's code (C211.5).
7. **Rate-limit policies** (C12): `flow-sandbox-chip` 10 an hour per inquiry; `flow-integrity` 5 an hour per inquiry; `client-unable` under the existing `flow-session` policy.
8. **Fixtures exported for Kotlin** (review F10). Each is asserted by a test on the TypeScript or Python side, which fails when the committed file differs from what the implementation produces. It writes only in an explicit update mode (`UPDATE_CONTRACT_FIXTURES=1`), so a plain `pnpm test` never changes the working tree under other agents. Each test declares its own case table and imports only the implementation, never another phase's test files. The fixtures: `contracts/fixtures/flow-spec/{visibility,pattern,digits}-vectors.json` (C196.4), `contracts/fixtures/theme/builtin-palettes.json` (C198.2), `contracts/fixtures/i18n/copy-keys.json` (C199.7), `contracts/fixtures/i18n/android-consent-digests.json` and Android rows in `consent-texts.json` (C200.3, C200.4), `contracts/fixtures/flow/selfie-guidance-vectors.json` (C204.1), `contracts/fixtures/flow/document-capture.json` and `document-quality-vectors.json` (C202.1, C202.2).
9. **The web flow:** C207.10's NFC screen and its mocked Playwright spec; the console preview's list of differences (Phase 9 C156.7) gains one line that Android renders with the system font and Material's components, so its screens differ from the preview in type and shape (review H21); and the console's consent-record view labels the record's `client` and its digests client-reported (C200.6; review V05).
