Both are borrowed edits by `integration-10a` to the Phase 9 console files the inventory names, each with its component test.
10. **OpenAPI and `@openverify/node`:** the new flow routes registered as plugins; the sandbox chip route documented as sandbox-only.
11. **Honesty:** every new server fact from a device is stored and served as client-reported; nothing a device sends passes a check.
12. **The session's language and the organisation's name** (C193.9, C200.2): `GET /api/v1/flow/session` takes an optional `language` (when Phase 9 built no equivalent, by the inventory) that sets the session's locale; `organization-display-name` is served NFC-normalised and refused on save when its first character is not a stable NFC boundary (C200.2; review V13). Both are owned by `api-android-10a`, as named borrowed edits with golden fixtures and Testcontainers tests (review V08).
13. **No handoff on a native session** (Phase 7 C96.1 amended; C194.2; reviews H20, V05): the engine skips a `device_handoff` step for every session whose `mobile-sdk-name` is set, whatever the step's `skip-on-mobile` says, since the handoff target would be the same phone. `api-android-10a` builds it in 10A, as a named borrowed edit to the file the inventory names, with a Testcontainers test over both values of `skip-on-mobile`.

### C222. Records, ADR and docs

- **ADR 0009** "Android: optional artifacts, device guidance and the licence boundary" (the five rules of the Summary; the install registry; the artifact table; what each owner decision gates), written by `contracts-10a` (decision 58).
- **PLAN** (corrected by `integration-10a`, and by `integration-10e` for the gated parts; decision 59): §3 technology table: `io.github.zxing-cpp:android` for Android PDF417 (replacing "zxing"), on-device document quality in `commonMain` with no OpenCV, ONNX Runtime and YuNet in the optional `openverify-face`, the optional `openverify-integrity-play`; §6 Phase 10: the five Workflows and the pending owner decisions P1–P5; §9: the risk rows for the optional artifacts and the release matrix's cost.
- **`THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`:** zxing-cpp's Android AAR with its bundled native code's licences; Roborazzi, Dokka, the publishing plugin, BCV if used, and the Accessibility Test Framework as build or test tooling; `onnxruntime-android` 1.31.0 with Eigen and YuNet as shipped in `openverify-face` (once P2 is decided); JMRTD, cert-cvc, SCUBA (or its fork) and Bouncy Castle moved from planned to adopted in `openverify-nfc` (once P1 is decided); Play Integrity as an optional proprietary adapter (once P3 is decided); "zxing (Java)" left as the documented fallback.
- **`docs/checks/nfc.md`:** the Android client exists; the sequence with the challenge before the tap; `client-unable`; the sandbox chip; what the device reports and why the server ignores it. **`docs/checks/selfie.md`:** on-device guidance on Android with `openverify-face`. **`docs/checks/signals.md`:** the device-integrity indicators and Play Integrity with their limits.
- **`docs/android/`** (new): integration guide (both contracts, `locale`, `appearance`, the install registry, the transport, the minimum server, App Links with the link checks of C218.2), a migration page mapping a builder-style mobile SDK's concepts (template entry, field prefill, client-side callbacks) onto this SDK's server-created inquiries, events and results, with no vendor named (behaviour M4; review H20), events, the failure-reason table, theming and languages, NFC and its artifact, Play Integrity and its recipe, accessibility, privacy and the Data safety entries, the release and support policy, sizes per ABI.
- **`android/openverify-nfc/README.md` and `NOTICE`**, **`android/CHANGELOG.md`**, the SDK README.
- **`docs/research/persona-surface.md`:** the Android SDK rows (entry points, result outcomes, events, options) marked built, kept or not built.
- **`docs/accessibility/statement.md`:** C212.5.

### C223. Deferred

- **Integration surface:** an inline Compose or fragment entry (behaviour M11, O6: the public inline API is still experimental, and the activity owns `FLAG_SECURE`, back handling and process death); client-side creation from a template (M3, O4); the SDK as a device-handoff target through App Links (M12: needs App Links on the operator's domain and the Phase 7 handoff on the phone side; next release, with the iOS target); the share token (M38: Phase 11 Connect, whose design creates inquiries on the server with `meta.share-token-id` and adds a `share-consent` step to `commonMain`, so `Request` needs no member); certificate pinning (B11-S1, Phase 11's threat model).
- **Capture:** the web flow's in-browser PDF417 pre-check (`zxing-wasm`), carried by Phase 3 C37 and decision 12 to "Phase 4 and Phase 10" and not built by Phase 4 (review H20): Phase 10 builds the Android half (C203); the web half is deferred to the next release beside the iOS reader, because it adds a WebAssembly decoder to the hosted flow's bundle and its CSP; on-device OCR and ID-type auto-classification (M27: ML Kit is proprietary; the server runs RapidOCR); video capture and WebRTC (M29, O11); silent network authentication, mdoc wallets, Japanese My Number cards, Relay, reusable wallets (M30: each needs a third-party account or licensed component); auto vs manual trigger in analytics (C206.2); a template setting that turns auto-capture off per step (the public product configures auto-capture from the server; ours stays on with the shutter always available, C202.4, so no setting is needed to proceed); a face runtime without MPL-2.0 code if P2 is declined (C205.6).
- **Themes and languages:** operator-uploaded fonts on native clients (Phase 9 C177; C198.4: no sanitiser evaluated); fonts the host app bundles and the theme names, which Phase 9 C175.1 and C177 also mention (review H20: not adopted, because a font chosen by name in the app is outside the theme's contrast gate and the server's review, and the system font already covers every locale the SDK ships); resource-name overrides (M32: not adopted, a fragile API); locales beyond the five (M33).
- **Release:** compileSdk 37 with Compose UI 1.12, CMP 1.12, Ktor 3.6, OkHttp 5.5 and CameraX 1.7 (decision 16; engineering K17, owner 8: a separate slice after the first published release, once Robolectric's API 37 support is confirmed, because host apps would have to compile with 37 too); iOS klibs on Maven Central (engineering owner 7); an OpenVerify-branded app (M42, O10).
- **Wallets:** crypto-wallet signing on Android beyond pasting (behaviour K8, engineering K9: a wallet-connection SDK needs a relay account; stays paste-only).
- **Next release (iOS):** dependency locking for the iOS link configurations, which Linux never resolves, so `lockAllConfigurations()` leaves them unlocked and the macOS job will re-write their lockfiles first (review F31); every iOS item: the `iosMain` implementations of the new seams (frames, face detection through ONNX Runtime's C API by cinterop in its own module, Apple Vision for PDF417, DeviceCheck or App Attest adapters, CoreNFC with NFCPassportReader behind `ChipReader`), the XCFramework and app shell, the macOS job, Burst for the parameterized suites, `commonTest` on the simulator, the iOS placeholders and `DocumentPicker`, skiko's notices (behaviour K9, K15, K16; engineering K11, the iOS half of K14, K15, K16, K18, K19, K21; PLAN "Next release: iOS").
- **Not needed:** `expect fun secureRandomBytes(n)` (decision 55; behaviour K14, engineering K13: the AA nonce is the server's and JMRTD draws its own randomness; `Sha256` is the only primitive common code needs, C200.2).

### C224. Fixtures and end to end

1. **Fixtures** (synthetic only, rule 3): golden v2 session answers for every step type and component kind the SDK renders, each decoded by `ContractFixturesTest` (Phase 9's `flow-session-v2.json` and the projection fixtures stay decoded); the vector files of C221.8; synthetic document renders; the synthetic AAMVA PDF417 PNGs committed under the device-test assets (a few kilobytes each, `ZC`-marked synthetic data), with a Python test that they equal the Phase 3 generator's output, so no Android build needs Python or the vision environment (review F19); synthetic chips from `@openverify/emrtd/testing` through the sandbox route; public-domain faces only, through the existing fixture manifest; magic Play Integrity tokens for the sandbox provider; names, emails at `example.com` and documentation IP ranges only.
2. **Testcontainers:** the `client-unable` route taking an `exhausted` edge and refusing a second call or a non-current step; the sandbox chip route refusing production and passing every scenario through the real C62 checks (`match` passes every required NFC check; `face-mismatch` fails the selfie comparison where fixture faces are configured and answers `sandbox_chip_scenario_unavailable` where they are not; no answer carries a selfie's bytes; `sandbox_chip_anchor_missing` without the anchor, and `trust:sandbox-utopia` installing it in production mode; `tampered-dg1` fails `dg_hash_mismatch`; `no-active-authentication` answers `chip_auth_not_server_verifiable`); a challenge taken before any upload accepted at submit; the integrity challenge and token routes with the sandbox provider, a replayed token, an expired challenge and an unbound provider; the five signals' values and `null` reasons; the publish rule `signal_not_decisive_alone`.
3. **Full-stack specs** (the reusable stack with shards, under `flock /tmp/openverify-e2e.lock`; each spec ≤ 90 s with its budget in a header line; an API-driven client sends the Android `User-Agent` and the spec header, since no Linux runner can drive the SDK's UI):
   - **10A:** a v2 flow with a screen, a branch and a completion driven through the flow API as the SDK drives it (position, client view, screen submit, session read), with Android consent digests accepted and a wrong digest refused, and `session.locale` set from the `language` the client sent (C193.9);
   - **10B:** an inquiry whose Android signals body carries emulator indicators: `inquiry/device_emulated` is `true` with reasons and nothing is declined; the sandbox Play Integrity provider's verdicts reach the two signals;
   - **10C:** an NFC flow completed with the sandbox chip (`match`), one with `face-mismatch`, and an `nfc` step whose client reports `nfc_unavailable` and lands on the document fallback in `needs_review`; the web flow's NFC screen offering "use another way" in the mocked suite.
4. **The Android side** of each spec is covered by host tests of the SDK against a fake `FlowApi` replaying the same golden answers, and by the device tests of C219.5, and in two ways that reach the server's own code (review H02):
   - **Request fixtures, cross-language.** Every request body the SDK's real `FlowApiClient` encodes is asserted, through Ktor's `MockEngine`, against a committed fixture under `contracts/fixtures/flow-requests/android/`. These cover the multipart fields with `capture-method` and `capture-trigger`, the chip uploads, the chip submit with its base64 `active-authentication-response`, the consent body with `text-sha256[]`, `client-unable`, `sandbox-chip`, `integrity-challenges`, `integrity-tokens` and the screen submission's `{values, button}`. The fixtures are assert-only, with an explicit `UPDATE_CONTRACT_FIXTURES=1` mode (C221.8). A TypeScript test parses each fixture with its route's zod request schema. Each Workflow's server-side Stage 2 agent adds the parse test for its routes (`api-android-10a`, `api-integrity`, `api-nfc`), and each client owner adds its fixtures. A mismatch on either side fails a test.
   - **The SDK against the stack.** A JVM test task, `:openverify-sdk:stackTest` (a JUnit tag excluded from `testAndroidHostTest`), runs the SDK's real `FlowApiClient` and the driver headless against the reusable e2e stack under the e2e lock. Its fake camera supplies synthetic renders, and `SandboxChipReader` supplies the chip. It runs the 10A flow (screen, branch, completion, consent digests) and the 10C NFC flow (`match`). It runs in Validation and at each integration checkpoint, not in CI, where it would add a JDK and Gradle setup to the `e2e` job; `integration-10a` creates it and `integration-10c` extends it.

   The acceptance names the client that drove each spec.

## Workflow 10A (the renderer: contracts, the driver, screens, themes, languages)

**Families and checkpoints:** (P) the public API and seams; (R) the renderer and its wire models; (L) languages; (S) the server's exports. Stage 1a and Stage 1b commit by family, each checkpoint with every suite green and recorded in its TDD file. Ten agents (decision 56). Every Gradle invocation runs under `flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock` (Rules).

- **Stage 1a: `contracts-10a`.** It runs alone. It first takes the inventory at the Phase 10 base into `docs/research/phase10-inventory.md` (every claim with its `path:line`): re-verifies the Starting point; reads Phase 9 as built (the session members and client views per step type, the header, the screen route, `COPY_KEYS`, `LOCALE_REVIEW_STATUS`, `consent-texts.json` and its placeholder fill, the extension route, the projection fixtures and `FlowCoordinatorProjectionTest.kt`) and Phase 7 as built (`FlowSignals`, `DeviceSignals`, the registry, `ON_NULL_DEFAULTS`), and records drift as "Workflow 10A Stage 1a amendments". It runs spike (a), in a scratch worktree that is discarded, with the outcome recorded in the inventory: whether Compose Multiplatform 1.11.1 resolves `Res.string` against a per-composition locale through a public API (C199.4). Spikes (b) and (c) are `foundation-10a`'s first task, since it wires their results, and spike (d) is `contracts-10c`'s, which adopts the publishing plugin (review F25). It also desk-checks the iOS reader's API (C207.3) and records Phase 7's as-built visibility of `DeviceSignals` (C210.1). Then it commits, in order:
  - **Common:** ADR 0009; `FailureReason.FLOW_HALTED`; the problem-code mapping for the new server problems; the public surface narrowed to C190.7's list (the wire model, `FlowApi`, `FlowApiClient(s)`, `FlowApiException`, `CapturedImage`, `CameraLens` and `OpenVerifyTheme` made `internal`, the demo given its own theme) with the expected declaration list (C214.2); the string facade `sdkString(key, args)` and `sdkPlural(key, count, args)` (internal, `commonMain/.../i18n/SdkStrings.kt`), with every `stringResource` and `pluralStringResource` call in the SDK migrated to it mechanically in one commit with no behaviour change, so `android-theme` implements C199.4 behind it whatever spike (a) finds (review F09); the test helpers `readContractFixture` (C196.4) and `FakeFlowServer` beside `FakeFlowApi` in `androidHostTest` (it turns a step list into the successive positions and client views a Phase 9 server would serve, so each existing coordinator test changes its fixture line, not its assertions; review F03).
  - **(P):** the public API of C190–C192 with KDoc (`Request` and `OneTimeLink` with `locale` and `appearance`, `Appearance`, `OpenVerifyAndroid.install`, `OpenVerifyComponents`, `OpenVerifyEvent` and its listener) and the seam interfaces it names, frozen now so the ABI dump starts complete: `ChipReader` with `ChipAccess`, `ChipRead`, `ChipProgress`, `ChipReadException` (with the iOS desk check of C207.3 and, if needed, `@ExperimentalOpenVerifyApi`); `FaceDetectorFactory`, `FaceDetector` and `YunetOutputs`; `IntegrityTokenProvider`; `FlowTransport` with `OpenVerifyAndroidTransport.okHttp(client: OkHttpClient)` (signature and KDoc; review V11), with OkHttp declared an `api` dependency of the Android target (C214.4). Bodies are stubs that change nothing at run time.
  - **(R):** the wire models for Phase 9's session members (`position`, `step` with every client view, the component catalogue, `values`, `theme`, `copy`, `locales`, `completion`, `spec-version`), `FlowApi.submitScreen` and `extendSelfieCapture` (signatures; the client methods throw `NotImplementedError` behind a test that no production path reaches them until built), `FlowState.Waiting`, `Halted` and `Screen` with their dispatch lines in `ui/InquiryFlowScreen.kt` calling stub composables in their owners' files (`ui/screen/ScreenRenderer.kt`, and the waiting and halted stubs in `ui/StatusScreens.kt`), `FailureScreen` moved unchanged from `ui/StatusScreens.kt:69` into `ui/FailureScreen.kt`, `android-screens`' file (review V06), and the one call from the dispatcher to `ui/scaffold/StepScaffold.kt` (a stub that renders its content; review F04), the `StepRegistry` skeleton with every type mapped to the unsupported screen, the `OpenVerify-Flow-Spec: 2` header, `flow-spec-members.json` with its shape and an entry for every member of Phase 9's export, each with its intended treatment from C194.2 and, for a member added after Phase 9's spec 2, whether it is appearance-only and why (C194.4; reviews V10, V14), and each new fixture in `ContractFixturesTest`'s list.
  - **(L):** the shapes of `android/openverify-sdk/i18n/key-map.json` and `review-status.json` with their JSON Schemas, and the per-owner resource file rule: Compose Multiplatform reads every XML file in a `values-*` folder, so each agent that adds strings adds them in its own `strings_<area>.xml` in all five folders (review F08). The strings that have a key in the key map, every legal text among them, move out of today's `values/strings.xml` and `values-es/strings.xml` into a generated `strings_catalog.xml` in each of the five folders. `android-i18n`'s generator writes that file from the fixtures and no other file, and a host test fails when a key-mapped string appears in any hand-written file (C199.1; review V07).
  - **(S):** the names and JSON Schemas of the exported vector files of C221.8 that 10A uses (visibility, pattern, digits, built-in palettes, copy keys, Android consent rows and digests), and of `contracts/fixtures/flow-requests/android/` (C224.4); the `language` query of C193.9 in the session route's zod schema with its golden fixture, if the inventory finds no Phase 9 equivalent; and the paths, by the inventory, of the four server files `api-android-10a` changes by named borrowed edits (C221.12, C221.13; reviews V05, V08): the flow session route, the organisation settings service that saves the display name, the consent digest check, and the engine's `device_handoff` skip (Phase 7 C96.1 as Phase 9 built it).

  It owns any path its deliverables need. The host-test count stays green, the iOS klibs compile, and the ABI dump is not yet enforced.
- **Stage 1b: `foundation-10a`** (plumbing only). It starts after Stage 1a's common and (P) commits, beside Stage 2. **First**, before any checkpoint: it measures the `android` and `android-instrumented` jobs on the latest CI run at the base and the local Android gate (each split command's wall time, with the load average), and splits the local gate into calls that each fit 10 minutes (Validation); then it runs spikes (b) and (c) in a scratch worktree that is discarded, recording the outcome in the inventory: (b) whether the Kotlin Gradle plugin's ABI validation dumps the Android target of `com.android.kotlin.multiplatform.library` and an AGP built-in-Kotlin `com.android.library` module such as `openverify-nfc`, else BCV 0.18.2 for whichever it does not cover (C214.2; review F23); (c) whether Roborazzi 1.76.0 and Compose's accessibility checks run in `testAndroidHostTest` on this plugin, each spike resolving its plugins through the existing `pluginManagement` (C212.2, C219.3); and it adds Roborazzi 1.76.0, the Compose accessibility test artifact and, if spike (b) needs it, BCV 0.18.2 to the catalog with their `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` rows (test scope) and their release-age dates (C214.7). Then: the Gradle hygiene of C214.1; `openverifyVersion` with the generated constant and its agreement test (C214.3); `explicitApi()`; ABI validation wired with the first committed dump (C214.2); dependency locking with the lockfiles and dependency verification with `verification-metadata.xml` (C214.5); the boundary test (C214.6); Roborazzi's plugin and `@GraphicsMode` wiring with an empty golden folder per owner (`screenshots/<agent>/`); Kover filters for the new packages; the source-scan tests of C213.2 and C213.3 with their allowlists (today's non-PII saved state allowlisted; review V03), and `SdkLog` with `platformLog`'s Android and iOS actuals (C213.3); the state hooks in `FlowCoordinator.kt` calling stubs in owned files that Stage 3 fills (`flow/ServerPosition.kt` returning the unsupported position, `consent/ConsentDigests.kt` returning no digests, `ui/theme/SessionTheme.kt` returning the built-in theme), the composable hooks being `contracts-10a`'s dispatch lines in `InquiryFlowScreen.kt` (review F04); and every 10A build task registered as a stub that applies a script owned by its builder (`scripts/generate-locale-strings.gradle.kts`, `android-i18n`; `scripts/export-consent-texts.gradle.kts`, `api-android-10a`; the `stackTest` task with its JUnit tag, `integration-10a`; the screenshot test task of C219.3), with the Kover exclusions each builder will name left as one entry per owner (review F18). It owns `android/gradle.properties`, `android/gradle/libs.versions.toml` (after Stage 1a), every `build.gradle.kts`, `android/settings.gradle.kts`, the lockfiles, `android/gradle/verification-metadata.xml`, `android/openverify-sdk/api/`, and the files it creates except the stubs, which pass to the owners named beside them.
- **Stage 2** (parallel, beside Stage 1b):
  - **`android-logic`** (after Stage 1a common and (R); checkpoints in order: (1) visibility and patterns, (2) the theme mapping, (3) digits and `Sha256`, (4) locale and copy resolution, so each Stage 3 agent waits only for the checkpoint it needs; review F25): C196 (the visibility evaluator, the pattern matcher, `normalizeDigits`), `Sha256` with the NIST vectors (C200.2), the locale chooser and copy resolver (C199.2, C199.6, C199.7's logic), and the theme mapping (C198.2), all pure in `commonMain` with `commonTest` suites over the vector files, read through `readContractFixture` (C196.4). It owns `commonMain/kotlin/dev/openverify/sdk/flow/logic/`, `commonMain/.../i18n/` (logic files, except `SdkStrings.kt`), `commonMain/.../ui/theme/ThemeMapping.kt` and their tests.
  - **`android-i18n`** (after Stage 1a common and (L)): C199.1, C199.5's resource half and C199.8: `values-fr`, `values-de` and `values-ar` for every SDK string, the key map, the `generateLocaleStrings` task reading `contracts/fixtures/i18n/<locale>.json` and writing `strings_catalog.xml` in all five folders, so Android's `en` and `es` legal texts become the web's words (review V07), the review-status file with per-key digests and its downgrade test, the plural completeness test, the NFC test for every string, and `ResourcesTest`'s extension to five locales. Every other agent that adds strings writes them itself in its own `strings_<area>.xml` in all five folders, drafts the non-English ones (never a legal text, C199.1), marks them `machine-drafted` and adds their keys to `review-status.json` through a named borrowed edit, so no agent waits for `android-i18n` (review F08). It owns the existing `strings.xml` of `commonMain/composeResources/values-{fr,de,ar}/`, the generated `strings_catalog.xml` in all five folders, `android/openverify-sdk/i18n/`, `scripts/generate-locale-strings.gradle.kts` and `ResourcesTest.kt`.
- **Stage 3** (each after its family's Stage 1b checkpoint **and** its named Stage 2 dependency):
  - **`android-driver`** (R; after Stage 1b (R); review F03): C193 (the header, the requirement, the driver, verification steps under it, current-step and single-use 409s, resume, the session's language of C193.9, the deletion of `FlowNavigator` and `JsonLogic` with their tests and the floor change recorded for the integration agent), C197.1–C197.2, the migration of the existing coordinator and view-model suites (about 217 tests, `FlowCoordinator*Test.kt` and `InquiryViewModelTest.kt`) onto `FakeFlowServer`, with their assertions unchanged, the client methods of `submitScreen` and `extendSelfieCapture` (the extension's UI is 10B's), and C213.3's exception rule (`FlowApiException` with a code and a fixed message, `InvalidResponse` keeping only the class name and JSON path) with its canary host test (review V03). Its checkpoints, in order, so a pause resumes from a working driver: (1) the header, the requirement and the driver over today's step types with every migrated suite green; (2) waiting, halted and the 409 handling; (3) the session's language. It owns `commonMain/.../flow/` (except `logic/`), `InquiryViewModel.kt`, `InquiryOutcomes.kt`, `commonMain/.../api/` (except `OneTimeLinks.kt`, `ApiBaseUrl.kt` and `LoopbackPolicy.kt`), `ui/StatusScreens.kt` (without `FailureScreen`, which `contracts-10a` moves out; review V06) and their host tests.
  - **`android-sdk-api`** (P; after Stage 1b (P) and `android-driver`'s first checkpoint; review F03): C190's plumbing (the new extras, `FLOW_HALTED`, the required `apiBaseUrl` on the link path and the loopback seam `loopbackHttpAllowed` with its actuals and test override, C190.2; review V01), C191 (the registry, absent-component behaviour, `FlowTransport` and `OpenVerifyAndroidTransport`), C192 (event emission), C200 (the consent body's digests through the real consent screen and the mismatch retry, after `android-logic`'s `Sha256` checkpoint and `api-android-10a`'s digests) and its part of C213.1 (the activity's `setHideOverlayWindows` on API 31+, `HIDE_OVERLAY_WINDOWS` in the SDK manifest, `filterTouchesWhenObscured` on the consent controls on API 26–30; review V03). Its checkpoints: (1) the request options, the link rule and the overlay protections; (2) the registry, the transport and events; (3) consent digests. It owns `InquiryResult.kt`, `commonMain/.../consent/`, `commonMain/.../api/{OneTimeLinks,ApiBaseUrl,LoopbackPolicy}.kt` with `LoopbackPolicy`'s `androidMain` and `iosMain` actuals, `androidMain/AndroidManifest.xml` (the SDK manifest; review V03), `ui/ConsentScreen.kt`, `androidMain/.../{OpenVerifyInquiry,OpenVerifyInquiryActivity,InquiryResults,InquiryViewModelFactory,OpenVerifyAndroid,OpenVerifyAndroidTransport}.kt` and their host tests.
  - **`android-screens`** (R; after Stage 1b (R) and `android-logic`'s first checkpoint): C195 (the screen renderer, every component, validation on the device with server pointers mapped back, values, `prefillFrom` on the verification steps' screens, submission through the driver, built-in accessibility, limits), C197.3 (failure screens with the contest route) and its part of C213.1 (accessibility-data-sensitive typed-value fields on API 34+ and `filterTouchesWhenObscured` on submit controls on API 26–30, in `ui/screen/` and in the verification steps of `ui/steps/`, the OTP code of `OtpStep.kt` included; review V03), with Robolectric tests and Roborazzi goldens per component state. It owns `commonMain/.../ui/screen/`, `ui/steps/` (for `prefillFrom`, C195.2's autofill and keyboard flags on the typed-value fields and C213.1's flags only), `ui/FailureScreen.kt` (review V06), its `strings_screen.xml` files and `screenshots/android-screens/`.
  - **`android-theme`** (R and L; after Stage 1b (R), `android-logic`'s theme-mapping checkpoint and `android-i18n`'s first checkpoint): C198 (decoding, applying the mapping through `OpenVerifyTheme`, the logo fetch and decode, appearance, window bars), C197.4 (the help slot and the language selector, in `ui/scaffold/StepScaffold.kt`, which wraps every step's content once from the dispatcher), C199.4 (the per-composition language behind `sdkString` and `sdkPlural`, by spike (a)'s answer; if Compose cannot do it, the generated per-locale tables with plural rules copied from CLDR for the five locales and tested over CLDR's examples) and C199.5's layout half (`LocalLayoutDirection`, mirrored icons, unmirrored prompts, bidi isolation), with goldens in `ar` and dark. It owns `commonMain/.../ui/theme/` (except `ThemeMapping.kt`), `ui/OpenVerifyTheme.kt`, `ui/scaffold/`, `ui/help/`, `commonMain/.../i18n/SdkStrings.kt`, the icon files it mirrors, its `strings_theme.xml` files and `screenshots/android-theme/`; `ui/InquiryFlowScreen.kt` is shared after Stage 1a.
  - **`api-android-10a`** (S; after Stage 1a common and (S), and for the consent rows after `android-i18n`'s last checkpoint): the assert-only TypeScript tests, each with an `UPDATE_CONTRACT_FIXTURES=1` mode that writes (C221.8), of `contracts/fixtures/flow-spec/{visibility,pattern,digits}-vectors.json` (over its own case table, Phase 9's golden cases copied as data plus the pathological ones, importing only the implementations, so Phase 9's test files stay unedited; review F10), `contracts/fixtures/theme/builtin-palettes.json` and `contracts/fixtures/i18n/copy-keys.json`; the Gradle task and the table rows of C200.3 with the append-only test; `android-consent-digests.json` from the server's own fill and hash (C200.4); a named borrowed edit to Phase 9's `apps/flow/scripts/export-flow-locales.ts` and its test so that they keep the rows whose `client` is not `web` (review F07); the zod parse test over the 10A request fixtures (C224.4); C221.12 (the session's `language`, when the inventory finds no Phase 9 equivalent, and the NFC-normalised `organization-display-name`); any server change the Android consent rows need in the digest check (by the inventory); C221.13 (the `device_handoff` skip for SDK sessions). Each server change is a named borrowed edit to the file `contracts-10a` names in the inventory, with a Testcontainers test: the `language` query sets `session.locale`; a display name whose first character is not a stable NFC boundary is refused on save (C200.2); the digest check accepts an Android row and refuses a wrong digest; an SDK session at a `skip-on-mobile: false` handoff step advances past it while a web session stops there (reviews V05, V08). It owns `packages/flow-spec/src/export-vectors.test.ts`, `packages/ui/src/export-palettes.test.ts`, `apps/api/src/services/templates/consent-texts.android.test.ts`, `apps/api/src/routes/flow/flow-requests.android.test.ts`, the exported files under `contracts/fixtures/{flow-spec,theme}/`, the Android rows of `contracts/fixtures/i18n/consent-texts.json`, `contracts/fixtures/i18n/{copy-keys,android-consent-digests}.json` and `android/openverify-sdk/scripts/export-consent-texts.gradle.kts`.
- **Stage 4: `integration-10a`:**
  - the 10A full-stack spec (C224.3) under the e2e lock; the API-driven client with the Android `User-Agent`; `:openverify-sdk:stackTest` with the 10A flow (C224.4);
  - CI: the ABI check and the screenshot comparison in the `android` job (C220.1), the floor set to the measured count, job times re-measured, `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh`;
  - `docs/android/` (the integration guide, events, failure reasons, themes and languages), the PLAN corrections of C222 that 10A settles, `docs/research/persona-surface.md`'s Android rows;
  - C221.9's two console edits (the preview's Android line and the consent record's client-reported label; review V05), as named borrowed edits;
  - the full validation; defects fixed test-first in any path; missing hooks added and reported; the amendments of Stages 2–3; a draft of the Phase 10A entry for `docs/PROGRESS.md` in its report (the orchestrator writes PROGRESS).

  It owns `e2e/`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh`, `docs/android/`, `docs/PLAN.md`, `docs/research/persona-surface.md` and `docs/plans/phase-10.plan.md`.

**Shared files (the C54 rule for Phase 10A).** Stage 2–4 builders never edit these (each hook is a call in the shared file to a stub in an owned file):
- **Android:** `FlowCoordinator.kt` outside its hook calls until `android-driver` owns it in Stage 3, `model/Flow.kt` and `model/Resources.kt` (after Stage 1a), the public API files of C190–C192 and the seam interfaces (after Stage 1a; only `contracts-10a` or an integration agent changes a public signature, with the ABI dump in the same commit), `ContractFixturesTest.kt`'s list, `FlowCoordinatorProjectionTest.kt` (Phase 9's; it is deleted with the projection path by `android-driver` as a recorded amendment, its assertions replaced by driver tests over the same projection fixtures' positions), the existing `strings.xml` of `composeResources/values/` and `values-es/` after `contracts-10a` moves their key-mapped strings into `strings_catalog.xml` (new keys go into each owner's own `strings_<area>.xml` in all five folders, C199.1; review F08), `ui/InquiryFlowScreen.kt` after Stage 1a (its dispatch lines are `contracts-10a`'s, review F04), `i18n/review-status.json` outside the named borrowed edits that add keys, `libs.versions.toml`, every `build.gradle.kts` and `verification-metadata.xml` (after Stage 1b), the ABI dump and its expected declaration list (regenerated only with a public change);
- **server and packages:** `packages/contracts/src/{index,flow-api}.ts`, `packages/flow-spec/src/{index,flow}.ts` and `src/v2/` (Phase 9's; 10A only reads them), `contracts/fixtures/i18n/<locale>.json` (Phase 9's export), every `package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` (after Stage 1a).

A missing hook is a `contractDeviation` that the integration agent adds. Every other path named in this plan has exactly one owner.

**Acceptance of 10A:** the SDK declares spec 2 and renders the server's position for every step type it supports, the `screen` step with C148's catalogue, waits and the halted state; no transition is evaluated on the device; the Kotlin visibility, pattern and digit ports pass every exported vector; the theme, appearance, logo, five locales, right-to-left, the selector, operator copy with legal keys failing closed, and consent digests matching the server's vectors all pass their tests; the public API and seams, and nothing else, are in the ABI dump; the migrated coordinator suites pass over `FakeFlowServer`; every 10A request fixture parses with its zod schema; the 10A spec passes on the shards and `:openverify-sdk:stackTest` drives the SDK's own client through it.

## Workflow 10B (capture and device integrity)

**Families and checkpoints:** (C) capture (frames, documents, PDF417, selfies); (I) device integrity (heuristics, Play Integrity on the server and the SDK's call). Nine agents. 10B starts after 10A is pushed.

- **Stage 1a: `contracts-10b`.** It runs alone. It first re-verifies 10A as built (the driver's hooks, the registry, the seams, the ABI dump, the screenshot folders), Phase 7's signal route and registry as built, Phase 5's provider bindings (C63), and records drift. Then, by family:
  - **Common:** `io.github.zxing-cpp:android` 3.1.1 in the catalog after reading the licences of the native code its AAR bundles, with the `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` row (C203.1); the identifier `ich`; the problem codes of C221.6 that 10B needs with golden fixtures.
  - **(C):** `AnalysisFrame`, `PixelLayout` and `CaptureCamera.frames()` with its empty default (C201.1), `BarcodeReader` (C203.2), the guidance overlay and auto-capture controller interfaces the capture screen exposes to the document and selfie owners, the shapes of `document-capture.json`, `document-quality-vectors.json` and `selfie-guidance-vectors.json` with their JSON Schemas, and the upload's `capture-trigger` (C206.2) in zod and in the SDK's multipart writer signature.
  - **(I):** the zod contracts of C210.2 and C211.3 (the signals wire's `integrity.android`, the session's `integrity`, the challenge and token routes), the five registry entries' declarations with `decisive-alone`, the new null reasons with their `ON_NULL_DEFAULTS` classes (C210.4, C211.5), the provider kind `device-integrity`, and the SDK's wire models with `ContractFixturesTest` decoders; the session's `integrity` member in `flow-spec-members.json`, classified (C194.4; review V14); the legal texts of decision 66 (`notice.session-integrity`'s new built-in text and the new `notice.play-integrity` key in `COPY_KEYS` with `legal: notice`, in the web catalogs' five locales as borrowed edits to Phase 9's catalogs, the locale export re-run, and their key-map entries so Android takes the same words, C199.1).

  It owns any path its deliverables need.
- **Stage 1b: `foundation-10b`** (plumbing only; one migration extended per checkpoint; the PGlite migration time measured before and after), beside Stage 2:
  - **(C):** `files.capture_trigger`, written by the upload service as received and validated by the zod enum, and the file attribute served;
  - **(I):** `inquiry_integrity_challenges` and `inquiry_integrity_verdicts` with sealing, rewrap, redaction and retention entries; the routes frozen at 501 in `routes/flow/integrity.ts`; the decode job's queue `integrity-decode` with an `unbuiltJob` stub; the provider kind's registration with stubs in `services/device-integrity/`; the rate policy `flow-integrity`; the registry entries returning `null` with `collector_missing` until built; in the SDK, the hook in `FlowCoordinator` after consent that calls `requestIntegrityVerdict(session)` (a no-op stub in `commonMain/.../signals/Integrity.kt`, owned by `device-integrity-android`);
  - **(C), the SDK hooks** (review F05), each a call in a shared file to a stub in an owned file: the capture trigger threaded from `CaptureScreen`'s capture action through `FlowCoordinator`'s upload call into the multipart writer (`camera/CaptureTrigger.kt`, `capture-pipeline`); the auto-capture action, through which the document and selfie controllers take the still (`capture/AutoCapture.kt`, `capture-pipeline`); the selfie capture's id and expiry exposed from `FlowCoordinator`'s selfie protocol state to `capture/selfie/Extension.kt` (`selfie-guidance`), which calls `extendSelfieCapture` and announces the warning; `keepScreenOn` on the waiting screen, set by `foundation-10b` itself in the shared `StatusScreens.kt` (C201.5; the capture screens are `capture-pipeline`'s and the NFC tap screen `nfc-sdk`'s); `foundation-10b` also registers the Kover exclusion entries 10B's builders name (C219.2) and owns no PDF417 asset task (the assets are committed, C219.5);
  - **after the last checkpoint:** the OpenAPI snapshot.
- **Stage 2** (parallel, beside Stage 1b; each after Stage 1a's common and its family):
  - **`capture-pipeline`** (C): C201 (the three-use-case binding, sampling, the fallback with its test hook, unmirrored analysis, buffer reuse), the capture screen's overlay and controller slots, C206.1–C206.2 on the client, `filterTouchesWhenObscured` on the capture controls on API 26–30 (C213.1; review V03), and the device tests of C219.5 for the camera. It owns `androidMain/.../camera/`, `commonMain/.../camera/`, `ui/CaptureScreen.kt` and `androidDeviceTest/.../camera/`.
  - **`document-guidance`** (C): C202 (the measures, the assert-only Python test of `document-quality-vectors.json` over `quality.py`, the client constants calibrated on synthetic ID renders with the calibration report in `docs/calibration/android-document-guidance.md`, hints, auto-capture, the manual shutter rule) through the capture screen's slots. It owns `commonMain/.../capture/document/`, `services/vision/tests/test_document_quality_vectors.py`, `contracts/fixtures/flow/{document-capture,document-quality-vectors}.json` and `screenshots/document-guidance/`.
  - **`pdf417-guidance`** (C): C203 (the zxing-cpp reader, the hint and its calibration at the analysis width, the barcode overlay, the committed synthetic PDF417 assets with their Python equality test, the device test). It owns `androidMain/.../capture/barcode/`, `commonMain/.../capture/barcode/`, its device test and assets, and `services/vision/tests/test_android_pdf417_assets.py`.
  - **`selfie-guidance`** (C): C204.1–C204.6 (the YuNet port and guidance machine over the exported vectors, asserted by a test it adds in `apps/flow/src/guidance/export-vectors.test.ts`; the `FaceDetector` outputs as `YunetOutputs`; auto-capture per prompt with a fake detector; announcements; the 2.2.1 warning and the extension call; the behaviour without a detector). It owns `commonMain/.../capture/selfie/`, `apps/flow/src/guidance/export-vectors.test.ts`, `contracts/fixtures/flow/selfie-guidance-vectors.json` and `screenshots/selfie-guidance/`.
  - **`device-integrity-android`** (I): C210.1–C210.3 (the probes over `DeviceProbe`, the wire, the timing device test) and the SDK half of C211.3 (`requestIntegrityVerdict`: the challenge, the installed provider, the token post, every failure sending nothing). It owns `android/openverify-sdk/src/*/kotlin/**/signals/` (after Phase 7's `android-signals`, whose files it extends) and its tests.
  - **`api-integrity`** (I): C210.4–C210.5 (the registry entries with their computation from the stored signal bodies, `decisive-alone`, the publish rule through Phase 9's publish checks and through Phase 8's workflow publish checks as borrowed edits, one shared function, the `heuristic:` widening as a borrowed edit to Phase 9's acknowledgement rule, golden tests with trivially true conjuncts) C211.9's publish rule `play_integrity_notice_missing` (a borrowed edit to Phase 9's publish checks beside `signal_not_decisive_alone`, with a Testcontainers test; review V05), and C211.3–C211.5, C211.7 (the routes, the `play-integrity` provider with its JWT and decode against a fake Google endpoint, the `sandbox` provider, the job, the two signals, the test that no Play Integrity field feeds a device list or the graph). It owns `apps/api/src/services/device-integrity/`, `routes/flow/integrity.ts`, `apps/api/src/jobs/integrity/`, `packages/db/src/repositories/inquiry-integrity.ts` and the registry entries' computation files the inventory names.
- **Stage 3: `integration-10b`:** the 10B full-stack specs (C224.3); `docs/checks/signals.md` and `docs/checks/selfie.md`; the compliance guide's strictly-necessary template with the probe list of C210.1 (review V05); `docs/android/` (capture, signals, Play Integrity and the host recipe); the OpenAPI snapshot, `@openverify/node` and the fixtures after Stage 1; CI with job times re-measured and the device tests running on the emulator; the floor raised; the full validation; defects fixed test-first; the amendments; a draft of the Phase 10B entry for PROGRESS. It owns `e2e/`, `docs/checks/{signals,selfie}.md`, `docs/compliance/` (or the compliance guide's file the inventory names), `docs/android/`, `docs/api/openapi.json`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh` and `docs/plans/phase-10.plan.md`.

**Shared files (Phase 10B):** the 10A list, with 10A's owned files now shared too (each change to the driver through a hook added by `foundation-10b`; the SDK manifest `androidMain/AndroidManifest.xml` changed only by `contracts-10b`, as in 10C by `contracts-10c`, in 10D by `fix-sdk` and in 10E by `contracts-10e`; review V03), plus Phase 7's signal route and services outside their hook calls, `services/publish/` and Phase 8's workflow publish checks outside the borrowed edits named above, and `CaptureScreen.kt`'s slot interfaces (after Stage 1a).

**Acceptance of 10B:** frames reach the guidance and the fallback keeps the manual shutter; the document measures match the server's vectors and the calibrated constants are in their fixture with a report; zxing-cpp shows "barcode found" on a decoded PDF417 as a hint (a gate only once the calibration shows it decodes at the analysis resolution) and nothing decoded leaves the device; the selfie port passes the web's vectors and auto-captures each prompt with a fake detector, with the extension usable ten times; the indicators reach the three signals as `true` with reasons or `null`, never decisive alone; the sandbox Play Integrity provider reaches the two signals; the 10B specs pass on the shards.

## Workflow 10C (NFC in the SDK, shipping and the demo)

**Families:** (N) NFC; (D) delivery (R8, release checks, publishing, the demo, docs, privacy checks). Eight agents. 10C starts after 10B is pushed.

- **Stage 1a: `contracts-10c`.** It runs alone. It re-verifies 10B as built and Phase 5's chip routes, confirms that a challenge on an `initiated` verification before any upload is accepted at submit (a Testcontainers test, C207.2), and records drift. Then:
  - **(N):** the SDK wire models of C207.1 with decoders; the zod contracts and golden fixtures of `client-unable`, `sandbox-chip`, `fallback-available`, `sandbox-chip-available` and `prefill-mrz` (C221.1), their problems and the `flow-sandbox-chip` policy; the frozen routes at 501 in `routes/flow/government-id-nfc.ts`'s neighbours (`routes/flow/client-unable.ts`, `routes/flow/sandbox-chip.ts`); the `trust:sandbox-utopia` script entry in `apps/api/package.json`, pointing at `api-nfc`'s stub `apps/api/src/commands/trust-sandbox-utopia.ts` (C208.1; review V14); the new client-view members `fallback-available`, `sandbox-chip-available` and `prefill-mrz` in `flow-spec-members.json`, each classified (C194.4). The two kinds of client-view member are placed apart (review F26). `fallback-available` is a pure fact of the flow (whether the `nfc` step has an `exhausted` edge), so it joins the `nfc` client view in `packages/flow-spec`'s pure `client-view` module, as a named borrowed edit with its golden fixture. `prefill-mrz` and `sandbox-chip-available` need the inquiry and the environment, so they come from a hook in the API's session-response builder (the Phase 9 file the inventory names) that calls `nfcSessionMembers` (a stub in `services/flow-views/nfc.ts`, owned by `api-nfc`). In the SDK (review F05), with each hook a call in a shared file to a stub in an owned file: `FlowState.Nfc`; the `nfc` entry in `StepRegistry`; its dispatch line in `InquiryFlowScreen.kt` to `ui/steps/NfcStepScreen.kt`; the coordinator's routing of `NfcStep` to `nfc/NfcStepController.kt` (both `nfc-sdk`'s); and the internal `NfcPlatform` seam with stub Android and iOS actuals (C207.12). On the web, the step dispatch in Phase 9's `apps/flow/src/flow/` to `apps/flow/src/steps/nfc/`, and an `nfc` entry in the mocked suite's shared mock registry, both `api-nfc`'s. Spike (d) runs first, in a scratch worktree that is discarded: whether `com.vanniktech.maven.publish` 0.37.0 publishes the KMP root and Android target to Maven Local on AGP 9.4.1, Gradle 9.8.2 and KGP 2.4.21, with the Dokka 2.2.0 javadoc jar built through it, resolving both plugins through the existing `pluginManagement` (C217.2; reviews F23, F25).
  - **(D):** Dokka 2.2.0 (or, if spike (d) shows it cannot read KGP 2.4 metadata here, the publishing plugin's empty javadoc jar, which the Central Portal accepts, with `docs/android/` as the API docs) and `com.vanniktech.maven.publish` 0.37.0 in the catalog with their licence rows; bundletool, pinned to its latest release dated on or before 2026-10-15 (Apache-2.0), with its SHA-256 and a `scripts/fetch_bundletool.py` step run before Gradle (review F22); the publication blocks for `openverify-sdk` and `openverify-nfc` with the POM metadata (no signing); the demo's `release` and `minifiedSmoke` build type skeletons (C215.2), with `okhttp-tls` (Apache-2.0, at the OkHttp version already pinned) and the catalog's existing `mockwebserver3` in the demo's device-test scope, and `okhttp-tls`'s licence row as test tooling (review V01); the `android-release.yml` skeleton that exits with a notice; the build-task stubs 10C's builders fill (the size and ELF checks, the consumer check) and the boundary check's extension point for every module now present (review F18).

  It owns any path its deliverables need.
- **Stage 2** (parallel):
  - **`nfc-sdk`** (N): C207 on the device (the step, the access screens, the challenge on "I'm ready" with its renewal and cap rules, the DG1 review, upload and submit, the states and screens, process death, the per-reason "cannot read document chips" screens over `NfcPlatform` (C207.12) and the client-unable call) and C208.2 (`SandboxChipReader`), with goldens and a fake `ChipReader` covering every `ChipReadFailure`. It also owns `keepScreenOn` on the tap screen (C201.5), C213.1's flags on the MRZ and CAN entry and the DG1 review (accessibility-data-sensitive on API 34+, `filterTouchesWhenObscured` on their controls on API 26–30; review V03), C195.2's autofill and keyboard flags on the MRZ and CAN fields, the fixed-message rule for the `ChipReadException`s `SandboxChipReader` throws (C213.3), the NFC wording test (C207.5) and its `strings_nfc.xml` files in all five locales (C199.1). It owns `commonMain/.../nfc/`, `androidMain/.../nfc/` (the `NfcPlatform` actual), `iosMain/.../nfc/`, `ui/steps/NfcStep*.kt`, `screenshots/nfc-sdk/` and their tests.
  - **`api-nfc`** (N): C207.9 on the server (the route, the `exhausted` advance through Phase 9's `advanceFlowInTx`, the step record note with the platform, the always-review rule and the `client-unable:<step id>` acknowledgement and the `nfc`-without-`exhausted` warning through Phase 9's publish checks as borrowed edits), `nfcSessionMembers` (`prefill-mrz` only for an SDK client at the current step, with its masking and the web-client fixture; `sandbox-chip-available`), the `trust:sandbox-utopia` command (C208.1) with its Testcontainers test, the zod parse test over the NFC request fixtures (C224.4), C208.1 and C208.3 (the sandbox route, its scenarios, its lazy import and the production test), C207.10 (the web flow's NFC screen in five locales with its mocked spec), and the Testcontainers tests of C224.2. It owns `routes/flow/{client-unable,sandbox-chip}.ts`, `apps/api/src/services/{client-unable,sandbox-chip,flow-views/nfc}.ts`, `apps/api/src/commands/trust-sandbox-utopia.ts`, `apps/flow/src/steps/nfc/` and `apps/flow/e2e/nfc-*.spec.ts`.
  - **`release-checks`** (D): C215.1, C215.2's build-type proof and C215.4 (the consumer rules, the minified demo proof), C216 (the 16 KB checks with `check-elf-alignment.py`, the size baseline with bundletool over `:demo-app:bundleRelease`, the SDK-only configuration measured on every push and the four configurations in `android-release.yml`, review F22), C217.2–C217.4 (the staged repository, the consumer check project, the release workflow with the device matrix and the secret-less notice) and C214.5's lockfile and verification-metadata refresh. It owns `android/*/consumer-rules.pro`, `android/scripts/`, `android/size-baseline.json`, `android/consumer-check/`, `.github/workflows/android-release.yml` and the publishing blocks of the build files (borrowed from `foundation-10a`'s ownership through `contracts-10c`'s skeleton).
  - **`demo-app`** (D): C218 (the paths, pickers, event log, optional components by Gradle property, the App Links example with its link checks, the `minifiedSmoke` smoke device test against a loopback `MockWebServer` over TLS with its `src/minifiedSmoke/` launcher activity (C215.2; review V01), the host test over `src/main`'s network configuration) and C215.3 (the merged-manifest allowlists). It owns `android/demo-app/`.
  - **`docs-android`** (D): C213.5 (the README per tier with the Data safety entries), C222's docs (`docs/android/` for NFC, privacy, release policy and sizes, `docs/checks/nfc.md`, `android/openverify-nfc/README.md` and `NOTICE` for the module's future dependencies, `android/CHANGELOG.md` with the 0.x entry), the failure-reason table. It owns those paths except `docs/android/` files another agent created, which it extends by borrowed edits.
  - **`privacy-checks`** (D): the cross-cutting checks of C213.1–C213.4: the `FLAG_SECURE` tests over every window and the source scan refusing `SecureOff` and the platform dialog builders; a scan that every typed-value field and every consent, capture and submit control carries the protection C213.1 names; the data-at-rest and saved-state scans' final allowlists (created by `foundation-10a`) and the saved-state canary across recreation; the exception canary extended over every module now present; the source-scan test's final allowlist over `SdkLog`; the network-security note. It also runs C191.2's boundary test against every module now present. The protections themselves are built by the owners of their files (C213.1; review V03), and a finding in another owner's file is a `contractDeviation` for `integration-10c`. It owns `androidHostTest/.../privacy/` and the scan tests; `SdkLog` is `foundation-10a`'s, extended here by borrowed edit.
- **Stage 3: `integration-10c`:** the 10C full-stack specs (C224.3) and the NFC flow in `:openverify-sdk:stackTest` (C224.4); CI with the minified build, the 16 KB and size steps in the `android` job and the release smoke test in `android-instrumented`, times re-measured; `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh` for both workflows; the OpenAPI snapshot and fixtures; the PLAN corrections of C222 for 10C; the full validation; defects fixed test-first; a draft of the Phase 10C entry for PROGRESS. It owns `e2e/`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `infra/`, `docs/api/openapi.json`, `docs/PLAN.md` and `docs/plans/phase-10.plan.md`.

**Shared files (Phase 10C):** the 10B list with 10B's owned files, plus Phase 5's chip services outside their hook calls, Phase 9's session-response builder outside the `nfcSessionMembers` call, `packages/flow-spec`'s `client-view` module outside `contracts-10c`'s borrowed edit, and the NFC dispatch lines and `NfcPlatform` expect declarations after Stage 1a.

**Acceptance of 10C:** an NFC step completes end to end with the sandbox chip and fails as each scenario should; a phone without NFC reaches the author's fallback through `client-unable`, and the web flow offers the same; the minified demo runs its smoke test; the 16 KB and size checks pass with a committed baseline; the staged repository resolves for an Android consumer; the README, changelog and `docs/android/` describe what ships; the 10C specs pass on the shards.

## Workflow 10D (accessibility, review, fixes)

Nine agents; 10D starts after 10C is pushed.

- **Stage 1: `android-a11y-audit`** (alone): C212 end to end: the automated checks over every screen and state, run on every golden of C219.3's budget, plus the clipped-text check of C212.2 in the five locales at font scale 2.0 and under `en-XA` (on the host, or on the device where spike (c) found Robolectric cannot run them), announcements counted, every defect fixed test-first in `android/openverify-sdk`; the manual TalkBack checklist with its empty results table; the statement's Android part. It owns `docs/accessibility/android-audit-*.md`, the Android part of `docs/accessibility/statement.md` and, for its fixes, any path in `android/openverify-sdk`.
- **Stage 2: review of all of Phase 10** (read-only, parallel):
  - **privacy, security and law:** what leaves the device and when (consent gates for signals and integrity, chip bytes, `prefill-mrz`, events, logs, storage, intents); `FLAG_SECURE`; the client-unable route as a way around a step; the sandbox chip route's isolation; the Play Integrity terms and the no-tracking rule; the LGPL and proprietary boundaries; consent digests; GDPR, UK GDPR and biometric law where guidance, consent or signals touch them;
  - **correctness and honesty:** the driver against Phase 9's engine (no client-chosen path, current-step handling, waits, fallbacks, halted); the Kotlin ports against the TypeScript vectors; operator copy and legal keys; the chip sequence and the server's checks; the guidance never acting as a result; the heuristics' and Play Integrity's limits stated; coverage of M1–M43 and every carried item;
  - **build, licences, performance and CI:** the ABI dump and `explicitApi`, R8 rules, 16 KB, sizes, the release-age record, licence rows, dependency locking, Gradle and CI times and minutes, the release workflow, the frame pipeline's per-frame cost;
  - **accessibility and UX on Android:** the audit's method and statement, TalkBack semantics, font scale, right to left, auto-capture's timing and the manual shutter, the NFC prompts.
- **Stage 3:** one verifier for every CRITICAL and HIGH finding. It records the 10D amendments.
- **Stage 4:** path-owned fixers:
  - **`fix-sdk`:** `android/` except `android/scripts/` and the release workflow;
  - **`fix-server`:** `apps/api`, `packages/`, `apps/flow`, `services/vision`, `e2e/`, `contracts/fixtures/`;
  - **`fix-records`:** `docs/`, `.github/workflows/`, `infra/`, `android/scripts/`, `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`; it measures the CI jobs again; after the 10D push, the orchestrator dispatches the release matrix once on that commit (C220.3) and records its run id and minutes (decision 72).

**Acceptance of 10D:** every CRITICAL and HIGH finding fixed or recorded with a reason; the automated accessibility checks pass on every screen state; the statement says "not yet evaluated for conformance" for Android until the manual pass runs; the release matrix's API 26 and 34 legs pass once, and its 16 KB API 35 leg passes too, or, when it cannot boot on the standard runner and P5 is pending or declined, is recorded open with the per-push 16 KB checks green (C220.3; review V02).

## Workflow 10E (the owner-gated artifacts)

Up to eight agents. 10E runs after 10D is pushed, and only the parts whose owner decision is recorded in `docs/PROGRESS.md` (P1–P4); a part whose decision is still pending is skipped and stays an open item. If no decision has been recorded, 10E does not start, and Phase 10 is recorded done with P1–P4 open once 10D's CI jobs and the release matrix's available legs are green (Acceptance, decision 72).

- **Stage 1a: `contracts-10e`.** It re-verifies 10A–10D as built and the decisions recorded, then for each part allowed: the dependency pins (ONNX Runtime 1.31.0; the JMRTD stack, or the fork's plan if P1 is declined; the Play Integrity library at a version whose publication date it verifies is at least 14 days old) with their `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` rows; the module skeletons (`android/openverify-face` with its internal `ModelSource`, C204.2; `android/openverify-integrity-play`; `openverify-nfc`'s dependencies) with `settings.gradle.kts` entries, Kover setups at 80%, consumer rules files, publication blocks and ABI dumps; the demo's Gradle properties; the boundary test extended; for P1, the synthetic Utopia chip's files and access keys exported from `@openverify/emrtd/testing` to `contracts/fixtures/emrtd/utopia-chip.json` by an assert-only TypeScript test (C209.3); the source scans of C213.1–C213.3 and the data-at-rest allowlist extended to each new module, and the merged-manifest allowlists of C215.3 for the new configurations (review P25); for P2, the package of ORT's library manifest, read from the AAR, for C205.2's `tools:selector` (review V14).
- **Stage 2** (parallel, each only if allowed):
  - **`face-onnx`** (P2 granted): C205.2–C205.5 (the telemetry removal with its manifest and device tests, the model fetch task with the digest check, inference, the 10 s promotion rule wired to the detector, the device test of YuNet through ORT); the custom-build spike runs in the background, the one background job allowed besides the e2e stack, with its log polled at each checkpoint; if it does not finish within the Workflow, the official AAR stays and the spike is recorded as not measured. It owns `android/openverify-face/`.
  - **`scuba-fork`** (only if P1 is declined): C209.5's clean-room rewrite of `CommandAPDU` and `ResponseAPDU` from ISO/IEC 7816-4 and the public `javax.smartcardio` API specification, by an agent instructed not to open the upstream files, with tests from the standard's encoding rules; the fork's publication as `openverify-scuba-smartcards`, the exclusion on JMRTD and the staged-POM test. It owns `android/openverify-nfc/scuba-fork/`.
  - **`nfc-reader`** (P1 granted, or after `scuba-fork`): C209.2–C209.4 (the reader, the orchestrator over `ChipSession` with scripted tests, the thin JMRTD adapter with the JVM test against the in-process synthetic chip responder, the no-adapter device test, the licence texts and `NOTICE`, the consumer rules), the demo's NFC wiring through its Gradle property and its debug-only reader-check mode (C209.6) as a borrowed edit to `android/demo-app/`. It owns `android/openverify-nfc/` except `scuba-fork/`.
  - **`play-integrity-client`** (P3 granted): C211.6 and C211.10 (the provider over a standard request, lazy preparation with its no-call host test, error mapping, the device test of the error path on an image without the Play Store). It owns `android/openverify-integrity-play/`.
- **Stage 3: review** (read-only, parallel): **licence and security** (the artifacts' POMs and licence texts, the boundary, telemetry, the clean room if used, what each artifact sends) and **correctness** (the reader's protocol order against C207.2 and C62, ORT's inference against the vectors, the size and 16 KB results).
- **Stage 4: `integration-10e`:** every finding fixed test-first; the size baseline for the new configurations; the 16 KB scan over the new AARs; `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`, PLAN and `docs/android/` for the parts built; with P4 granted, the release workflow's secrets wired by the owner and one deployment to the Central Portal left for the owner's manual release; the full validation and the release matrix; a draft of the Phase 10E entry for PROGRESS. It owns `docs/`, `.github/workflows/`, `infra/`, `android/size-baseline.json` and `docs/plans/phase-10.plan.md`.

**Acceptance of 10E (per part):** `openverify-face` runs YuNet through ONNX Runtime on the emulator over a public-domain fixture frame supplied as an `AnalysisFrame`, with boxes, landmarks and the yaw ratio within the tolerance of C204.1's vectors, with no telemetry provider, no extra permission and no bytes counted over C205.2's window (the emulated camera shows no face, so per-prompt auto-capture stays proven on the host with the fake detector, 10B; reviews H05, F14); the JMRTD reader passes its orchestrator tests and the JVM test of the real JMRTD service against the in-process synthetic chip (C209.3), and its device test covers the no-NFC-adapter path, with the owner's real-document result from the demo's reader check recorded as pass or fail only (C209.6); the Play Integrity client maps every error to "nothing sent"; the first Central deployment validates. Each declined part's outcome (C205.6, C209.5, C211.6, C217.5) is in the README and PROGRESS.

## Rules for every agent

- **TDD:** RED first, seen failing for the intended reason; the RED commit compiles (Kotlin and TypeScript) and typechecks; then GREEN; then refactor on green. Evidence, with the commands run and their output, goes in `docs/tdd/phase10-<agent>.tdd.md`.
- **Shared files:** builders never edit the shared-file lists above. Public Kotlin signatures, the ABI dumps, route schemas, the OpenAPI snapshot, `@openverify/node`, the API fixtures and the Android fixture list change only through a Stage 1a or integration agent. A missing hook or schema change is a `contractDeviation`. Dependencies land only in a Stage 1a, with one exception: in 10A, `foundation-10a` adds the test tooling its spikes choose (Roborazzi, the accessibility test artifact, BCV if needed). 10B adds zxing-cpp; 10C adds Dokka, the publishing plugin, bundletool and the demo's test-scope `okhttp-tls`; 10E adds the gated libraries. Whoever adds a dependency refreshes `verification-metadata.xml` and the lockfiles in the same commit (C214.5).
- **iOS-ready** (PLAN §6; ADR 0004; decision 54): new code goes to `commonMain` unless it needs a platform API; every platform seam is an interface in `commonMain` or an internal `expect` with an iOS actual that claims nothing; no `java.*` or `android.*` import in `commonMain`; every `commonMain` dependency publishes `iosArm64` and `iosSimulatorArm64` variants; no cinterop in `:openverify-sdk`; `:openverify-sdk:assemble` (the iOS klibs) stays green at every checkpoint; per-frame code reuses its buffers.
- **Shared resources:**
  - commits are path-scoped under `flock /tmp/openverify-git.lock`, after `git diff --cached --quiet`;
  - every Gradle invocation runs under `flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock` (ours: Gradle builds of one project directory contend for its locks and outputs), in the foreground with a 600,000 ms timeout, split so each call fits 10 minutes (Validation; decision 61). `flock -E 75 -w 240` bounds the wait and gives a timeout its own exit status, 75 (`EX_TEMPFAIL`; review V04): only 75 means "lock busy, retry", recorded as such and never as a test failure. Every other non-zero status is the wrapped command's own failure, since `flock` returns the command's status once it holds the lock, and a failed Gradle build exits 1. The wait is written beside every timing. The red-green loop uses narrow calls (`--tests` filters, `compileCommonMainKotlinMetadata`); the full split gate runs only at checkpoints (review F24);
  - the full-stack e2e runs under `flock /tmp/openverify-e2e.lock`, as the mocked flow suite plus shards on the reusable stack, with the load average written beside every timing. `:openverify-sdk:stackTest` runs inside the same e2e-locked section as the shards and takes the Gradle lock inside it. The order is fixed: the e2e lock outside, the Gradle lock inside, and no agent asks for the e2e lock while it holds the Gradle lock (review V14); only the stack start (and, in 10E, the ORT custom-build spike) may run in the background;
  - flaky tests are made deterministic, never retried; time comes from the injected `Clock` or `TimeSource`; tests that order rows stamp `created_at`.
- **Device tests** (no KVM locally): compiled and lint-checked locally, run only in CI; each SDK assertion has a host twin; a device test that skips itself fails the job; a CI failure after a push is the next Workflow's first fix (C219.7).
- **CI cadence** (owner, 2026-10-28 and 2026-10-29): Workflow agents never push. The orchestrator pushes `main` once per Workflow after a green full local validation, never for a single fix; there are no check branches or pull requests; only failed jobs are re-run; the next Workflow starts after the push without waiting for CI, but a phase is recorded done only once its last push's device jobs and the release matrix's available legs are green (decision 72). The release matrix of `android-release.yml` is dispatched on `main` by the orchestrator only, once after 10D's push, once after 10E's push when 10E runs, and once per release, with its minutes recorded (C220.3).
- **No network in tests:** no outside call from any test (no Google endpoint, no Maven download during a test); the pinned `scripts/fetch_*` downloads (YuNet through `fetch_models.py --only`) run before tests.
- **Honesty (ADR 0002, 0008, 0009):**
  - guidance is never a result: no on-device measure, pose, barcode read, chip protocol outcome or integrity indicator passes or fails anything; nothing measured on the device is sent as a result, and what is sent is marked client-reported;
  - a step the SDK cannot render is unsupported, never skipped; the SDK never chooses a path;
  - heuristics ship `beta` with reasons and a confidence and are never decisive alone; a clean probe is never "not rooted";
  - no translation is called reviewed without a record; no accessibility conformance is claimed beyond the evidence; no liveness, PAD, ICAO or BSI certification is claimed;
  - every constant is provisional, with a basis; no threshold is tuned on a test's expected answer.
- **PII:** no field value, typed value, image, chip byte, MRZ, token, integrity token or verdict may appear in logs, events, intents beyond today's credential extras, saved state, files, problems, metadata, screenshots or git. Synthetic data only: synthetic documents, synthetic chips, public-domain faces through the existing manifest. A real document is read only by the owner, in the demo's reader-check mode, which sends nothing and stores nothing; only pass or fail is recorded (decision 48, C209.6).
- **Licences and versions:** rule 4 (permissive only; MPL-2.0 and EPL-2.0 only as unmodified tooling never shipped; AGPL, GPL and LGPL fail even as tooling; LGPL code lives only in `android/openverify-nfc`); the reserved items P1–P5 are never assumed granted; every pin at least 14 days old on its adoption day (decision 62); no new Gradle plugin or build-script dependency without a licence row.
- **Content:** no vendor name, branding or copied text in the SDK's UI, strings, docs or messages; public identifiers only as behaviour (behaviour note conventions).

## Validation

```bash
pnpm install --frozen-lockfile && pnpm format:check
pnpm turbo run lint typecheck --force
pnpm test                                     # every package >= 80%; the vector exports, Android consent rows and digests, copy keys and palettes are asserted here
pnpm test:integration                         # client-unable, the sandbox chip scenarios, the challenge before upload, integrity challenges and tokens, the five signals, signal_not_decisive_alone
python3 scripts/fetch_models.py && python3 scripts/fetch_data.py   # YuNet before any Gradle call (C205.3)
python3 -I scripts/fetch_bundletool.py                             # 10C on (C214.7); before any Gradle call, as ci.yml's android job runs it
python3 -I scripts/fetch_fixtures.py --only person_a_id            # 10E on: the one public-domain face openverify-face's tests read; before any Gradle call
(cd services/vision && uv run ruff check && uv run ruff format --check && uv run mypy src tools && uv run pytest)   # the document-quality vectors, the PDF417 assets
(cd android && python3 -I -m unittest discover -s scripts/tests)   # the release checks' own tests (83), which need no build output
# The Android gate (rewritten by Workflow 10D, KP10-33, to what ci.yml's android job and the 10D decisions' item 9 run): eleven calls
# in this order, each fitting 10 minutes, each Gradle call under the Gradle lock with --no-daemon (only exit 75 = lock busy, retry; any
# other non-zero status is a failed gate). A check that reads a build output counts only after the call that builds it ran in the same session (KP10-34).
(cd android && flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock ./gradlew --no-daemon :openverify-sdk:testAndroidHostTest -PhostTestGroup=ui)     # (1), three calls: ui, then flow, then rest;
(cd android && flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock ./gradlew --no-daemon :openverify-sdk:testAndroidHostTest -PhostTestGroup=flow)   #      the count is their sum, at or above
(cd android && flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock ./gradlew --no-daemon :openverify-sdk:testAndroidHostTest -PhostTestGroup=rest)   #      the SDK floor in ci.yml (2,691 at 10E), none skipped
(cd android && flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock ./gradlew --no-daemon :openverify-sdk:screenshotTest -Proborazzi.test.verify=true)   # (2) C219.3
(cd android && flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock ./gradlew --no-daemon koverVerify checkKotlinAbi)   # (3) the ABI dumps of every published module (spike (b)); never in one build with updateKotlinAbi
(cd android && flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock ./gradlew --no-daemon testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug)   # (4) testDebugUnitTest also runs :demo-app:testMinifiedSmokeUnitTest and both optional artifacts' host tests; the floors at 10E are the demo's 97 on debug and 9 smoke twins, openverify-nfc's 116 and openverify-face's 51
(cd android && flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock ./gradlew --no-daemon :demo-app:assembleRelease :demo-app:bundleRelease :openverify-nfc:assembleRelease :openverify-face:assembleRelease)   # (5) the minified APK, its bundle, and the AAR check-consumer-rules.py reads
(cd android && flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock ./gradlew --no-daemon :openverify-sdk:assemble)     # (6) the boundary check and the iOS klibs
(cd android && flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock ./gradlew --no-daemon lint)                         # (7)
(cd android && flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock ./gradlew --no-daemon :openverify-sdk:assembleAndroidDeviceTest :demo-app:assembleAndroidTest :openverify-nfc:assembleDebugAndroidTest :openverify-face:assembleDebugAndroidTest)   # (8) the device tests compile (the demo's on minifiedSmoke, with checkSmokeTestReferences); they run on a device or an emulator, never here
(cd android && python3 -I scripts/check-elf-alignment.py && python3 -I scripts/check-consumer-rules.py && python3 -I scripts/check-size.py)   # (9) after (5): 16 KB, the consumer rules as R8 applied them, the SDK-only size against the baseline
(cd android && flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock ./gradlew --no-daemon publishAllPublicationsToStagingRepository && flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock ./gradlew --no-daemon -p consumer-check build)   # (10) the staged repository and its consumer (android-release.yml's gate job)
(cd android && flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock ./gradlew --no-daemon -PopenverifyDemoArtifacts=sdk,nfc :demo-app:testDebugUnitTest)   # (11) the sdk,nfc merged manifest against its allowlist; last, because it rebuilds the demo's manifests; since 10E the same call again with sdk,face and with sdk,nfc,face
# The CI form, as ci.yml's android job runs it after the bundletool fetch and the scripts' tests (two Gradle steps, then the checks, the floors and the sdk,nfc call):
(cd android && ./gradlew testDebugUnitTest testAndroidHostTest koverVerify assembleDebug :openverify-sdk:assemble lint --stacktrace)
(cd android && ./gradlew :openverify-sdk:screenshotTest -Proborazzi.test.verify=true checkKotlinAbi :demo-app:bundleRelease :demo-app:assembleRelease :openverify-nfc:assembleRelease --stacktrace)
(cd android && python3 -I scripts/check-elf-alignment.py && python3 -I scripts/check-consumer-rules.py && python3 -I scripts/check-size.py)
# then the SDK host-test floor (at least 2,677, none skipped; C219.6), the demo's floor (at least 81 on debug and 8 smoke twins, none skipped), and last:
(cd android && ./gradlew -PopenverifyDemoArtifacts=sdk,nfc :demo-app:testDebugUnitTest --stacktrace)
pnpm --filter @openverify/flow e2e           # the mocked flow suite, with the web NFC screen (10C)
OPENVERIFY_E2E_REUSE_STACK=1 pnpm --filter @openverify/e2e e2e:stack:up     # background allowed
for i in $(seq 1 $N); do OPENVERIFY_E2E_REUSE_STACK=1 pnpm --filter @openverify/e2e exec playwright test --shard=$i/$N; done
(cd android && flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock ./gradlew :openverify-sdk:stackTest)   # the SDK's own client against the stack (C224.4), inside the e2e-locked section of the shards: e2e lock outside, Gradle lock inside (Rules)
pnpm --filter @openverify/e2e e2e:stack:down
docker build -f infra/docker/apps.Dockerfile . && docker build services/vision
for t in infra/tests/*.test.sh; do bash "$t"; done   # includes the CI and release workflow checks
gitleaks git --redact .
```

The parts that need a device: the SDK's device tests (14 in seven classes at 10D) and the demo's smoke test on its minified build (2), which `android-instrumented` runs on an API 34 emulator at every push and the release matrix (API 26, 34 and the 16 KB API 35 image, on the runner P5 allows) on dispatch (C220); `infra/scripts/check-connected-results.sh . openverify-sdk=14 demo-app=2` reads their results. Since Workflow 10D they also run on the owner's phone, by the agents and under the device lock the 10D decisions name, with the result read from the instrumentation's own output (KP10-31). No emulator runs on the build host. The floors and device counts named in this block are the ones `ci.yml` holds; when they differ, `ci.yml` is right and this block is corrected.

## Risks

| Risk | Likelihood | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| A client chooses a lower-friction path or skips a step | Low (the server decides) | The driver renders only the server's position; current-step 409s; no condition evaluated on the device except same-screen visibility; facts a device reports are marked by source; `client-unable` is self-asserted, takes only the author's `exhausted` edge, always goes to review unless the version carries its own `client-unable:` acknowledgement, and never passes (C193, C207.9) |
| On-device guidance is read as a verdict, by operators or by code | Med | ADR 0009's first rule; guidance never sent as a result; the README and `docs/checks/` say so; the server re-measures every upload (C202.6, C204.7) |
| Root, emulator or hooking heuristics decline real people (custom ROMs, developers, emulators used for accessibility) | Med | `beta` signals with reasons; `decisive-alone: false` enforced at flow and workflow publish over what a route reads, with `heuristic:` on every route that reads them; review recommended; the rule's limit stated; CI's emulator expected to report itself (C210.5) |
| A Kotlin port disagrees with the server (visibility, patterns, digits, consent digests, document measures, YuNet decoding) | Med | Every port passes vectors that an assert-only test on the reference side checks against the reference implementation, written only in an explicit update mode, so drift fails a test on both sides (C196.4, C200.4, C202.1, C204.1, C221.8) |
| Operator legal text replaced by the SDK's own words | Low | Legal keys fail closed to the unsupported screen; custom consent copy rendered from `copy` and digested (C199.7, C200.5) |
| The owner declines or delays P1–P5 | Med | Everything else ships in 10A–10D; each gated part is a separate artifact or workflow with a stated outcome on decline (C205.6, C209.5, C211.6, C217.5); without P5, a 16 KB leg that cannot boot on the standard runner is recorded open and the per-push 16 KB checks are the proof (C220.3) |
| ONNX Runtime's telemetry survives the removal | Med | Four layers (the scoped manifest removal, the environment variable, `setTelemetry(false)`, the tests); the device test measures from before the environment to a quiet period after it closes, checks for queued-event files and re-measures after a restart; the custom build without telemetry as the fallback (C205.2, C205.5) |
| The LGPL or Google code leaks into the core SDK's dependency tree | Low | The boundary test on `:openverify-sdk`'s runtime classpath; separate artifacts; no shading (C191.2, C214.6) |
| CameraX cannot bind three use cases on some devices | Med | The two-use-case fallback with the manual shutter, tested on the emulator with a forced failure (C201.3) |
| Compose Multiplatform cannot switch language per composition | Med | Spike (a) in 10A Stage 1a; the generated-table fallback with `ResourcesTest` proving both paths agree (C199.4) |
| Tooling does not work on AGP 9.4.1, KGP 2.4.21 and the KMP library plugin (ABI validation, Roborazzi, Dokka, the publishing plugin) | Med | Spikes (b) and (c) as `foundation-10a`'s first task and (d) as `contracts-10c`'s, each with a named fallback: BCV for any module ABI validation misses, Roborazzi's runtime libraries without its plugin, the plugin's empty javadoc jar with `docs/android/` as the API docs, the staged repository only (C214.2, C217.2, C219.3) |
| Device tests fail only in CI after a push | High | Host twins for every assertion; device tests limited to platform glue; fixed first in the next Workflow; never retried (C219.7) |
| CI minutes grow past the budget | Med | No nightly matrix; per-push additions estimated at +12–16 minutes (+15–19 once 10E's face test runs) and re-measured by each integration agent; three of the four size configurations and the 16 KB leg only in the release matrix, dispatched once after 10D's push, once after 10E's push when 10E runs, and once per release (C220) |
