| The SDK host-test floor masks a dropped suite when `JsonLogic` and `FlowNavigator` are deleted | Med | The floor lowered once, by `integration-10a` in its CI commit, by exactly the count `android-driver` records with the removed tests named; every other change only raises it (C219.6) |
| The NFC challenge expires during a slow upload, or while a person prepares | Med | The challenge is fetched when the person presses "I'm ready", an unused one that expires returns to that screen, a 409 after a slow upload asks for one more tap, and at most 9 challenges are requested per verification, under the server's 10 (C207.2) |
| Machine-drafted translations shown as fluent | Med | Every non-English catalog `machine-drafted` with per-key digests, shown in the README; the operator's own copy unaffected (C199.1) |
| Phase 7 or Phase 9 lands differently from its plan | Med | Each Stage 1a takes the inventory and records amendments before any Phase 10 code (C193, C210) |
| Parallel Gradle builds in one tree corrupt outputs or time out | High | The Gradle lock and split gate commands (Rules, Validation) |

## Acceptance

- [ ] **Public API (C190–C192):** both contracts accept `locale` and `appearance`; results carry no field value or token; `FLOW_HALTED` and the reason table are published; events carry no PII by an allowlist test; optional components and the transport install through one registry and their absence has its tested behaviour; one-time links never choose the API; the ABI dump holds exactly the public surface of C190.7, checked against its expected list.
- [ ] **Renderer (C193–C197):** the SDK declares spec 2, renders the server's position, never evaluates a transition, answers 409s by reading the session, and shows the unsupported screen for anything outside the registry; every Phase 9 feature of C194.2 is handled or shown unsupported by a contract test over the schema export, and every client-view and component member is listed as honoured, ignored or server-only (C194.4); screens render C148's catalogue with device validation mapped to server pointers; visibility, patterns and digits pass every exported vector; waits, halted, failure with the contest route and the help slot are rendered.
- [ ] **Themes and languages (C198–C200):** the theme maps onto Material 3 with the built-in palettes shared with the web; logos are checked before decoding; five locales with right-to-left set by the SDK; the selector; operator copy with legal keys failing closed; plurals complete; consent digests match the server's vectors and Android rows are in the append-only table.
- [ ] **Capture (C201–C206):** frames flow beside preview and capture, with the fallback; document and selfie guidance pass their vectors and auto-capture with the shutter always available and promoted after 10 s; PDF417 shows "barcode found" as a hint, a gate only once calibrated, with nothing decoded leaving the device; the 2.2.1 extension works ten times; `capture-method` and `capture-trigger` are sent and stored as client-reported.
- [ ] **NFC (C207–C208):** the NFC step completes with the sandbox chip and each scenario ends as the server's checks say (the API-driven client for every scenario; the SDK's own client for `match`, C224.4); the challenge precedes the tap; DG1 is reviewed before upload; a phone without NFC reaches the author's fallback; the web flow offers the same; chip bytes never reach disk or logs.
- [ ] **Device integrity (C210–C211):** the indicators reach three `beta` signals with reasons, never decisive alone (flow and workflow publish refuse a failure or decline that follows from them without a verification outcome, golden tests with trivially true conjuncts); the sandbox Play Integrity provider reaches two signals; no Play Integrity field feeds a device list or the graph.
- [ ] **Quality and shipping (C212–C220):** the automated accessibility checks pass on every screen state, and the manual TalkBack pass is ready for a person; privacy scans pass; `explicitApi`, the ABI check, dependency locking and the boundary test run in CI; consumer rules are proven on the minified demo; 16 KB and size checks pass with a committed baseline; the staged repository resolves for an Android consumer; the release matrix's available legs passed once (the 16 KB leg as P5 allows, C220.3); the README, changelog, Data safety entries and `docs/android/` are written.
- [ ] **Owner-gated (C205, C209, C211.6, C217.5):** each part is either built and accepted in 10E, or recorded open with its decision pending, or declined with its stated outcome in the README and PROGRESS.
- [ ] **End to end (C224.3, C224.4):** the 10A, 10B and 10C specs pass on the shards with the API-driven client; every SDK request fixture parses with its zod schema; `:openverify-sdk:stackTest` drives the SDK's own client through the 10A flow and the 10C NFC flow on the stack.
- [ ] **Deferred (C223):** every deferral is listed in `docs/PROGRESS.md` under "Carried forward", the iOS items under "Next release".
- [ ] **Records (C222):** ADR 0009, the PLAN corrections, licence rows, `docs/checks/` updates and the accessibility statement's Android part are written.
- [ ] **Process:** every existing suite stays green at each checkpoint and stage; every validation command passes locally and on GitHub (device tests and the release matrix in CI only); each agent writes its `docs/tdd/phase10-*.tdd.md`; `docs/PROGRESS.md` records Phase 10, with the manual TalkBack pass and the owner's real-document NFC test as open items for the release gate (Phase 11's acceptance). **Done means CI-green** (decision 72; review H23): the orchestrator records Phase 10 done only after the final push's `android` and `android-instrumented` jobs and the available legs of one `android-release.yml` matrix run on that commit are green (C220.3), with their run ids and minutes in PROGRESS; until then PROGRESS says "done locally; CI pending", and a failure found then is the first fix of Phase 11's Stage 1a (C219.7).

## Decisions for the owner (made here; confirm or change)

Each item states the default the build uses until the owner decides, so none blocks a Workflow, and its source: the behaviour note's owner questions (behaviour O1–O12), the engineering note's "Needs the owner" (engineering 1–15), either note's fact-check or gaps, or this plan. The owner's standing approval is to take the industry-best default and record it. Where a note's default conflicts with a binding rule or with the other note, the item says so and why. Five items are **not decided by this plan**, because they are an exception to `CLAUDE.md` rule 4 or need the owner's own accounts, domains, keys or billing; each is recorded as pending with its recommended default, and the Workflows are ordered so that everything else builds and ships without it (10A–10D), with the dependent part last (10E, or for P5 one emulator leg of the release matrix), in a separate artifact or workflow, and a stated outcome if the owner declines.

**Pending the owner (not decided here)**

- **P1. SCUBA's two `GPL-2.0-only WITH Classpath-exception-2.0` files** (behaviour O1, K7; engineering 1, K7; Phase 5 decision 40; PLAN §9). Rule 4 fails GPL code even inside `openverify-nfc`. **Recommended default:** grant a narrow, recorded exception: those two files, unmodified, from Maven Central, only inside the separate `openverify-nfc` artifact, with the GPL-2.0 and Classpath-exception texts and the files' source location in its `NOTICE` and `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` (the exception exists so that independent modules may link the files without taking on the GPL; not legal advice). **Gates:** the JMRTD reader (C209, 10E). **If declined:** the reader is built on a clean-room fork of `scuba-smartcards` whose two files are rewritten from ISO/IEC 7816-4 and the public `javax.smartcardio` API, published as its own artifact so no host resolves the upstream files (C209.5, one more 10E agent), which needs no exception; until either lands, Android NFC works with the sandbox test chip only and the docs say a production NFC step needs the reader.
- **P2. Eigen (MPL-2.0) compiled into `onnxruntime-android`** (engineering 3, K4; Phase 4 decision 11, which is pending for `onnxruntime-web` too). MPL-2.0 is allowed only as unmodified tooling never shipped; here it ships inside `libonnxruntime.so`. **Recommended default:** grant an exception for Eigen unmodified (file-level copyleft), with ONNX Runtime's third-party notices in the AAR. **Gates:** `openverify-face` (C205, 10E), and therefore selfie auto-capture on Android. **If declined:** `openverify-face` is not built; selfies keep the manual shutter with the server's prompts and decisions (C204.6); a runtime without MPL-2.0 code is evaluated in the next release (C223); and the same answer applies to the web flow's `onnxruntime-web`, which already ships Eigen pending decision 11.
- **P3. Google Play Integrity: the client library's terms and a Google Cloud project** (behaviour O3; engineering 9; Gaps: the library's release date could not be read). The client library is under Google's Play Integrity API terms, not a rule 4 licence, and real calls need the operator's Cloud project and service account. **Recommended default:** build the optional `openverify-integrity-play` artifact, off by default, with the operator bringing the project; CI uses the sandbox provider. **Gates:** C211.6 (10E). **Not gated:** the common `IntegrityTokenProvider`, the server's `play-integrity` provider (our REST code, an A-tier adapter under rule 5) and its sandbox (10B). **If declined:** OpenVerify ships no Google code; the README gives a host-side recipe implementing `IntegrityTokenProvider` with the host's own dependency.
- **P4. Maven Central: namespace, Central Portal account and token, GPG primary key, the `maven-central` environment** (behaviour O2; engineering 6). **Recommended default:** `dev.openverify` if the owner holds `openverify.dev` (a DNS TXT record), otherwise `io.github.<the owner's sign-up username>`; keys and tokens created by the owner and held only as secrets of the `maven-central` environment, reachable only from `main` (review P18; environments on a private repository need a paid GitHub plan, and without one the publish job stays off and the staged repository is the release); the Kotlin packages stay `dev.openverify.*`. **Gates:** the upload (C217.5, 10E). **Until then and if declined:** the release is the staged local repository, validated and uploaded as a workflow artifact (C217.3); hosts build from source or from it.

- **P5. A larger runner for the 16 KB emulator leg** (review V02; engineering 12 and 7.3; review F13). GitHub offers larger runners to organisations on its Team or Enterprise Cloud plans, set up by an owner in a runner group and billed with no included minutes under a spending limit the owner sets; the repository is on a personal account, so this needs the owner's account, plan and billing. **Recommended default:** the standard 2-vCPU runner with a 900 s boot budget and AVD snapshot caching first; a 4-core larger runner (about $0.18–0.24 a run) only if the API 35 `google_apis_ps16k` image does not boot there and the owner provides one. **Gates:** the 16 KB emulator leg of the release matrix only (C220.3); nothing else, since zipalign, bundletool's `PAGE_ALIGNMENT_16K` and the ELF `p_align` scan run on every push (C216.1). **If declined or unavailable:** the leg runs on the standard runner; if it cannot boot there, it is recorded as an open item for the release gate, and "done" (decision 72) and the 10D acceptance count the API 26 and 34 legs with the per-push 16 KB checks green. Making the repository public, which gives standard runners 4 vCPUs at no charge, is the owner's alternative (decision 50).

**Owner steps (not decisions; carried to the release gate):** the manual TalkBack, font-scale and keyboard pass by a person (decision 47); a real-document NFC read on the owner's phone with no data recorded (decision 48); the release dispatches of `android-release.yml` (C220.3).

**The public API**
1. **Entry points** (behaviour O6, M11). Default: the two activity-result contracts only; an inline Compose or fragment entry later, marked experimental (the public inline API is still experimental after two years; the activity owns `FLAG_SECURE`, back handling and process death).
2. **No client-side creation or identity links** (behaviour O4, M3, M6, M7). Default: no template entry, no client-set reference id, account or prefill on any client; the customer's server creates inquiries.
3. **Request options** (behaviour M8, M32). Default: `locale` and `appearance` on both requests; the `data class`'s binary change accepted under 0.x before the first publication.
4. **Results** (behaviour M9). Default: three outcomes with no field values and no token (ours, stricter than the public result, which returns fields and a token); one new reason, `FLOW_HALTED`; the reason table published.
5. **Events** (behaviour M10). Default: an optional, PII-free listener with a small closed event set, delivered through the install registry, documented as UI coordination only.
6. **Optional components** (behaviour proposal 5; ours). Default: one explicit `OpenVerifyAndroid.install` in `Application.onCreate`; no `ServiceLoader`, reflection or startup initializer; `:openverify-sdk` depends on no optional artifact.

**The renderer**
7. **Spec 2 only** (Phase 9 C175.1, decision 2 of Phase 9). Default: the SDK sends `OpenVerify-Flow-Spec: 2`, requires a Phase 9 server, and deletes its transition evaluation (`FlowNavigator`, `JsonLogic`). This departs from C175.1's `1, 2`: the header lists what a client renders, and the SDK no longer renders the v1 projection; nothing has been released, so no older server needs it.
8. **Unsupported, never skipped.** Default: anything outside the registry, a client view that fails to decode, or a component kind unknown to the SDK renders the whole step unsupported.
9. **Kotlin ports with shared vectors** (Phase 9 C148.3 and its review V10). Default: the visibility evaluator, the pattern matcher and digit normalisation in `commonMain`, each passing vectors that an assert-only TypeScript test checks against the TypeScript implementation, written only in an explicit update mode (C221.8; review V12).
10. **Redirects on Android.** Default: `completion.redirect-url` ignored; the activity result replaces it (documented).

**Themes and languages**
11. **Themes** (behaviour M31, M32; Phase 9 C160.7, C177). Default: the session theme onto Material 3 with the built-in palettes shared through a fixture; `appearance` with no resource-name overrides; the system font always, operator fonts on native clients deferred (Android parses font bytes without the browsers' sanitiser and does not load WOFF2, and no permissive sanitiser has been evaluated).
12. **Languages** (behaviour O8, M33, M34). Default: `en`, `es`, `fr`, `de`, `ar` for every SDK string, the non-English catalogs `machine-drafted` until a fluent person reviews them; RFC 4647 choice; the selector on every screen; right-to-left set by the SDK whatever the host's `supportsRtl`; the language applied per composition (spike (a) decides the mechanism).
13. **Operator copy** (Phase 9 C162). Default: overrides mapped onto the SDK's strings through the key map; an unmapped legal key renders the step unsupported, an unmapped other key keeps the built-in text.
14. **Consent evidence** (Phase 9 C162.9). Default: digests over each text's logical form (placeholders filled, NFC, without the display-only bidi isolates; reviews H07, F06), our own `Sha256` in `commonMain` (no new dependency for iOS), NFC asserted at build time, Android rows in the append-only table, cross-language digest vectors.

**Platform levels**
15. **minSdk** (behaviour O7, M2). Default: keep 26 (`ANDROID_ID` per signing key from Android 8.0, `java.util.Base64` for the NFC stack); the public SDK's 23 is not followed.
16. **compileSdk and targetSdk** (engineering 8; engineering K17). Default: stay on 36 for the first published release (Play has required target 36 for app updates since 2026-08-31, which the demo meets; the rule applies to apps, not libraries); compileSdk 37 with Compose 1.12, CMP 1.12, Ktor 3.6, OkHttp 5.5 and CameraX 1.7 is a separate slice after it, once Robolectric's API 37 support is confirmed, because host apps would have to compile with 37 too.

**Capture**
17. **The frame pipeline** (engineering 3.1). Default: `ImageAnalysis` beside preview and capture in one bind, RGBA at 8 Hz, buffers reused, the two-use-case fallback to the manual shutter.
18. **Document guidance** (engineering 3.2). Default: our own measures in `commonMain` ported from the server, no OpenCV (a 153 MB AAR for a few hundred lines), client constants in `document-capture.json`, calibrated on synthetic renders with a report.
19. **Auto-capture** (engineering 14). Default: on for documents and selfies, the shutter always on screen and primary after 10 s, at once without frames or after a failed auto-capture.
20. **PDF417 library** (behaviour K2 and summary 5 proposed zxing core 3.5.4; engineering 4.1 and K1 recommend zxing-cpp; PLAN §3 says "zxing"). Default: `io.github.zxing-cpp:android` 3.1.1 (Apache-2.0; arm64 1.75 MB uncompressed, 0.73 MB compressed; 16 KB-aligned), because it is the server's decoder family, actively released, and reads raw bytes; zxing's Java core (610 KB, no native code) is the documented fallback, set aside for its maintenance mode. Both pass rule 4; PLAN §3 is corrected (C222).
21. **Selfie guidance.** Default: the web flow's YuNet pre- and post-processing and guidance machine ported to `commonMain` and proven on vectors; the detector behind an interface; announcements once per change.
22. **The ONNX Runtime build** (engineering 4; conditional on P2). Default: the official 1.31.0 AAR with its telemetry provider and extra permission removed by the manifest merger, `ORT_DISABLE_TELEMETRY=1` set first, and both tested; a custom `--no_telemetry` build adopted only if it saves at least 20 MB on arm64 and builds reproducibly.
23. **Shipping the YuNet weights** (engineering 5). Default: ship the 230 KB MIT model inside `openverify-face`, fetched at build time with its digest checked (never committed), with the WIDER FACE training-data caveat recorded as the web flow already ships it.
24. **`openverify-face` as its own artifact** (ours; departs from PLAN §6's "in `androidMain`"). Default: ONNX Runtime and YuNet in a separate optional artifact, so the 34 MB runtime and its pending Eigen question (P2) reach only hosts that add it; PLAN is corrected (C222).
25. **2.2.1 on Android** (Phase 9 decision 57). Default: the warning 20 s ahead and the extension route, usable at least ten times.
26. **Upload metadata** (behaviour M28; Phase 9 C153.2). Default: `capture-method` from `captureMethodsByDevice.native`; production photos only from the live camera; a client-reported `capture-trigger` stored but not yet in analytics.

**NFC**
27. **Protocols and data** (behaviour M19–M21). Default: PACE first (MRZ or CAN), BAC fallback; Chip Authentication for secure messaging, reported only in `device-report`; Active Authentication over the server's nonce, fetched when the person presses "I'm ready" on the tap screen, so one tap does everything (the server accepts it before any upload, re-checked in 10C); exactly the server's file kinds, never DG11 or DG12; DG1 reviewed before upload; DG2 not decoded.
28. **When the device cannot read a chip, and the web flow** (behaviour O5, K6; engineering K6). Default: a `client-unable` route (ours) that takes the author's `exhausted` fallback, self-asserted and always sent to review unless the version carries `client-unable:<step id>` (decision 64); one message per reason; the web flow keeps NFC unsupported, reworded ("a browser cannot read it"), and offers the same "use another way".
29. **The sandbox test chip** (behaviour M43). Default: a sandbox-only flow route that builds a synthetic chip signed by the sandbox Utopia anchor, with scenarios, and a `SandboxChipReader` that drives the real sequence.
30. **The licence of `openverify-nfc`'s own code** (engineering 2). Default: keep LGPL-3.0 (Phase 5 C67) until a legal review of LGPL-3.0 §4(e) for store-distributed apps; Apache-2.0 for the module's own code is the alternative if the review agrees (not legal advice). This is a choice about OpenVerify's own code, not an exception to rule 4, so it is decided here.

**Device integrity**
31. **Root, emulator and hooking indicators** (behaviour O12; engineering 10). Default: our own probes in `androidMain` (RootBeer as a reference only, its package checks being inert without `<queries>`), the closed code lists of C210.2, no package-visibility probe and no `<queries>` entry (decision 66), no `QUERY_ALL_PACKAGES`, no accessibility services, no ADB or developer settings, no local port probing.
32. **Never decisive alone** (ours; reviews P13, H01). Default: `decisive-alone: false` in the registry, enforced at publish for flows and for Phase 8 workflows alike (`signal_not_decisive_alone`). The check is over what a route reads: no failure or decline may follow a route on such a signal without a verification outcome read in between. `heuristic:` is needed on every route reading one, whatever its tier and direction. `beta`, with reasons and a confidence.
33. **Play Integrity on the server** (engineering 9). Default: an A-tier `device-integrity` provider with `play-integrity` (REST, our JWT, no Google library) and a deterministic `sandbox`, off by default, production refusing the sandbox binding; the client artifact is P3.
34. **Play Integrity budget and use.** Default: one request per inquiry after consent; no field ever feeds device lists, the graph or a fingerprint (the API's no-tracking term).
35. **No App Set ID** (behaviour M25). Default: Phase 7's `ANDROID_ID` HMAC stays the device identifier; no Play services in the core.

**Security and privacy**
36. **Permissions** (behaviour M13). Default: `INTERNET` and `CAMERA` in the SDK; `NFC` and the optional `android.hardware.nfc` feature only in `openverify-nfc`; no audio, phone state or location; no `<queries>` entry (decision 66); `HIDE_OVERLAY_WINDOWS` (normal) for the flow activity on API 31+ (C213.1, decision 70).
37. **Data on the device** (behaviour M36; Phase 11 B11-I1). Default: `FLAG_SECURE` documented; captures, chip bytes, typed values and tokens in memory only; no logging outside `SdkLog`'s constant codes, enforced by a source-scan test.
38. **Certificate pinning** (Phase 11 B11-S1). Default: deferred to Phase 11's threat model, since each self-hoster has its own certificates. The seam is the `transport` member of `OpenVerifyComponents` with `OpenVerifyAndroidTransport.okHttp(client)`. It exists from 10A and is in the first ABI dump, so pinning needs no public signature change; the activity path could not take a host client before (review P11).

**Shipping**
39. **API compatibility** (engineering 6.6). Default: `explicitApi()` and the Kotlin Gradle plugin's ABI validation with committed dumps, BCV 0.18.2 if the spike shows the Android target is not covered.
40. **Versioning and support** (behaviour O9, M37, M40). Default: 0.x until the first published release, then semantic versioning with breaking changes only in majors, patches on the latest minor only, a dated changelog with minSdk, compileSdk and the minimum server.
41. **R8** (behaviour M14). Default: consumer rules per artifact, proven by a minified, resource-shrunk R8 full-mode demo build in CI.
42. **16 KB pages** (engineering 6.3). Default: zipalign, bundletool and an ELF `p_align` scan of every AAR in CI, and a 16 KB emulator image in the release matrix.
43. **Size budget** (engineering 13). Default: measure first: a committed per-ABI baseline for four configurations and a 10% regression gate; an absolute cap after the ORT spike; per-ABI costs in the README.
44. **What this release publishes** (engineering 7). Default: the root KMP module and its Android target, `openverify-nfc`, and the gated artifacts as their decisions allow; no iOS klibs (compiled on Linux, never linked or run); the upload is P4.
45. **API docs** (behaviour M1). Default: real Dokka 2.2.0 docs in the javadoc jar, not the empty placeholder the public SDK publishes.
46. **Gradle hygiene and dependency locking** (engineering K20; Phase 11 hardening item 7). Default: engineering K20's fixes before the first POM; lockfiles committed so Phase 11 can scan the resolved tree; dependency verification with SHA-256 checksums (decision 67).

**Tests and CI**
47. **Accessibility on Android** (behaviour M35). Default: WCAG 2.2 AA applied through WCAG2ICT with automated checks on every screen state; the manual TalkBack pass by a person, never an agent, carried to the release gate; no conformance claimed before it.
48. **A real-document NFC test** (ours; review H09). Default: the owner reads a real passport or ID card on a real phone once the reader exists, in the demo's debug-only reader-check mode, which never calls the API and keeps nothing; only pass or fail per document type and protocol is recorded (rule 3). Without it, the release notes say the reader is proven only by the JVM test of JMRTD against a synthetic chip (C209.3).
49. **Screenshot tests** (engineering 15; review F20). Default: Roborazzi 1.76.0 in its own tagged `screenshotTest` task with comparison on in the gate, synthetic content only, under C219.3's budget (every state in `en`, light, 1.0; one state per screen in `ar`, dark, 2.0; the full matrix for text-heavy screens only); recorded only by an explicit call; the runtime-library fallback if the plugin cannot run on the KMP library plugin.
50. **The emulator CI matrix and its minutes** (engineering 12, which proposed a nightly matrix). Default: no nightly run, because three nightly emulator jobs would bill about 1,350 minutes a month against the owner's one-run-per-push budget; the per-push emulator job keeps API 34 and grows by about 4–6 minutes; the API 26 (`google_apis`), 34 and 16 KB API 35 matrix runs on `workflow_dispatch` of `android-release.yml`, about 30–40 emulator minutes, once after 10D's push, once after 10E's push when 10E runs, and once per release; the 16 KB leg on the standard runner first, with a 900 s boot budget and the snapshot cache (review F13); a 4-core larger runner is pending the owner (P5; review V02); the repository's visibility, which would make standard runners free (with 4 vCPUs on a public repository, engineering 7.3), is the owner's call and is not assumed.

**Scope**
51. **Crypto wallets on Android** (behaviour K8; engineering 11, K9). Default: paste-only this release; wallet signing needs a relay account and would be an adapter.
52. **Not built** (behaviour O10, O11, M29, M30, M42). Default: no OpenVerify-branded app, no video or WebRTC, no silent network authentication, mdoc, My Number, Relay or reusable wallets.
53. **One-time links and handoff** (behaviour M5, M12). Default: App Links guidance in the demo for one-time links; the SDK as a device-handoff target in the next release with the iOS target.
54. **iOS** (behaviour K9, K15, K16; engineering K11, K14's iOS half, K15, K16, K18, K19, K21). Default: the next release; Phase 10 keeps every new seam in `commonMain` with iOS placeholders that claim nothing, and Linux CI compiles the iOS klibs.
55. **`secureRandomBytes`** (behaviour K14; engineering K13). Default: not added; the AA nonce is the server's and JMRTD draws its own randomness.

**Records and process**
56. **Five Workflows.** Default: 10A (10 agents, `android-driver` split from `android-sdk-api` by review F03), 10B (9), 10C (8), 10D (9), 10E (up to 8, gated part by part), each pushed once after a full local validation; 10E's parts start only when their owner decisions are recorded.
57. **Every carried item, placed:** C225's tables place every gap row M1–M43, behaviour K1–K16, engineering K1–K21, Phase 9's C175.1 hand-overs and the Phase 11 coordination items.
58. **ADR 0009.** Default: written by `contracts-10a` in 10A Stage 1a.
59. **Correct the records.** Default: the PLAN §3, §6 and §9 corrections of C222 (zxing-cpp, the optional artifacts, the five Workflows, P1–P4), `docs/checks/nfc.md`'s order, and `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`, by the integration agents.
60. **Fact-check corrections.** Default: taken as corrected (behaviour: the minor releases with breaking changes include 2.2.0, which only reinforces decision 40); the engineering note's 30 facts stand; its gaps are each answered by a spike or a test here (Google Maven unread: Play Integrity's date in 10E; ORT without its provider: C205.2's device test; tooling on our versions: spikes (b)–(d); accessibility under Robolectric: spike (c); Robolectric and API 37: decision 16; R8 needs: C215.2; Play Integrity without Google Play: C219.5; booting API 35 on 2 vCPUs: C220.3).
61. **The Gradle lock** (ours; review F24). Default: every agent's Gradle call under `flock -E 75 -w 240 /tmp/openverify-gradle.lock`, beside the git and e2e locks; a busy lock (exit 75 through `-E 75`, never 1, which is also a failed build's status; review V04) is a retry, never a failed gate; narrow calls for red-green, the full split gate at checkpoints.
62. **The release-age record.** Default: the pins of C214.7, each at least 14 days old on 2026-10-29 (released on or before 2026-10-15) and re-checked by each Stage 1a on its adoption day; newer releases seen during research (ONNX Runtime 1.31.1, Roborazzi 1.77.0, CameraX 1.7.0-rc01) are too young and are not used.

**Added by the review** (`phase-10-review-resolution.md`)
63. **`prefill-mrz`** (review P01). Default: served only to an SDK client (spec 2, `mobile-sdk-name` set) while its `nfc` step is current, never to the web flow, as a stated amendment of Phase 9 C151.5's P16 rule. The three values are sent, not the derived seed, because the seed is recoverable from low-entropy fields and is itself the chip's access secret. Alternative: no prefill (the person always types the MRZ), at the cost of the vendor-parity MRZ reuse (behaviour M19).
64. **`client-unable` always goes to review** (reviews P02, H11, H12). Default: a client-unable exit sends the inquiry to `needs_review` whatever `onFallback` says, unless the version carries `client-unable:<step id>`; one message per reason, `nfc_disabled_declined` among them; a publish warning for an `nfc` step without an `exhausted` edge. Alternative: follow `onFallback`, which lets any web visitor skip the chip on a template that acknowledged `continue`.
65. **One-time links never choose the API** (review P03). Default: `fromOneTimeLink` requires `apiBaseUrl`; plain-http loopback only in a debuggable host or under the SDK's own internal test override, through the `loopbackHttpAllowed` seam (C190.2; review V01). This is a 0.x signature change before the first publication.
66. **Notices, Data safety and no package visibility** (review P04). Default: `notice.session-integrity`'s new built-in text names the probes; `notice.play-integrity` names Google and is required where a `device-integrity` provider is bound; a tested wire-to-Data-safety table; no `<queries>` entry and no package-visibility probe at all. Alternative: keep `root_manager_package_declared` with a short `<queries>` list, which adds "Installed apps" to every host's Data safety form for a probe that root-hiding defeats.
67. **Dependency verification** (review P17). Default: `verification-metadata.xml` with SHA-256 for every resolved artifact and plugin, refreshed only by the agent that adds a dependency.
68. **The public surface** (review F02). Default: only C190.7's list is public, and the wire model, `FlowApi` and its client are internal before the first ABI dump, a 0.x break accepted before publication (ADR 0004 decision 6).
69. **The sandbox chip's data and anchor** (reviews P15, F27). Default: DG2 attached on the server, so no image leaves it; `face-mismatch` only where fixture faces are configured; `sandbox-chip-available` from the server; `trust:sandbox-utopia` installs the sandbox-only Utopia anchor in any mode.
70. **Overlays and sensitive views** (review P08). Default: hide overlays on API 31+, `filterTouchesWhenObscured` on API 26–30, and accessibility-data-sensitive on the views that show personal data on API 34+, never the whole tree. Alternative: the whole root view, which blocks assistive apps that do not declare themselves tools.
71. **The session's language** (reviews H15, F29). Default: the SDK sends its chosen base tag on the first session read and after a selector change, through the mechanism Phase 9 built, else a `language` query parameter on `GET /flow/session`.
72. **When Phase 10 is done** (reviews H23, V02). Default: recorded done only once the final push's `android` and `android-instrumented` jobs and the available legs of one release-matrix run on that commit are green, with run ids in PROGRESS; the 16 KB leg counts as available only if it boots on the runner P5 allows, and otherwise is recorded open with the per-push 16 KB checks green (C220.3); until then "done locally; CI pending".

**Review**

Three adversarial reviews read the first draft on 2026-10-29:
- privacy, security and law: P01–P26;
- honesty and product coverage: H01–H23;
- feasibility and fit with the code: F01–F31.

That is 80 findings: 0 CRITICAL, 17 HIGH, 47 MEDIUM and 16 LOW. `docs/plans/phase-10-review-resolution.md` disposes of each against the code at `5c6421a2` and the Phase 5, 7, 8 and 9 plans: 75 accepted, 5 accepted in part (P01, P08, P15, H03, F25) and none rejected. Ten findings raised by two lenses share one fix: P09 and H06, P11 and H21's trust-store point, P13 and H01, P16 and F12, P02 with H11 and H12, H05 and F14, H07 and F06, H09 and F15, H14 and F01, H15 and F29. Decisions 63–72 are new, and 14, 28, 31, 32, 36, 38, 46, 48, 49, 50, 56 and 61 changed in place, as did P1 and P4. The main changes:
- **Skipping the chip** (P02, H11, H12): a `client-unable` exit always goes to review unless the version carries `client-unable:<step id>`, with one honest message per reason and "a browser cannot read it" on the web (C207.9, C207.10).
- **What leaves the server and the device** (P01, P15, P03, P12):
  - `prefill-mrz` only for an SDK client at the current step, as a stated amendment of Phase 9's P16 rule;
  - no selfie bytes from the sandbox chip route;
  - one-time links that never choose the API;
  - a stored projection of the Play Integrity verdict, not the whole verdict (C207.2, C208.1, C190.2, C211.4).
- **Notices and consent** (P04, P05, P14, H07, F06, H16):
  - a new session-integrity notice and a required `notice.play-integrity`;
  - no package-visibility probe;
  - a tested Data safety table;
  - lazy Play Integrity preparation after consent;
  - consent digests over the logical text, which the server can match (display-only isolates), and called client-reported;
  - legal texts only from the web's words (C210.1, C211.9, C211.10, C213.5, C200, C199.1).
- **Never decisive alone** (P13, H01): the publish rule is defined over what a route reads, refused in flows and workflows alike, and needs `heuristic:` in every direction (C210.5).
- **The device's own protections** (P06, P07, P08, P19, P09, H06, P11):
  - sanitised exceptions, silenced dependency loggers and debuggable-only logs;
  - no PII in saved state, autofill or keyboard learning;
  - overlay and sensitive-view protections and `FLAG_SECURE` on every window;
  - a telemetry test that can fail;
  - the trust store stated as the host's, with a transport seam for Phase 11's pinning (C213, C205.2, C191.1).
- **Honest evidence** (H02, H05, H09, H18, H23):
  - SDK request fixtures parsed by the server's zod schemas, and the SDK's own client run against the stack;
  - a face acceptance the emulator can meet;
  - a JVM test of the real JMRTD service against a synthetic chip, with the owner's read in a mode that sends nothing;
  - sizes labelled by how they were measured;
  - Phase 10 recorded done only when its last push's CI is green (C224.4, C209.3, C216.3, decision 72).
- **Coverage** (H03, H04, H08, H10, H13, H15, H17, H20):
  - per-component style overrides and a member test over the schema export;
  - a complete colour mapping with a pair test;
  - non-Latin-digit device locales;
  - NFC wording that never implies a verdict;
  - registered null reasons, with `provider_not_bound` for the integrity signals;
  - the session's language sent to the server;
  - an NFC challenge that starts on "I'm ready";
  - `device_handoff` always skipped for SDK sessions, and the screen kept on.
- **Fit with the code** (F02–F05, F08–F10, F16–F28):
  - the public surface narrowed before the first ABI dump;
  - `android-driver` split, with a `FakeFlowServer` to migrate 217 tests;
  - the dispatch file and every 10B and 10C hook named, with stub owners;
  - per-owner string files and a string facade;
  - assert-only fixture exports with OpenCV-exact frames and tolerances;
  - `NfcPlatform`, `YunetOutputs` and committed PDF417 assets;
  - a screenshot budget in its own task;
  - the real release-matrix images and costs, pinned bundletool and the missing pins;
  - extended spikes with named fallbacks, a bounded Gradle lock, per-checkpoint dependencies;
  - the sandbox anchor installer, and the SCUBA fork published as its own artifact if P1 is declined.
- **Workflows:** 10A 10 agents (`android-sdk-api` added), 10B 9, 10C 8, 10D 9, 10E up to 8 (decision 56).

A verification round (`verify-round.md`, 2026-10-29) then checked the plan against each finding: 70 resolved, 10 partly resolved (P04, P06, P07, P08, P14, P16, H20, F10, F12, F24), none unresolved, and 14 new defects, V01–V14 (2 HIGH, 6 MEDIUM, 6 LOW). The resolution's "Verification round" section records the edit made for each. The counts above hold after it: P16's held-certificate TLS is now taken (V01), so P16 is accepted in full; H16's reviewer offered two alternatives, and H16 is accepted with the first, as F16 is with its second. The round added P5 (the 16 KB leg's larger runner, V02) and changed decisions 9, 50, 61 and 72 in place. Its main changes:
- **Loopback and the smoke test** (V01): an internal `loopbackHttpAllowed` seam with a test override; the minified smoke test over TLS through the transport seam (C190.2, C215.2).
- **The owner's runner** (V02): P5, with the 16 KB leg on the standard runner first and recorded open if it cannot boot (C220.3, decision 72).
- **Builders for every protection and amendment** (V03, V05, V06, V07, V08): the privacy protections with the owners of their files and the SDK manifest owned in every Workflow (C213.1); the notice publish rule, the compliance template, the console label and the handoff skip each with a named agent (C221.4, C221.9, C221.13); `FailureScreen` in its own file; Android's `en` and `es` legal texts generated from the web's words (C199.1); `api-android-10a`'s server files named by the inventory.
- **Smaller corrections** (V04, V09–V14): `flock -E 75`; `integrity_unevaluated` in `needs_review`; the missing amendments listed; `OpenVerifyAndroidTransport` frozen in 10A and OkHttp in the ABI promise; `ModelSource` inside `openverify-face`; stale text repaired; a stable-boundary rule for the organisation's name; the 10E matrix run and the telemetry minutes costed; the lock order; the anchor script, the members file's editors and ORT's selector value named.

## Orchestrator decisions before Workflow 10A (2026-11-08)

The owner (2026-11-08): "start Phase 10: refresh the Phase 10 plan and the 10A–10E scripts against Phase 9 as built, add KP10-1 and KP10-2 as known items, decide according to best practices about P1–P5, then commit the 10A decisions section, make the 10A launch copy, dry-run it and launch 10A. Make the other open decisions yourself and record them in the plan." The orchestrator decides in the owner's place and records each decision here. Taken from Phase 9 as built (`c60d51b9`, the Phase 9 PROGRESS entry, `phase-9.plan.md`'s Handovers, decision sections and 9D review amendments) and CI 42476248527 on `c60d51b9`, through five read-only refresh memos, one per Workflow (orchestrator records). Binding on Workflows 10A–10E; last and binding over every earlier section of this plan it contradicts. Later sections ("## Orchestrator decisions before Workflow 10x", "## Owner decisions after Workflow 10x") bind in turn, the newest winning.

1. **The Phase 10 base is `c60d51b9`** (PROGRESS: Phase 9 done, 2026-11-08, pushed; `e56e4e9c..c60d51b9` holds Phase 9's 1,121 commits). This section's commit is 10A's `start`. contracts-10a's inventory re-takes the Starting point at the base; the drift the refresh found, which the inventory confirms or corrects with `path:line`:
   - `FlowCoordinator.kt` is 1,422 lines; `model/Flow.kt`'s `known` map is at :439 and `uploadAllowed` at `FlowCoordinator.kt:618`. Strings: 312 `<string>`/`<plurals>` entries in seven files of `values/`. Tests: 65 host-test files (674 `@Test`, 55 parameterized), 16 `commonTest`, 4 `iosTest`. ADRs 0006–0008 exist; the pins are unchanged. `ConsentTexts` holds seven texts (with Phase 8's `consent.automated-decision`) and three checkbox labels (`ui/Messages.kt:11-34`).
   - **C219.6 and the CI floor:** the SDK host-test floor at the base is **1,366** (`ci.yml`'s floor step and the 9D run), not 1,264 or 1,327.
   - **The gates at the base:** the API suite is 9,428 tests in 692 files, run as 8 blob shards plus a merge when alone and 12 under parallel load, each coverage run with its own reports directory outside the blob directory (Vitest's merge refuses coverage folders inside it); the other unit suites are three calls plus `@openverify/js`; lint is three calls, typecheck one; integration five calls (db, graph, three api); the next migration is **0033**; the OpenAPI test compares compact JSON texts and Prettier runs only on `openapi:write` (`a7920d11`).
   - **e2e:** the full stack is four `--project=chromium --shard=i/4` calls (47, 46, 46 and 44 tests), then one `--project=console-sweep --no-deps` call (104 tests) on the same stack, then `:openverify-sdk:stackTest`, then the stack down; never `--shard` over all projects (the sweep depends on the chromium shards' objects). The stack comes up in about 90 s, in the foreground (Phase 7 Stage 4 amendment 7). The mocked flow suite is 328 tests; WebKit's theme-csp runs locally only through Playwright's browser server in Docker (9D decision 3), once per Validation. Every e2e-lock call is `flock -E 75 -w 240`.
   - **CI** has 15 job keys and 18 runs, not 14. Playwright's `globalTimeout` is 60 minutes inside the e2e job's 80.
   - **Shared resources (Rules):** Phase 9's heavy-call slot lock (`bash /tmp/openverify-heavy.sh <command>`, slots 0–3; S4B-1) holds for every Phase 10 agent; agents running beside others run the gates of what they touched and no e2e gate (decisions 2 after 8A and 1 after 8B), their own specs in one locked call; every Testcontainers RustFS starts through `startIntegrationRustfs` and Postgres through `startIntegrationPostgres`, both on `startWithRetries`.
   - **Validation:** vision splits at file 58; `workflow-docs:write` with its diff and the code-runner image join the list; the infra tests are one call each; the benchmarks are reported.
2. **Phase 10 owner items P1–P5, decided** (the owner delegated them: "decide according to best practices about P1–P5"). They are named "Phase 10 P1–P5" wherever they could be confused with the P1–P10 of Phase 9's decisions before 9D. They gate only 10E and one release-matrix leg, so each stays reversible by the owner until 10E launches; none is legal advice.
   - **P1 granted, narrowly.** SCUBA's two `GPL-2.0-only WITH Classpath-exception-2.0` files, unmodified, from Maven Central, only inside the separate optional `openverify-nfc` artifact (already the one LGPL module), with the GPL-2.0 and Classpath-exception texts and the files' source location in its `NOTICE` and `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`. Why: the Classpath exception exists so that independent modules may link those files on their own terms; the module is opt-in and already carries copyleft obligations; and the declined path's "clean-room" rewrite cannot show clean-room provenance when written by agents, which is the weaker position. The rule 4 exception's wording in `CLAUDE.md` lands with the artifact in 10E (integration-10e drafts it; the orchestrator or the owner applies it, since agents cannot edit `CLAUDE.md`).
   - **P2 granted, narrowly.** Eigen (MPL-2.0, file-level copyleft), unmodified, compiled into the official `onnxruntime-android` AAR, only inside the optional `openverify-face` artifact, with ONNX Runtime's third-party notices in the AAR and Eigen's source location (MPL-2.0 §3.2(a)). Why: MPL-2.0's obligations attach only to Eigen's own files, which ship unmodified; declining would also force the web flow, which already ships Eigen inside `onnxruntime-web`, to drop selfie auto-capture. **The same answer settles Phase 4 decision 11** for `onnxruntime-web` (and the vision image's `onnxruntime` wheel): contracts-10a's common commit records `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md:145` and `:493` as granted 2026-11-08 (Phase 10 P2), with Eigen's source location beside the flow's vendored notices; 10E adds only the `onnxruntime-android` row.
   - **P3 declined.** OpenVerify ships no Google code: no `openverify-integrity-play` artifact (C211.6). Why: the Play Integrity client is under Google's API terms, which are not a rule 4 licence; an F-Droid-clean SDK is the stronger default; and real calls need each operator's own Cloud project anyway. The README gives the host-side recipe implementing `IntegrityTokenProvider` with the host's own dependency. Unaffected: the common `IntegrityTokenProvider` seam, the server's `play-integrity` REST provider (our JWT code, an A-tier adapter under rule 5) and its deterministic sandbox, all in 10B.
   - **P4 pending (an owner account step).** The namespace, Central Portal account and token, GPG key and the `maven-central` environment (a paid plan on a private repository) need the owner. Meanwhile the release is the staged local repository, validated and uploaded as a workflow artifact (C217.3); group `dev.openverify` is provisional (one constant), the Kotlin packages stay `dev.openverify.*`, and the publish job stays off.
   - **P5 declined.** No larger runner: the 16 KB emulator leg runs on the standard runner with a 900 s boot budget and AVD snapshot caching; if the API 35 `google_apis_ps16k` image cannot boot there, the leg is a release-gate open item and decision 72 counts the API 26 and 34 legs with the per-push 16 KB checks (zipalign, bundletool's `PAGE_ALIGNMENT_16K`, the ELF `p_align` scan) green. The CI-minutes budget is the reason; making the repository public (decision 50) stays the owner's alternative.
   - So 10D's `args.p5` is `declined`, and 10E's `args.owner` is `{"P1":"granted","P2":"granted","P3":"declined","P4":"pending","P5":"declined"}`.
3. **Eleven agents in 10A (decision 56 amended).** **`carry-10a`** runs beside Stage 1b to Stage 3, after Stage 1a, and owns the known items below. Its paths (`e2e/`, `infra/`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s e2e upload and gitleaks steps, `apps/flow/src/selfie/` and `steps/selfie-capture-step.tsx`, `apps/dashboard/src/app/workflows/[id]/draft/`, `apps/api/src/services/themes/assets.ts`, `packages/flow-spec/src/locale-review-status.ts`, `apps/api/scripts/analytics-benchmark.ts`) are disjoint from every Android agent's and from `api-android-10a`'s, and it touches no 10A shared file; a file another 10A agent owns is a named borrowed edit or a finding for that agent. Its evidence goes in `docs/tdd/phase10-carry-10a.tdd.md`. Why beside, not after: the owner asked to parallelize, KP10-1 and KP10-3 are load races that load reproduces, KP10-3 must land before 10B's selfie port copies its rule, and integration-10a is already the heaviest agent. If `carry-10a` commits nothing, integration-10a receives its items.
4. **Known items: eight** (the launch's `KNOWN`): seven for `carry-10a`, each fixed test-first (RED for the intended reason, then GREEN), never by a retry, a raised limit or a skipped engine:
   - **KP10-1 (MEDIUM, e2e).** CI's `ui-a11y-focus.spec.ts:55` (2.4.11 with a toast on screen) fails while it passes locally. Likely cause, not proven: `ToastProvider` moves the stack one animation frame after `focusin` (`apps/dashboard/src/components/toasts.tsx`), while `coveredWhileTabbing` (`e2e/tests/support/ui-a11y.ts`) reads coverage straight after the Tab. RED: reproduce under Chromium CPU throttling or by checking before two frames have passed. GREEN: let the check settle (two `requestAnimationFrame`s, or `expect.poll` held to a short bound) so that lasting coverage still fails; if a reproduction shows real coverage, fix the placement instead. Also: the e2e job uploads its Playwright traces and reports on failure (`include-hidden-files: true` for `e2e/.artifacts`, which `upload-artifact` skips as a hidden directory by default), proven by the CI workflow test.
   - **KP10-2 (MEDIUM, CI/infra).** `infra/scripts/install-gitleaks.sh` fails the infra and secrets jobs on one 5xx from GitHub's release host (about ten failures in two days). RED in `infra/tests/install-gitleaks.test.sh`, deterministic and offline (a curl shim or a loopback-only test base URL answering 504 first); GREEN: bounded retries on 5xx and timeouts (`curl --retry 5 --retry-all-errors --retry-delay 2 --retry-max-time 120 --connect-timeout 20`), keeping the pinned version, the release checksums check and the pinned SHA-256. Optionally CI caches the verified tarball with `actions/cache`, keyed on version and hash, and the cache hit re-verifies the hash.
   - **KP10-3 (MEDIUM, flow).** `e2e/tests/flow-bundle.spec.ts` failed once under load: the web selfie's auto-capture uploads the live frame after the detector's latency, not the frame the guidance judged (`apps/flow/src/selfie/use-guidance.ts`, `steps/selfie-capture-step.tsx`, `lib/capture-frame.ts`). RED: a unit test whose fake video frame changes between the sample and the capture; GREEN: upload the judged frame's full-resolution copy on an auto-capture (the manual shutter keeps the live frame; no frame kept after the upload).
   - **K9-7 (MEDIUM, console).** The workflow draft page passes no `modules` list to `DraftEditor`, so the palette never offers a module; it needs a read that says which workflows start with `module-input`.
   - **R002, R004, R008 (LOW, 9D review).** The flow's theme-asset read is scoped to the session's environment, not to the assets of the theme that session renders; `effectiveReviewStatus` is unused by production code; the analytics benchmark's refresh step dirties only inquiries.
   - **K9-4 (deferred, not fixed in Phase 10).** The drag-and-drop screen canvas and per-component style overrides. The overrides need a `style` member in flow-spec 2's component schema (C148.2), a contract change across the web renderer, the contrast gate, the console's Design tab and Android; no Phase 10 acceptance line needs it, and the keyboard and single-pointer editor 2.5.7 requires exists. Decided with the canvas in a later console slice. Consequence: **C198.9 and C194.2's style-override row are dormant**: Android maps no per-component override and `flow-spec-members.json` records the member as absent. integration-10a lists K9-4 under "Carried forward".
5. **The session's language (C193.9, C221.12, decision 71).** Phase 9's mechanism for every client is the consent's `locale` (the engine's `session.locale`, `flow-engine/scope.ts:266`); 10A adds no `language` query parameter and no stored per-session locale (no migration). A selector change after consent is not reported, as on the hosted flow, and `docs/android/` says so.
6. **`organization-display-name` (C200.2, C221.12; Phase 9's P5 before 9D deferred it to Phase 10).** contracts-10a freezes it as an optional, nullable flow-session member (1–100 characters, NFC, the environment's display-name setting) and api-android-10a serves it; it is additive, so today's clients decode it. The built-in consent texts hold no `{{organization}}`, so only custom copy needs it. Phase 9's other deferrals from that P5: the font's `unicode-range` (TH5) has no Android consequence (decision 11 keeps the system font) and stays deferred with operator fonts on native clients; `ProblemMembers` lists stay deferred (C223).
7. **The handoff skip (C221.13).** Nothing in `apps/api/src/flow-engine/` calls `skipsHandoff` (`services/handoff/steps.ts:94`) at the base, so the engine change is required, not conditional.
8. **The consent record's console label (C221.9, C200.6).** No console view or API shows a consent record's `client` or digests at the base, so the "client-reported" label is carried until a consent-record view exists; meanwhile `docs/android/` and the API reference state it. The preview's list of differences is `preview-banner.tsx` with `lib/copy/preview.ts:20-29`.
9. **Phase 9's P7–P9 before 9D** (the theme asset list route, device dimensions on rollups, editing case-action `placement` and `visible-when`) are console features no Phase 10 line needs: deferred past Phase 10 with K9-4, recorded in Phase 10's "Carried forward".
10. **CI at the 9D push** (CI 42476248527 on `c60d51b9`, minutes of the timeout): unit-api shards 18.4, 19.9 and 19.0 of 40; unit-dashboard 10.8 and 11.5 of 20 (the highest, 58%); node 13.4 of 25; unit-db 12.2 of 25; lint 10.3 of 30; integration 10.2 of 30; vision 9.3 of 30; android 9.3 of 45; android-instrumented 9.8 of 60; format 1.7; secrets 0.2; the coverage merges 0.6 and 0.7. No job is inside the last 25% of its timeout, so no timeout or shard changes. Two failures: infra (KP10-2) and e2e at 29.4 of 80 (KP10-1; the full stack's 182 tests ran in 20.5 minutes, so the sweep's CI time is still unmeasured). 10A's push is their CI proof; no dispatch runs.
11. **Decisions for 10B–10E taken now** (from their refresh memos; each Workflow's own decisions section may refine them after the Workflow before it as built):
    - Everywhere: `stackTest` runs after the console sweep, on the same stack, so the sweep sees exactly what the chromium shards made. One Docker WebKit run per Validation. The heavy-slot lock and the parallel-gate rule as in item 1.
    - **10B:** the file resource serves both `capture-method` and `capture-trigger`; the shared decisive-alone predicate lives in `packages/core`; api-integrity runs its own packages' gates and no e2e gate; the strictly-necessary assessment is a new `docs/compliance/session-integrity.md`; foundation-10b takes slot 0; the publish rules' borrowed edits name `packages/flow-spec/src/v2/analyses.ts` and `packages/workflow-engine/src/graph/analysis.ts`, and `apps/dashboard/src/lib/signal-words.ts` gains the new signals' words.
    - **10C:** the new nfc members are camelCase (`fallbackAvailable`, `sandboxChipAvailable`, `prefillMrz`), as every `step` member is; `client-unable` takes the author's `exhausted` fallback through a new `FlowExit` kind and a settlement hook that contracts-10c adds to the shared engine (Phase 9's `leaveIfDecided` takes `exhausted` only with no attempts left), with api-nfc owning the stubs and a new acknowledgement kind `client-unable` in the flow-spec analyses and the template contracts; simulate lists `client-unable` as a third known difference rather than simulating it; contracts-10c checks that `origin/main` contains the PROGRESS entry's commit instead of a hash written inside it.
    - **10D:** a tenth agent, `validation-10d`, runs the Validation list alone and last (as 9D's); a verifier that returns nothing stops the Workflow instead of failing closed; the parallel fixers run scoped gates; `args.manualAudit` is accepted; root config files are `fix-records`'; the accessibility statement's records test gains a borrowed edit to read `android-audit-*.md`; `fix-records`' clean-tree check ignores the owner's untracked note; integration-10c writes a "### Handover to 10D".
    - **10E:** six agents with the P1–P5 states of item 2 (contracts-10e, then `face-onnx` beside `nfc-reader`, the two reviewers, integration-10e); a guard that all five owner keys are passed; `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` gains a section for owner exceptions in shipped code; no Android NDK is installed, so ONNX Runtime's custom-build spike is recorded "not measured" (decision 22's official AAR stands); Docker WebKit and the benchmarks run only if 10E's diff touches what they cover.
12. **Still the owner's:** the manual accessibility pass (14 rows in `docs/accessibility/audit-2026-11-07.md`, a release gate) and Phase 10's manual TalkBack, font-scale and keyboard pass (decision 47); a real-document NFC read (decision 48); `logicalPropertiesConfig()` in the root `eslint.config.js`; the `CLAUDE.md` wording of 9A amendment 15 and of item 2's two exceptions; the fluent review of the es, fr, de and ar catalogs; P4's accounts.

## Workflow 10A Stage 1a amendments (contracts-10a)

Recorded by `contracts-10a` on 2026-11-08 at the base `c60d51b9` (10A decisions `c7bc6f49`), from `docs/research/phase10-inventory.md` and what Stage 1a froze. Binding on 10A's later stages; each names the contract it amends.

1. **Starting point (drift).** The base has 167 explicitly `public` declaration lines in `commonMain` and `androidMain` plus three top-level declarations with no modifier (`Mrz`, `BacKeySeed`, `OpenVerifyTheme`), not 161. Every other row holds as the 10A decisions corrected it (1,422 lines, `known` at `Flow.kt:439`, `uploadAllowed` at `:618`, 312 strings in seven files, 1,366 host tests and floor). CI 42476248527 failed only on KP10-1 and KP10-2.
2. **Spike (a), C199.4.** Compose Multiplatform 1.11.1 has **no** public per-composition locale: `LocalComposeEnvironment`, `ComposeEnvironment` and `ResourceEnvironment`'s constructor are Kotlin-internal and `LanguageQualifier` is `@InternalResourceApi`; an overridden `LocalConfiguration` does not change what `stringResource` resolves. android-theme builds C199.4's fallback: generated per-locale tables behind `sdkString` and `sdkPlural`, with CLDR plural rules for the five locales and `ResourcesTest` proving both paths agree.
3. **The chip seam's iOS fit, C207.3 (review H22).** NFCPassportReader accepts an external Active Authentication challenge (`aaChallenge`) and the MRZ key, but not a CAN, and the version read is its default branch, not a tag named 2.3.3; ADR 0009 says the fit is unverified. `@ExperimentalOpenVerifyApi` (level `ERROR`) marks **`ChipReader.read` and every chip value type**, not the `ChipReader` interface: marking the interface made every `OpenVerifyComponents(...)` call need the opt-in, since Kotlin checks a constructor's parameter types even with `@OptIn` on the constructor. Implementing or calling `read` still needs it.
4. **C190.7 and C191.1 amended (the public list).** Public, beyond the plan's names: `SandboxSignalOutcome` (`BOT`, `HUMAN`; owner decisions after Workflow 7A, "Later phases", C90.9) and `OpenVerifyComponents.sandboxSignalOutcome`; the events' `CaptureFailureReason` and `InquiryResultKind`; `ChipDeviceReport.Attempt`, `.Protocol` and `.Outcome` (nested, mirroring C62.5's `device-report`); the public members `InquiryStatus.wireValue` and `FileKind.wireValue` (the demo reads the first; `WireValue` itself is internal). The seams live in `dev.openverify.sdk.chip`, `dev.openverify.sdk.face` and `dev.openverify.sdk.integrity`; the registry, events, `Appearance`, `FlowTransport`, `SandboxSignalOutcome` and `ExperimentalOpenVerifyApi` in `dev.openverify.sdk`. `android/openverify-sdk/api/expected-public.txt` lists them; `PublicSurfaceTest` holds its top-level lines to the sources until foundation-10a's ABI dump holds every line.
5. **Shapes that differ from the plan's sketches.** `FlowTransport` is `public abstract class FlowTransport internal constructor()`: "an opaque interface a host does not implement" cannot be an interface in Kotlin without a sealed hierarchy across source sets; the internal constructor gives the same guarantee. `IntegrityTokenProvider` is a plain interface (a `fun interface` cannot hold a `suspend` member). `BacKeySeed` is a value class (`BacKeySeed(documentNumber, dateOfBirth, dateOfExpiry)`, redacted text, `mrzInformation`), keeping `BacKeySeed.mrzInformation(…)` on its companion so Phase 5's tests are unchanged. `ChipAccess.Mrz(seed)` and `ChipAccess.Can(can)` are redacted; `ChipReadException(failure)` has the fixed message `Chip read failed: <failure>`.
6. **C190.2's link rule, staged.** `fromOneTimeLink(url, apiBaseUrl, locale = null, appearance = SYSTEM)` is the public signature, `apiBaseUrl` required. The Phase 2 one-argument reader stays as an `internal` overload for its tests only; android-sdk-api removes it when it writes C190.2's tests (checkpoint 1). `Request` and `OneTimeLink` carry `locale` and `appearance` and show them in their text; validating `locale` (BCP 47, at most 35 characters) and the intent extras are android-sdk-api's. Two exact-text assertions (`OpenVerifyInquiryRequestTest`, `OpenVerifyInquiryOneTimeLinkTest`) gained `, locale=null, appearance=SYSTEM`.
7. **(R) amended: the theme-asset fetch.** `FlowApi.fetchThemeAsset(assetId): ByteArray` (`GET /api/v1/flow/theme-assets/:id`, C198.3) joins `submitScreen(stepId, values: Map<String, FlowValue>, button: String?): FlowSession` and `extendSelfieCapture(captureId): FlowSelfieCapture`. In `FlowApiClient` each throws `NotImplementedError("flow_api_not_built: <name>")`; `FlowSpecHeaderTest` proves no production path calls them. `FakeFlowApi` answers all three.
8. **The header.** `FlowHttp` sends `OpenVerify-Flow-Spec: 2` on every request it makes, the one-time-link redemption included (C193.1). The server then also serves `step` and `values`; today's coordinator ignores them and still walks `flow` until android-driver's checkpoint 1. A `screen` in `flow.steps` now decodes as `ScreenStep`, and the coordinator keeps it unsupported, as before.
9. **The member export (C194.4).** Phase 9's export of client-view members is a new file, `contracts/fixtures/flow-spec/client-view-members.json` (`{format, description, session: {member: [own members]}, steps: {type: [members]}, components: {kind: [members]}}`), written from the zod schemas by the assert-only `packages/contracts/src/client-view-members.export.test.ts` (rule 21). `android/openverify-sdk/flow-spec-members.json` (schema `flow-spec-members.schema.json`) treats each member; a later Stage 1a that adds a member re-runs the export with `UPDATE_CONTRACT_FIXTURES=1` and adds its row in the same commit. Session members classified: `handoff` ignored and `handoff-context` server-only (the server skips `device_handoff` for every SDK session, C221.13), `embed` and `completion` ignored, `flow` ignored once the driver lands; `links` and `organization-display-name` are members added after spec 2 and are **not** appearance-only (each changes what a person is told).
10. **C199.1 (L) amended: two app wordings, not one, and where they live.** Two SDK legal texts differ from the web's words: the session-integrity notice (the plan's case) and `consent.processing` ("closing this screen" against "this page"). Both, and the three optional-consent checkbox labels, joined the five web catalogs as the group **`nativeApp`** (`nativeApp.consent.processing`, `nativeApp.notice.session-integrity`, `nativeApp.consent.{biometric-matching,device-recognition,automated-decision}-checkbox`), not `COPY_KEYS`: they are words, not operator-overridable keys; the web flow never shows the group, which is not a consent or notice group, so the web catalog version and `consent-texts.json` did not change. The key map's entry carries `flowKey` (`consent.processing`, `notice.session-integrity`; `null` for the checkbox labels, which no operator copy can target and no digest covers) and `legal` (COPY_KEYS' mark, or `consent` for a checkbox label). The owner confirms the app wordings later; no build step waits.
11. **`strings_catalog.xml` in `values` and `values-es` only, for now.** The fourteen key-mapped strings moved unchanged. `values-fr`, `values-de` and `values-ar` receive theirs from android-i18n's generator together with each locale's full catalog and `openverify_locale`: a folder with only the consent texts would make the consent screen record `openverify_locale = en` beside French words.
12. **`review-status.json`'s shape.** `{format, description, locales: {<locale>: <Phase 9 status>}, keys: {<resource>: {file}}}`; a `reviewed` (and a downgraded `partially-reviewed`) record also holds `keySha256` (each key's SHA-256 of its NFC text as reviewed), which android-i18n's downgrade test compares. An agent adding strings adds only `{file}` rows (rule 20).
13. **C193.9, D2.** No `language` query is frozen: the consent body's `locale` is the mechanism (`flow-engine/scope.ts:266`), as the 10A decisions say.
14. **Problems.** `FlowProblemCodes` gains `FLOW_STEP_NOT_CURRENT`, `CONSENT_TEXT_MISMATCH` and `SELFIE_CAPTURE_NOT_EXTENDABLE`, and `ContractFixturesTest` now reads `problems/flow-engine/` (six golden problems with their codes and statuses).
15. **Existing tests changed by the narrowing, with no assertion changed:** `FlowApiClientTest`, `CameraFactsTest` and `ContractFixturesTest` are `internal class` (a public member of each names a now-internal type). The demo's preview carries `@Suppress("ViewModelConstructorInComposable")`, which lint asked for once the theme became the demo's own composable.
16. **Phase 4 decision 11 recorded.** `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md:145` and `:493` record Eigen in `onnxruntime-web` as granted 2026-11-08 (Phase 10 P2), with Eigen's source location.

## Workflow 10A Stage 1b amendments (foundation-10a)

Recorded by `foundation-10a` on 2026-11-08 (its commits `85ea12cb..b14f5a0e`; evidence in `docs/tdd/phase10-foundation-10a.tdd.md`, the spikes in `docs/research/phase10-inventory.md` §8). Binding on 10A's later stages and on 10B–10E's Android agents; each names the contract it amends or settles.

1. **The Android gate's split (rule 17, C220.1, Validation).** At the base, rule 17's six calls each fit 10 minutes (the longest 201 s from scratch at load 7 to 13). With three agents' TypeScript gates beside them (load 25 to 40), `testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug :demo-app:assembleRelease :demo-app:bundleRelease` took 448 s and `:openverify-sdk:assemble lint` 326 s, which with the 240 s lock wait would pass 10 minutes. **The split every later agent uses**, each its own foreground call under the Gradle lock: (a) `:openverify-sdk:testAndroidHostTest` (1,555 tests; 83 s at load 3, 306 s at load 25), or, once it passes 300 s, its three groups `-PhostTestGroup=ui`, `flow` and `rest` (227 + 368 + 960 at checkpoint 3; the sum is the count); (b) `:openverify-sdk:screenshotTest -Proborazzi.test.verify=true`; (c) `koverVerify checkKotlinAbi`; (d1) `testDebugUnitTest assembleDebug` (94 s from scratch at load 3); (d2) `:demo-app:assembleRelease :demo-app:bundleRelease` (125 s); (e1) `:openverify-sdk:assemble` (109 s; it runs the boundary check); (e2) `lint` (179 s); (f) `:openverify-sdk:assembleAndroidDeviceTest :demo-app:assembleAndroidTest` (84 s from scratch). Any call past 300 s is split again by the same rule.
2. **Spike (b), C214.2 settled.** KGP 2.4.21's `abiValidation` (experimental; the block itself enables it, `enabled.set(true)` no longer compiles) covers `:openverify-sdk` on `com.android.kotlin.multiplatform.library`: `api/android/openverify-sdk.api` (the Android target) and `api/openverify-sdk.klib.api` (the iOS klibs). The Compose compiler's `ComposableSingletons$<File>Kt` classes and `$stableprop`/`$stableprop_getter` declarations are excluded by name (`**.ComposableSingletons**`, `**$stableprop`, `**$stableprop_getter`): they are not API, and their names change with every composable. KGP does **not** cover `openverify-nfc` (AGP 9's built-in-Kotlin `com.android.library`: no variant reaches its dump task), nor does BCV 0.18.2's plugin (it registers nothing without `org.jetbrains.kotlin.android`); BCV 0.18.2's own `KotlinApiBuildTask` and `KotlinApiCompareTask`, wired by hand over `compileReleaseKotlin`'s output and registered under KGP's names, do. **`$ABI_CHECKS` is `checkKotlinAbi`** (both modules); `updateKotlinAbi` rewrites the dumps. Never run the two in one build (Gradle refuses the implicit dependency). Every later optional module (`openverify-face`, 10E) copies `openverify-nfc`'s wiring if it is a plain `com.android.library`.
3. **The ABI dump and the list (C214.2, review F02).** `AbiDumpTest` holds the Android and iOS dumps to `api/expected-public.txt`, nested lines included: a companion is a member of its class, as a function is, and the iOS dump lacks only the three androidMain declarations (`OpenVerifyInquiry`, `OpenVerifyAndroid`, `OpenVerifyAndroidTransport`). **Finding for contracts-10b/10c and integration-10a:** `FileKind` and `InquiryStatus` are public `@Serializable` enums, so their companions publish `serializer(): KSerializer`, a kotlinx.serialization type, in the ABI; a later Stage 1a may make their wire serializers internal (a custom serializer in `model/`) with the dump regenerated in the same commit.
4. **Spike (c), C212.2 and C219.3 settled.** Roborazzi 1.76.0's plugin applies on the KMP library plugin, but its tasks drive `testAndroidHostTest`, which must exclude the screenshots, so the build takes C219.3's runtime-library route: `roborazzi`, `roborazzi-compose`, `roborazzi-junit-rule` and `roborazzi-accessibility-check` in `androidHostTest`, no Roborazzi plugin. `:openverify-sdk:screenshotTest` runs the JUnit tag `dev.openverify.sdk.testing.ScreenshotTests` (a JUnit 4 `@Category`), compares unless `-Proborazzi.test.record=true` (so a missing golden fails), declares the goldens as inputs, and never caches a record call. Compose's own `enableAccessibilityChecks()` raises nothing under Robolectric; Roborazzi's module runs Google's Accessibility Test Framework 4.1.1 under Robolectric and fails an unlabelled 10 dp target. So **the accessibility checks run on every golden on the host** (C212.2's first route) through `verifyGolden`/`checkAccessibility` (ATF's latest preset, failing on its errors, its warnings printed), and `ui-test-junit4-accessibility` is a device-test dependency only.
5. **Tooling adopted (C214.7, rule 3).** Roborazzi 1.76.0 (2026-09-29), ATF 4.1.1 (2024-03-29), `ui-test-junit4-accessibility` 1.11.4 (2026-07-01), BCV 0.18.2 (2026-09-02) with `java-diff-utils` 4.12 and `kotlin-metadata-jvm` 2.4.21 (2026-10-08), all Apache-2.0, each date read from its POM's publication metadata; their transitive test-scope modules (differ 0.3.0, protobuf-javalite 3.19.1 BSD-3-Clause, jsoup 1.15.1 and checker-qual 3.22.1 MIT, AndroidX view libraries and Material 1.2.0-rc01) in `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` §2.1.17. `kotlinx-io-core` 0.9.1 and `skiko` 0.144.6 are declared at the versions already resolved (C214.1).
6. **The state hooks (review F04).** `FlowCoordinator.kt` calls: `ServerPosition.drives(session)`/`stateOf(session)` in `load()` (`flow/ServerPosition.kt`, android-driver; stub: never drives, `Unsupported("position")`); `consentEvidence(step, optionalTextKeys, locale, session): ConsentEvidence` in `sendConsent` (`consent/ConsentDigests.kt`, android-sdk-api; stub: `ConsentEvidence.NONE`); `SessionTheme.of(session): FlowTheme?` in `adopt` (`ui/theme/SessionTheme.kt`, android-theme; stub: `null`, the built-in theme), published as `FlowCoordinator.theme: StateFlow<FlowTheme?>`. **C200.1 seam, amended:** `FlowApi.acceptConsent` gained `evidence: ConsentEvidence = ConsentEvidence.NONE`, which `FlowApiClient` encodes as the body's `text-sha256`, `copy-source` and `catalog-version`, each left out when absent, so android-sdk-api fills only its own file. android-theme reads `theme` through the view model (android-driver's `InquiryViewModel.kt` exposes it when the driver lands; the integration agent adds the line if not).
7. **The build tasks (review F18).** `generateLocaleStrings` and `exportConsentTexts` are registered in `openverify-sdk/build.gradle.kts` and configured by their owners' scripts (`scripts/generate-locale-strings.gradle.kts`, android-i18n; `scripts/export-consent-texts.gradle.kts`, api-android-10a), applied when present, else the task fails "not written yet". **Finding for android-i18n:** `generateLocaleStrings` writes `strings_catalog.xml` under `src/`, which Compose's resource tasks read, so it cannot run in the same build as a compilation (Gradle refuses the implicit dependency); run it alone, or declare `mustRunAfter` in its script. `stackTest` (JUnit tags `dev.openverify.sdk.testing.StackTests` and `stack`; `failOnNoDiscoveredTests = false` until integration-10a's first stack test, which removes it) and `screenshotTest` are registered; `testAndroidHostTest` excludes all three tags; Kover's instrumentation is off for both.
8. **Kover (C219.2, review F18).** Each owner's exclusions live in its own file, `android/openverify-sdk/kover-excludes/<owner>.txt` (android-driver, android-sdk-api, android-screens, android-theme, android-logic, android-i18n, integration-10a; a class pattern per line), read by the shared build file; the shared build file is never edited for an exclusion.
9. **The version (C214.3).** `android/gradle.properties`: `openverifyVersion=0.1.0`; `generateSdkVersion` writes `internal const val OPENVERIFY_SDK_VERSION` into `commonMain`; `FlowHttp.SDK_VERSION` is that constant; `SdkVersionTest` holds the property, the constant and the `User-Agent` together. `exportConsentTexts` reads the same property.
10. **`SdkLog` (C213.3).** `internal object SdkLog { fun log(code: String) }` (a lower-case dotted constant, at most 64 characters) over `internal expect fun platformLog(code: String)`; Android writes `Log.w("OpenVerify", code)` only when `HostDebuggable.debuggable`, iOS nothing. **The SDK logs nothing until `OpenVerifyInquiryActivity` calls `HostDebuggable.read(applicationContext)`** in `onCreate`: android-sdk-api adds that line (its file) with its `LoopbackPolicy` actual, and adds that actual's path to `NeverReadTest.DEBUGGABLE_READERS`.
11. **The scans (C213.2, C213.3; review V03).** `privacy/LogScanTest` (allowlist: `Log` in `log/PlatformLog.android.kt` only) and `privacy/DataAtRestScanTest` (storage allowlist none; saved state exactly `ConsentScreen.kt`'s `agreed` and `ticked` and `CameraPermission.android.kt`'s `deniedState`, by file and variable), over the SDK's main sources and each optional module's `src/main` (`privacy/SourceScan`). A builder that needs saved state for non-PII UI state names it as a contractDeviation for `privacy-checks` (10C), which finalises both lists; nobody else edits them.
12. **NeverReadTest (rule 22, the named borrowed edit).** `ANDROID_ID`, `isDebuggerConnected` and `smallestScreenWidthDp` keep their one reader; `FLAG_DEBUGGABLE` has the exact reader list `DEBUGGABLE_READERS` (`signals/AndroidDeviceSignals.kt`, `log/PlatformLog.android.kt`), and the log writer's source names no API, signal, model or flow package, `FlowApi`, `sendSignals`, `HttpClient`, `Intent`, `Bundle` or `SharedPreferences`.
13. **The boundary (C214.6).** `:openverify-sdk:checkDependencyBoundary`, run by `:openverify-sdk:assemble` and `check`, fails on an optional artifact (project or module `openverify-nfc`, `openverify-face`, `openverify-integrity-play`) or the groups `org.jmrtd`, `net.sf.scuba`, `org.ejbca.cvc`, `org.bouncycastle`, `com.microsoft.onnxruntime`, `com.google.android.play` in `androidRuntimeClasspath` or the two iOS klib classpaths. `contracts-10c` extends this check (its extension point) rather than adding another.
14. **Locking and verification (C214.5, decision 67).** `allprojects { dependencyLocking { lockAllConfigurations() } }` (default lock mode) with `openverify-sdk/gradle.lockfile`, `openverify-nfc/gradle.lockfile`, `demo-app/gradle.lockfile` and `settings-gradle.lockfile`; `android/gradle/verification-metadata.xml` holds the SHA-256 of 745 components (metadata verified, signatures not). **Whoever adds a dependency, in the same commit:** `./gradlew --no-daemon resolveAndLockAll --write-locks` (each project resolves its own configurations' graphs), then the gate's calls with `--write-verification-metadata sha256` **in a scratch worktree**, then the gate with verification on, then copy the lockfiles and the metadata into the main tree: a partial file in the shared tree fails every other agent's build. The iOS link configurations, which Linux never creates, stay unlocked (C223). Robolectric's `android-all-instrumented:16-robolectric-13921718-i7` is fetched by Robolectric itself (checked against its `.sha512`) and the Kotlin/Native prebuilt is unpacked into `~/.konan` after Gradle resolves (and verifies) it.
15. **CI minutes (C220.1).** At the base (CI 42476248527): `android` 9.3 of 45 minutes (its Gradle step 8 min 22 s), `android-instrumented` 9.8 of 60 (the emulator step 8 min 52 s). 10A adds to the `android` job the ABI check, the screenshot task and the boundary check; integration-10a measures them on the push. **Risk for integration-10a:** the emulator job resolves AGP's test-platform runner, `com.android.tools.utp:gradle-work-action` 32.4.1, whose checksum is in the metadata; any other artifact it resolves only with a device attached would fail verification there (no device runs locally), and its fix is the metadata entry from that job's verification report, test-first in 10B's Stage 1, never a lenient mode.

## Workflow 10A Stage 2 amendments

Recorded by `integration-10a` on 2026-11-09 from the Stage 2 agents' reports (`docs/tdd/phase10-<agent>.tdd.md` holds each one's evidence). Binding on 10B–10E; each names the contract it amends.

### android-logic

1. **C199.2, the language order.** The chooser takes the person's selector choice **before** `Request.locale`, then the device's locales, then `locales.default`, then `en` (`chooseLanguage(selected, requested, device, locales)`, `i18n/LanguageChoice.kt`). C199.2's literal order would make the selector (C199.3) do nothing whenever a host passes a locale; the web flow's selector also overrides its `language` parameter.
2. **C198.2, the surface-container roles.** `surfaceContainer` and `surfaceDim` take `canvas`; `surfaceContainerLow`, `High`, `Highest`, `Lowest`, `surfaceBright` and `surfaceVariant` take `surface`, because Material 3 1.4.0 draws `primary` text on them and the gate checks `primary` on `surface` only; `surfaceTint` is `surface`; `inversePrimary` is `surface` on `inverseSurface` = `ink`; the fixed roles take `canvas` under `ink`; `scrim` is black. `ThemeContrastTest` states every pair drawn (its 34 rows). `errorContainer`: see the Stage 4 amendments (integration-10a, item 2).
3. **C198.1, C198.5: the fail-closed check lives in the mapping.** `mappedThemeOf` replaces a session theme whose light palette, dark palette (when sent) or radius breaks its contract with the built-in theme for the whole session (`MappedTheme.fellBack`); a forced `DARK` with no session dark palette draws the built-in dark one; `SYSTEM` follows the web's `chooseVariant`. Radius maps onto `Shapes` as the web's scale around `rounded-md` (r−2, r, r, r+2, r+6), with the built-in radius 6.
4. **C199.6, C199.7: Kotlin mirrors of the run-time tables.** `COPY_KEY_RULES` is held to `contracts/fixtures/i18n/copy-keys.json` by `CopyKeysFixtureTest`, and `SDK_COPY_KEYS` (the key map's reverse) to `key-map.json` by `SdkCopyKeysTest`. Whoever adds a key-map entry with a `flowKey` adds its `SDK_COPY_KEYS` row in the same commit.
