## Workflow 10C Stage 1a amendments (contracts-10c)

Recorded by `contracts-10c` on 2026-11-10 at the base `f71b0f6d` (10C decisions `4e2a0c32`), from `docs/research/phase10-inventory.md` section 10 and what Stage 1a froze. Evidence: `docs/tdd/phase10-contracts-10c.tdd.md`. Binding on 10C's later stages; each names the contract it amends.

1. **The base.** CI 42762952128 on `f71b0f6d` failed only in `android-instrumented`, on KP10-21 and KP10-22 (carry-10c's). Owner items as recorded: P1 and P2 granted narrowly, P3 and P5 declined, P4 pending.
2. **C207.2 confirmed.** A chip challenge taken on an `initiated` verification before any chip file is created, stays open and is accepted at submit (`apps/api/src/services/chip-challenges/challenge-before-upload.integration.test.ts`). No change to Phase 5.
3. **C194.4, C151.7: the three nfc members ride on spec 2.** `fallbackAvailable`, `sandboxChipAvailable` and `prefillMrz` are camelCase members of a client view of their own, `nfcClientViewSchema` (`nfcStepSchema.safeExtend`, `packages/flow-spec/src/v2/schemas/client-view.ts`); the v1 `nfcStepSchema`, which validates flows, is unchanged. None is appearance-only. No spec version changes: no client released or built before 10C renders an `nfc` step. `prefillMrz` is `{documentNumber, birthDate, expiryDate}` as the MRZ holds them (dates YYMMDD), PII-marked.
4. **F26: who fills which member.** `clientViewOf(step, flow?)` gained an optional second argument, the flow's edges, and fills `fallbackAvailable` for an `nfc` step when it is given. `flowSessionMembers` passes the flow and merges what `nfcSessionMembers(db, {inquiry, step, sessionId, environmentKind, verifications})` (`apps/api/src/services/flow-views/nfc.ts`, api-nfc's) answers. `FlowSessionMembersInput` gained optional `sessionId` and `environmentKind`; api-nfc reads the stored session's `mobile-sdk-name` itself.
5. **C207.9: the client-unable contract.** Body `{reason}`; answer `{verification-id, status: 'canceled'}` (200). Platforms a note may name: `android`, `ios`, `web` (`CLIENT_UNABLE_PLATFORMS`).
6. **C207.9: the engine's two hooks, and no separate settlement rule.** `FlowExit` gained `{kind: 'client-unable', verificationId, reason, platform}`. `exits.ts` asks `clientUnableLeave({stepId, onFallback, hasExhaustedEdge, acknowledgements, reason, platform})` and walks the port it names; it moves nothing when the hook answers `null`, when the step has no `exhausted` edge, or when the current step is not an `nfc` step. The always-review rule is `flowOutcome`'s existing fallback rule: a step left through `exhausted` without the note `fallback-continue` sends the inquiry to review. So api-nfc writes `fallback-continue` only where the version carries `client-unable:<step id>` and the step's `onFallback` is `continue`. What was missing is a decided outcome for the step (a canceled attempt reads `pending`, and a completion never settles over a pending step), so `decideOutcome` reads the outcomes through `clientUnableStepOutcomes(record, outcomes)`. Both stubs are in `apps/api/src/services/client-unable.ts`.
7. **C207.9: the step record's note, no migration.** `FLOW_STEP_NOTES.clientUnable` is `client-unable`; the reason and platform ride as `client-unable-reason:<reason>` and `client-unable-platform:<platform>` (`CLIENT_UNABLE_NOTE_PREFIXES`). `inquiry_flow_steps.notes` is `text[]` with no format constraint.
8. **C208.1: migration 0034, the Workflow's one.** `verifications` has no metadata column, so `sandbox-chip: <scenario>` is recorded on the challenge the chip was built for: `chip_challenges.sandbox_scenario` (nullable, held to the five scenarios by `chip_challenges_sandbox_scenario_valid`), written by `setChipChallengeSandboxScenario(tx, environmentId, {id, verificationId, scenario})` on the verification's open challenge only, and served as `ChipChallenge.sandboxScenario`. The PGlite migration, timed under the e2e lock on a fresh PGlite each time (load 0.9): 4.1 to 4.9 s up to 0033 and 4.2 to 4.3 s up to 0034, so no measurable change (one `ALTER TABLE` with a CHECK).
9. **C208.4: the label on the verification.** The `government-id-nfc` attributes gained the optional member `sandbox-chip` (the scenario), left out for every other chip. `services/attributes/government-id-nfc.ts` asks `sandboxChipLabelOf(db, verification)` (`services/sandbox-chip.ts`, api-nfc's). **The console's "Test chip (sandbox)" label has no owner in the 10C section**: `apps/dashboard/src/lib/government-id-nfc-evidence.ts` and its component are outside api-nfc's paths, so it is integration-10c's, as a borrowed edit with its component test.
10. **C208.1: the answer's shape.** `{files[] {kind, content}, dg2-attached: true, active-authentication-response?}`; `kind` is one of the seven chip kinds other than `chip-dg2`; `content` is base64 of at most 64 KiB, PII-marked. `attachSandboxChipPortrait(tx, deps, {environmentId, verificationId, dg2})` is api-nfc's stub for the server-side DG2; no hook in a Phase 5 chip service was needed, since the route's own service writes the file.
11. **C52.1 holds for both new routes.** Both are mutations of a verification, so `verification-mutations.contract.test.ts` lists them, and both stubs run `requireFlowVerification(..., {types: ['government_id_nfc']})` before they answer 501. api-nfc keeps that call. The sandbox route's environment check comes first: outside a sandbox it answers the 404 of an unknown route, whatever the verification.
12. **C221.6: one error class for the three problems.** `Phase10FlowProblemError(code, detail)` (`apps/api/src/errors.ts`) answers `client_unable_not_allowed` (409), `sandbox_chip_anchor_missing` (409) and `sandbox_chip_scenario_unavailable` (422) as their goldens; api-nfc throws it and edits no shared file.
13. **C207.1: the SDK's hooks (review F05).** `FlowCoordinator` makes `NfcStepController(host, environment)` (`nfc/NfcStepController.kt`, nfc-sdk's) and calls `resolve(session, step): FlowState` for an `NfcStep` and `handle(event)` for each `NfcEvent` a screen sends through `FlowActions.nfc(event)`. `NfcStepHost` gives the controller the flow API, the monotonic time source, `show(state)`, `readAndRender()` and `failed(error, retry)`. `FlowState.Nfc(step, view)` carries `NfcView`; `NfcEvent` and `NfcView` are sealed interfaces in nfc-sdk's file, each with one placeholder member that nfc-sdk replaces. `NfcEnvironment(chipReader, platform)` is made per launch (`InquiryViewModelFactory`); a coordinator made without one gets `NfcEnvironment.None`.
14. **C207.12: `NfcPlatform` is an `expect class`.** The Android actual needs a `Context`, so common code cannot construct it: platform code makes it (`NfcPlatform(applicationContext)` in the activity) and common code receives it. Kotlin reports `expect`/`actual` classes as Beta (a warning, not an error). The stub Android actual reports `NO_HARDWARE` and reads nothing.
15. **C207.1: the sandbox-chip call.** `FlowApi.requestSandboxChip(verificationId, challengeId, scenario = MATCH): FlowSandboxChip` is the call `SandboxChipReader` needs. The five NFC methods of `FlowApiClient` delegate to `NfcFlowRequests.{chipChallenge, chipFile, chipSubmit, clientUnable, sandboxChip}(http, apiBase, authorization, ...)`, each a stub that throws `NotImplementedError("flow_api_not_built: <name>")`.
16. **Existing tests changed, each because the plan changes what it checks.** `Phase5BWireModelTest`, `WireModelTest` and `StepRegistryTest` (an nfc step decodes as `NfcStep` and runs); `flow-position.test.ts` (an nfc step is its own position); `phase10b-contracts.test.ts`, `phase10b-foundation.test.ts`, `rate-limits.test.ts`, `fixtures.test.ts`, `phase5-problems.test.ts`, `rate-limit.test.ts` (the frozen lists gained 10C's members); `verification-mutations.contract.test.ts` (two more routes); `chip-challenges.test.ts` (`sandboxScenario: null`).
17. **C217.2, spike (d): the plugin and Dokka, neither fallback.** `com.vanniktech.maven.publish` 0.37.0 and Dokka 2.2.0 publish the root module, the Android target and `openverify-nfc` on AGP 9.4.1, KGP 2.4.21 and Gradle 9.8.2. The iOS targets' publish tasks are skipped by name. The KMP plugin's DSL here is `optimization { consumerKeepRules.publish = true; consumerKeepRules.file("consumer-rules.pro") }` (a property, not a block). Setting the group changed one header line of `api/openverify-sdk.klib.api` (the klib's unique name); `updateKotlinAbi` rewrote it in a build of its own.
18. **C217.1, P4: the group.** `openverifyGroup=dev.openverify` in `android/gradle.properties`, provisional. The POM's `url` and `scm` name `https://github.com/openverify/openverify`, a placeholder the owner replaces with the repository's address before any upload (C217.6: no document names coordinates as if they resolved).
19. **C215.2: the demo's build types.** `release` (minified, resources shrunk, R8 full mode by AGP's default) and `minifiedSmoke` (`initWith(release)`, the debug signing config, `matchingFallbacks += "release"`), with `testBuildType = "minifiedSmoke"`. A minified build type minifies its device-test APK too, so `android/demo-app/test-proguard-rules.pro` silences the compile-time annotations AndroidX Test and `okhttp-tls` name. `androidx.test:runner` and `ext:junit` joined the demo's device-test scope beside `mockwebserver3` and `okhttp-tls`.
20. **C218.1: the optional-artifact property.** `openverifyDemoArtifacts` (`android/gradle.properties`): `sdk` (default) or `sdk,nfc`; any other name fails the build.
21. **C214.6, F18: the boundary check's extension point.** `:openverify-sdk:checkDependencyBoundary` now also refuses every module the build includes other than the SDK and the demo, so `openverify-face` (10E) is covered without a second check.
22. **C214.5.** Lockfiles and `verification-metadata.xml` were refreshed from a fresh `GRADLE_USER_HOME` in a scratch worktree: 53 components added (the publishing plugin with its Central Portal client's Retrofit, Moshi, OkHttp 5.1.0 and Okio; Dokka with Jackson, Woodstox, FreeMarker, jsoup and JetBrains markdown; `okhttp-tls`).
23. **C214.7: bundletool.** The newest release on or before 2026-10-15 is 1.18.3 (2025-12-15, Apache-2.0), SHA-256 `a099cfa1543f55593bc2ed16a70a7c67fe54b1747bb7301f37fdfd6d91028e29`, 32,520,401 bytes; `python3 -I scripts/fetch_bundletool.py` writes it to `android/build/bundletool/`.
24. **Gate call (7) is not a proof yet.** `android/scripts/check-elf-alignment.py` and `check-size.py` are stubs that exit 0 and print `not-built`; `android/consumer-check/` only checks that the staged repository exists. release-checks builds all three.
25. **The 10C section's "Stage 1b".** 10C has none: the hooks, stubs, frozen routes and skeletons above are all Stage 1a's.

## Workflow 10C Stage 2 amendments

Recorded by the orchestrator on 2026-11-10 from the reports of carry-10c and the six Stage 2 agents (each agent's contractDeviations, condensed, and checked against the tree at `b8bde96c3`), because `integration-10c` was cut off by a usage limit before it wrote this section. The agents' TDD files in `docs/tdd/phase10-<agent>.tdd.md` hold the evidence. Binding on 10D onward. Where Stage 3 settled an item, the item says so and the Stage 3 section below says how. No device ran anything in this Workflow (10C decisions items 3 and 7).

### carry-10c (Stage 1a, beside contracts-10c)

The Stage 1a section above holds contracts-10c's amendments only, so carry-10c's are recorded here.

1. **C201.2 and F11 (KP10-21): the barcode side's 1,280 px analysis is asked, not guaranteed.** The 10C decisions (item 6) kept "an analysis of 1,280 px or more, or a full-resolution crop of the guide area, chosen by evidence". As built, the request to CameraX is unchanged (1280x960, 4:3), and the SDK judges each barcode side by its first frame: `AnalysisSizes.barcodeRead(width, height, rotationDegrees)` answers `STREAM` from 1,280 px on the long side, `NARROW` while the guide still spans `READABLE_SYMBOL_PX` (512), and `NONE` otherwise (`commonMain/.../camera/AnalysisSizes.kt`). `CameraXCaptureCamera.barcodeRead` holds the answer and logs `camera.barcode_analysis_narrow` or `camera.barcode_analysis_unreadable` (`6b99c5790`). The reason: beside the preview and a maximum-size still, CameraX 1.6.2 holds the analysis stream to its preview class (the display's area, at most 1920x1080, never under 640x480). This was read from the bytecode of `GuaranteedConfigurationsUtil`, `SurfaceConfig` and `DisplayInfoManager` and from one CI log; it is not confirmed on a device. F11's full-resolution crop is not built, because no guaranteed combination gives a YUV stream above the preview class beside a maximum-size JPEG, and the still is the person's photo. A smaller still on the barcode side was not tried (outside F11's text).
2. **C201.2: only a barcode-bearing side asks 1,280 px.** `AnalysisTarget.of(kind)` is replaced by `AnalysisTarget.of(kind, barcodeBearing)`, and `ui/CaptureScreen.kt` passes `isBarcodeBearing(state)`. Before, every ID back was read at 1,280 px and a front-only ID's barcode side was not. This settles the second open point of Workflow 10B Stage 2 pdf417-guidance item 5. `AnalysisTargetTest` and `CaptureScreenSlotsTest` changed with it (rule 12). Consequence for KP10-24: `isBarcodeBearing` still reads the retry reason `barcode_not_found`, so until the session names the barcode-bearing side the 1,280 px request starts on a retry.
3. **KP10-21: the device test is unweakened and will fail on the CI emulator.** `CameraXFramePipelineTest.theBarcodeSideIsReadAtLeast1280WideAndItsCopyIsMeasuredAgainstAFullResolutionCrop` keeps `longSide >= min(1280, largest offered)` and prints its facts first (`09d8ec4d1`). The CI emulator's camera lists no 4:3 size of 1,280 px inside the preview class on any display, so no `ci.yml` display or profile change is proposed. carry-10c asked for a decision it may not take: (a) run the test on a physical phone, or (b) amend the requirement to "at least min(1280, the largest size the preview class allows)", which the test already asserts as a second condition. The decision is open (see "Open after 10C").
4. **C210.3 (KP10-22) as built, amending Workflow 10B Stage 2 device-integrity-android.** `IntegrityProbes.collect` wraps the probe in the private `OncePerCollection`, so each `DeviceProbe` read happens at most once per collection (build 7 to 1, process maps 2 to 1, mounts 2 to 1). A read that threw an `Exception` rethrows the same exception to each rule that needs it and is not tried again. `/proc` lines are split by `ProcFields` without a Regex, to the same fields (`660aaa50d`). No rule, order, read or wire member changed. The device test logs each read's time before any assertion (`dfb18a2bf`); its 500 ms bound, cold single run and C210.6 assertions are unchanged.
5. **KP10-23: one constant.** `DocumentCaptureConstants.BARCODE_ANALYSIS_WIDTH_PX` (1280) is the one statement of the width, held to `document-capture.json`; `AnalysisTarget.BARCODE` and `Pdf417Guidance.ANALYSIS_WIDTH_PX` refer to it (`fbe9f2302`). Closed.
6. **Tests corrected with GREEN (recorded in its TDD file).** The reads test's fake holds the last QEMU file and the last `su` directory, because rules stop at their first hit. `AnalysisSizesTest`'s "only a shortfall has a log code" case was removed, because `LogScanTest` requires a string literal at every `SdkLog.log` call, so the two codes are literals in `CameraXCaptureCamera` and not enum values. The probe cost test's budget moved from 30 ms to 45 ms; Stage 3 item 3 then replaced both millisecond budgets.
7. **Two hooks carry-10c may not build, left for 10D.** (a) On a frame whose `barcodeRead` is `NONE` the barcode guide still shows "Fit the barcode in the box" (`capture/barcode/BarcodeGuidance.kt`, borrowed only for the width constant). (b) The document guidance measures a 1280x960 frame in 39.8 to 44.3 ms of host CPU against 9.8 to 10.9 ms at 640x480 (`capture/document/DocumentFrameAnalyzer.kt`); proposed: read a 2x2-subsampled 640 px copy when the frame's long side is 1,280 px or more, with `DocumentMeasuresCostTest` extended.
8. **Measurements (host only; no device figure exists).** Probes, one collection over a large synthetic process (24,000 map regions, 300 threads, 400 mounts), thread CPU, median of 9: 69.6 ms before, 22.1 to 25.4 ms after; cold first run 326.7 ms before, 41.8 to 111.6 ms after. `RgbaFrames.copy`: a 1280x960 frame 0.56 to 0.88 ms against 0.81 to 1.14 ms for the 1670x1053 guide crop. carry-10c added 40 host tests.

### nfc-sdk

1. **C207.9: the reason at the challenge cap.** The contract's four reasons had none for "the read was tried and did not finish", so nfc-sdk reported `nfc_unavailable` when the person took another way after the ninth challenge, which is not literally true of a phone that has NFC. Settled in Stage 3 (item 2): the fifth reason `read_failed`.
2. **C207.2: the 9-challenge count lives in memory.** The plan says "at most 9 challenges per verification". As built, `NfcStepController.MAX_CHALLENGES = 9` is counted in memory per verification, resets when the session shows another attempt, and starts again on a relaunch (C207.6: nothing about it is saved). After a relaunch the server's own limit answers 409 `chip_challenge_invalid` and cancels the verification, and the SDK answers that 409 by reading the session.
3. **C207.2: the challenge's lifetime is the device's.** `CHALLENGE_LIFETIME` is 300 s on the host's monotonic `TimeSource` from the answer's arrival, never computed from `expires-at`. An expiry stops only a read in which no chip has answered yet and returns the screen to "I'm ready" with a notice. A read that is already connected goes on, and a later submit 409 asks for one more tap.
4. **C207.2: one more tap uploads everything again.** After a submit 409 `chip_challenge_invalid` with the attempt still `initiated`, the files are wiped, the challenge is dropped and the screen returns to "I'm ready" with the notice `ONE_MORE_TAP`. Every file is uploaded again from the new read. Whether the upload route replaces a chip kind already uploaded was not tested (open, 10D).
5. **C207.2: which access entry a card needs is not known to the SDK.** The MRZ entry offers "use the card access number instead" and the CAN entry the reverse. With a prefilled MRZ the tap screen has no CAN route unless the chip refuses the key.
6. **C207.5: the DG1 review.** Dates are shown as the chip holds them with separators (YY-MM-DD) and a note; the century is not on the chip and none is inferred. After the second "this is not my data" the controller drops the files and the screen itself calls `actions.cancel()`, because `NfcStepHost` gives the controller no way to end the flow.
7. **C207.12 and C207.5: two seams the plan does not name.** `NfcPlatform` is an `expect class` and cannot be faked, so nfc-sdk added the internal interface `NfcPhone` (`availability()`, `openSettings()`) and a third `NfcEnvironment` parameter, `phone`; a launch with no platform seam is treated as `NO_HARDWARE`. `commonMain` has no lifecycle-compose dependency, so "resumed" is the internal expect composable `WhileResumed(onResumed, onPaused)` (`nfc/NfcResumed.kt`), with an Android actual over `LifecycleResumeEffect` and an iOS actual that follows composition only.
8. **C208.2: `SandboxChipReader` as built.** It is made per read with the verification and challenge ids, since `ChipReader.read` receives only the nonce. With no installed reader and the test chip offered, the step opens on the tap screen and skips the access entry. The scenario selector shows the route's wire names untranslated. The controller never uploads `chip-dg2` when the reader reports `dg2Attached` (`NfcStepController.kt:530`). The route's failures pass through as `FlowApiException` (a 409 reads the session; other errors show the error screen with a retry); only a malformed answer becomes `ChipReadException(UNSUPPORTED_DOCUMENT)` (C193.5, C213.3).
9. **C207.1: names and limits as built** (all internal; the ABI dumps are unchanged). `NfcEvent.None`, `NfcView.Pending` and `UNSUPPORTED_REASON` are gone. `POSITION_READS` is `FlowApi.DEFAULT_POLL_ATTEMPTS` (60), read at `FlowApi.DEFAULT_POLL_INTERVAL`. `NfcFlowRequests.CHIP_KINDS` gives the upload order; `DG2_MAX_BYTES` is 1 MiB and `FILE_MAX_BYTES` 64 KiB; the file part is `<kind>.bin`. Two `SdkLog` codes were added: `nfc.read_failed` and `nfc.settings_unavailable`.
10. **An existing test changed with the plan (rule 12).** `NfcStepHooksTest`'s two stub pins now assert the built behaviour, its "calls are stubs" case is removed (`NfcRequestFixturesTest` replaces it), and its caller scan allows `nfc/NfcStepController.kt` and `nfc/SandboxChipReader.kt`. The single-use POST tests are regression tests, not RED-first.
11. **What was not written.** No activity-level NFC test and no device test exist: `androidDeviceTest/` is outside nfc-sdk's paths, so the `NfcPlatform` actual has a host twin only (`NfcPlatformTest`). C195.2 on Compose 1.11: the MRZ and CAN fields cannot set `IME_FLAG_NO_PERSONALIZED_LEARNING` or mark one node not important for autofill; this joins KP10-17's manual pass.
12. **Measurements.** 12 request fixtures (the twelve 10C entries of `contracts/fixtures/flow-requests/android/index.json`, 25 entries in all); 75 strings in each of five locales (`strings_nfc.xml`; non-English machine-drafted, KP10-16); 33 goldens, 923,814 bytes, in `screenshots/nfc-sdk/`; 113 host tests added and 1 removed. With six agents queuing, `flock -w 240` gave exit 75 about 15 times in its session.

### api-nfc

1. **C207.10: an unnamed edit of shared web files.** Stage 1a gave the web NFC screen a client with no client-unable call. api-nfc added `FlowApiClient.reportClientUnable(verificationId, reason, signal?)` to `apps/flow/src/api/flow-api-client.ts`, its guarded pass-through in `apps/flow/src/api/final-outcome.ts`, and one member in the literal client of `submit-verification.test.ts` (`8bbb2af32`, `f58fb7a7f`). It is recorded here as a borrowed edit. The web screen reports `platform_unsupported`.
2. **C207.9: `client-unable:<step id>` is asked, not offered.** The plan says a client-unable exit always goes to review "whatever the step's `onFallback` says", except for a version that carries the acknowledgement. As built, publish stores exactly the acknowledgements its rules ask for, so the key is asked whenever an `nfc` step has an `exhausted` edge and `onFallback: continue`, right after `fallback:<step id>`, in sandbox and production (`packages/flow-spec/src/v2/analyses.ts`, `7064e3f76`). An author cannot give `fallback:` and decline `client-unable:` on one step; `onFallback: review` (the default) is how a client-unable exit stays in review. So "review despite continue" arises only for a version published before 10C, which carries no such key. `docs/android/nfc.md` and `docs/checks/nfc.md` say this.
3. **C207.9: the publish warning** is `nfc_fallback_missing` (severity warning, never blocking), raised only for an `nfc` step the entry can reach.
4. **C207.9: "at most once per verification" is held by C52.1.** A second report answers the plain 409 of a verification that is no longer `initiated`, or 401 once the inquiry settled and the session ended. `client_unable_not_allowed` answers only the position rule: the flow is not active, or the current step is not an `nfc` step with an `exhausted` edge.
5. **C207.9: where the outcome is recorded.** The plan says the verification ends "`canceled` with `client_unable`". The verification has no reason column, so the step record carries the outcome `client_unable` (`CLIENT_UNABLE_OUTCOME`), the exit port `exhausted` and the notes `client-unable`, `client-unable-reason:<reason>` and `client-unable-platform:<platform>`, plus `fallback-continue` only with the acknowledgement and `onFallback: continue` (`apps/api/src/services/client-unable.ts`, `9e8e0c875`). The platform comes from the session's `mobile-sdk-name` (`openverify-android`, `openverify-ios`, else `web`). The engine's entry is imported dynamically inside `reportClientUnable`; a static import makes a cycle with `exits.ts` and `engine.ts`, so it stays dynamic.
6. **C207.2: who gets `prefillMrz`.** "A TD1 or TD3 document" is read from the ID's established class, because the extraction does not keep the MRZ's format: `passport`, `national_id`, `residence_permit` and `passport_card` are served; other classes and an unknown class are not; an old TD2 card of a served class is served too. "Decided" means `passed` or `needs_review` (`apps/api/src/services/flow-views/nfc.ts`). The members are served only when the `nfc` step is the inquiry's current step and the stored session is an SDK client's.
7. **C208.2 and F26: `sandboxChipAvailable`** is served only to an SDK client at the active `nfc` step, as the route accepts it; the plan names only the environment and the anchor. `nfcSessionMembers` gained an optional third parameter `clock: Clock = systemClock`, which dates the test anchor (refines Stage 1a amendment 4); the session builder passes none.
8. **C208.1: the route's refusals, in order.** Outside a sandbox, the generic 404; then C52.1; then a plain 409 Conflict with no code for a web client or a step that is not the current `nfc` step (the contract defines no code); then 409 `sandbox_chip_anchor_missing`; then 422 `sandbox_chip_scenario_unavailable`; then 409 `chip_challenge_invalid` unless the challenge is this verification's, `initiated` and unexpired. The answer never holds `chip-dg2`: the server stores it as the verification's file.
9. **C208.1: a sandbox chip is always a Utopia TD3 passport.** The reading does not keep its MRZ lines, so DG1 is rebuilt from the MRZ-read fields with issuer `UTO`, or from a fixed synthetic holder. The MRZ comparison finds its reference only when the ID is itself a Utopia passport; for any other ID a `match` chip ends in review with `no_reference_data`. `mrz-mismatch` changes the document number's last character.
10. **C208.1: a limit that needs a decision.** DG2 carries the stored selfie centre as uploaded, so every scenario but `face-mismatch` needs a JPEG selfie whose DG2 fits 1 MiB, else 422 `sandbox_chip_scenario_unavailable`. The API has no image encoder. A full-resolution phone selfie can exceed 1 MiB, so the sandbox chip may answer 422 on a real device. The options (cap the SDK's selfie upload, shrink through vision, build DG2 in the worker) change product behaviour and are listed for the owner.
11. **C208.1: the faces variable.** `OPENVERIFY_SANDBOX_CHIP_FACES` names a directory and the file is fixed, `head-turn-b-center.jpg` (`SANDBOX_CHIP_OTHER_FACE_FILE`). The fetched originals are about 3 MB and 28 megapixels, beyond a DG2, so "the fetched fixture faces" is read as the generated set made from them (`fixtures/generated/selfie`). The scenario assumes the session's selfie is not person B; no check enforces it. The variable is read from `process.env` at each request (`sandboxChipFacesDirectory`), not through `config.ts`.
12. **C208.1: the Active Authentication response** is computed over the challenge's stored nonce SHA-256, because `chip_challenges` keeps only that digest. `signNonceDigest` in `services/sandbox-chip.ts` does P-256 ECDSA with integer arithmetic over `node:crypto`'s ECDH, since `node:crypto` signs only what it hashes itself and `@openverify/emrtd/testing` exports no prehashed signer. It verifies under the server's real check. Proposed for 10D or Phase 11: export a prehashed signer from `packages/emrtd/src/testing/keys.ts` and delete the arithmetic.
13. **C208.4: the label's source.** `sandboxChipLabelOf` answers the latest challenge of the verification that carries a scenario, whichever challenge the submit then named, so the label survives a later challenge.
14. **C221.1: masking.** `api-logs.ts` nulls `content` inside any `files` array (`maskChipFileContent`), beside contracts-10c's `prefillMrz`; `logger.ts` gained `prefillMrz` redaction paths at depths 0 to 3, outside `PERSONAL_LOG_KEYS`.
15. **C221.10: left for Stage 3.** Both routes still declared 501 when api-nfc finished; settled in Stage 3 item 1.
16. **C208.1: the anchor command.** `pnpm --filter @openverify/api trust:sandbox-utopia` answers `installed` or `refreshed` and prints the CRL's next update; it needs `DATABASE_URL` only. api-nfc's report gives the CRL 90 days, so the command is run again before then. `seed:dev` installs the anchor on the e2e stack.
17. **O3: simulate does not take the client-unable exit.** A simulated path through an `nfc` step's `exhausted` edge settles by `onFallback` and can show completed where a client-unable exit of the same version would end in review (`docs/flows/preview-and-simulate.md`).
18. **Existing tests changed because the plan changes what they check:** `routes/nfc-routes.test.ts` (both routes answer through their services), `steps/nfc/nfc-step.test.tsx`, `e2e/support/a11y-states.ts` with `nfc.aria.yml` (the one `nfc:arrival` state is served with `fallbackAvailable: true`; no state was added to the matrix), `submit-verification.test.ts`.
19. **Measurements.** API unit suite 9,673 tests in 726 files at 94.41% statements; flow 1,533; flow-spec 486; dashboard 6,757; the API integration project 304 tests, 14 of them in `nfc-10c.integration.test.ts`; the mocked flow suite 332 (4 in `nfc-step.spec.ts`). Twelve API blob shards took 139 to 180 s each alone and 301 to 326 s each three at a time.

### release-checks

1. **C215.1: two rules, and none for the models.** The plan expected rules for the SDK's `@Serializable` models. As built, every serializer is named in code, so no model needs a rule. `android/openverify-sdk/consumer-rules.pro` holds two lines, `-keepnames class kotlinx.serialization.SerializationException` and `-keepnames class * extends kotlinx.serialization.SerializationException`, because `FlowApiException.InvalidResponse.causeClass` is the cause's simple class name and R8 had renamed `MissingFieldException` to `gj2` (`34acafe4`). `android/openverify-nfc/consumer-rules.pro` has no active rule; the `org.jmrtd`, `net.sf.scuba` and `org.ejbca.cvc` rules are comments for 10E.
2. **C215.4: the proof is static.** "Host apps need no rules of their own" is proven on R8's outputs of the demo's `release` and `minifiedSmoke` builds and on a consumer of the staged AAR. The minified app was not run.
3. **C216.1: one script runs all three 16 KB checks.** `android/scripts/check-elf-alignment.py` runs the ELF `p_align` scan, `zipalign -c -P 16` and bundletool's `PAGE_ALIGNMENT_16K` check. Beyond the plan it scans every `lib/*/*.so` of the minified demo APK besides the AARs, fails when the APK holds no native library, and is strict on the 32-bit ABIs although Google Play's rule covers 64-bit devices only. Result: 0 native libraries in 7 AARs; 16 in the demo APK (4 per ABI), all 16 KB-aligned. Nothing was loaded on a 16 KB device.
4. **C216.2: what the two figures are.** bundletool has no install-size command, so install size is defined as the bytes of the APK files `extract-apks` selects for one reference device per ABI (API 34, 480 dpi, `en`), without ART's compiled code or app data. Download size is `get-size total`'s MAX column. The figures are the demo with the SDK, not the SDK's own cost. Two configurations exist, `sdk` and `sdk,nfc` ("without the reader"); `check-size.py` tells them apart by the NFC permission in the bundle's manifest, and its tolerance is 10%. The committed figures are Stage 3's (item 12).
5. **Scripts the plan does not name,** under `android/scripts/`: `check-consumer-rules.py`, `check-staged-repository.py`, `trust-staged-group.py`, `scan-staged-repository.sh`, with 82 unittest tests in `android/scripts/tests`. **Gate call (7) therefore reads** `python3 -I scripts/check-elf-alignment.py && python3 -I scripts/check-size.py && python3 -I scripts/check-consumer-rules.py`; `ci.yml` runs all three (Stage 3 item 14).
6. **C217.2: no local validation by the plugin.** The publishing plugin validates a deployment only at the Central Portal, so `check-staged-repository.py` stands in for it. Both POMs gained `scm.developerConnection` with the same placeholder address as `url`.
7. **C217.3: the consumer check** (`android/consumer-check/`) is a single-project Android application that depends on `openverify-sdk` (root) and `openverify-nfc`. Only its minified release variant is built, and its lint tasks are disabled (lint is the parent build's gate). The `androidx.activity` line it needed is settled in Stage 3 item 10.
8. **C214.5: the consumer check has its own lockfiles and verification metadata** (`gradle.lockfile`, `settings-gradle.lockfile`, `gradle/verification-metadata.xml`, 443 components). The group `dev.openverify` is trusted there without a checksum and left out of the lock state, because the call before builds its artifacts. Binding on 10E: after any change to the SDK's dependencies, refresh these three files from a fresh `GRADLE_USER_HOME` by the recipe in its TDD file (`--write-locks --write-verification-metadata sha256`, then `trust-staged-group.py`, then a verify from a second fresh home), or gate call (8) fails on a cold runner.
9. **C217.4: the release workflow as built.** `android-release.yml` has a second input, `publish` (boolean, default false). The `publish` job runs only with it, on `refs/heads/main`, in the environment `maven-central`; without the secrets it prints a notice and does nothing. Signing and Central publishing are switched on per call (`-PmavenCentralPublishing=true -PmavenCentralAutomaticPublishing=false -PsignAllPublications=true`), so no build file signs or uploads by default. The `gate` job does not repeat `ci.yml`'s SDK host-test floor. The `publish` job has never run: whether the Central Portal accepts a root module whose Gradle metadata names two iOS modules that are not uploaded is unknown (it joins P4 and 10E).
10. **C220.3: the device legs.** Each leg boots its image once in its own step (which saves the AVD snapshot), then runs `:openverify-sdk:connectedAndroidDeviceTest :demo-app:connectedAndroidTest` only if the boot succeeded. Only the API 35 `google_apis_ps16k` leg's boot may fail without failing the job (`boot-may-fail: true`), with a warning naming the open item; a test failure always fails a leg. The matrix has never been dispatched.
11. **A check that can pass without checking.** The `android-release.yml` section of `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh` skips its secret-scan cases when gitleaks or openssl is missing (`ci-workflow.test.sh:1322`), and `ci.yml`'s `infra` job runs that test (`ci.yml:315`) before it installs gitleaks (`ci.yml:324`). No commit changed this (open).
12. **Two findings recorded, with no change.** `FlowApiException.InvalidResponse.causeClass` is a class name read as text; a fixed code per failure kind would need no consumer rule, and is a public-API decision for 10D. The Android target's Gradle metadata names its AAR `openverify-sdk.aar` at the URL `openverify-sdk-android-0.1.0.aar`; consumers resolve it, but a file-name check would trip on it.
13. **Measurements and timings.** The staged repository holds 95 files in three modules (`dev.openverify:openverify-sdk`, `openverify-sdk-android`, `openverify-nfc`, each `0.1.0`, unsigned). The consumer check resolves 113 modules, none for iOS. `-p consumer-check build` took 139 to 167 s warm and 220 to 232 s from a fresh Gradle home; `publishAllPublicationsToStagingRepository` 68 s; an R8 bundle of the demo 141 to 239 s; the demo's release, bundle and `minifiedSmoke` builds with the SDK's `assemble` and the staging in one call 386 s. `ci-workflow.test.sh` grew from 266 to 341 checks.

### demo-app

1. **C215.2, amending Stage 1a amendment 19: the demo's host tests.** AGP 9.4.1 builds host tests only for `testBuildType`, so with `testBuildType = "minifiedSmoke"` the task `:demo-app:testDebugUnitTest` did not exist, and gate call (4) and CI's `testDebugUnitTest` ran none of the demo's tests. demo-app enables the debug variant's host tests again (`androidComponents.beforeVariants`, `adcfeee4a`). The demo's host tests now run on the debug variant (80) and, for the smoke test's host twins only, on `minifiedSmoke` (8, `src/testMinifiedSmoke/`, filtered to `dev.openverify.demo.smoke.*`). `:demo-app:testDebugUnitTest` is `finalizedBy` `:demo-app:testMinifiedSmokeUnitTest`, so the gate's `testDebugUnitTest` runs both. The demo had 18 tests before, none run by the gate.
2. **C215.2: `minifiedSmoke` is `release` plus one keep rule.** The plan says R8 minifies it "exactly as `release`". As built it adds `android/demo-app/smoke-proguard-rules.pro` (`-keep class dev.openverify.demo.smoke.** { *; }`) for the launcher's own package, which exists only in that build, because the device tests' APK is built against the minified app and R8 removes or inlines app members only the test calls (`48859e301`). The SDK and the demo's screen are minified as in `release` (checked with dexdump: 5,620 classes, one keeping an SDK name, `OpenVerifyInquiryActivity`).
3. **C215.2: the smoke device test's shape.** `MinifiedSmokeTest` (2 methods) names no SDK class: `SmokeReport` turns events and the result into text inside the smoke package. It ends the flow with a BACK key at the consent step (result `Canceled`), because the device-test scope holds no Compose or UI test library and none was added.
4. **C215.2: the host twins' TLS server.** `mockwebserver3` and `okhttp-tls` are on the device-test classpath only, so the twins use the JDK's `HttpsServer` with a key and certificate made at run time by `keytool` in a temporary folder, on the JVM's default JCA providers (Robolectric installs Conscrypt first, and a SunJSSE-pinned server reset the handshake).
5. **C215.2 and rule 27: the served session is a committed asset.** `android/demo-app/src/androidTest/assets/smoke-session.json` is derived from `contracts/fixtures/api/flow-session.json` at its consent step; `SmokeSessionFixtureTest` fails on any difference and writes only under `UPDATE_CONTRACT_FIXTURES=1`. It lies outside `contracts/fixtures` and `e2e/fixtures/android-10c/`.
6. **C215.3: the allowlists.** They are `manifest-allowlist/sdk.json` and `sdk-nfc.json`, and they describe the `release` build's merged manifest (handed to the debug host tests as the system property `openverify.demo.releaseManifest`). Beyond the plan's list they also hold activities, `uses-feature` entries and declared permissions. A default run compares `sdk.json` for real and holds `sdk-nfc.json` to `sdk.json` plus `openverify-nfc`'s permission and feature. `sdk-nfc.json` is compared with a real merged manifest only by `./gradlew -PopenverifyDemoArtifacts=sdk,nfc :demo-app:testDebugUnitTest` (72 s here), **which joins the Android gate**; `ci.yml` runs it (Stage 3 item 14). Rewrite an allowlist with `UPDATE_MANIFEST_ALLOWLIST=1`. The `sdk` list holds components that come from dependencies (`androidx.profileinstaller.ProfileInstallReceiver`, `androidx.startup.InitializationProvider`, `org.jetbrains.compose.resources.AndroidContextProvider`, `androidx.camera.core.impl.MetadataHolderService`, and the signature permission `dev.openverify.demo.DYNAMIC_RECEIVER_NOT_EXPORTED_PERMISSION`).
7. **C213.4: the demo's trust anchors are stated.** `src/main/res/xml/network_security_config.xml` now states `<certificates src="system" />` in its base config, with cleartext off. Before, it relied on the platform default.
8. **C218.2: App Links as built.** The intent filter is a comment, but `MainActivity` is exported (the launcher), so an explicit VIEW intent from another app reaches `AppLinks`. The link must be https on `AppLinks.HOST` (`verify.example.com`, a placeholder) at the default port with no user, and is opened against `AppLinks.API_BASE_URL` only. A recreated activity does not open the link again; after process death the system redelivers the intent and the spent link ends `LINK_INVALID`.
9. **C218.1: what `sdk,nfc` adds.** Only `openverify-nfc`'s manifest entries: the demo has no NFC code and nothing references `OpenVerifyNfc`. `DemoComponents.of` is the one place 10E adds `chipReader` and `faceDetectorFactory`. No Play Integrity configuration, allowlist or provider exists (P3 declined).
10. **C90.9: a limit.** After process death `DemoApplication` installs no sandbox outcome; the picked outcome is installed again only by the next launch from the screen.
11. **Build file changes beyond the skeleton** (`android/demo-app/build.gradle.kts`): `buildConfig` with `BuildConfig.OPENVERIFY_DEMO_ARTIFACTS` (`sdk` or `sdk-nfc`), two test system properties, the debug host tests, the `minifiedSmoke` test filter and `finalizedBy`, and the smoke keep rule. The manifest gained `android:name=".DemoApplication"`. No dependency, lockfile or verification entry changed. `MainActivityTest` was rewritten around its two original cases.
12. **Timings (rule 8).** The first `:demo-app:assembleRelease :demo-app:bundleRelease` took 440 s at load 25.7, past the 300 s split line; under such load run the two tasks as two calls. `demo-app-minifiedSmoke.apk` is 9,897,570 bytes.

### docs-android

1. **C213.5: seven categories plus None, not the plan's four.** `WireDataSafetyTableTest.CATEGORIES` is closed at Photos, Personal info, Device or other IDs, App info and performance, App activity, Files and docs, Financial info and None. The README's Data safety table must name exactly the used ones. `docs/android/privacy.md` holds a 49-row wire-member table that the test holds to the real client's requests, the wire models and every Android request fixture; a new `FlowApi` method fails the test until it has a row.
2. **C213.5: the category of each member is the agent's reading,** for the owner to confirm before a release. The less obvious ones: consent members and a screen's button as App activity; the ID's country and class, the chip files other than DG2 and the Active Authentication response as Personal info; the camera snapshot, the chip device report, the client-unable reason and the integrity token as App info and performance; tokens, the OTP code, challenge ids and nonces as None.
3. **C190.4: the README's failure-reason table** is grouped by what the host should do (five rows naming all twelve reasons) and links `docs/android/failure-reasons.md`, instead of repeating 10A's per-reason table. `FailureReasonDocsTest` holds both to the enum.
4. **C216.3: the sizes page is held to the baseline.** `SizesPageTest` ties `docs/android/sizes.md` to `android/size-baseline.json` at a tenth of a megabyte. A baseline change that moves a figure by a tenth, or adds a configuration, fails that test until the page is updated.
5. **C222: no accessibility page here.** C212.3 gives `docs/android/accessibility.md` to 10D; `docs/android/README.md` says so.
6. **The owner items' wording.** P1 and P2 were granted by the orchestrator under the owner's delegation and stay the owner's to reverse until 10E, so `android/openverify-nfc/README.md` and `NOTICE` say "granted under the owner's delegation". No licence text is reproduced there yet.
7. **What was written.** New: `docs/android/privacy.md`, `nfc.md`, `release-and-support.md`, `sizes.md` and `android/CHANGELOG.md` (an "Unreleased" 0.1.0 entry). Extended: the SDK README, `docs/checks/nfc.md`, `openverify-nfc`'s README and NOTICE. Borrowed edits of six 10A and 10B pages removed every claim that rested on a CI emulator run. docs-android added 12 host tests (`WireDataSafetyTableTest` 8, `FailureReasonDocsTest` 2, `SizesPageTest` 2).
8. **C217.6: statements that depend on other agents' code were not all checked.** The NFC, release and privacy pages were written while api-nfc's sandbox chip and trust command were still RED. Stage 3 corrected the fifth reason and the sizes; a statement-by-statement check is open for 10D's review.

### privacy-checks

1. **C213.1: ten window call sites, not one.** Beside the screen step's date picker, every `Selector` (`ui/steps/StepParts.kt`, a Material `DropdownMenu`) is a popup window. `WindowSecureFlagTest` has 11 rows over 10 call sites, each inheriting `FLAG_SECURE` (`SecureFlagPolicy.Inherit`); `WindowSitesScanTest` holds the site list and the refused builders. 10B added no window.
2. **C213.2: the saved-state allowlist is final at four entries, not three** (amends Workflow 10A Stage 1b item 11): `agreed`, `ticked` and `TICKED_SAVER` in `ui/ConsentScreen.kt`, and `deniedState` in `CameraPermission.android.kt`. The storage allowlist is empty; intents write 10 extras; one clipboard writer exists (the crypto step's message to sign).
3. **C213.1: the controls table.** `ProtectionScanTest` tables 87 controls in 16 files, 22 of them with `submitProtection()`, and 9 text-field calls in 5 files. A new control fails the test with the scanned list printed in the table's form. Three findings in other owners' files are recorded as built and left for a decision: `DocumentStep.kt`'s choose-file and take-photo buttons carry no `submitProtection()` (recorded `OPENS`), while `CaptureScreen.kt`'s `pickPhoto` does; `OtpStep.kt`'s continue-after-limit button carries none (`LEAVES`); in `NfcStepScreen.kt`, `ChooseAccess(other)` carries it and `ChooseAccess(NfcAccessKind.MRZ)` does not, which is harmless.
4. **C213.3: the log allowlist is one line,** `Log.w(TAG, code)` in `log/PlatformLog.android.kt`. No closed list of `SdkLog` codes was built (it would have failed nfc-sdk's parallel work); `LogScanTest` holds every call to a literal code instead. 31 codes are in use.
5. **C213.3: exception messages.** `ExceptionMessageScanTest` allows 19 templates in 11 files, each with its reason. `model/WireEnums.kt:35` is the one message built from a server value; it does not reach a host today, and `ExceptionCanaryTest` feeds a canary through 24 client calls against 7 answers. Suggested for 10D: a fixed message there, then remove the `$value` line from the allowlist.
6. **C191.2: the boundary over every module.** `SdkModule.ALL` lists `openverify-sdk` (SDK), `openverify-nfc` (OPTIONAL) and `demo-app` (CONSUMER). Bouncy Castle is on `:openverify-sdk`'s test and lint classpaths (Robolectric, the lint tool), so `ModuleBoundaryTest` reads the lockfile only for configurations whose names hold neither `Test` nor `Lint`. All three modules pass. Binding on 10E: `ModuleBoundaryTest` and `ExceptionCanaryTest`'s "a module without rows" case fail on purpose when `openverify-nfc` gains Kotlin sources or a module is added; add the module to `SdkModule.ALL` and give its exceptions rows then.
7. **A borrowed edit outside the named list.** `signals/NeverReadTest.kt` now reads `SdkModule.SHIPPED`, so the one list of forbidden reads covers `openverify-nfc`; no list, pattern or assertion changed (`5b4356b6a`).
8. **C213.4: network trust.** `NetworkTrustScanTest` holds that redirects are off in all three places an HTTP client is made or derived, and that no shipped source installs a trust manager, hostname verifier, pinner, proxy or WebView. Trust is the host app's network security configuration. Pinning is not built; its seam is the installed transport.
9. **Judged as asked (10A Stage 3 android-driver item 9, part of KP10-18).** `flow.copy_key_ignored`, logged on each render, is a constant code written only in a debuggable host; it has no privacy cost and stays as built.
10. **Process deviation from rule 2.** Both RED states and the first GREEN were developed and run in a scratch worktree, so no failing test sat in the shared tree during other agents' gates; the RED commit stood in the shared tree for under a minute. Its gate calls then found the tasks up to date, so their times are not a measurement.
11. **Result and measurements.** No privacy defect was found in the SDK as built. `privacy/` grew from 4 to 94 tests. Negative controls: 43 of 101 tests failed on stubs, 14 of 96 on a planted violation, and the Gradle boundary check exited 1 on a planted dependency.

## Workflow 10C Stage 3 amendments (integration-10c)

Recorded by the orchestrator on 2026-11-10 from `integration-10c`'s 27 commits (`098ededb6` to `b8bde96c3`), its transcript and its validation logs, because a usage limit stopped the agent at 22:13:24 UTC before it wrote this section, its TDD file, a handoff or a PROGRESS draft. Evidence: `docs/tdd/phase10-integration-10c.tdd.md`, assembled afterwards by the orchestrator; the agent's reasons are given only where a command, a code comment or a commit message states them. Binding on 10D onward.

1. **C221.10: both NFC routes document no 501.** `routes/flow/client-unable.ts` and `routes/flow/sandbox-chip.ts` declare `problemResponses(400, 401, 404, 409, 410, 422, 429)`; client-unable gained C52.1's 422. `docs/api/openapi.json` was regenerated once (sha256 `cd54e6168dfe49d4c638a74a2562c744107b27f73f7e8cc24ffeac8dbee071a0`; `098ededb6`, `0a33b1f17`). `@openverify/node` was not regenerated: the agent grepped `packages/node/src` for the routes' names, found no match and committed nothing there.
2. **C207.9: a fifth client-unable reason, `read_failed`.** `CLIENT_UNABLE_REASONS` gained `read_failed`, for a client that can read chips but whose reads of this one did not finish (`packages/contracts/src/flow-nfc.ts`; `dddd49852`, `0a33b1f17`). The SDK's `ClientUnableReason.READ_FAILED` is what "use another way" reports once the nine challenges are spent (`NfcStepController.reasonOf`; `5ceb214b0`, `5ed713c2d`). `docs/android/nfc.md`, `docs/android/privacy.md` and `docs/checks/nfc.md` name it. This settles nfc-sdk item 1.
3. **Rule 6: the two host CPU budgets are ratios.** `ContextDeviceProbeCostTest` and `BarcodeSideCostTest` failed under load in three agents' gates. `testing/CpuCost.kt` measures each cost beside a fixed reference workload on one thread and gives the median of the pairs' ratios; both tests assert the ratio, and the milliseconds are printed only (`5b7989d10`). The budgets are provisional: `ContextDeviceProbeCostTest.BUDGET = 1.8` (measured 0.88 to 0.94) and `BarcodeSideCostTest.INTERVAL_BUDGET = 2.55` (measured 1.59 on a quiet host, 1.76 to 1.91 beside 36 busy loops). No separate RED commit exists; the RED is the old tests under load (83.7 ms against the 62.5 ms bound).
4. **The host tests' declared inputs; the wire table's tolerance.** `testAndroidHostTest`'s `sourcesTheTestsRead` inputs now name the build files, the lockfile, the settings file, `openverify-nfc`'s sources, the demo's manifest, the README, `size-baseline.json` and the three docs pages the tests read, so a change to one of them reruns the tests (`4b587331e`). `WireDataSafetyTableTest` no longer tolerates an unbuilt NFC call (`847925eff`). Neither commit has a RED of its own.
5. **C208.1: the faces on the e2e stack.** `generateE2eEnv` sets `OPENVERIFY_SANDBOX_CHIP_FACES` to `fixtures/generated/selfie` (`e2e/src/paths.ts`, `e2e/src/env.ts`); `.env.example` names the variable commented out; `infra/tests/compose.test.sh` holds that the `api`, `worker` and `migrate` services receive none (`86477afa1`, `521e62182`, `60aa3fcb4`). The Utopia anchor needed no change: `seed:dev` installs it and the e2e stack runs the seed.
6. **C224.3, C224.4, rule 27: the 10C flow fixture.** `e2e/src/android-nfc-flow.ts` builds `e2e/fixtures/android-10c/flow.json`: the default template's consent with `notice.play-integrity`, the Utopia TD3 passport page as a one-sided government ID, a selfie, the `nfc` step, a proof-of-address document on the chip step's `exhausted` edge, and a completion (`ae4a6b52b`, `fe78a7a8f`, `cb5e21764`). The fixture holds `selfie_liveness_detection` advisory (`enabled: true, required: false`), because the clients here upload stills, which liveness answers `not_applicable` with `capture_not_live`.
7. **C224.3: the 10C full-stack spec.** `e2e/tests/flow-nfc-android.spec.ts` has three tests with a 90 s budget each, driven by an API client that sends the Android `User-Agent` and `OpenVerify-Flow-Spec: 2`: `match` (the inquiry completes), `face-mismatch`, and a client-unable report of `nfc_unavailable` that takes the document fallback and lands in `needs_review` (`72f1e66d1`). ## Orchestrator decisions before Workflow 10D (2026-11-10)

Made by the orchestrator (Claude Code, claude-fable-5-2) after re-checking the 10D script against Workflows 10B and 10C as built at `19430fe88` (memos `resume/tasks/2026-11-10-recheck-10d-after-10c.memo.md` and `2026-11-10-known-10d.memo.md`). This section's commit is 10D's `start`.

1. **Base and start.** 10D's `base` is the Phase 10 base `c60d51b9` (the reviews read `base..HEAD`); this section's commit is its `start`, required. It is not pushed alone: with `6c2ffc9af` and `19430fe88` it rides with 10D's one push.
2. **The 10C push's CI** (run 42937477615 on `67244eb78`): 15 jobs green. `android` failed at "The consumer rules as R8 applied them", because no step built `openverify-nfc-release.aar`; `19430fe88` fixes both workflows, proved locally only. `unit-api-coverage` never started (the account's spending limit). `android-instrumented` was skipped. `args.ciFailures` is empty.
3. **No CI run to count on** (10C decisions items 3 and 4) holds for 10D unchanged. Nothing in 10D rests on "the push's CI proves it"; nobody dispatches, re-runs or pushes; `gh` is read-only. The release-matrix dispatch (C220.3, `orchestratorAfterPush`) is recorded open, not attempted. This amends the 10D section's fix-records line and the 10D acceptance's release-matrix clause: both read "ci-pending, waiting on Actions minutes".
4. **Args.** `{base, start, prior, known, p5: "declined", ciFailures: [], device}`. `args.device` is the phone's adb serial, or `''`.
5. **The phone** (amends 10C decisions item 7 and "Closed by the orchestrator" item 3). One agent at a time, under `flock -E 75 -w 1800 /tmp/openverify-device.lock` with `ANDROID_SERIAL` set; `adb -s <serial> get-state` first; if the phone is not reachable, no waiting and no hunting, a "device proof open: <test>" line and on. A phone run never passes `openverifyDeviceKind`. Results are read from the instrumentation's own output (KP10-31). No device test is weakened, skipped or retried, and no bound is raised. Device-only behaviour is still proved in the three parts of 10C decision 3. The lock order is the e2e lock, then the device lock, then the Gradle lock.
6. **Who uses the phone.** android-a11y-audit (Stage 1, alone) for a device check it needs; fix-sdk, which owns KP10-31 and KP10-32 and proves KP10-32 on it; validation-10d (alone, last) for `:openverify-sdk:connectedAndroidDeviceTest`, `:demo-app:connectedMinifiedSmokeAndroidTest` and one flow of the demo's debug build against the local stack (30 minutes, else open). Reviewers, the verifier, fix-server and fix-records never.
7. **KP10-34** (MEDIUM, validation-10d). Local validation hid CI's failure behind a stale AAR. validation-10d runs the android job's steps of `ci.yml`, and the gate steps of `android-release.yml` that need no device and no secret, once, in order, after `./gradlew clean`, from a fresh `GRADLE_USER_HOME`, and fixes test-first whatever a step reads that no earlier step built. The script hands it over through the known-item owner `validation`.
8. **KP10-32** (HIGH, fix-sdk). Never by dropping or relaxing minification of the smoke build, never by an SDK consumer rule, never by a new dependency. This amends Workflow 10C Stage 2 demo-app 2 once the fix says how minifiedSmoke then differs from release.
9. **The Android gate as 10C left it.** Eleven foreground calls: (1) in three `-PhostTestGroup` calls; (5) with `:openverify-nfc:assembleRelease`; (9) `fetch_bundletool.py`, the scripts' own tests, then `check-elf-alignment.py`, `check-consumer-rules.py`, `check-size.py`; (10) the staged repository and the consumer check; (11) `-PopenverifyDemoArtifacts=sdk,nfc :demo-app:testDebugUnitTest`. The floors are 2,602 and 80 + 8. **The plan's Validation block** gains `check-consumer-rules.py`, the scripts' tests, `:openverify-nfc:assembleRelease` and the `sdk,nfc` call (part of KP10-33; fix-records writes it).
10. **No "Handover to 10D".** integration-10c was cut off; "Closed by the orchestrator after the usage limit", "Open after 10C, with owners" (items 9-24) and the 10C PROGRESS entry stand for it.
11. **The audit.** It creates `docs/android/accessibility.md` (C212.3) with its row in `docs/android/README.md`. The manual TalkBack, font-scale and keyboard pass stays the owner's (`args.manualAudit` empty); the checklist is written with an empty results table and gains two rows (the MRZ and CAN fields' keyboard limits; the capture hint's live region and the time warning with TalkBack). The statement names no CI or emulator run as evidence.
12. **The 10D section says "Nine agents".** 10D has ten: validation-10d runs alone and last (D1 of the 2026-11-08 refresh).
13. **CARRIED_10A.** K10A-1 to K10A-3 are still open at `19430fe88`; fix-sdk's.
14. **CLAUDE.md.** No agent edits it. The two lines KP10-33 names (`trust:sandbox-utopia`; `testDebugUnitTest` also runs the minifiedSmoke twins) are the orchestrator's.
15. **The CI job table.** fix-records writes the table integration-10c did not ("Open after 10C" item 21) from runs that exist; no job is split on an estimate.
16. **The push.** The orchestrator's, once, after `validate-full`. validation-10d's closing text promises no CI proof and lists what stays unproved on a device or an emulator.
17. **`e2e/README.md`'s Shards bullet** still holds 10B's run; validation-10d rewrites its measurement from its own e2e gate.
18. **The known items.**
    - **Known items (twelve, `args.known`), with K10A-1 to K10A-3 from the script.** fix-sdk: KP10-32 (HIGH), KP10-31, KP10-38, KP10-35, KP10-36, KP10-37, KP10-29, KP10-25, K10A-1 to K10A-3 (KP10-18). fix-server: KP10-39, KP10-40. fix-records: KP10-33. validation-10d: KP10-34.
    - **KP10-32.** `:demo-app:connectedMinifiedSmokeAndroidTest` runs no test on the Pixel 9: the runner dies in the minified app's process (`androidx.tracing.Trace`, then `kotlin.LazyKt`). C215.2's device proof is disproved until it runs. fix-sdk fixes it test-first on the phone, by keep rules for what the runner shares with the app on the smoke build only, or by a test that drives the minified app from outside. The smoke build stays minified as release; no keep rule names the SDK, the demo's screen or kotlinx.serialization; the consumer-rules check keeps its negative control.
    - **KP10-31.** Gradle's `:openverify-sdk:connectedAndroidDeviceTest` exits failed on the phone with 9 of 9 passing. Until its cause is found, a device result is read from the instrumentation's own output. No `ignoreFailures`, no retry.

This run is also the cold proof of `android/consumer-check`'s lockfile and verification metadata after `01464231e`. On CI run 42937477615 `check-size.py`, both floors and the `sdk,nfc` manifest call were never reached.

Until KP10-24 is built, the barcode guide and the 1,280 px request come on a retry.
