## Workflow 10D Stage 1 amendments (android-a11y-audit)

Recorded by the Stage 3 verifier on 2026-11-11 from `android-a11y-audit`'s report and its commits (`624ea7e60` to `e27108976`; evidence in `docs/tdd/phase10-android-a11y-audit.tdd.md`). Each item is a decision as built and binds the 10D fixers, validation-10d and every later Workflow. None of them claims conformance: the manual pass (KP10-17) has not been run, and the statement says "not yet evaluated for conformance" for Android.

1. **C212.2, the hardware keyboard: the host holds only that a control can take focus.** Under Robolectric a Tab sent with `performKeyInput`, `FocusManager.moveFocus(Next)` and a `RequestFocus` action each leave every node unfocused, so no host test can show focus moving. `A11yKeyboardTest` therefore asserts only that every usable control of every state is focusable. Tab order, the focus highlight and keyboard use of menus are proved by a person: row A8 of the manual checklist, known gap K2. No record may say the keyboard order is tested.
2. **C212.2, clipped text: the pseudo-locale is the SDK's own.** The check runs with `SDK_PSEUDO_LANGUAGE = "en-XA"` provided as `LocalSdkLanguage`; `pseudoLocalized()` accents every letter and adds about half the length, and keeps `%n$s` and `%n$d`. Android's `en-XA` resource locale is not used, because the SDK reads its strings from its own per-locale tables (spike (a)) and Android's pseudo-locale would not reach them. No locale choice resolves to the pseudo-language (`PseudoLocaleTest`). Operator copy is not pseudo-localized (known gap K11).
3. **C212.2, "announcements counted" means changes of live regions.** The class `Announcements` counts one announcement for each appearance of a live region with words and for each change of its words in the semantics tree. It does not hear TalkBack; what TalkBack speaks is the manual pass's (C212.6).
4. **C212.2, colour alone: the rule as built.** Every node the inventory names as a status (`A11yState.announced`) is a live region with words; every live region carries words; every field marked as an error carries its words. One named exception: the document guidance's hint keeps an empty live region in place between hints.
5. **C212.2, heading first: the rule as built.** The first readable node of a screen in reading order, outside the chrome (brand header, sandbox banner, help slot), is a heading. Reading order is computed from positions (top, then line start), which is how Compose orders nodes that have no traversal index.
6. **C212.2, touch targets** are measured by `touchBoundsInRoot` on a 360 x 4000 dp window: a Material button's node is 40 dp high inside a 48 dp touch area, and the touch area is what the rule holds. Options of a long menu that are scrolled out of view are not measured.
7. **C219.3, the budget for the audit's states.** Every state has a golden of its own or names, by `variantOf`, a state of the same screen whose layout it shares with another message (76 of 181). The matrix for the new screens: each state in `en`, light, font scale 1.0; one state per screen in `ar`, dark, 2.0, and in an operator palette light and dark (the palette is `ThemeFixtures.theme()`); consent in the five languages at 1.0 and 2.0.
8. **C219.3, how the audit's goldens are drawn.** Two test methods (`A11yAuditScreenshotTest`, `A11yAuditLargeFontScreenshotTest`) each loop over their goldens and report every one that differs, because a `ParameterizedRobolectricTestRunner` class is not found by the screenshot task's category filter. The screenshot task's test count is therefore 95 for 205 goldens; a count of tests is not a count of goldens. A changed audit golden is re-recorded only by the explicit record call filtered to `dev.openverify.sdk.ui.a11y.*`.
9. **C212.3, the lifetimes are six.** The plan's four server lifetimes gain the two the SDK also meets: a crypto wallet message's `expires-at`, and the inquiry's own expiry with the one-time link. `docs/android/accessibility.md` tables six rows. "No other SDK time limit exists" is held by `A11yTimingTest`: a pinned list of every duration literal, timeout call, `_MS` constant and HTTP timeout in the shipped sources, each classified on that page; a new one fails the test until it is listed.
10. **C212.3 and C202.4, "the manual shutter is always on screen", amended.** The capture screen is one scrolling column (the fix for text cut off at font scale 2.0), so at 2.0 the shutter can be below the fold and is reached by scrolling. At font scale 1.0 it is wholly inside a 360 x 800 dp window in every preview state in the five languages (`A11yShutterTest`). The docs say "always reachable", not "always on screen", at large font sizes. Pinning the shutter outside the scrolling area is an open decision for Phase 11.
11. **C204.5, the selfie time warning, left open.** The warning card is drawn over the top of the capture screen and covers the heading while it shows, and its "More time" button is composed after the screen it covers. A reorder with `zIndex` was tried and withdrawn, because the host cannot show focus order and the change could not be verified. It is known gap K7 and manual rows A7 and A8; a redesign is a Phase 11 decision, and nobody in 10D reorders it without a way to verify focus order.
12. **Behaviour changed with no existing test changed (rule 12).** On the capture screen the heading comes before "Photo n of m". The spinner of the loading, processing, finalizing and waiting screens is not exposed to a screen reader (`clearAndSetSemantics`, its test tag kept). The loading message is a heading. The consent's saving row is a merged polite live region.
13. **New internal test tags on existing nodes:** `TestTags.ERROR_MESSAGE`, `BUSY`, `CONSENT_SAVING`, `CAPTURE_SHUTTER`, `CAPTURE_PROBLEM`, `CAPTURE_RESTARTED`, `CAPTURE_SENDING` and `WAITING_MESSAGE`. No public API, ABI dump, manifest, string, dependency or wire member changed, and no `strings_a11y.xml` exists because no string was added. Every SDK text is drawn through the one internal text composable (`ui/text/SdkText.kt`, held by `SdkTextSourceTest`); new code does the same.
14. **Borrowed edits and one shared file.** `apps/dashboard/src/accessibility-statement-records.test.ts` holds the statement's "## Android SDK" section to the newest `android-audit-<date>.md`: the section names that file, says "not yet evaluated for conformance" while the audit's manual results are empty, and a later claim must cite passed rows A1 onward; the earlier rule "names the Android SDK as not covered" became "has an Android SDK section". `docs/android/accessibility.md` was created, and `docs/android/README.md` gained its row. The shared test file `testing/Goldens.kt` gained `GoldenOwner.A11Y_AUDIT` for the new golden folder.
15. **The host tests' declared inputs.** `testAndroidHostTest` in `android/openverify-sdk/build.gradle.kts` also declares `docs/android/accessibility.md`, `docs/accessibility/android-audit-*.md` and `src/androidHostTest/screenshots`, which the new tests read from disk, so a change to one of them reruns the tests.
16. **The SDK's device suite is 11 tests in 5 classes, not 9 in 4.** `androidDeviceTest` gained `ui/CaptureScreenLayoutDeviceTest` (2 methods), which lays the capture screen out over the real CameraX preview at the device's font size and at 2.0. On a device with no camera it fails and never skips. It ran on the Pixel 9 only (2 of 2); its emulator run is a device proof left open. Every record that counts the suite, and validation-10d's final device run, uses 11.
17. **`docs/accessibility/statement.md` beyond the Android section.** Its date is 2026-11-11; the Scope's "Not covered" line no longer lists the Android SDK; the Known gaps row says the Android manual pass has not been run (KP10-17); the "Assistive technologies used" paragraph says the Android checks ran on the build host. The web's status sentence is unchanged, word for word.
18. **The audit's date is 2026-11-11** (`date -u +%F` when it was written), not the Workflow's start date 2026-11-10; the file is `docs/accessibility/android-audit-2026-11-11.md`.
19. **Measured, as the audit closed** (`e27108976`).
    - **Screens and states:** 20 screens and 181 states, held as code in `A11yCatalog`; the build fails when a flow state, NFC view, message enum value or golden on disk has no entry. 105 states have a golden of their own and 76 share a layout by name.
    - **Goldens:** 205 on disk (7,479,747 bytes): 113 new in `screenshots/android-a11y-audit/` (3,831,693 bytes) and the 92 earlier ones, which compare unchanged.
    - **How the checks ran:** on the host, under Robolectric with native graphics (spike (c)). `:openverify-sdk:screenshotTest -Proborazzi.test.verify=true` runs Google's Accessibility Test Framework 4.1.1 on every golden (95 tests, 49 to 58 s): 0 errors and 118 warnings left for a person (83 text contrast, most on disabled buttons; 35 duplicate speakable text on 10A's screen-step goldens). `:openverify-sdk:testAndroidHostTest` (group `ui`, package `dev.openverify.sdk.ui.a11y`) runs the SDK's own rules over every state as the activity draws it, on 360 x 800 dp: names 181, touch targets 181, heading first 362 (`en` and `ar`), error summary 2, status nodes 181, cut-off text 1,086 (181 states in `en`, `es`, `fr`, `de`, `ar` and `en-XA` at font scale 2.0), focusable 181, and the announcement (7), right-to-left (4), shutter (2), timing (3) and inventory (10) tests. The announcement, right-to-left, shutter and timing tests passed on their first run: they are regression tests of 10A to 10C's behaviour, not RED-first. One device test class ran on the Pixel 9 (Android 17) for the capture layout: the SDK suite was 11 of 11, read from the instrumentation's own output (Gradle still exits failed, KP10-31). No emulator and no CI run is evidence.
    - **Defects:** 4 found and 4 fixed test-first: the capture screen did not scroll, so at font scale 2.0 text was cut off and Cancel (sometimes the shutter) was unreachable in every language; the heading was not read first on 46 states (38 capture, 8 status); "Saving your consent" was not announced; texts were drawn outside the one text composable in 22 files. Left open: the wallet's expired-message wording (to fix-sdk, known gap K9), and the time warning over the heading and the shutter below the fold at 2.0 (to the manual pass and Phase 11).
    - **Host tests:** 2,651, none skipped (2,602 before; +49: `ui` 476 to 520, `flow` 401, `rest` 1,725 to 1,730). The SDK host-test floor becomes 2,651 when fix-records raises it in `ci.yml` and `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh` together.
    - **Manual part:** not run and never by an agent: 14 rows (A1 to A14) with an empty results table and 13 known gaps, carried to the release gate as KP10-17.
    - **Size:** unchanged within its 10% bound (arm64-v8a 3,378,401 download and 7,066,674 install bytes).

## Workflow 10D review amendments

Recorded by `fix-records` on 2026-11-11, as the plan owner of 10D's review, from the three fixers' reports and commits after the Stage 1 section (`bb2b31984`): `fix-sdk` (`fa7f7af9b` to `fe31e05b3`), `fix-server` (`31e17e3ad` to `d99cd2c94`) and `fix-records` (`fc5e3632e` onward). Evidence: `docs/tdd/phase10-fix-sdk.tdd.md`, `docs/tdd/phase10-fix-server.tdd.md`, `docs/tdd/phase10-fix-records.tdd.md`. Each item is a decision as built and binds validation-10d, 10E and Phase 11. No item rests on a CI run: none exists for these commits (10D decisions, item 3).

### fix-sdk

1. **KP10-32; amends Workflow 10C Stage 2, demo-app 2: `minifiedSmoke` is no longer "release exactly".** R8 minifies it as it does `release`, and it differs in two sets of keep rules that exist for the device test alone: the committed rule for the smoke package, and rules traced at build time by `:demo-app:traceSmokeTestKeepRules` (the Android Gradle plugin's bundled R8; no dependency added) for what the test's own classes name in the app's classpath. At `7d1f8d179` that is 153 classes (kotlin 71, okhttp3 41, okio 12, kotlinx.coroutines 9, androidx.annotation 8, others 12), kept with their names and their packages' names. The tracer refuses a rule naming `dev.openverify.sdk.`, `dev.openverify.demo.` outside the smoke package, or `kotlinx.serialization`. Measured: `release` 6,219 dex classes, `minifiedSmoke` 6,645 (6,251 before). What the smoke build no longer proves: that the members of those library classes which only the test calls can be removed or renamed. The SDK, the demo's screen and kotlinx.serialization are minified as in `release`, and `check-consumer-rules.py` keeps its negative control.
2. **KP10-32: a host check guards the device test's start.** `:demo-app:checkSmokeTestReferences` is a dependency of `assembleMinifiedSmokeAndroidTest`, so gate call (8) and every device-test compile run it: it fails when the minified app lacks a class or member the test's APK refers to. Three named allowances are not the app's: `android.test.`, `androidx.test.internal.runner.hidden.` and `androidx.concurrent.futures.SuspendToFutureAdapter`. No Gradle task name or module changed, so no workflow line changed.
3. **KP10-32, rule 12: `MinifiedSmokeTest` gained one assertion per method** (the launcher was destroyed after its result) and a lifecycle callback; nothing was removed. `SmokeBuildTest` gained one test, so the demo's debug host tests are 81.
4. **KP10-32, proved on one phone.** `MinifiedSmokeTest` ran 2 of 2 on a Pixel 9 (Android 17) at `7d1f8d179`, read from the instrumentation's XML. C215.2's device proof stands on that phone; its emulator run is open.
5. **KP10-31 stays open, with its cause found and not ours.** The Android Gradle plugin 9.4.1 looks a device up by its serial in a table keyed by JUnit's unique id, which encodes a colon, so on any serial holding a colon (every wireless adb connection) the connected task ends as failed whatever the tests did. Nothing was changed: no `ignoreFailures`, no retry. A device result on the phone is read from the XML or from `am instrument`. Not verified: a run on a serial without a colon.
6. **A002, C196.3: typed digits are normalised twice.** Arabic-Indic and Persian digits become ASCII in the field and again in `FlowCoordinator.confirmOtp`, `sendOtp` (the phone number) and `submitInputs`, for every `InputField` but `EMAIL_ADDRESS` and `WEBSITE_URL` (and the three chosen from lists) and for all three `PersonField` members. The email address and the website stay as typed, as in the web flow.
7. **A010, audit gap K9: an expired wallet message has its own button.** New internal `FlowActions.renewCryptoChallenge(walletAddress)` and `FlowCoordinator.renewCryptoChallenge` ask the server for a new message on the same chain, address and verification. Submit is not disabled on expiry: the server decides. New string `openverify_crypto_new_message` in `strings_crypto_wallet.xml` in the five folders, machine-drafted, with its `review-status.json` row. No public API, ABI dump or wire member changed.
8. **KP10-36: `ProtectionScanTest` counts 27 protected controls,** the decision's 26 and the wallet's "Get a new message" button.
9. **KP10-37: an unknown wire enum value's message names the enum only.** `FlowApiException.InvalidResponse.causeClass` stays a class name; a fixed code per failure kind is a public-API decision for Phase 11's freeze.
10. **KP10-38: the SDK's device suite is 14 tests in seven classes** (11 in five after the audit): `nfc/NfcPlatformDeviceTest` (2) and `nfc/NfcTapLifecycleDeviceTest` (1) joined, with the host class `NfcActivityTest` (4) driving the real activity. The lifecycle test's session is a copy of `contracts/fixtures/api/flow-session-nfc-sdk.json` under `src/androidDeviceTest/resources/nfc/`, held equal by `NfcActivityTest`: whoever changes that fixture copies it there in the same commit. The wiring needed no fix. Every record that counts the suite, and validation-10d's final device run, uses 14 and the demo's 2.
11. **K10A-2, C199.5, rule 12: a value shown inside a sentence is bidi-isolated at every call site.** 21 display call sites moved to `sdkText` and `sdkPluralText`; `SdkStringsSourceTest` refuses `sdkString` or `sdkPlural` there, with one named exception (the selfie warning's spoken text). Existing tests changed because the displayed text now carries U+2068 and U+2069 around each value (the `text()` and `plural()` helpers of five screen tests and two literal expectations). Nine audit goldens were recorded again by the explicit record call for a sub-pixel shift, and `wallet-message-expired` for item 7.
12. **KP10-35, part 1 done, part 2 to Phase 11.** `BarcodeGuidance.visible` starts false: the barcode guide is drawn, and a frame read, only while the frame can carry a barcode read, and nothing shows before the first frame. Three existing tests feed a real-sized frame first. Part 2 (a 640 px copy for the document gates on a 1,280 px frame) is not built: the committed vectors hold no paired 1,280 px and 640 px scene, so equal hints could only be shown by a tautology. The numbers stay carry-10c's (39.8 to 44.3 ms against 9.8 to 10.9 ms of host CPU; no phone figure).
13. **KP10-25: a selfie extension's outcome travels with the launch's own `SelfieCaptureWindow`** (new internal member `extension`); `extendCapture` and the coordinator's hook return the window. The process-wide `SelfieExtensions` object and `SelfieExtensionReport` are deleted. All internal; the ABI dumps are unchanged.
14. **K10A-3: `flow.copy_key_ignored` is logged once per launch.** "Once per session" is built as once per `FlowCoordinator`: it keeps one `CopyNotes` (a flag in memory, no key or id), and `ServerPosition.render` takes it.
15. **KP10-29: `StateHooksTest` pins the real integrity request** (a consented session that asks takes one challenge and sends the provider's token; one that does not ask sends none), in two tests where one stood.
16. **Counts at fix-sdk's close** (`7d1f8d179`): 2,677 SDK host tests, none skipped (`ui` 524, `flow` 414, `rest` 1,739); 95 screenshot tests over 205 goldens; the demo 81 and 8; on the phone the SDK suite 14 of 14 and the demo's smoke test 2 of 2.

### fix-server

1. **A001; C211.9, C211.1, C211.3: `notice.play-integrity` is also a run-time condition of the integrity request,** not only a publish rule. The session's `integrity.request` is true, and `POST /api/v1/flow/integrity-challenges` draws a challenge, only when the consent the session relies on showed `notice.play-integrity`, beside the earlier conditions (a bound provider, the session-integrity tier, an in-progress unredacted inquiry, no token and no verdict). A version published before a provider was bound, and every v1 flow whose consent lacks the key, is never asked; the challenge route answers 409 `integrity_challenge_invalid` and stores no challenge. Published versions are not re-validated when a provider is bound. No route schema, problem code, fixture or wire member changed.
2. **A001, rule 12: two existing tests changed with the behaviour.** In `routes/flow/integrity.test.ts` the default consent of a session that is asked shows both notices; in `integrity-request-fixtures.test.ts` `NOTICE_KEYS` gains `notice.play-integrity`. No assertion was removed. Internal signatures: `IntegrityRequestQuery.playIntegrityNotice`, `IntegritySessionMemberInput.consentTextKeys`, and the export `playIntegrityNoticeShown(textKeys)`.
3. **KP10-39; C207.2, C52.4 as built: a verification holds at most one file of each chip kind.** A second upload of a kind replaces the first (its row in the upload's transaction, its stored object after the commit), so after a refused submit and a new read the server's checks read the new read's files only, and the earlier read is not evidence of the result. The item's hypothesis (two files of a kind) was wrong in the safe direction; three Testcontainers cases pin it, with two negative controls run and restored. This closes "Open after 10C" item 17. `docs/checks/nfc.md` says so.
4. **KP10-40; amends Workflow 10C Stage 2, api-nfc item 12, and closes "Open after 10C" item 23.** The sandbox chip's Active Authentication response is signed by `testSignDigest` of `@openverify/emrtd/testing` (RFC 6979 through `@noble/curves`, plain r||s over the challenge's stored nonce SHA-256), loaded by the same lazy import as the chip builder. `signNonceDigest` and its integer arithmetic are deleted from `apps/api/src/services/sandbox-chip.ts`; item 12's sentence naming it is history. `@openverify/emrtd/testing` gains the export `testSignDigest`; no dependency was added, and the production path still never loads the testing module.
5. **Counts at fix-server's close:** the API unit suite 9,677 tests in 726 files at 94.41% (9,673 before); the API integration project 309 tests (304 before: `nfc-10c.integration.test.ts` holds 17, `notice-at-runtime.integration.test.ts` 2); `@openverify/emrtd` 332 tests in 19 files. `openapi:write` changes nothing.

### fix-records

1. **KP10-33, the infra job: gitleaks is installed before the CI workflow contract, and in CI a missing tool there is a failure.** `ci.yml`'s infra job runs the gitleaks cache and install steps before `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh`, whose release section scans a synthetic staged repository with gitleaks. That section's skip became `skip_unless_ci`: with `CI=true` a missing gitleaks or openssl fails the contract; on a developer machine it is still a skip. The contract holds the order. The other skips (actionlint, the remote tag check) are unchanged.
2. **KP10-33, the records.** `docs/android/nfc.md` and `docs/checks/nfc.md` say that `face-mismatch` needs the generated fixture set (`fixtures/generated/selfie/`, the file `head-turn-b-center.jpg`), not "fetched fixture faces". The plan's `## Validation` block was rewritten in place to the eleven gate calls and to `ci.yml`'s android job as it runs (the scripts' tests, `:openverify-nfc:assembleRelease`, `check-consumer-rules.py`, both floors, the `sdk,nfc` call); device tests are no longer called CI-only. The root `README.md` names `docs/android/`. `@openverify/node` is closed on evidence: `packages/node/package.json` has no generation script, and `packages/node/src` names none of `client-unable`, `sandbox-chip` or `read_failed` in any spelling (a grep with no match), so there was nothing to regenerate after `0a33b1f17`. The two CLAUDE.md lines are handed to the orchestrator (no agent edits that file).
3. **A006: a device run is held to a minimum per module.** `infra/scripts/check-connected-results.sh <dir> [<module>=<minimum>...]` also fails when a module's results hold fewer tests than its minimum; a module is the directory that holds `build/outputs/androidTest-results`. `ci.yml`'s emulator step and `android-release.yml`'s devices leg pass `openverify-sdk=14 demo-app=2`. `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh` counts the `@Test` methods in `android/openverify-sdk/src/androidDeviceTest` and `android/demo-app/src/androidTest` and holds both workflows to those numbers, so whoever adds or removes a device test changes the two workflow lines in the same commit. Without arguments the script is what it was. It was run on the phone's result files that fix-sdk's run left on disk (16 passed; a minimum of 15 for the SDK fails); no emulator has run it.
4. **A005, C219.6: the floors.** The SDK host-test floor is **2,677** (2,602, plus the audit's 49, plus fix-sdk's 26), measured again here with `--rerun` as three `-PhostTestGroup` calls: `ui` 524 (138 s), `flow` 414 (41 s), `rest` 1,739 (92 s), none skipped, load 0.3 to 2.9, no lock wait. The demo's floor is **81** on the debug variant and **8** smoke twins (36 s). In 10E and Phase 11 every floor only rises.
5. **A004: the android job's timeout is 60 minutes, and nothing is split.** A raise is not a split: Phase 8 F08's rule splits a job on a measured time, and this job has no measured whole time since 10B. `ci-workflow.test.sh` holds "at least 60". The table below says what a first run must show.
6. **The device suite's count in the records.** `docs/android/release-and-support.md` counts 14 SDK device tests in seven classes and the demo's two, says where they ran (one Pixel 9, Android 17) and where they did not (no emulator since before 10C's fixes, no other phone, neither workflow), records KP10-31's cause, and says how `minifiedSmoke` differs from `release` in the words of fix-sdk item 1. Rule 18's and rule 25's "9 tests in four classes" is superseded by 14 in seven.

**Phase 11 notes from the fixers, no defect today.**

19. When the best-effort storage delete of a replaced upload fails, ciphertext with no row stays in the bucket; it is unreadable (its data key went with the row) and no retention pass sweeps it. A sweep of objects without rows would be new behaviour.
20. Binding a `deviceIntegrity` provider does not re-validate published versions; a version without `notice.play-integrity` keeps working and its sessions are never asked for a token. Whether the console flags such versions is a product decision.

### Validation as run (validation-10d)

Recorded by `validation-10d`, which ran alone and last on `83b39eca5` on 2026-11-11. Each call, with its exit code, seconds, lock wait and load, is in `docs/tdd/phase10-validation-10d.tdd.md`. Binding on 10E and Phase 11. No item rests on a CI run: none exists for these commits and none is promised.

1. **The tree.** The list found no defect in the repository, so `validation-10d` made no code commit. Its commits are records: `e2e/README.md`'s Shards bullet (`cefe36f91`), its evidence file and this section. `git status` shows only the owner's untracked note.
2. **The list.** Every step ran in the order of Phase 9's S4B-16, S9C-13 and S9C-15 with Phase 10's steps, each call in the foreground with its exit code read from the command, and every step passed. No call waited for a lock or reached the 10-minute cap; no test was retried.
   - Unit tests: 34,962 in 17 packages, each at or above 80%. The API has 726 files and 9,677 tests at 94.41%, run as eight shards (139 to 162 s at a load of 7.3 to 12.7) and a 37 s merge. The dashboard has 581 files and 6,761 tests at 95.59%, in one call of 352 s.
   - Integration: db 83, graph 13 (template built in 15.7 s), and the api shards 99, 103 and 107 (309).
   - Vision: 1,423 and 1,212 tests, split at file 58, at 97.41% coverage.
   - Android, the eleven gate calls: 2,677 SDK host tests with `--rerun` (`ui` 524 in 138 s, `flow` 414 in 41 s, `rest` 1,739 in 92 s), none skipped, equal to the floor; the demo's 81 and 8; 95 screenshot tests over 205 goldens (7,481,824 bytes); 83 script tests; the 16 KB, consumer-rules and size checks (arm64-v8a 3,379,507 download and 7,070,770 install bytes for `sdk`, inside the 10% bound). Calls (2) and (4) to (11) found their outputs up to date from fix-records' session; item 3 is the pass that executed them.
   - OpenAPI: sha256 `cd54e616…dbee071a0`, unchanged by `openapi:write`; no diff in `docs/workflows`.
   - Images: apps, vision and code-runner. Infra tests: 11 scripts, 1,230 checks; `ci-workflow.test.sh` 379 with 0 skipped (gitleaks, openssl and actionlint on PATH); `install-gitleaks.test.sh` 31, with no release-host error.
   - gitleaks: 6,630 commits, no leaks.
3. **KP10-34 is done: the android job's steps pass from a clean tree and a cold Gradle home.** After `./gradlew clean` and the removal of `android/build` and `android/consumer-check/build`, from a new empty `GRADLE_USER_HOME` with dependency verification on and a new project cache directory, `ci.yml`'s android job ran in its order and every step exited 0: the bundletool fetch, the scripts' tests, the gate line, the screenshot and release step, the three static checks, both floors (2,677; 81 and 8; none skipped) and the `sdk,nfc` manifest call. So did the steps of `android-release.yml`'s gate job that need no device and no secret: the device-test compile, the `sdk,nfc` bundle and its size, the staged repository, the consumer check and the secret scan of the staged repository. No step read a file that no earlier step of its job had built, so no workflow changed.

The second step fits one call (171 s). The Gradle calls of both jobs took 1,521 s here, downloads included; that is a local figure, not a CI time.
   - **The consumer check is proved cold.** `android/consumer-check`'s lockfiles and verification metadata resolve from the fresh home after `01464231e` (47 tasks executed). This closes the part of KP10-33 that fix-records left to this pass.
   - **Not run:** the release workflow's version check (it needs the dispatch input), its wrapper-JAR check and upload (a runner), and its `devices` legs (an emulator).

That job proves the consumer rules on `release` only; the release workflow's gate job and the local gate, where call (8) precedes call (9), check both. Building the smoke variant in that job is a Phase 11 decision, made with the job's first whole time.

## Orchestrator decisions before Workflow 10E (2026-11-11)

Made by the orchestrator (Claude Code, claude-fable-5-2) after re-checking the 10E script against Workflows 10B, 10C and 10D as built at `ced4603e9` (memos `resume/tasks/2026-11-11-recheck-10e-after-10d.memo.md` and `2026-11-11-known-10e.memo.md`). This section's commit is 10E's `start`. Where an item says "decision n", it means the script's header decision of that number.

1. **Base and start.** 10E's `base` is `b826da9b5`, the commit of 10D's PROGRESS entry; this section's commit is its `start`, required. It is not pushed alone: it rides with 10E's one push.
2. **The 10D push's CI** (run 43018061783 on `b826da9b5`): no job started. Fifteen jobs ended failed with no step run and three were skipped, within seconds of the push (the account's spending limit). It spent no minutes and proves nothing; it is not a "completed run with a failed job" in the sense of item 5, and `args.ciFailures` is empty. The orchestrator's `validate-full` on `ced4603e9` passed 25 of 25 rows.
3. **Args (decision 1).** `{base, start, prior, known, owner, namespace, ciFailures, device}`. `base` is the commit of 10D's PROGRESS entry; this section's commit is `start`, required. `owner` is `{P1: granted, P2: granted, P3: declined, P4: pending, P5: declined}`, `namespace` and `ciFailures` are empty, `device` is the phone's adb serial or `''`. Known items are handed to the 10E agent their `owner` names; an item whose owner's part is not built goes to integration-10e; `deferred` items are carried, not fixed.
4. **No CI run to count on, and the phone (decision 2).** 10D's rules 24 and 25 hold for 10E unchanged (rules 26 and 27 in its script). Who uses the phone: nfc-reader for openverify-nfc's device tests (the NFC adapter on, no chip present); face-onnx for openverify-face's; integration-10e, alone and last, for both existing suites, the new modules' device tests and one demo flow. Reviewers, contracts-10e and any other builder never. The device lock serialises the two builders.
5. **This amends the 10E section and its acceptance.** "10E runs after 10D is pushed": contracts-10e checks 10D's PROGRESS entry and this section, not a push or a CI run. "The CI of the 10D push": none is expected; only a completed run with a failed job is a first fix. "On the emulator" (the YuNet and telemetry tests): on a device, here the Pixel 9, with the README naming it; the emulator runs are "device proof open" lines. "Its device test covers the no-NFC-adapter path": the test asserts what the device reports and never skips; on the Pixel 9 it proves reader mode with no chip present, and its no-adapter branch stays open. "The full validation and the release matrix": the matrix waits on Actions minutes, recorded open, not attempted.
6. **The clean-build pass (decision 3).** integration-10e's Validation has 10D's step 9b: the android job's steps of `ci.yml` and the gate steps of `android-release.yml` that need no device and no secret, once, from `./gradlew clean` and a fresh `GRADLE_USER_HOME`, split to fit 10-minute calls as validation-10d ran it. It is the cold proof of every lockfile and verification entry 10E adds.
7. **The Phase 10 close (decision 4).** integration-10e drafts, and the orchestrator commits, two PROGRESS entries: the phase close, whose heading is exactly `## <date>: Phase 10 done (<summary>)`, status "done locally; CI pending", with the release-gate list (the manual TalkBack pass KP10-17, the fluent text review KP10-16, P4, the release matrix, the emulator proofs, the owner's real-document chip read, Actions minutes); then the Workflow 10E entry.
8. **The push (decision 5).** Nobody pushes, dispatches or re-runs. The push is the orchestrator's, once, after `validate-full`.
9. **New dependencies (decision 6).** Licences by CLAUDE.md rule 4 and P1's and P2's narrow grants: LGPL code and SCUBA only inside `android/openverify-nfc`; Eigen only inside `onnxruntime-android` in `android/openverify-face`. Every Gradle artifact is pinned to one exact version. Lockfiles and `verification-metadata.xml`, and `android/consumer-check`'s own three files, are written from a cold Gradle home (the recipe comment in `android/build.gradle.kts`). The face model is the one `models/manifest.json` pins; any other weight comes only through `scripts/fetch_*` with a pinned SHA-256.
10. **A real document.** A real passport or identity-card chip read needs a real document held to the phone by a person. No agent does it, asks for it or waits for it (decision 48, C209.6). 10E proves the reader on the host against JMRTD's and ICAO's test vectors and the synthetic Utopia chip, which is also the server's sandbox chip (`testSignDigest`).
11. **The Android gate as 10D left it.** Eleven calls; the floors are 2,677 and 81 + 8; the device minimums are `openverify-sdk=14 demo-app=2`. 10E adds, in order: each new library's `assembleRelease` to call (5), its device-test assemble task to call (8), and one merged-manifest call per new demo configuration to call (11). A check that reads a build output counts only after the call that builds it ran in the same session (KP10-34, now standing).
12. **Where 10E plugs in, as built.** `DemoComponents.of` is the one place the demo installs `chipReader` and `faceDetectorFactory`; it calls one hook function per artifact. Each new module is one line in `SdkModule.ALL` and gets rows in `ExceptionCanaryTest`; openverify-nfc gets its rows with its first Kotlin source. `check-consumer-rules.py` learns each new AAR; `check-size.py` learns each new configuration and the `sdk,nfc` label loses "without the reader". The smoke build's traced keep rules and `checkSmokeTestReferences` must hold after every new dependency; a failure is never answered by relaxing minification, an SDK consumer rule or a keep rule naming the SDK.
13. **New UI.** 10E is expected to add no SDK screen, state or text. If a fix does, it enters `A11yCatalog` with a golden or a `variantOf`, its text goes through `ui/text/SdkText.kt`, and the build fails otherwise.
14. **The workflows.** integration-10e writes them as if they ran: the YuNet fetch before Gradle, the new modules' host tests, floors and device tests, a `<module>=<n>` minimum per new module with `ci-workflow.test.sh`'s `@Test` count extended in the same commit. No job is split on an estimate. validation-10d's followup (the per-push android job builds no minifiedSmoke before `check-consumer-rules.py`) is taken only if integration-10e's own edit of that Gradle line makes it free; otherwise it is Phase 11's, with the job's first whole time.
15. **CLAUDE.md.** No agent edits it. integration-10e drafts the rule 4 wording of P1's and P2's exceptions; the orchestrator applies it.
16. **The licence rows moved.** `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`: the section 5 Eigen row is line 532 and the SCUBA row line 424.
17. **K10A-1** goes to contracts-10e (as `known-10e.json` names it), test-first, with the floor kept at or above 2,677.
18. **The ORT spike.** `~/Android/Sdk` has no NDK and the orchestrator installs none. face-onnx records "not measured: no Android NDK" and the official AAR stays (C205.5).
19. **Known items (33, `args.known`).** contracts-10e: K10A-1, KP10-41, KP10-42, KP10-44, KP10-45, KP10-47, KP10-49. nfc-reader: KP10-43. integration-10e: KP10-46, KP10-48, KP10-50. Deferred, built by nobody in 10E and reported by nobody as a finding: KP10-31, KP10-51 to KP10-57, KP10-35 part 2, KP10-24, KP10-26, KP10-19, KP10-27, KP10-28, KP10-30, KP10-15, KP10-17, KP10-16, KP10-58 to KP10-61.

The SDK floor stays at or above 2,677.
21. **10D's LOW review findings, recorded here because 10D fixed none by its rule.** A003 (`docs/android/nfc.md:27`: nine challenges are counted per run of the step, in memory) is KP10-48, integration-10e's. A008 (Espresso 3.7.0's licence row) is KP10-47 and A011 (the statement's "three defects" is four) is KP10-41, both contracts-10e's. A009 (`explicitApi()` in openverify-nfc) is contracts-10e's deliverable. A007 (no retry or cache on the bundletool fetch) and A012 (the font-scale sweep exempts controls inside a scroll area) go to Phase 11 as KP10-52 and KP10-53.
22. **KP10-42.** Each new module enters `SdkModule.ALL`, and a module with sources has its rows in `ExceptionCanaryTest` through an extension point its builder fills from its own paths. No module is exempted.
23. **KP10-43.** nfc-reader changes `test_check_consumer_rules.py`'s "ships no rule" test first (a named borrowed edit), then fills `openverify-nfc/consumer-rules.pro`. The rules are proved on the minified `sdk,nfc` demo, and the smoke build's traced rules still refuse the SDK, the demo's screen and kotlinx.serialization.
24. **KP10-44.** The commit that adds a dependency refreshes every lockfile and both verification-metadata files, `android/consumer-check`'s included, from a fresh `GRADLE_USER_HOME`. The consumer check also resolves `openverify-face`. integration-10e proves it again from clean in step 9b.

## Workflow 10E Stage 1a amendments (contracts-10e)

Recorded by contracts-10e on 2026-11-11, from the code as built at `0e5a5ec68` and from what Stage 1a measured (`docs/research/phase10-inventory.md`, section 11; `docs/tdd/phase10-contracts-10e.tdd.md`). Binding on Stage 2, the reviewers and integration-10e; where an item differs from a contract above, the item wins.

1. **Owner items and the base.** Phase 10 P1 and P2 are granted narrowly, P3 and P5 declined, P4 pending, as launched. Parts built: `face-onnx` and `nfc-reader`. No `openverify-integrity-play` module, configuration, allowlist, size row or publication exists, and no `scuba-fork`. The base and start checks held. CI run 43018061783 on the base started no step and is not a failure of the code.
2. **C205.2, first layer, changed: no `tools:selector`, and the permission is the host's.** Measured on AGP 9.4.1: a remove marker that carries `tools:selector` rejects the named library's element but stays in the merged manifest as a declaration of its own (a provider with no authorities, which no device installs; the permission still declared). So:
   - `openverify-face`'s manifest removes `ai.onnxruntime.TelemetryInitializer` with `tools:node="remove"` and **no selector**. No other library declares that provider, so this is safe for every host.
   - `openverify-face` does **not** remove `ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE`. A library's marker without a selector would also take the permission from another library of the host that is merged after it (review P10), and one with a selector keeps it declared. A host with no other use for it removes it in its own manifest, with no selector; the module's README says so, and the demo does it (`demo-app/src/face/AndroidManifest.xml`).
   - The package of ONNX Runtime's library manifest is `ai.onnxruntime` (review V14). It is recorded and held by `OrtManifestTest`, which reads the AAR's manifest as the build resolved it; nothing uses it as a selector.
   - **For face-onnx:** the fourth layer's merge test proves the provider is gone, that this module's manifest leaves a fake library's `ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE` alone, and that a host's marker removes it. The device test's own APK is a host: its manifest removes the permission the same way. **For integration-10e:** C213.5's permission lists, `docs/android/privacy.md`, `sizes.md` and the SDK README say that `ACCESS_NETWORK_STATE` arrives with `openverify-face` from ONNX Runtime's manifest unless the host removes it, and give the line.
3. **ORT's notices are not in the AAR.** `onnxruntime-android-1.31.0.aar` holds no third-party notices file (only `META-INF/LICENSE-1DS` in `classes.jar`). ORT's `LICENSE` and `ThirdPartyNotices.txt` are copied by command from the `v1.31.0` tag (commit `402979ac`) to `android/openverify-face/licenses/onnxruntime/`. face-onnx packages both into the AAR. Eigen's source location is revision `1d8b82b0740839c0de7f1242a3585e3390ff5f33` of `https://github.com/eigen-mirror/eigen` (ORT's `cmake/deps.txt` at the tag), in the module's `NOTICE` and in `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` section 6.
4. **`THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`.** Section 2.1.20 tables what Stage 1a adopted, with Espresso 3.7.0 (KP10-47). A new **section 6, "Owner exceptions to rule 4 (shipped code)"**, holds P1's two SCUBA files, P2's Eigen in `onnxruntime-android`, and the two rows P2 also settles (Eigen in `onnxruntime-web`, moved out of section 5, and in the vision image's `onnxruntime` wheel). Section 2.2's three "planned" rows point at 2.1.20.
5. **The pins** are the plan's, each at least 14 days old on 2026-11-11 (inventory 11.4). Added in test scope: `com.microsoft.onnxruntime:onnxruntime` 1.31.0 (the host twin of the inference) and `com.android.tools.build:manifest-merger` 32.4.1 (the merge test). Not adopted: the AndroidX test orchestrator (the telemetry test restarts through a second process declared in its own test manifest) and a test-only library module.
6. **cert-cvc's `bcprov-jdk15on` 1.68 is excluded** on `jmrtd` and `cert-cvc` in `openverify-nfc` (the same classes as `bcprov-jdk18on`); the exclusions travel in the POM.
7. **A host of `openverify-nfc` needs one packaging line.** Both Bouncy Castle jars carry `META-INF/LICENSE.md`, and the packager refuses two files at one path: `packaging { resources { pickFirsts += "META-INF/LICENSE.md" } }`. `openverify-nfc` (for its device-test APK), the demo and the consumer check have it; the consumer check therefore shows the one line a host writes. nfc-reader keeps it in the module's README; integration-10e adds it to `docs/android/nfc.md` and the SDK README. C215.4's "host apps need no rules of their own" still holds for R8 rules; this is a packaging rule.
8. **The modules.** `android/openverify-face` (namespace `dev.openverify.face`, Apache-2.0, `api(project(":openverify-sdk"))`, `implementation` ONNX Runtime) and `android/openverify-nfc` (namespace `dev.openverify.nfc`, LGPL-3.0-or-later, `api(project(":openverify-sdk"))`, `implementation` the reader's stack). Both: `explicitApi()`, Kover at 80% with one exclusion line in `kover-excludes/<builder>.txt` (the build refuses a second), `consumer-rules.pro` wired, `checkKotlinAbi` and `updateKotlinAbi` by BCV's tasks, the publication block, a lockfile. `openverify-nfc` opts in to `ExperimentalOpenVerifyApi` module-wide.
9. **Public factories, frozen:** `public object OpenVerifyNfc { public fun chipReader(context: Context): ChipReader }` and `public object OpenVerifyFace { public fun detectorFactory(context: Context): FaceDetectorFactory }`. Each returns a stand-in until its builder replaces it: the reader answers every read with `ChipReadException(READER_BUSY)`; the factory's `create()` throws `IllegalStateException` with a fixed message, which the selfie screen treats as "no detector". A signature changes only through integration-10e, with the dump.
10. **`ModelSource`** is `internal interface ModelSource { val name: String; val sha256: String; fun open(): InputStream }` in `dev.openverify.face` (C204.2; review V11).
11. **Each artifact's logger.** `SdkLog` is internal to the SDK, so `dev.openverify.nfc.NfcLog` and `dev.openverify.face.FaceLog` each hold the same grammar and the debuggable-host rule (`attach(context)`, `log(code)`). `LogScanTest` holds both files and every call of them, and allows one more file to name `java.util.logging`: `openverify-nfc/src/main/kotlin/dev/openverify/nfc/LibraryLoggers.kt`, which may only set levels (C209.2). `NeverReadTest` names the two loggers as readers of the debuggable flag.
12. **KP10-42, the exception tables.** Each optional artifact tables every exception its main sources create or throw on in `src/test/resources/privacy/exception-rows.txt` (`file | call | message`). `ExceptionCanaryTest` holds the table to the sources both ways and each row to the rule (`privacy/ModuleExceptions.kt`): a message is one string literal with no value in it, a `ChipReadException` takes one `ChipReadFailure` constant, no cause is passed, and only an exception caught as a `CancellationException` or a `ChipReadException` is thrown on. A builder changes its own table, never the SDK's test. What a library throws through the artifact's code is not visible to this scan: the reader's own tests prove it maps every library exception to a `ChipReadException`.
13. **`DataAtRestScanTest`** no longer reads a `<provider ... tools:node="remove" />` marker as a declared provider; a declared one is still refused.
14. **The demo (KP10-45).** `openverifyDemoArtifacts` accepts `sdk`, `sdk,nfc`, `sdk,face` and `sdk,nfc,face`. One hook per artifact in `dev.openverify.demo.artifacts`: `src/nfc/kotlin/.../NfcArtifact.kt` (nfc-reader's) or the stub in `src/withoutNfc/`, `src/face/kotlin/.../FaceArtifact.kt` (face-onnx's) or the stub in `src/withoutFace/`. `DemoApplication` calls `DemoComponents.attach`, which asks the hooks once; `DemoComponents.of` keeps its signature. `src/face/AndroidManifest.xml` (face-onnx's) is merged only with `face`. The reader-check mode's location is `src/readerCheck/` (kotlin, res and `AndroidManifest.xml`; nfc-reader's), compiled into the `debug` build type only, and only with `nfc`.
15. **Lockfiles per demo configuration** (C214.5): `demo-app/gradle.lockfile` for `sdk`, `gradle-sdk-nfc.lockfile`, `gradle-sdk-face.lockfile` and `gradle-sdk-nfc-face.lockfile` for the others, written by `:demo-app:resolveAndLockAll --write-locks -PopenverifyDemoArtifacts=<artifacts>`. One lockfile cannot serve them: the default lock mode fails on a locked module that a configuration does not resolve.
16. **KP10-44.** The consumer check resolves `openverify-face` too. Every lockfile, `android/gradle/verification-metadata.xml` (34 components added, none removed) and the consumer check's three files were written from a fresh `GRADLE_USER_HOME` in a scratch tree and proved by the gate's calls from a second fresh home with verification on.
17. **Manifest allowlists:** `sdk-face.json` equals `sdk.json` but for its name; `sdk-nfc-face.json` equals `sdk-nfc.json` but for its name. Nothing beyond C213.5's lists reaches a merged manifest of the demo. `scuba-sc-android`'s manifest asks only for `NFC`, which the module declares already.
18. **Release checks.** `check-consumer-rules.py` reads `LIBRARIES` (the SDK, `openverify-nfc`, `openverify-face`; each AAR must ship its committed rules). `check-staged-repository.py` expects four modules and holds each optional artifact's POM to `RESERVED_FOR`: the reader's stack only in `openverify-nfc`, ONNX Runtime only in `openverify-face`, no `com.google.android.play` module anywhere, no optional artifact naming another. Both workflows build `:openverify-face:assembleRelease` before the consumer-rules check. Left to integration-10e: `check-size.py`'s configurations and labels, the baseline, the YuNet fetch step, the new modules' floors and device-test minimums.
19. **The gate's placeholders** (rule 17): `$JVM_MODULE_TESTS` is empty (`testDebugUnitTest` runs both modules' host tests); `$MODULE_RELEASE_AARS` is `:openverify-face:assembleRelease`; `$MODULE_DEVICE_TESTS` is `:openverify-nfc:assembleDebugAndroidTest :openverify-face:assembleDebugAndroidTest`; `$STAGED_CHECKS` is empty; `$DEMO_CONFIGURATIONS` is `sdk,face` and `sdk,nfc,face`. The connected tasks are `:openverify-nfc:connectedDebugAndroidTest` and `:openverify-face:connectedDebugAndroidTest`, with results under `android/<module>/build/outputs/androidTest-results/connected/`.
20. **Counts at the end of Stage 1a:** 2,690 SDK host tests (ui 522, flow 416, rest 1,752), none skipped; the demo's 88 and 8; `openverify-nfc` 11; `openverify-face` 11; 95 screenshot tests over 205 goldens; 89 release-check tests; `ci-workflow.test.sh` 381 checks. `A11yCatalog` holds **180 states of 20 screens** (K10A-1; the Stage 1 amendments' "181" is the count at 10D). The floors in `ci.yml` (2,677; 81 and 8) are unchanged and below what runs; integration-10e raises them and gives each new module its own.
21. **K10A-1, done.** `FlowState.Finalizing`, `FinalizingScreen`, their mappings, the two strings in five languages with their review rows, three tests and the inventory row are gone; the screen's label is "Loading and processing"; the audit's counts follow (180; C4 360; C7 1,080). `A11yInventoryTest`'s guard that the sealed type was read is "at least 21 flow states". No golden changed. KP10-16's list loses `openverify_finalizing_title` and `openverify_finalizing_message`.
22. **The synthetic chip** is `contracts/fixtures/emrtd/utopia-chip.json`, written by `packages/emrtd/src/testing/utopia-chip-export.test.ts` (assert-only). It holds no private value: Active Authentication responses are precomputed for four fixed nonces, so the JVM responder answers INTERNAL AUTHENTICATE for those nonces only, and a reader test passes one of them as its challenge. `openverify-nfc`'s host tests find the directory through the system property `openverify.fixtures.emrtd`. No Android fixture list reads it.
23. **Existing tests changed, each because the plan changes what it checks:** the `Finalizing` cases and operands of K10A-1; `A11yInventoryTest`'s guard (22 to 21); `ModuleBoundaryTest`'s list of optional modules; `ExceptionCanaryTest`'s "a module without rows" case, replaced by the tables; `DemoComponentsTest`'s "no chip reader ..." case, renamed "before the artifacts are attached ..."; `DemoArtifactsTest` and `MergedManifestTest`, extended to four configurations; `test_check_staged_repository.py`'s synthetic repository, which gains `openverify-face`.

## Workflow 10E Stage 2 amendments

Recorded by integration-10e on 2026-11-11 from the Stage 2 reports (`docs/tdd/phase10-face-onnx.tdd.md`, `docs/tdd/phase10-nfc-reader.tdd.md`) and the code as built. Binding; where an item differs from a contract above, the item wins.

### face-onnx (`android/openverify-face`, P2 granted)

1. **C205.2, first layer, one more limit.** A library's remove marker reaches only manifests merged after it. A host that itself depends on ONNX Runtime and declares it ahead of `openverify-face` keeps `ai.onnxruntime.TelemetryInitializer` unless its own manifest removes it. `MergedManifestTest` holds both cases; the module's README, the SDK README and `docs/android/privacy.md` say so.
2. **C205.2, fourth layer, is two device tests.** One needs no network: no ONNX Runtime telemetry class loaded (read from the process's own heap dump, because `ClassLoader.findLoadedClass` loads what it is asked about on Android), `ORT_DISABLE_TELEMETRY` set, no new file, in a new process and again after a restart. The other counts the app UID's bytes from before a new process through the first environment, inference, close and a 120 s quiet period, twice, against a 30 s control of the runner's own traffic; it asserts the device awake and not dozing at each end, because a dozing phone restricts the network and a zero would be void. The quiet period is never shortened.
3. **A host-created `OrtEnvironment`** is detected through public API and recorded as the log code `face.ort_environment_host_created`; no public event kind exists for it. `setTelemetry(false)` is still called before each of the module's sessions, which switches telemetry off for the process: the README tells a host so.
4. **C205.4: one `OrtSession` per detector,** which the SDK creates once per selfie prompt, not once per capture screen. The factory caches no session.
5. **C204.8 against "buffers reused":** the native input and output buffers are reused per frame size and zeroed before `detect` returns; the arrays handed to the SDK are new per frame and not retained. A release call on the seam, if the allocation matters, is Phase 11's.
6. **C219.5: the tests carry a reference copy of the SDK's YuNet input and decoding** (`src/sharedTest/YunetReference.kt`), because `AnalysisFrame`, `YunetTensor` and `YunetDecoder` are internal to the SDK. The copy is held to committed vectors; nothing compares it with the SDK's own code. An SDK host test over `contracts/fixtures/flow/yunet-face-frame-vectors.json` is Phase 11's.
7. **C219.5: the tolerance is the vector file's own** (0.25 px, 0.005 score, 0.005 yaw ratio; raw outputs 1e-4), not C204.1's 1e-9, which compares two decoders on identical floats. Provisional, with the measured basis in the file.
8. **C219.2: no Kover exclusion is used.** The session glue is covered on the host by ONNX Runtime's JVM build of the same version.
9. **C215.1: one consumer rule,** `-keep class ai.onnxruntime.** { *; }`: without it R8 renamed `OrtException` and removed `TensorInfo` in the minified demo. `test_check_consumer_rules.py` pins it and refuses a rule naming `dev.openverify` or kotlinx.serialization.
10. **The notices travel as assets** under `assets/openverify-face/` (`NOTICE`, ONNX Runtime's `LICENSE.txt` and `ThirdPartyNotices.txt`, YuNet's `LICENSE.txt` and the module's `NOTICE.txt` for the model), about 0.3 MB in a host's APK.
11. **The build file** gained `-PopenverifyModelsDir`, `src/sharedTest/kotlin` in both test source sets, the system property `openverify.face.libraryManifest`, more test inputs, and a component metadata rule that gives Guava's Android variants the JRE jar for the manifest merger in test scope. No dependency, lockfile, publication or ABI setting changed.
12. **Before any Gradle call that builds the module:** `python3 -I scripts/fetch_models.py --only face_detection_yunet` and `python3 -I scripts/fetch_fixtures.py --only person_a_id`. The Validation block's fetches cover the first; the second is added to it.
13. **`uv run pytest tests/test_android_yunet_vectors.py` alone needs `--no-cov`** (the vision project's 80% gate applies to a whole run).
14. **C205.5: the custom-build spike was not started** (no Android NDK on the build host). Recorded "not measured"; the official AAR ships.

### nfc-reader (`android/openverify-nfc`, P1 granted)

1. **C207.4, C209.2: EF.CardSecurity is not read.** JMRTD 0.8.9 gives no secured route to a master-file file after PACE, and no hand-written protected command ships untested. The reader sends seven of the eight file kinds the server takes; the server takes `chip-card-security` only when a client sends it. Reading it is Phase 11's, once a responder implements PACE.
2. **C209.3: PACE and Chip Authentication run in no automated test.** The in-process responder implements Basic Access Control, secure messaging, the reads and INTERNAL AUTHENTICATE for the fixture's four nonces; the fixture has no EF.CardAccess and no Chip Authentication key. Every page that describes the reader says so.
3. **C209.3: the device test asserts per device.** With an adapter that is on (the Pixel 9) it proves reader mode for the resumed screen with no chip present; its no-adapter and NFC-off branch has run on no device.
4. **C209.2: Bouncy Castle is not passed as a provider object:** JMRTD 0.8.9 makes its own and takes none. The test asserts that `Security.getProviders()` is the same before and after a read.
5. **C209.2: response checksums are not checked on the device** (`shouldCheckMAC = false`, JMRTD's own default). The server's Passive Authentication holds the bytes. The READMEs and `docs/android/nfc.md` say so.
6. **C209.4, KP10-43: six consumer rules:** keeps for `org.jmrtd.**`, `net.sf.scuba.**`, `org.ejbca.cvc.**`, `org.bouncycastle.jcajce.provider.**` and `org.bouncycastle.jce.provider.**`, and `-keepnames` for `org.bouncycastle.**`. Proved on the minified `sdk,nfc` demo (6,192 to 9,813 dex classes; no library class renamed); the smoke build's traced rules and reference check still hold.
7. **C209.4: the licence texts are Java resources** under `META-INF/openverify-nfc/` in the AAR's `classes.jar` (LGPL-2.1, LGPL-3.0 with the GPL-3.0 it adds to, GPL-2.0, the Classpath exception, `SOURCE.txt`, `NOTICE`), each third-party text copied by command from SPDX license-list-data v3.27.0. The resource `NOTICE` is a copy of the module's, held equal by a test.
8. **C209.6: the reader check** is `dev.openverify.demo.readercheck.ReaderCheckActivity`, in `src/readerCheck/`, compiled only into debug builds with `nfc`, with its own launcher entry "OpenVerify reader check". It uses the installed reader with a random local challenge, sends and stores nothing, and zeroes what it read. Its no-network test installs a default `ProxySelector` that records and throws, because `FlowTransport`'s constructor is internal.
9. **The reader's constants** (provisional, each with its basis in KDoc): 5 s per exchange, 1,024-byte blocks on an extended-length tag (224 otherwise), 5 minutes of waiting for a document, 5 minutes for a read once a chip answers. The three durations are classified in `docs/android/accessibility.md` and `A11yTimingTest` (integration-10e).
10. **The route from reader to activity** needed no public change: the reader keeps the application context and follows the resumed activity through `Application.ActivityLifecycleCallbacks`.

## Workflow 10E review amendments

Two read-only reviewers (licence and security; correctness): 5 findings, CRITICAL 0, HIGH 0, MEDIUM 3, LOW 2. There was no separate verifier; integration-10e confirmed each by reading the code. Four are fixed test-first, one is carried to the owner, none was refuted. Evidence: `docs/tdd/phase10-integration-10e.tdd.md`, section 2.

1. **E001 (MEDIUM), fixed** (`287d4ef85`, `3de1a525d`). `android/scripts/check-staged-repository.py` opens each optional artifact's staged AAR and fails unless its licence papers are there, byte for byte the committed files (`LICENCE_PAPERS`: six in `openverify-nfc`'s `classes.jar`, five among `openverify-face`'s assets), and unless each committed third-party text has its recorded SHA-256 (`PINNED_TEXTS`). Binding: a change to an artifact's notices changes `LICENCE_PAPERS` in the same commit; a change to a third-party text changes its pin with the reason. Tests: five new cases in `test_check_staged_repository.py`.
2. **E002 (MEDIUM), carried to the owner.** `contracts/fixtures/flow/yunet-face-frame-vectors.json` holds one box, five landmarks and a yaw ratio computed on the public-domain portrait `person_a_id`, as this plan's 10E acceptance asks ("a public-domain fixture frame ... within the tolerance of the vectors"). Whether detector numbers of a public-domain portrait may stay in git is the owner's decision. Until it is made: no other photograph's numbers are added, and the fixture tool's comment names the file and the open decision (`2daf68101`). If the owner declines, the vectors are rebuilt on a drawn synthetic face and the device test reads that frame.
3. **E004 (MEDIUM), fixed** (`ba1546afd`, `0b03d8b6b`). An optional file (EF.CardAccess, EF.COM, DG14, DG15) that the chip says is empty is read as absent. An EF.CardAccess that cannot be parsed offers no PACE: the MRZ key is tried with Basic Access Control and the file is not returned; a card access number then has nothing to open (`unsupported_document`); a lost chip is `tag_lost` as before. The size ceiling stays a hard refusal for every file, and an empty required file is still `unsupported_document`. Tests: four new cases in `ChipReadOrchestratorTest`.
4. **E003 (LOW), fixed** (`ab5058cad`). `LogScanTest` fails when `LibraryLoggers.kt` is missing instead of returning. No RED run exists: it would need another owner's file moved.
5. **E005 (LOW), fixed** (`ba1546afd`, `0b03d8b6b`). The reader names the activity before it asks for reader mode, clears the name when the platform refuses, and turns reader mode off again when the activity was left meanwhile. Test: `NfcChipReaderTest`, "a screen left while reader mode is being turned on does not keep it on".

## Workflow 10E Stage 4 amendments (integration-10e)

Recorded by integration-10e on 2026-11-11. Evidence: `docs/tdd/phase10-integration-10e.tdd.md`. Binding on Phase 11 and on whoever closes the phase.

### What was settled

1. **The Stage 2 blockers.** `A11yTimingTest` classifies the reader's three durations with their row in `docs/android/accessibility.md` (`d2166e8d1`); `test_openverify_face_ships_no_rule_of_its_own_yet` is replaced by the pin of the one rule (`d90b8132d`); the demo's face lines say the build holds the face detector (`32ffa9448`, `cfe1a9ef1`).
2. **C216.2, C216.3: sizes.** `check-size.py` knows four configurations (`sdk`, `sdk,nfc`, `sdk,face`, `sdk,nfc,face`); the `sdk,nfc` label lost "without the reader"; a bundle is recognised by the NFC permission and by `libonnxruntime.so` among its entries. The baseline was re-measured for all four with bundletool 1.18.3 (`docs/android/sizes.md` holds the table; `SizesPageTest` holds the page to the file). ONNX Runtime's measured cost replaces the "about 34 MB" label: on `arm64-v8a`, +13.2 MB to download and +34.7 MB installed, `libonnxruntime.so` 34.2 MB uncompressed.
3. **C216.2: the absolute cap exists.** `CAPS` in `check-size.py`, per configuration and for every ABI: the largest ABI's measured figure plus 25% (`CAP_MARGIN_PERCENT`), rounded up to a whole MiB: `sdk` 5 MiB to download and 9 installed; `sdk,nfc` 6 and 14; `sdk,face` 23 and 58; `sdk,nfc,face` 25 and 63. The basis is the official ONNX Runtime AAR, since the spike was not measured. A baseline change cannot move a cap; a cap changes only with a recorded reason. Provisional.
4. **C216.1: ONNX Runtime's 16 KB alignment** is checked on a demo build with `face`, because `openverify-face`'s own AAR holds no native library: `android-release.yml`'s gate gives the `sdk,nfc,face` APK and bundle to `check-elf-alignment.py`, and the local gate does the same. The per-push job checks the default configuration only; a per-push face build was not added (an R8 run in a job with no measured whole time).
5. **C220: the workflows, written as if they ran** (`fc3f7a6e3`, `89e01b9d9`; `infra/tests/ci-workflow.test.sh` 381 to 409 checks, 0 skipped). `ci.yml`'s android job fetches YuNet and `person_a_id` before Gradle, holds the SDK's floor at **2,691**, the demo's at **97 and 9**, a new step holds **116** for `openverify-nfc` and **51** for `openverify-face`, and three merged-manifest steps run last (`sdk,nfc`, `sdk,face`, `sdk,nfc,face`). `android-instrumented` fetches both, runs `:openverify-nfc:connectedDebugAndroidTest :openverify-face:connectedDebugAndroidTest` after the smoke test, and passes `openverify-sdk=14 demo-app=2 openverify-nfc=2 openverify-face=4` (KP10-46: the contract counts the `@Test` methods of every module that holds a device test and fails a module without a minimum). `android-release.yml` does the same, compiles both modules' device tests and measures the three non-default configurations.
6. **KP10-51 is not taken:** no edit of the per-push Gradle line builds `minifiedSmoke`, so `check-consumer-rules.py` still prints "minifiedSmoke: not built here" there. Phase 11, with the job's first whole time.
7. **P2 on the web.** `apps/flow/licenses/onnxruntime-web/EIGEN-SOURCE.txt` is served at `/licenses/onnxruntime-web/EIGEN-SOURCE.txt`, pinned by SHA-256 like ONNX Runtime's two files (`efdf1e5ba`, `9d0d76030`). Phase 4 decision 11 is settled. The revision ONNX Runtime 1.30.0 and 1.31.0 both build is `1d8b82b0740839c0de7f1242a3585e3390ff5f33` of `https://github.com/eigen-mirror/eigen`. The vision image's `onnxruntime` wheel is covered by the same answer through decision 11 and is named for the owner in the PROGRESS entry.
8. **Records (C222).** `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` section 6 says what holds each exception's conditions and names the declined and pending parts; `docs/PLAN.md` sections 2, 3, 6 and 9; `docs/android/` (nfc, capture, privacy, sizes, integration, release and support, accessibility, the index); `docs/checks/nfc.md` and `selfie.md`; `docs/research/persona-surface.md`; the SDK README; both module READMEs; the demo's README; `android/CHANGELOG.md`. No public signature changed, so the changelog has no new "Breaking" entry. KP10-48 and KP10-50 are in those edits.
9. **A defect found by the clean-build pass, fixed test-first** (`9ab4ac4de`, `aa477f674`, `d6af3cc37`). The review photo of the capture screen is decoded off the main thread, and the audit's screenshot harness compared a golden before the photo was drawn when the machine was loaded (R8 in the same Gradle call, as `ci.yml`'s second step runs it). The photo now says when it is decoded through the test-only semantics property `ReviewPhotoDecoded`, and `A11yStageDriver.show` settles a screen with a review photo only once it is. One 10D golden, `capture-review-ar-dark-2x`, had been recorded without its photo and was re-recorded by an explicit record call. **Binding: a host test that compares a screenshot of something decoded or loaded off the main thread waits for a stated condition, never for idle alone.** The golden count is unchanged (205); no SDK screen, state or text was added.

10. **The Validation block gains** `python3 -I scripts/fetch_fixtures.py --only person_a_id` beside the model fetch, before any Gradle call. Call (5) is `:demo-app:assembleRelease :demo-app:bundleRelease :openverify-nfc:assembleRelease :openverify-face:assembleRelease`; call (8) adds `:openverify-nfc:assembleDebugAndroidTest :openverify-face:assembleDebugAndroidTest`; call (11) is three calls (`sdk,nfc`, `sdk,face`, `sdk,nfc,face`). The floors named in the block are `ci.yml`'s: 2,691; 97 and 9; 116; 51. The device minimum is `openverify-sdk=14 demo-app=2 openverify-nfc=2 openverify-face=4`.
11. **Times at 10E's close** (lock wait 0 s on every call): call (1) with `--rerun` 159, 45 and 97 s for 522, 416 and 1,753 tests; call (3) 231 s; call (7) 179 s; call (8) 184 s; the consumer check 210 s; each manifest call 96 to 113 s. From an empty Gradle home the gate line's six tasks took **1,519 s (25.3 minutes)** with the downloads (10D: 779 s) and 871 s without them; the second step 196 s. No call neared 10 minutes; the gate line does not fit one call.
12. **The clean-build pass** ran as section 10 of the TDD file says. Its truly cold run proved the downloads of the gate line; after the fix of item 9, Maven Central answered 429 to two further empty homes, so the counting run was made from clean in the first cold home with its build cache removed. Every step of `ci.yml`'s android job and every device-less, secret-less step of `android-release.yml`'s gate exited 0.
